# Art, Culture, Literature & Monuments — RRB NTPC Complete Study Material

---

## Overview
| Aspect | Details |
|--------|---------|
| **Topic** | Indian Art, Culture, Literature, Monuments & Heritage — State-by-State Encyclopedic Guide |
| **Section** | General Awareness (40 Qs, 40 Marks) |
| **Expected Questions** | 3–5 questions per exam shift |
| **Importance** | HIGH — One of the most frequently tested topics across RRB NTPC, SSC, and State-level exams |
| **Coverage** | All 28 States + 8 Union Territories in Alphabetical Order |

> **How to use this guide:** Each state/UT entry covers Languages, History, Dances, Music & Art Forms, Literature, Festivals, Tribes, Important Cities, Monuments & Heritage Sites, UNESCO World Heritage Sites, and High-Yield Exam Facts. Focus on the **High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts** bullet points at the end of each state for last-minute revision.

---

## States of India (Alphabetical Order)

### 1. Andhra Pradesh

**Capital:** Amaravati  
**Formation:** 1 October 1953 (Andhra State, first linguistic state in India, carved from Madras State with Kurnool as capital); 1 November 1956 (merged with Telangana region as unified Andhra Pradesh); 2 June 2014 (bifurcated into residuary Andhra Pradesh and Telangana under Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2014)  
**Official Language(s):** Telugu (Primary Official Language; Eighth Schedule & Classical Language since 2008), Urdu (Second Official Language across all districts via 2022 Amendment)  
**Other Languages:** English, Tamil, Odia, Kannada, Lambadi (Banjara), Savara, Gondi, Koya, Kui

#### Brief History
- **Ancient Period:** Mentioned in early texts (*Aitareya Brahmana*) as home to the *Andhras*; Mauryan rule left Ashokan Rock Edicts at **Erragudi** and **Rajulamandagiri** (Kurnool). The **Satavahana Dynasty** (capital *Dhanyakataka*/Amaravati) patronized Prakrit and Amaravati Buddhist art, followed by the **Ikshvakus** of Vijayapuri (Nagarjunakonda), **Salankayanas**, and **Vishnukundinas** (Undavalli Caves).
- **Early & Late Medieval Period:** The **Eastern Chalukyas of Vengi** (notably Rajaraja Narendra in the 11th century) fostered the birth of Telugu literature under poet Nannaya. Subsequent rulers included the Cholas, Velanati Chodas, **Kakatiyas** (port of Motupalli), and **Reddi Kings** of Kondavidu and Rajahmundry.
- **Vijayanagara Empire (1336–1646 CE):** Rayalaseema and coastal Andhra flourished under **Sri Krishnadevaraya** (Tuluva dynasty), patron of the *Ashtadiggajas*, Lepakshi temple arts, and later the Aravidu dynasty at **Chandragiri** and **Penukonda** forts.
- **Colonial & Freedom Movement:** British East India Company established its first factory on the Coromandel Coast at **Machilipatnam (1611)**; early anti-British resistance included the **Battle of Padmanabham (1794)** and the **Poligar revolt of Uyyalawada Narasimha Reddy (1846–47)**. During the nationalist era, **Alluri Sitarama Raju** led the **Rampa Rebellion (1922–24)**; **Duggirala Gopalakrishnayya** led the **Chirala-Perala movement (1921)**; **Tanguturi Prakasam Pantulu** (*Andhra Kesari*), **Kandukuri Veeresalingam** (social reformer), and **Pingali Venkayya** (designer of the Indian National Flag) played pivotal roles. **Potti Sriramulu's** 56-day fast unto death in 1952 led to the creation of Andhra State on 1 October 1953.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Kuchipudi | Classical | Originated in Kuchipudi village (Krishna district), codified by **Siddhendra Yogi** (*Bhamakalapam*); famous for **Tarangam** (dancing on the rim of a brass plate with a pot on the head) and combining *Nritta*, *Nritya*, and *Natya* (spoken dialogue). |
| Andhra Natyam | Classical | Ancient temple and court dance lineage (*Nattuva Mela* / *Aradhana Nrityam*) revived in modern times by **Nataraja Ramakrishna**. |
| Vilasini Natyam | Classical | Traditional *Devadasi* temple-and-court dance of the Telugu region focusing on expressive *abhinaya* and lyrical storytelling; revived by **Swapnasundari**. |
| Burrakatha | Folk | Oral ballad storytelling performed by a trio (main narrator with *tambura* and *andela* rings, flanked by two *dholak/dakki* co-singers). |
| Veeranatyam | Folk | Vigorous "Dance of the Brave" performed by Veerashaiva communities (especially around Draksharama/East Godavari) featuring long *Veerabhadra* spears, flaming camphor bowls, and rhythmic drums. |
| Dhimsa | Folk / Tribal | Joyous circular group dance performed by **Araku Valley** tribes (Bagata, Valmiki, Khond, Porja) during festivals, fairs, and weddings to foster tribal unity. |
| Tappeta Gullu | Folk | High-energy rain-invoking dance of North Coastal Andhra (Srikakulam/Vizianagaram) where performers strap crescent-shaped tin drums (*gullu*) to their chests and execute acrobatic leaps. |
| Butta Bommalu | Folk | "Basket-doll" mask dance from West Godavari (Tanuku region) in which dancers wear giant hollow bamboo-and-papier-mâché figures of folk/mythological characters. |
| Kolattam | Folk | Rhythmic stick dance performed in pairs and concentric circles to devotional and harvest songs; *Chekka Bhajana* is a related clap-and-plank dance. |
| Dappu | Folk | Energetic percussion dance led by beats on circular goat-skin frame drums (*dappu*), central to village processions and festivities. |
| Lambadi | Folk / Tribal | Colorful dance of the nomadic Banjara/Lambadi community in mirror-worked attire and heavy ivory/brass bangles, depicting harvesting and daily life. |
| Garagalu | Folk | Ritual dance in which performers balance decorated earthen pots (*garagas*) filled with offerings on their heads during village goddess *Jataras*. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Music Traditions:** Deeply anchored in **Carnatic Music**; **Saint Tallapaka Annamacharya** (*Pada Kavita Pitamaha*, 15th c., Tirumala) composed over 32,000 *Sankirtanas* in praise of Lord Venkateswara. **Kshetrayya** (17th c., Muvva, Krishna dist) pioneered romantic *Padams*, while **Adibhatla Narayana Dasu** is hailed as *Harikatha Pitamaha* (father of Harikatha storytelling).
- **Musical Instruments:** **Bobbili Veena** (*Ekanda Veena* carved from a single log of jackfruit wood in Bobbili, Vizianagaram — **GI Tag**).
- **Painting Styles:** **Srikalahasti Kalamkari** (hand-drawn bamboo *kalam* pen art using vegetable dyes on cotton, depicting temple narratives — **GI Tag**); **Machilipatnam Kalamkari** (wooden block-printed floral/Persian-influenced textiles — **GI Tag**); **Lepakshi Mural Paintings** (16th-century Vijayanagara frescoes on the ceiling of Veerabhadra Temple); **Savara Wall Paintings (*Idital*)** (tribal geometric murals in Srikakulam/Manyam).
- **Theatre & Puppetry:** **Tholu Bommalata** (traditional translucent colored leather shadow puppetry — Nimmalakunta craft holds a **GI Tag**); **Surabhi Theatre** (century-old family-run travelling proscenium theatre known for mythological spectacles); **Yakshagana** (folk dance-drama tradition).
- **Handicrafts & Textiles (GI Tagged):** **Kondapalli Bommalu** (lightweight *Tella Poniki* softwood toys), **Etikoppaka Toys** (*Ankudu* wood coated with natural lac/dyes), **Budithi Bell & Brass Metal Craft** (Srikakulam), **Udayagiri Wooden Cutlery** (Nellore), **Durgi Stone Carvings** (Palnadu), **Allagadda Stone Carving** (Nandyal), **Narasapur Crochet Lace**, **Uppada Jamdani Sarees**, **Venkatagiri Sarees**, **Mangalagiri Sarees & Fabrics**, **Dharmavaram Handloom Pattu Sarees**, and **Ponduru Khadi** (ultra-fine handspun cotton of Srikakulam).

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Ugadi | Chaitra Shukla Padyami (Mar/Apr) | Telugu New Year marked by reading *Panchangam* and tasting *Ugadi Pachadi* (six flavors symbolizing life's emotions). |
| Makar Sankranti | Mid-January (13–16 Jan) | Major four-day harvest festival comprising *Bhogi* (bonfires), *Sankranti*, *Kanuma* (cattle worship), and *Mukkanuma*, with *Gangireddulu* and *Haridasu* processions. |
| Srivari Brahmotsavam | Sep/Oct (Ashwayuja) | Nine-day grand annual festival at Tirumala Venkateswara Temple attracting millions of devotees. |
| Flamingo Festival | Dec/Jan | Three-day eco-tourism and migratory bird festival celebrated at Pulicat Lake and Nelapattu Bird Sanctuary (Tirupati/Nellore region). |
| Visakha Utsav | Dec/Jan | Annual cultural, culinary, and beach tourism festival held in Visakhapatnam. |
| Lumbini Festival | December | Three-day state festival celebrating Andhra's ancient Buddhist heritage at Nagarjunakonda and Amaravati. |
| Rottela Panduga | Muharram (Islamic Calendar) | Unique communal harmony festival at **Bara Shaheed Dargah** (Nellore) where devotees exchange *rotis* across Swarnala Cheruvu tank. |
| Maha Shivaratri | Phalguna (Feb/Mar) | Major Shaivite pilgrimages and *Prabha* fairs at Srisailam Mallikarjuna Jyotirlinga and Kotappakonda. |
| Atla Tadde | Ashwayuja (Oct) | Traditional women's moonrise festival with swings (*uyyala*) and *atlu* (rice pancakes). |
| Deccan / Lepakshi Utsav | Winter/Spring | Heritage cultural festival showcasing Vijayanagara art, classical dance, and handicrafts at Lepakshi. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|----------|
| Chenchu | Nallamala Hills (Kurnool, Nandyal, Prakasam) | **PVTG**; traditional forest hunter-gatherers and honey collectors; deeply associated with Srisailam Mallikarjuna and Chenchu Lakshmi legends. |
| Savara (Sora) | Srikakulam, Parvathipuram Manyam | **PVTG** (Hill Savara); famous for sacred **Idital** geometric wall paintings depicting spirits and village life. |
| Konda Reddi | Papikonda Hills (Alluri Sitharama Raju, Eluru) | **PVTG**; bamboo craftsmanship, shifting (*Podu*) cultivation, and *Mamidikotha* (mango harvest) festival. |
| Koya | East & West Godavari, Alluri Sitharama Raju | Bison-horn headgear dances (*Permakokata*), *Bijja Pandum* (seed festival), and *Konda* worship. |
| Bagata, Valmiki, Khond, Gadaba | Araku Valley / Visakhapatnam Agency | Performers of **Dhimsa** dance; **Itikala Panduga** (Chaitra hunting festival); cultivators of GI-tagged **Araku Valley Arabica Coffee**. |
| Jatapu | Vizianagaram, Srikakulam | Agrarian hill tribe celebrating *Kandulakotha* (red gram harvest) and *Jakara* goddess worship. |
| Yanadi | Nellore, Tirupati, Krishna Delta | Traditional fishing, medicinal herb gathering, snake-catching skills, and folk songs. |
| Sugali (Lambadi) | Rayalaseema & Coastal AP | Distinct *Ghumar/Lambadi* dance, mirror-work attire, *Teej* festival, and settled *Thanda* hamlets. |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Amaravati | Official **State Capital** on the Krishna River; ancient Satavahana capital (*Dhanyakataka*) and major Buddhist Mahachaitya site. |
| Visakhapatnam | Largest city ("City of Destiny"); natural deep-water port, **Eastern Naval Command HQ**, Visakhapatnam Steel Plant, INS Kurusura Submarine Museum, Kailasagiri. |
| Vijayawada | Major commercial and railway junction on the Krishna River; **Kanaka Durga Temple** (Indrakeeladri), Prakasam Barrage, Undavalli & Mogalarajapuram caves nearby. |
| Tirupati | World-renowned pilgrimage city housing **Tirumala Venkateswara Temple** (richest Hindu temple), SV University, and GI-tagged **Tirupati Laddu**. |
| Rajamahendravaram (Rajahmundry) | "Cultural Capital of Andhra Pradesh" on the Godavari River; birthplace of Telugu poetry under Nannaya, paper mills, Godavari Pushkaralu hub. |
| Kakinada | Deep-water port city, fertilizer & petrochemical hub, **Coringa Mangroves**, Kaja sweet, and nearby **Uppada** Jamdani weaving center. |
| Guntur | Major agricultural trading hub famous for **Guntur Sannam Chilli (GI Tag)**, cotton, and tobacco. |
| Machilipatnam | Historic Coromandel port (*Masulipatam*); first English factory (1611), **Kalamkari** block printing, **Bandar Laddu (GI Tag)**, and rolled gold jewellery. |
| Nellore | Aquaculture ("Shrimp Capital of India") and paddy hub on Penna River; **Sriharikota (SDSC-SHAR)** nearby (Tirupati dist) and Bara Shaheed Dargah. |
| Kurnool | First capital of Andhra State (1953–1956); Konda Reddy Fort, Orvakal Rock Garden, and gateway to **Belum Caves** (Nandyal dist). |
| Srikalahasti | "Dakshina Kailasam" on Swarnamukhi River; famous Vayu Linga Shiva temple (Rahu-Ketu shrine) and hand-painted **Srikalahasti Kalamkari**. |
| Kadapa | Mining & limestone hub; **Gandikota** ("Grand Canyon of India" on Penna River) and Ameen Peer Dargah. |
| Anantapur / Dharmavaram | Drought-prone Rayalaseema belt turned auto/renewable hub; **Dharmavaram silk sarees (GI Tag)** and nearby **Lepakshi Temple**. |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Sri Venkateswara Temple, Tirumala | Pallavas, Cholas, Vijayanagara (Krishnadevaraya) | Located on Seshachalam Hills (Tirupati); one of the most visited and wealthiest religious shrines in the world; Dravidian architecture with gilded *Ananda Nilayam* vimana. |
| Veerabhadra Temple, Lepakshi | Vijayanagara Empire (Virupanna & Veeranna, c. 1530 CE) | Famous for its **hanging pillar**, world's largest **monolithic Nandi (bull)**, giant Nagalinga, and Vijayanagara ceiling frescoes. |
| Amaravati Mahachaitya Stupa | Mauryan / Satavahana Dynasty (3rd c. BCE–3rd c. CE) | Great Buddhist stupa showcasing the Amaravati School of Art using white/greenish Palnad limestone (*chaya* slabs). |
| Nagarjunakonda | Ikshvaku Dynasty (3rd–4th c. CE) | Ancient Buddhist learning center (*Vijayapuri*); holds India's **only island museum** reconstructed above Nagarjunasagar reservoir. |
| Undavalli Caves, Guntur | Vishnukundina Dynasty (4th–5th c. CE) | Four-storeyed monolithic rock-cut sandstone caves featuring a massive 5-metre reclining **Anantapadmanabha (Vishnu)** statue. |
| Mallikarjuna Swamy Temple, Srisailam | Satavahanas, Chalukyas, Kakatiyas, Vijayanagara | Situated in Nallamala Hills (Nandyal dist); uniquely revered as both one of the **12 Jyotirlingas** and one of the **18 Maha Shakti Peethas**. |
| Kanaka Durga Temple, Vijayawada | Ancient / Eastern Chalukyas | Presiding deity of Vijayawada atop Indrakeeladri Hill overlooking the Krishna River. |
| Varaha Lakshmi Narasimha Temple, Simhachalam | Eastern Ganga Dynasty (Narasimhadeva I, 13th c. CE) | Unique fusion of Kalinga (Odisha) *Rekha Deul* tower and South Indian Dravidian mandapas; deity covered in sandalwood paste (*Chandanotsavam*). |
| Gandikota Fort, Kadapa | Kamma / Pemmasani Nayakas & Qutb Shahis (12th–17th c.) | Spectacular red-granite gorge fortress on the Penna River, popularly called the **"Grand Canyon of India."** |
| Chandragiri Fort, Tirupati | Yadavarayas & Vijayanagara Aravidu Dynasty (11th–16th c.) | Last capital of Vijayanagara kings; Raja Mahal Palace showcases Indo-Saracenic Vijayanagara arches. |
| Bojjannakonda, Bavikonda & Thotlakonda | Theravada / Mahayana / Vajrayana eras (3rd c. BCE–3rd c. CE) | Major rock-cut Buddhist monastic hill complexes near Visakhapatnam and Anakapalli. |
| Suryanarayana Temple, Arasavalli | Eastern Ganga King Devendravarma (7th c. CE) | Ancient Sun Temple in Srikakulam designed so sunrays touch the deity's feet twice a year (March & October). |
| Belum Caves & Borra Caves | Natural Geological Formations | **Belum Caves** (Nandyal) is the **2nd largest cave system in the Indian subcontinent**; **Borra Caves** (Alluri Sitharama Raju) feature karst stalactites/stalagmites. |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **First Linguistic State:** Andhra State (1 Oct 1953) was the first state formed on a linguistic basis; **Telugu** was conferred **Classical Language** status in **2008**.
- **Coastline:** Andhra Pradesh has the **2nd longest coastline in India** (~974 km) after Gujarat.
- **Spaceport:** **Satish Dhawan Space Centre (SDSC-SHAR)**, India's primary orbital launch center, is located on **Sriharikota** island (Tirupati district).
- **GI Tags:** Tirupati Laddu, Bandar Laddu, Atreyapuram Pootharekulu, Guntur Sannam Chilli, Banganapalle Mangoes, Araku Valley Arabica Coffee, Srikalahasti & Machilipatnam Kalamkari, Kondapalli Bommalu, Etikoppaka Toys, Bobbili Veena, Budithi Bell & Brass Craft, Udayagiri Wooden Cutlery, Nimmalakunta Leather Puppetry, Durgi & Allagadda Stone Carvings, Narasapur Crochet Lace, Uppada Jamdani, Venkatagiri, Mangalagiri & Dharmavaram Handloom Sarees.
- **National Parks (3):** **Sri Venkateswara National Park** (Tirupati/Kadapa), **Papikonda National Park** (Alluri Sitharama Raju/Eluru — Bison Hills on Godavari), **Rajiv Gandhi (Rameswaram) National Park** (Kadapa).
- **Notable Wildlife Sanctuaries & Ramsar Site:** **Nagarjunsagar-Srisailam Tiger Reserve** (India's largest Tiger Reserve, shared with Telangana), **Coringa Wildlife Sanctuary** (Kakinada — 2nd largest mangrove forest in India), **Pulicat Lake Bird Sanctuary** (2nd largest brackish water lagoon in India), **Rollapadu Wildlife Sanctuary** (Great Indian Bustard), **Sri Lankamalleswara Wildlife Sanctuary** (only known natural habitat of the critically endangered **Jerdon's Courser**), **Koundinya Wildlife Sanctuary** (only Asian elephant sanctuary in AP). **Ramsar Site (1):** **Kolleru Lake** (designated 2002; largest shallow freshwater lake between Krishna & Godavari deltas).
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Blackbuck** (*Krishna Jinka*); State Bird: **Rose-ringed Parakeet** (*Rama Chiluka*); State Tree: **Neem** (*Vepa Chettu*); State Flower: **Jasmine** (*Mallepuvvu*); State Fruit: **Mango**.

### 2. Arunachal Pradesh

**Capital:** Itanagar  
**Formation:** 20 January 1972 (Union Territory carved out of NEFA — North-East Frontier Agency); 20 February 1987 (Full-fledged 24th State of India via 55th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1986)  
**Official Language(s):** English  
**Other Languages:** Nyishi, Adi, Apatani, Galo, Monpa, Mishmi (Idu, Digaru, Miju), Nocte, Wancho, Tangsa, Tai Khamti, Tagin, Hindi (widely spoken lingua franca)

#### Brief History
- **Ancient & Epic Connections:** Mentioned in the *Kalika Purana* and *Mahabharata*; associated with **Parashuram Kund** (Lohit), **Bhishmaknagar** (linked to King Bhishmaka and Rukmini), and **Malinithan**.
- **Medieval Rule:** The **Chutia Kingdom** (12th–16th c. CE) constructed **Ita Fort**, **Bhishmaknagar**, and **Malinithan**. In the west, **Tawang Monastery** was founded in **1680–81 CE** by **Merak Lama Lodre Gyatso** under the patronage of the 5th Dalai Lama. The **Ahom rulers** maintained peaceful relations with hill tribes via the traditional ***Posa* system**.
- **British Colonial Administration:** Introduced the **Inner Line Regulation (1873)** under the Bengal Eastern Frontier Regulation; in **1914**, the **Shimla Accord** drew the **McMahon Line** as the boundary between India and Tibet. The region was governed as the *North-East Frontier Tracts* (1914) and renamed **North-East Frontier Agency (NEFA)** in 1954.
- **Modern Era & 1962 Conflict:** Witnessed fierce resistance during the **1962 Sino-Indian War** (at Tawang, Sela Pass, Jaswant Garh, and Walong — immortalized by **Rifleman Jaswant Singh Rawat**, MVC). Administered by the Ministry of External Affairs until 1965, NEFA became the Union Territory of Arunachal Pradesh on 20 January 1972 and gained statehood on **20 February 1987**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Ponung | Folk / Tribal | Pre-harvest women's dance of the **Adi** tribe led by a male *Miri* singer narrating myths and agricultural tales with rhythmic swaying. |
| Aji Lamu | Folk / Tribal | Tibetan-Buddhist masked pantomime dance of the **Monpa** tribe depicting stories from the Ramayana and Buddhist folklore. |
| Lion and Peacock Dance | Folk / Tribal | Vibrant Monpa festival dance mimicking the graceful movements of lions and peacocks accompanied by cymbals and drums. |
| Bardo Chham | Folk / Tribal | Masked dance-drama of the **Sherdukpen** tribe portraying the eternal battle and triumph of good over evil forces. |
| Popir | Folk / Tribal | Graceful women's dance of the **Galo** tribe performed during the **Mopin** festival honoring Goddess Mopin Ane. |
| Pasi Kongki | Folk / Tribal | Heroic ballad dance of the **Adi** tribe narrating ancestral migrations and social harmony. |
| Roppi | Folk / Tribal | Traditional warrior and victory dance performed by men of the **Nyishi** tribe. |
| Buiya & Chalo | Folk / Tribal | Celebratory community dances of the **Digaru Mishmi** and **Nocte** tribes respectively during harvest and *Loku* festivals. |
| Wancho Dance | Folk / Tribal | Rhythmic ceremonial dance performed by **Wancho** men and women around bonfires during the **Oriah** festival. |
| Ka Fifai | Folk / Tribal | Narrative theatrical dance-drama of the **Tai Khamti** Buddhist community. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Music & Oral Traditions:** Rich priestly and shamanistic chants such as **Nyibu** chants (Nyishi), **Aabang** cosmological epics (Adi/Tani tribes), **Jajin** ceremonial songs (Apatani), and **Baryi** ballads.
- **Traditional Instruments:** *Dungchen* (long Tibetan brass horns), *Kham* (Tai Khamti drum), *Yangkong* (bamboo jaw harp), *Yoksha* (sword-shaped percussion), and *Emul* (metal disc).
- **Painting & Manuscripts:** **Thangka** Buddhist scroll paintings on cloth/silk (Monpa/Sherdukpen); **Monpa Handmade Paper (*Mon Shugu*)** made from the medicinal bark of the *Shugu Sheng* tree for Buddhist scriptures (**GI Tag**); **Tai Khamti** palm-leaf manuscripts.
- **Handicrafts & Textiles (GI Tagged):** Loin-loom weaving is the hallmark of Arunachal women — **Idu Mishmi Textiles**, **Apatani Textiles**, **Nyishi Textiles**, **Galo Textiles**, **Adi Textiles**, **Tangsa Textiles**, and **Tai Khamti Textiles** all hold **GI Tags**. Other notable crafts include **Wancho Wooden Craft (GI Tag)**, **Dao** (hand-forged traditional broadsword — **GI Tag**), cane-and-bamboo suspension bridges, and woven headgear (like the Nyishi *Bopa* cane helmet).

| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Oral *Aabang* & *Miri* Epics | Traditional cosmological myths, migration ballads, and *Nyibu* chants | Adi / Nyishi / Apatani / Galo |
| Lummer Dai | *Paharor Xile Xile*, *Prithibir Hahi*, *Mon Aru Mon* ("Doyen of Arunachalee Literature") | Assamese |
| Yeshe Dorjee Thongchi | *Mauna Ounth Mukhar Hriday* (Sahitya Akademi Award, 2005), *Sava Kota Manuh* | Assamese |
| Mamang Dai | *The Black Hill* (Sahitya Akademi Award, 2017), *The Legends of Pensam* (Padma Shri) | English |
| Yumlam Tana | *The Man and the Tiger* | English |
| Taro Sindik | Contemporary poetry and prose (*Yuva Puraskar* recipient) | Hindi / Nyishi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Losar | Feb/Mar | 15-day New Year festival of **Monpa**, **Sherdukpen**, and **Memba** Buddhist tribes featuring monastic *Chham* dances. |
| Solung | Early September | Premier socio-agricultural festival of the **Adi** tribe celebrating harvest, livestock (*Mithun*), and *Ponung* dance. |
| Dree | 4–7 July | Major agricultural festival of the **Apatani** tribe in Ziro Valley praying for pest-free bumper crops. |
| Mopin | April (5–7 Apr) | Harvest & prosperity festival of the **Galo** tribe; celebrants smear sacred rice-flour paste (*Etting*) on each other's faces. |
| Nyokum (Nyokum Yullo) | February (26 Feb) | Principal festival of the **Nyishi** tribe invoking Goddess *Nyokum* for harmony between humans, spirits, and nature. |
| Sangken | Mid-April | Three-day Theravada Buddhist water-splashing New Year festival of the **Tai Khamti** and **Singpho** tribes. |
| Si-Donyi | January (4–6 Jan) | Major festival of the **Tagin** tribe worshipping the Earth (*Si*) and Sun (*Donyi*). |
| Reh | February (1–3 Feb) | Festival of the **Idu Mishmi** tribe worshipping divine mother *Nanyi Inyitaya* for family prosperity. |
| Oriah | Mar/Apr | Agricultural spring festival of the **Wancho** tribe marking sowing and social solidarity. |
| Torgya | January | Three-day monastic festival at **Tawang Monastery** featuring ritual masked dances to ward off evil spirits. |
| Ziro Festival of Music | September | India's renowned eco-friendly outdoor indie music festival set in the Apatani rice fields of Ziro Valley. |
| Pangsau Pass Winter Festival | January | Cross-border Indo-Myanmar cultural festival held at Nampong (Changlang). |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|----------|
| Nyishi | Papum Pare, East & West Kameng, Kurung Kumey | Largest tribe in Arunachal; cane *Bopa* headgear adorned with hornbill crest, *Nyokum* festival, GI-tagged textiles. |
| Adi | East Siang, Upper Siang, Siang | Traditional *Kebang* village democracy, *Moshup* (boys' dormitory), cane suspension bridges, *Solung* festival. |
| Apatani | Lower Subansiri (Ziro Valley) | Globally acclaimed for **sustainable wet-rice-cum-fish farming without ploughs**, *Dree* & *Myoko* festivals, traditional facial tattoos & *Yaping Hullo* nose plugs (historical custom). |
| Monpa | Tawang, West Kameng | Mahayana Buddhists following Gelugpa sect; *Tawang Monastery*, *Yak Churpi*, *Mon Shugu* handmade paper, stone-masonry architecture. |
| Galo | Leparada, West Siang | *Mopin* festival, *Popir* dance, black-and-white geometric handloom weaves (GI Tag). |
| Mishmi (Idu, Digaru, Miju) | Dibang Valley, Lower Dibang, Lohit, Anjaw | Intricate diamond-motif handloom weaving (**Idu Mishmi Textiles GI Tag**), *Reh* & *Tamladu* festivals. |
| Tai Khamti & Singpho | Namsai, Changlang | Theravada Buddhists with own script; *Sangken* water festival, **Tai Khamti Rice (GI Tag)**, and **Singpho Phalap** bamboo-smoked tea. |
| Wancho & Nocte | Longding, Tirap | Skilled wood-carvers (**Wancho Wooden Craft GI Tag**), *Oriah* & *Loku* festivals, traditional tattooing heritage. |
| Tagin, Sherdukpen, Tangsa, Bugun | Upper Subansiri, West Kameng, Changlang | Rich blend of animism (*Donyi-Polo*) and Buddhism; community conservation (Buguns protect **Eaglenest** bird habitats). |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Itanagar | **State Capital**; historic **Ita Fort**, Ganga Lake (*Gyaker Sinyi*), Gompa Buddhist Temple, and Jawaharlal Nehru State Museum. |
| Tawang | High-altitude town (~10,000 ft); **Tawang Monastery**, **Sela Pass**, Madhuri (Sangestar) Lake, and birthplace of the 6th Dalai Lama. |
| Ziro | Lower Subansiri HQ; scenic **Apatani Plateau** (UNESCO Tentative List), sustainable paddy-fish culture, **Ziro Music Festival**, and Talle Valley. |
| Pasighat | **Oldest town in Arunachal Pradesh** (est. 1911); gateway to the Siang (Brahmaputra) River and Daying Ering Wildlife Sanctuary. |
| Bomdila | West Kameng HQ; Gaden Rabgyeling Monastery, apple orchards, and gateway to **Eaglenest Wildlife Sanctuary**. |
| Namsai & Tezu | Theravada Buddhist hub; **Golden Pagoda (Kongmu Kham)** at Namsai and sacred **Parashuram Kund** near Tezu. |
| Roing | Lower Dibang Valley HQ; **Bhishmaknagar Fort**, Mayodia Pass, and Mehao Lake & Wildlife Sanctuary. |
| Miao | Changlang district; gateway to **Namdapha National Park** and historic **Stilwell (Ledo) Road** / Pangsau Pass. |
| Dong & Kibithoo | Anjaw district; **Dong Valley** receives the **first sunrise in India**; Kibithoo is India's easternmost inhabited border village. |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Tawang Monastery (*Galden Namgey Lhatse*) | Merak Lama Lodre Gyatso (1680–81 CE) | **Largest Buddhist monastery in India** and 2nd largest in the world (after Potala Palace); houses an 18-ft gilded Buddha and rare Buddhist manuscripts. |
| Ita Fort, Itanagar | Chutia / Jitari Dynasty (14th–15th c. CE) | Literally **"Fort of Bricks,"** constructed using over 8 million bricks; gave Itanagar its name. |
| Malinithan, Likabali | Chutia Era (14th–15th c. CE) | Archaeological temple complex in Lower Siang with finely carved granite sculptures of Durga, Indra, Surya, and Ganesha. |
| Bhishmaknagar Fort, Roing | Chutia / Kalita Kings (12th–15th c. CE) | Oldest archaeological mud-and-brick fort site in Arunachal Pradesh; linked to Princess Rukmini of Mahabharata lore. |
| Urgelling Monastery, Tawang | Urgen Sangpo (15th c. CE) | Sacred birthplace of the **6th Dalai Lama, Tsangyang Gyatso** (1683 CE). |
| Jaswant Garh & Tawang War Memorials | Modern Era (Post-1962) | Commemorates Indian soldiers of the 1962 Sino-Indian War, notably Rifleman **Jaswant Singh Rawat**. |
| Golden Pagoda (*Kongmu Kham*), Namsai | Modern (2010) | Ornate Burmese-style Theravada Buddhist monastery complex surrounded by landscaped gardens. |
| Jairampur World War II Cemetery | World War II Era | Rests ~1,000 Allied soldiers who died constructing the **Stilwell Road**. |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| None inscribed yet | — | — |

*(Note: **Apatani Cultural Landscape**, **Thembang Fortified Village**, and **Namdapha National Park** are on UNESCO's Tentative List.)*

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- Known as **"Land of the Dawn-Lit Mountains"** (*Arunachal* = sunrise mountains) and **"Orchid State of India"** (hosts 500+ orchid species).
- **Lowest population density in India** (**17 persons per sq. km**, 2011 Census); **Dong Valley** (Anjaw) witnesses **India's first sunrise**.
- **Inner Line Permit (ILP)** is required for entry under the Bengal Eastern Frontier Regulation, 1873.
- **GI Tags (20+ items):** Arunachal (Wakro) Orange, Idu Mishmi Textiles, Apatani Textiles, Nyishi Textiles, Galo Textiles, Adi Textiles, Tangsa Textiles, Tai Khamti Textiles, Monpa Textiles, Monpa Handmade Paper (*Mon Shugu*), Wancho Wooden Craft, Dao (traditional sword), Adi Kekir (ginger), Adi Apong, Tai Khamti Rice (*Khaw Tai*), Yak Churpi, Singpho Phalap (tea), Angnyat Millet, Marua Apo.
- **National Parks (2):** **Namdapha National Park** (Changlang — 4th largest NP in India; **only national park in the world harboring all 4 big cats**: Tiger, Leopard, Snow Leopard, and Clouded Leopard; also Western Hoolock Gibbon) and **Mouling National Park** (Upper Siang).
- **Notable Wildlife Sanctuaries & Tiger Reserves:** **Pakke (Pakhui) Tiger Reserve** (famous for Hornbill Nest Adoption Programme), **Kamlang Tiger Reserve** (Lohit), **Eaglenest Wildlife Sanctuary** (West Kameng — global birding hotspot where **Bugun Liocichla** was discovered), **Dibang Wildlife Sanctuary** (largest WLS in the state; Mishmi Takin), **Sessa Orchid Sanctuary**, **Talle Valley WLS**, **Mehao WLS**, **Daying Ering Memorial WLS**.
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Mithun** (*Bos frontalis*); State Bird: **Great Hornbill** (*Buceros bicornis*); State Tree: **Hollong** (*Dipterocarpus macrocarpus*); State Flower: **Foxtail Orchid** (*Rhynchostylis retusa*).

### 3. Assam

**Capital:** Dispur (located within Guwahati)  
**Formation:** 26 January 1950 (Constituted as a State of India; Assam Province earlier formed in 1874 and 1912; Nagaland, Meghalaya, Mizoram & Arunachal Pradesh were later carved out)  
**Official Language(s):** Assamese (Primary Official; Eighth Schedule & **Classical Language** conferred on 3 October 2024), Bodo (Associate Official Language statewide via 2020 Amendment; Eighth Schedule), Bengali (Official in Barak Valley districts: Cachar, Karimganj/Sribhumi, Hailakandi), Manipuri (Associate Official Language in Cachar, Karimganj/Sribhumi, Hailakandi & Hojai via 2024 Amendment)  
**Other Languages:** Karbi, Mishing, Dimasa, Tiwa, Rabha, Deori, Sadri, Nepali, Hindi, English

#### Brief History
- **Ancient Kamarupa (Pragjyotisha):** Ruled by the **Varman Dynasty** (4th–7th c. CE; **Bhaskaravarman** was an ally of Harshavardhana and hosted Chinese traveler **Xuanzang / Hiuen Tsang**), followed by the **Mlechchha (Salastambha)** and **Pala Dynasties** of Kamarupa.
- **Ahom Kingdom (1228–1826 CE):** Founded by Tai prince **Chaolung Sukapha**; ruled Assam for nearly 600 years, introducing the *Paik* system and *Buranji* historical chronicles. Repelled 17 Mughal invasions, culminating in the decisive **Battle of Saraighat (1671)** led by **Lachit Borphukan** against Mughal general Ram Singh I.
- **Contemporary Regional Kingdoms & Bhakti Renaissance:** **Koch Dynasty** (Naranarayan & general **Chilarai**, who rebuilt Kamakhya Temple in 1565), **Chutia Kingdom**, and **Dimasa/Kachari Kingdom**. In the 15th–16th centuries, **Srimanta Sankaradeva** spearheaded the **Neo-Vaishnavite Bhakti Movement (*Ekasarana Dharma*)**, establishing *Satras* (monasteries) and *Namghars* (prayer halls).
- **Colonial Era & Freedom Struggle:** After Burmese invasions, the **Treaty of Yandabo (1826)** annexed Assam into British India. Key resistance milestones include **1857 Revolt martyrs Maniram Dewan and Piyali Phukan**, agrarian uprisings at **Phulaguri Dhawa (1861)** and **Patharughat (1894)**, **1921 INC Pandu session**, and **1942 Quit India martyrs Kanaklata Barua and Bhogeswari Phukanani**. **Gopinath Bordoloi** (Bharat Ratna) prevented Assam's inclusion in East Pakistan during Partition.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Sattriya | Classical | Created in the 15th c. CE by **Srimanta Sankaradeva** in Vaishnavite *Satras* (especially Majuli) as part of *Ankiya Naat* / *Bhaona*; recognized as India's 8th Classical Dance by Sangeet Natak Akademi in **2000**. |
| Bihu Dance | Folk | Exhilarating spring folk dance of **Bohag (Rongali) Bihu** featuring brisk steps, hand gestures, and accompaniment of *Dhol*, *Pepa*, *Gogona*, and *Toka*. |
| Bagurumba | Folk / Tribal | Graceful **"Butterfly Dance"** of the **Bodo** tribe mimicking butterflies, birds, and swaying trees in green-and-yellow *Dokhona* attire. |
| Bortal Nritya | Folk | Devotional dance developed by **Narahari Burha Bhakat** in Barpeta Satra, where dancers wield large brass cymbals (*Bortal*) in intricate choreography. |
| Ojapali | Folk | Ancient narrative dance-drama led by a principal singer (*Oja*) and chorus (*Pali*) using hand mudras; two forms: *Byahgoa* (epics) and *Suknanni* (Manasa Devi). |
| Jhumur | Folk | Vibrant folk dance of Assam's **Tea-Tribe (Adivasi)** communities performed to *Madal* drums during Karam and Tusu festivals. |
| Deodhani | Folk | Ecstatic shamanistic ritual dance dedicated to Snake Goddess **Manasa** (Maroi Puja) and Kamakhya, featuring sword-and-shield sequences. |
| Gumrag (Gumrag Soman) | Folk / Tribal | Joyous harvest and spring dance of the **Mishing** tribe performed during **Ali-Aye-Ligang**. |
| Baidima | Folk / Tribal | Traditional circle dance of the **Dimasa** tribe performed during **Bishu** festival. |
| Chomangkan | Folk / Tribal | Elaborate four-day ceremonial dance of the **Karbi** tribe performed to honor departed ancestors. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Music Traditions:** **Borgeet** (classical devotional ragas in *Brajavali* composed by Sankaradeva and Madhavadeva); **Zikir** and **Zari** (Assamese Sufi devotional songs by 17th-century saint **Ajan Pir**); **Goalpariya Lokageet** (immortalized by Padma Shri **Pratima Barua Pandey**); **Kamrupi Lokageet**; **Tokari Geet**; and **Bihu Geet**.
- **Musical Instruments:** **Assam Bihu Dhol** (**GI Tag**), **Pepa** (buffalo-horn windpipe), **Gogona** (bamboo jaw harp), **Khol** (terracotta double-headed drum), **Bortal** (cymbals), **Toka** (split bamboo clapper), **Sifung** (long 5-hole Bodo flute — **GI Tag**), **Serja** (Bodo bowed lute — **GI Tag**), and **Kham** (Bodo drum — **GI Tag**).
- **Theatre & Performance:** **Ankiya Naat** and **Bhaona** (15th-century one-act Vaishnavite plays introduced by Sankaradeva); **Bhramyaman (Mobile Theatre)** of Assam (world's unique commercial repertory touring theatre pioneered by **Achyut Lahkar** in Pathsala).
- **Paintings, Manuscripts & Textiles (GI Tagged):** **Majuli Manuscript Painting** (illustrated Vaishnavite texts on *Sanchipat* agar-bark — **GI Tag**); **Majuli Mask Craft (*Mukha Shilpa*)** (Samaguri Satra bamboo-and-clay theatrical masks — **GI Tag**); **Muga Silk** ("Golden Silk" unique to Assam — **GI Tag**), **Pat Silk**, **Eri Silk** ("Peace Silk"), and **Gamosa** (**GI Tag**).
- **Other Handicrafts (GI Tagged):** **Sarthebari Bell Metal Craft** (*Kanh* utensils of Barpeta — **GI Tag**), **Assam Jaapi** (decorated conical bamboo sun-hat — **GI Tag**), **Asharikandi Terracotta Craft** (Dhubri — **GI Tag**), **Pani Meteka Craft** (water hyacinth basketry — **GI Tag**), **Bodo Textiles** (*Dokhona*, *Aronai*, *Jwmgra*, *Gamsa*, *Eri Silk* — **GI Tags**), and **Mishing Handloom Products** (**GI Tag**).

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Bohag Bihu (Rongali Bihu) | Mid-April (14–15 Apr) | Assamese New Year and spring sowing festival; celebrated with Bihu dance, *Husori* carols, and *Gamosa* gifting. |
| Magh Bihu (Bhogali Bihu) | Mid-January (14–15 Jan) | Harvest feasting festival featuring *Uruka* community dinners, bamboo *Meji* bonfires, *Pitha* sweets, and traditional sports. |
| Kati Bihu (Kongali Bihu) | Mid-October | Solemn agricultural festival of lighting *Akash Banti* (sky lamps) in paddy fields and *Saki* under *Tulsi* plants to protect crops. |
| Ambubachi Mela | June (Ashadha) | Massive Tantric Shakti congregation at **Kamakhya Temple** (Guwahati) celebrating the annual symbolic menstruation of Mother Goddess Kamakhya. |
| Majuli Raas Mahotsav | November (Kartik Purnima) | Grand theatrical festival enacting Lord Krishna's *Raas Leela* across Vaishnavite *Satras* on Majuli Island. |
| Ali-Aye-Ligang | Feb/Mar (1st Wednesday of Phagun) | Spring paddy-sowing festival of the **Mishing** tribe with *Gumrag* dance and *Apong* rice beer. |
| Me-Dam-Me-Phi | 31 January | Annual **Ahom** community ancestor-worship festival honoring departed forefathers (*Dam*) and gods (*Phi*). |
| Jonbeel Mela | January (post-Magh Bihu) | Unique centuries-old **Tiwa** tribal **barter fair** held at Dayang Belguri (Morigaon) where money is bypassed in favor of goods exchange. |
| Baishagu & Bathow Puja | Mid-April | New Year and supreme-deity (*Bathow*) festival of the **Bodo** tribe featuring *Bagurumba* dance. |
| Dehing Patkai & Kaziranga Festivals | Jan/Feb | Annual state heritage, eco-tourism, and wildlife conservation festivals. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|----------|
| Bodo | Kokrajhar, Chirang, Baksa, Udalguri (BTR) | Largest plains tribe of Assam; **Bodo** is Associate Official & 8th Schedule language; *Bathow* faith, *Bagurumba* dance, GI-tagged textiles & *Sifung/Serja* instruments. |
| Mishing (Miri) | Majuli, Dhemaji, Lakhimpur, Jorhat, Golaghat | Riverine tribe living in elevated **Chang Ghar** bamboo stilt houses; *Ali-Aye-Ligang* festival, *Gumrag* dance, GI-tagged handloom. |
| Karbi | Karbi Anglong & West Karbi Anglong | *Chomangkan* death ritual, *Rongker* festival, vibrant handwoven jackets, and GI-tagged **Karbi Anglong Ginger**. |
| Dimasa Kachari | Dima Hasao (North Cachar Hills) | Historic medieval kingdom lineage; *Bishu Dima* festival, clan-based dual descent, and GI-tagged **Judima** rice wine. |
| Tiwa (Lalung) | Morigaon, Nagaon | Organizers of the historic **Jonbeel Mela** barter system; *Sagra Misawa* and *Wanshuwa* festivals. |
| Rabha, Sonowal Kachari, Deori, Tai Phake | Goalpara, Dibrugarh, Tinsukia, Lakhimpur | Skilled weavers and bamboo artisans; Tai Phake preserve Theravada Buddhist Tai traditions in Upper Assam. |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Guwahati / Dispur | **Dispur** is the State Capital; **"Gateway to North-East India"**; Kamakhya Temple, Umananda Island, IIT Guwahati, Saraighat Bridge, Lokpriya Gopinath Bordoloi Airport. |
| Sivasagar | Former capital (*Rangpur*) of the **Ahom Kingdom**; Rang Ghar, Talatal Ghar, Kareng Ghar, Sivadol, and nearby **Charaideo Moidams** (UNESCO site). |
| Majuli | **World's largest inhabited river island** (Brahmaputra) and **India's first Island District**; heart of Neo-Vaishnavism with historic *Satras* (Auniati, Kamalabari, Samaguri). |
| Jorhat | **"Tea Capital of India"** — hosts **Tocklai Tea Research Institute** (world's oldest & largest tea research station, 1911); gateway to Majuli. |
| Digboi | **"Oil City of Assam"** (Tinsukia dist); site of **Asia's oldest continuously operating oil refinery** (1901) and first oil well in India (1889). |
| Dibrugarh | **"Tea City of India"**; major Brahmaputra port city connected via **Bogibeel Bridge** (India's longest rail-cum-road bridge, 4.94 km). |
| Sualkuchi | **"Manchester of Assam / East"** (Kamrup dist); premier weaving hub for GI-tagged **Muga**, **Pat**, and **Eri** silk. |
| Tezpur | Cultural capital on north bank of Brahmaputra (Sonitpur); Agnigarh Hill, Da-Parbatia Gupta ruins, and **Tezpur Litchi (GI Tag)**. |
| Hajo | Unique **"Pancha Tirtha"** pilgrimage center sacred to Hindus (Hayagriva Madhava Temple), Muslims (**Poa Mecca**), and Buddhists. |
| Silchar | Commercial and educational capital of **Barak Valley**; NIT Silchar and historic Kachari heritage. |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Kamakhya Temple, Guwahati | Rebuilt by Koch King Naranarayan & Chilarai (1565 CE) | Situated atop Nilachal Hill; one of the foremost **51 Shakti Peethas** and center of Tantric worship in *Nilachal* dome architecture. |
| Charaideo Moidams, Charaideo | Ahom Dynasty (13th–19th c. CE) | Hemispherical earthen funerary mounds (pyramids of Assam) enshrining Ahom kings and queens (**UNESCO Site, 2024**). |
| Rang Ghar, Sivasagar | Ahom King Pramatta Singha (1744–1751 CE) | Two-storeyed royal sports pavilion; widely regarded as **Asia's oldest surviving amphitheatre**. |
| Talatal Ghar & Kareng Ghar, Sivasagar | Ahom Kings Rudra Singha / Rajeswar Singha (18th c. CE) | Monumental Ahom palaces at Rangpur and Garhgaon featuring hidden underground escape tunnels. |
| Sivadol, Sivasagar | Queen Ambika / King Siba Singha (1734 CE) | Towering 104-ft Shiva temple on the banks of Sibsagar Tank, flanked by Vishnudol and Devidol. |
| Umananda Temple, Guwahati | Ahom King Gadadhar Singha (1694 CE) | Shiva shrine on Peacock Island in the Brahmaputra — **world's smallest inhabited river island**. |
| Da-Parbatia Temple Ruins, Tezpur | Gupta Period (5th–6th c. CE) | Oldest surviving brick-and-stone temple doorway in Assam bearing exquisite carvings of Goddesses Ganga and Yamuna. |
| Madan Kamdev Ruins, Kamrup | Pala Dynasty of Kamarupa (9th–10th c. CE) | Archaeological temple complex renowned for erotic stone carvings, dubbed the **"Khajuraho of Assam."** |
| Poa Mecca, Hajo | Mir Lutfullah-i-Shirazi (1657 CE, Mughal era) | Revered tomb of Sufi saint Ghiyasuddin Auliya, believed to hold one-fourth (*poa*) the sanctity of Mecca. |
| Surya Pahar, Goalpara | Ancient Period (5th–12th c. CE) | Multi-faith rock-cut pilgrimage site with Hindu Shiva lingas, Jain Tirthankaras, and Buddhist stupas. |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| Kaziranga National Park | Natural | 1985 |
| Manas Wildlife Sanctuary | Natural | 1985 |
| Moidams – the Mound-Burial System of the Ahom Dynasty | Cultural | 2024 |

*(Note: Assam's **Charaideo Moidams** was inscribed as India's 43rd UNESCO World Heritage Site during the 46th WHC session hosted in New Delhi in July 2024.)*

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Classical Language Status:** **Assamese** was officially conferred **Classical Language** status by the Union Cabinet on **3 October 2024**.
- **GI Tags:** **Muga Silk**, **Gamosa**, **Assam Orthodox Tea**, **Joha Rice**, **Boka Chaul** (magic no-cook rice), **Kaji Nemu** (state fruit / aromatic lemon), **Tezpur Litchi**, **Karbi Anglong Ginger**, **Judima** (Dimasa rice wine), **Majuli Mask**, **Majuli Manuscript Painting**, **Sarthebari Bell Metal Craft**, **Assam Jaapi**, **Assam Bihu Dhol**, **Asharikandi Terracotta**, **Pani Meteka Craft**, **Bodo textiles & musical instruments**, and **Mishing handloom**.
- **National Parks (8 — 2nd highest in India after MP):** **Kaziranga NP** (UNESCO; hosts two-thirds of the world's **Great Indian One-Horned Rhinoceros**), **Manas NP** (UNESCO; Tiger & Biosphere Reserve; Golden Langur & Pygmy Hog), **Nameri NP** (White-winged Wood Duck), **Orang NP** ("Mini Kaziranga"), **Dibru-Saikhowa NP** (Biosphere Reserve; Feral Horses), **Raimona NP** (6th NP, 2021; Golden Langur), **Dehing Patkai NP** (7th NP, 2021; "Amazon of the East" rainforest), and **Sikhna Jwhwlao NP** (8th NP, Kokrajhar–Chirang along Indo-Bhutan border).
- **Ramsar Site (1):** **Deepor Beel** (Guwahati — designated 2002; freshwater riverine wetland).
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Indian One-Horned Rhinoceros**; State Bird: **White-winged Wood Duck** (*Deo Hanh*); State Tree: **Hollong**; State Flower: **Foxtail Orchid** (*Kopou Phul*); State Fruit: **Kaji Nemu**; State Song: *O Mor Apunar Desh*.

### 4. Bihar

**Capital:** Patna (ancient *Pataliputra*)  
**Formation:** 22 March 1912 (separated from Bengal Presidency as Bihar & Orissa Province — *Bihar Diwas* is celebrated on 22 March); 1 April 1936 (Orissa separated); 26 January 1950 (State of India); 15 November 2000 (Jharkhand carved out via Bihar Reorganisation Act, 2000)  
**Official Language(s):** Hindi (Primary Official), Urdu (Second Official Language); Maithili (Eighth Schedule Language)  
**Other Languages:** Bhojpuri, Magahi, Angika, Bajjika, Santali, Tharu, Kurukh (Oraon), English

#### Brief History
- **Cradle of Religions & Republics:** Home to major *Mahajanapadas* — **Magadha**, **Anga**, and **Vajji (Lichchhavi)**, with **Vaishali** acclaimed as the **world's first republic**. Deeply sacred to Buddhism (**Gautama Buddha** attained enlightenment at **Bodh Gaya**), Jainism (**Lord Mahavira** born at Kundagrama/Vaishali and attained *Nirvana* at **Pawapuri**), and Sikhism (**Guru Gobind Singh** born at Patna Sahib).
- **Imperial Empires:** Ruled by the **Haryankas** (Bimbisara, Ajatashatru — shifted capital from Rajgriha to Pataliputra), **Shishunagas**, **Nandas**, and the mighty **Mauryan Empire** (Chandragupta Maurya, **Ashoka the Great** — 3rd Buddhist Council at Pataliputra). Later thrived as the center of the **Gupta Empire** (Golden Age; Kumaragupta I founded **Nalanda University**) and the **Pala Dynasty** (Dharmapala founded **Vikramshila University**).
- **Medieval Era:** Flourished under the **Karnata Dynasty of Mithila** (1097–1324 CE). In the 16th century, **Sher Shah Suri** of Sasaram defeated Humayun, established the Sur Empire, built the **Grand Trunk Road**, and standardized the **Rupiya** silver coin.
- **Freedom Movement:** **Babu Kunwar Singh** of Jagdishpur led the 1857 Revolt in Bihar at age 80. In **1917**, invited by **Rajkumar Shukla**, Mahatma Gandhi launched the **Champaran Satyagraha** (his first Satyagraha in India) against the oppressive *Tinkathia* indigo system. **Swami Sahajanand Saraswati** pioneered the Kisan Sabha movement; **Dr. Rajendra Prasad** became India's first President; **Jayaprakash Narayan (*Loknayak*)** led Quit India underground action and Total Revolution; and **Karpoori Thakur (*Jananayak*)** was posthumously awarded the **Bharat Ratna (2024)**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Jat-Jatin | Folk | Popular women's monsoon dance of the **Mithila** region enacted in two opposing groups depicting the tender love, quarrels, and separation of couple Jat and Jatin. |
| Bidesia | Folk | Famous Bhojpuri dance-drama created by **Bhikhari Thakur**, expressing the agony of migrant laborers (*Bidesiya*) and social injustices. |
| Jhijhiya | Folk | Navratri devotional dance of **Mithila** where women balance perforated earthen pots containing lit oil lamps on their heads to appease Goddess Durga. |
| Sama-Chakeva | Folk | Winter festival folk dance of Mithila performed in the month of Kartik using clay birds to celebrate the bond between brothers and sisters. |
| Kajari | Folk | Traditional monsoon song-and-dance performed by women across Bhojpuri and Magadh regions celebrating dark rain clouds. |
| Domkach | Folk | Humorous, lively nocturnal folk dance performed exclusively by women in the groom's household during wedding nights. |
| Jhumari (Jhumar) | Folk | Joyful group circle dance akin to Garba performed during harvests and weddings. |
| Paika | Folk | Energetic martial folk dance featuring shields, wooden swords, and vigorous leaps. |
| Sohar-Khilona | Folk | Traditional auspicious dance-song performed by women to celebrate childbirth. |
| Launda Naach | Folk | Historic male folk-dance tradition of the Bhojpur region associated with *Bidesia* performances and weddings. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Music Traditions:** Bihar preserves two premier schools of **Dhrupad** classical singing — the **Darbhanga Gharana** (Mallik family) and **Bettiah Gharana** (Mishra family). Folk genres include *Kajari*, *Chaiti*, *Sohar*, *Birha*, *Fagua* (*Jogira*), and *Vidyapati Geet*. Legendary Shehnai maestro **Ustad Bismillah Khan** (Bharat Ratna) was born in **Dumraon** (Buxar), and **Sharda Sinha** (*Bihar Kokila*) popularized Chhath and Maithili/Bhojpuri folk music.
- **Painting Styles (GI Tagged & Traditional):** **Madhubani (Mithila) Painting** (**GI Tag** — practiced traditionally by women on mud walls and paper/silk using natural pigments, bamboo twigs, and double-line borders in *Bharni*, *Kachni*, *Tantrik*, *Godna*, and *Kohbar* styles; Padma Shri maestros **Jagdamba Devi**, **Sita Devi**, **Ganga Devi**, **Baua Devi**, **Dulari Devi**); **Manjusha Art** (**GI Tag** — Bhagalpur/Anga scroll-and-temple-box art painted in pink, green, and yellow depicting the *Behula-Lakhindar* legend); **Patna Qalam (Company Painting)** (18th–19th c. Indo-British realist watercolors depicting common artisans); **Pidiya Painting** (**GI Tag** — Bhojpur); and **Tikuli Art** (Patna decorative hand-painted hardboard craft).
- **Handicrafts & Textiles (GI Tagged):** **Bhagalpur Silk** (*Tussar* silk of the "Silk City" — **GI Tag**), **Sujini Embroidery Work** (Muzaffarpur narrative quilted textiles — **GI Tag**), **Applique (Khatwa) Work** (**GI Tag**), **Sikki Grass Products** (golden river-grass crafts of Mithila — **GI Tag**), **Bawan Buti Saree & Fabric** (Nalanda weaves with 52 motifs — **GI Tag**), and **Patharkatti Stone Craft** (Gaya black-stone carvings — **GI Tag**).

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Chhath Puja | Kartik Shukla Shashthi (Oct/Nov) & Chaitra | Foremost 4-day ancient solar festival worshipping **Surya** and **Chhathi Maiya** with *Nahay-Khay*, *Kharna*, river *Arghya*, and *Thekua* offerings. |
| Sonepur Cattle Fair | Kartik Purnima (Nov/Dec) | **Harihar Kshetra Mela** at the Ganga-Gandak confluence (Saran/Vaishali); **Asia's largest cattle fair**. |
| Pitripaksha Mela | Ashwin Krishna Paksha (Sep/Oct) | 15-day Hindu ancestral *Pind Daan* pilgrimage held at **Vishnupad Temple** along the Falgu River in Gaya. |
| Sama-Chakeva | Kartik (Nov) | Week-long Mithila festival celebrating brother-sister affection through handmade clay birds. |
| Rajgir Mahotsav | Nov/Dec | Three-day classical music, dance, and crafts festival celebrating the ancient capital of Magadha. |
| Bihula-Bishari Puja | August (Shravana) | Anga/Bhagalpur serpent-goddess festival closely linked to **Manjusha Art**. |
| Buddha Jayanti | Vaishakh Purnima (May) | Grand international celebration at Mahabodhi Temple (Bodh Gaya) marking Buddha's birth, enlightenment, and Mahaparinirvana. |
| Prakash Parv | Paush Sudi Saptami (Dec/Jan) | Birth anniversary of **Guru Gobind Singh** celebrated with grand processions at Takht Sri Patna Sahib. |
| Makar Sankranti (Til Sankranti) | 14 January | Harvest festival marked by kite flying and eating *Dahi-Chura* with sesame (*Til*). |
| Saurath Sabha | June/July | Historic annual Maithil Brahmin open-air matrimonial assembly in Madhubani district. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|----------|
| Tharu | West Champaran (Valmikinagar Terai) | Largest distinct Himalayan-Terai community in northern Bihar; *Maghi* and *Fagu* festivals, *Jhumra* dance, and bamboo-grass handicrafts. |
| Oraon (Kurukh) | Rohtas, Kaimur, Supaul, Purnia | *Sarhul* and *Karma* festivals, traditional *Dhumkuria* youth institution, and agrarian heritage. |
| Santal | Banka, Jamui, Katihar, Bhagalpur | *Sohrai* cattle festival, *Baha* flower festival, *Ol Chiki* script, and wall decorations. |
| Kharwar | Rohtas, Kaimur, Bhojpur | Ancient Kaimur plateau dwellers observing *Karma* festival and forest crafts. |
| Gond & Munda | Saran, Siwan, Buxar, Rohtas, Katihar | Rich *Karma* and *Sarhul* traditions alongside folk percussion music. |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Patna | **State Capital** (ancient *Pataliputra*); **Golghar**, **Takht Sri Patna Sahib**, Bihar Museum, Patna Museum (**Didarganj Yakshi**), NIT/IIT Patna. |
| Bodh Gaya | Holiest Buddhist site where **Siddhartha Gautama attained Enlightenment**; **Mahabodhi Temple** (UNESCO site); adjacent to **Gaya** (Vishnupad Temple). |
| Nalanda & Rajgir | Ruins of **Nalanda Mahavihara** (UNESCO site) and **Silao Khaja (GI Tag)**; **Rajgir** hot springs, Vishwa Shanti Stupa, Glass Skywalk, Cyclopean Wall. |
| Bhagalpur | **"Silk City of India"** (*Tussar* silk); ruins of **Vikramshila Mahavihara**, **Manjusha Art**, **Zardalu Mango**, **Katarni Rice**, and Gangetic Dolphin Sanctuary. |
| Muzaffarpur | **"Litchi Kingdom of India"** (**Shahi Litchi GI Tag**) and **Sujini Embroidery** craft. |
| Madhubani & Darbhanga | Cultural heartland of **Mithilanchal**; **Madhubani Paintings**, **Mithila Makhana**, Darbhanga Raj Forts, and **Darbhanga Gharana** of Dhrupad. |
| Vaishali | Site of the **world's first republic** (*Lichchhavi*), Ashokan Lion Pillar, Buddha's Relic Stupa, and **birthplace of Lord Mahavira** (Kundagrama). |
| Sasaram | Rohtas district HQ; famous for the lakeside octagonal **Tomb of Sher Shah Suri** and **Rohtasgarh Fort**. |
| Munger | **"Yoga Capital"** (Bihar School of Yoga), historic Munger Fort, traditional gun manufacturing, and Bhimbandh hot springs. |
| Pawapuri | Sacred Jain pilgrimage in Nalanda; **Jal Mandir** built in a lotus pond where **Lord Mahavira attained Nirvana**. |
| Begusarai | Industrial hub (Barauni Refinery) and home to **Kabartal Wetland** (Bihar's first Ramsar site). |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Mahabodhi Temple Complex, Bodh Gaya | Original shrine by Ashoka (3rd c. BCE); present brick tower Gupta Era (5th–6th c. CE) | Marks the exact spot of **Lord Buddha's Enlightenment** beneath the sacred **Bodhi Tree** (UNESCO Site). |
| Nalanda Mahavihara, Nalanda | Founded by Gupta Emperor **Kumaragupta I** (5th c. CE); patronized by Harshavardhana & Palas | Ancient world's premier residential international university attracting scholars like Xuanzang and I-Tsing (UNESCO Site). |
| Vikramshila Mahavihara, Bhagalpur | Pala King **Dharmapala** (late 8th–9th c. CE) | Major center of Tantric/Vajrayana Buddhism (*Atisha Dipankara* studied/taught here). |
| Tomb of Sher Shah Suri, Sasaram | Built 1540–1545 CE (Architect: Mir Muhammad Aliwal Khan) | Grand 122-ft red sandstone Indo-Islamic octagonal mausoleum set in the middle of an artificial lake ("2nd Taj Mahal" by some historians). |
| Barabar & Nagarjuni Caves, Jehanabad | Mauryan Emperor **Ashoka** & grandson **Dasharatha** (3rd c. BCE) | **Oldest surviving rock-cut caves in India**; mirror-like "Mauryan polish" interiors; **Lomas Rishi Cave** displays an iconic carved chaitya arch. |
| Mundeshwari Devi Temple, Kaimur | Gupta/Post-Gupta Period (c. 108 / 625 CE) | Recognized by the ASI as the **oldest functional Hindu temple in India**, built in a unique octagonal stone plan. |
| Kesaria Stupa, East Champaran | Mauryan to Gupta Periods | **Tallest and largest excavated Buddhist stupa in India** (~104 ft high), marking where Buddha handed over his alms bowl before Parinirvana. |
| Ashokan Pillars (Lauriya Nandangarh, Lauriya Areraj, Rampurva, Vaishali) | Emperor Ashoka (3rd c. BCE) | Monolithic Chunar sandstone pillars inscribed with Edicts; Rampurva Bull capital sits at Rashtrapati Bhavan. |
| Takht Sri Harmandir Ji Patna Sahib | Maharaja Ranjit Singh (rebuilt 18th–19th c.) | One of the five **Takhts of Sikhism**; marks the birthplace of **Guru Gobind Singh** (1666 CE). |
| Golghar, Patna | Captain John Garstin (1786 CE) | Iconic 29-m beehive-shaped British-era granary constructed after the 1770 famine. |
| Cyclopean Wall & Son Bhandar Caves, Rajgir | Pre-Mauryan / Haryanka & 3rd–4th c. CE | 40-km ancient dry-stone fortification encircling Rajgir hills; Son Bhandar rock caves hold ancient Jain inscriptions. |
| Vishnupad Temple, Gaya | Rebuilt by Queen **Ahilyabai Holkar** (1787 CE) | Octagonal basalt temple enshrining Lord Vishnu's footprint (*Dharmasila*), central to *Pind Daan* rituals. |
| Maner Sharif (Badi & Chhoti Dargah), Patna | 17th c. CE (Mughal Era, Ibrahim Khan) | Intricate sandstone tomb of Sufi saint Makhdoom Shah Daulat. |

|------|------|-------------------|

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Bihar Diwas** is observed on **22 March** (separation from Bengal Presidency in 1912).
- **Demographics (2011 Census):** **Most densely populated state in India** (**1,106 persons/sq. km**); **3rd most populous state**; **lowest overall literacy rate** among Indian states (61.8%).
- **GI Tags (16+):** Madhubani Painting, Bhagalpur Silk, Sujini Embroidery Work, Applique (Khatwa) Work, Sikki Grass Products, Manjusha Art, Bawan Buti Saree & Fabric, Patharkatti Stone Craft, Pidiya Painting, Shahi Litchi (Muzaffarpur), Bhagalpuri Zardalu Mango, Katarni Rice, Maghai Paan, Silao Khaja, Mithila Makhana, Marcha Rice (West Champaran).
- **National Park (1) & Tiger Reserve:** **Valmiki National Park & Tiger Reserve** (West Champaran, Himalayan Terai); **Kaimur Wildlife Sanctuary** (largest WLS in Bihar, developed as Bihar's 2nd Tiger Reserve).
- **Notable Sanctuary:** **Vikramshila Gangetic Dolphin Sanctuary** (50-km stretch of Ganga in Bhagalpur) — **India's only sanctuary dedicated to the Gangetic River Dolphin** (India's National Aquatic Animal). Other key sanctuaries: Bhimbandh WLS (hot springs), Gautam Budha WLS.
- **Ramsar Sites (6):** **Kabartal Wetland / Kanwar Lake** (Begusarai — **Asia's largest freshwater oxbow lake**, Bihar's 1st Ramsar site, 2020), **Nagi Bird Sanctuary** (Jamui), **Nakti Bird Sanctuary** (Jamui), **Gokul Jalashay** (Buxar, 2025), **Udaipur Jheel** (West Champaran, 2025), and **Gogabil Lake** (Katihar, 2025).
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Gaur** (Indian Bison); State Bird: **House Sparrow**; State Tree: **Peepal** (Sacred Fig); State Flower: **Kachnar** (*Bauhinia variegata*); State Fish: **Mangur** (*Clarias batrachus*).

---

### 5. Chhattisgarh

**Capital:** Raipur (Nava Raipur / Atal Nagar is the planned new administrative capital)  
**Formation:** 1 November 2000 (carved out of Madhya Pradesh as the 26th State of India via Madhya Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2000)  
**Official Language(s):** Hindi (Primary Official), Chhattisgarhi (Additional Official Language)  
**Other Languages:** Gondi, Halbi, Kurukh (Oraon), Bhatri, Sadri, Surgujia, Odia, Marathi, Bengali, Telugu

#### Brief History
- **Ancient Dakshina Kosala & Dandakaranya:** Identified in epics as **Dakshina Kosala** (maternal home of Lord Rama — **Mata Kaushalya** is associated with Chandkhuri near Raipur) and **Dandakaranya**. Ruled successively by the Mauryas, Satavahanas, Vakatakas, Guptas (Samudragupta's Dakshinapatha campaign), **Sharabhapuriyas**, and **Panduvamshis** of **Sirpur** (visited by Chinese pilgrim **Xuanzang** in 639 CE).
- **Medieval Dynasties:** The **Kalachuri Dynasty of Ratanpur** (1000–1741 CE) was the longest-ruling regional power; the **Phani Nagavanshis** built Bhoramdeo Temple; and **Annamdeo** founded the **Kakatiya Dynasty of Bastar** in 1324 CE, establishing the Danteshwari cult and Bastar Dussehra.
- **Maratha & British Rule:** Maratha Bhonsles of Nagpur annexed the region in 1741; passed to British Central Provinces & Berar in 1854.
- **Freedom & Tribal Movements:** **Shaheed Veer Narayan Singh** (zamindar of Sonakhan) led the **1857 Revolt** in Chhattisgarh, distributing hoarded grain during famine before his execution on 10 December 1857. In 1910, **Gundadhur** led the **Bhumkal Rebellion** in Bastar against British forest exploitation. **Pandit Sundarlal Sharma** ("Gandhi of Chhattisgarh") spearheaded the **Kandel Nahar Satyagraha (1920)**; **Jungle Satyagrahas (1930)** erupted at Rudri-Nawagaon and Tamora; and **Thakur Pyarelal Singh**, **Dr. Khoobchand Baghel**, and **Mini Mata** shaped modern Chhattisgarh.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Pandavani | Folk Narrative | Vibrant musical storytelling of *Mahabharata* episodes with a *tambura*; **Vedamati** style (sitting — Jhaduram Devangan, Ritu Verma) and **Kapalik** style (standing & enacting — popularized globally by **Dr. Teejan Bai**, Padma Vibhushan). |
| Panthi | Folk | High-energy devotional dance of the **Satnami** community praising **Guru Ghasidas**, famous for acrobatic human pyramids to *mridang* and *jhanjh* beats. |
| Raut Nacha | Folk | Colorful warrior-style cowherd dance performed by the **Yadava/Raut** community post-Diwali (Dev Uthani Ekadashi) with sticks, shields, and rhythmic *dholak*. |
| Sua Nacha (Suwa) | Folk | Graceful women's "Parrot Dance" performed during Diwali around a bamboo basket holding an earthen parrot effigy symbolizing Goddess Gauri. |
| Karma | Folk / Tribal | Harvest and rain-invoking dance of **Gond**, **Oraon**, and **Baiga** tribes around a sacred *Karam* tree branch. |
| Saila (Danda Nacha) | Folk | Post-harvest men's stick dance popular in Surguja and Bilaspur, evolving into energetic circular patterns. |
| Gaur Maria (Bison Horn Maria) | Folk / Tribal | Dramatic Bastar tribal dance of the **Dandami Maria** tribe wearing feathered bison/buffalo-horn headgears (*Gaur*). |
| Gendi Dance | Folk / Tribal | Unique stilt-balancing dance performed during **Hareli** by **Muria** youth and children across Bastar. |
| Kaksar Dance | Folk / Tribal | Sacred harvest and courtship dance of the **Abujhmaria** tribe in Narayanpur/Bastar. |
| Chandaini & Bharthari | Folk | Narrative folk ballad dances telling the stories of *Lorik-Chanda* and King *Bhartrihari* (Suraj Bai Khande). |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Folk Music:** **Dadariya** (acclaimed as the "King of Chhattisgarhi folk songs" — call-and-response love duets sung in fields), *Pandavani*, *Bharthari*, *Chandaini*, *Bans Geet* (bamboo-flute pastoral epic singing of Yadavas), *Sohar*, and *Bihaav* wedding songs.
- **Theatre & Ancient Performing Heritage:** **Nacha** folk theatre (elevated to international acclaim through **Habib Tanvir's** *Naya Theatre* and iconic Chhattisgarhi actress **Fida Bai Markam** in *Charandas Chor*); **Sitabenga & Jogimara Caves** (Ramgarh Hills, Surguja — 3rd–2nd c. BCE Mauryan period) constitute **Asia's/world's oldest surviving rock-cut amphitheatre/natyashala**.
- **Handicrafts & Textiles (GI Tagged):** **Bastar Dhokra** (ancient lost-wax non-ferrous bell-metal casting by *Ghadwa* artisans — **GI Tag**), **Bastar Iron Craft** (*Loha Shilpa* wrought iron lamps & figurines — **GI Tag**), **Bastar Wooden Craft** (carved tribal pillars, masks & deities — **GI Tag**), and **Champa Kosa Silk** (wild *Antheraea mylitta* golden Tussar silk woven in Janjgir-Champa — **GI Tag**).
- **Painting & Folk Arts:** **Godna Art** (traditional tribal body and fabric tattoo art practiced by Baiga and Gond women), **Pithora/Wall Relief (*Chawk* & *Nohdora*)** mud-wall art, Terracotta, and Bastar Bamboo/Sisal crafts.

| Pandit Sundarlal Sharma | *Chhattisgarhi Danleela* (pioneering modern Chhattisgarhi poetic work), *Satsai* | Chhattisgarhi / Hindi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Bastar Dussehra | Shravan Amavasya to Ashwin Shukla 13 (Jul–Oct) | **World's longest festival (75 days)**; uniquely dedicated to **Goddess Danteshwari** (not Rama-Ravana), featuring *Pata Jatra*, *Jogi Bithai*, giant wooden chariots, and *Muria Darbar*. |
| Hareli | Shravan Amavasya (Jul/Aug) | First festival of the Chhattisgarhi calendar; farmers worship ploughs and iron tools, cook *Cheela*, and children walk on *Gendi* stilts. |
| Madai Festival | December–March | Traveling tribal fair and market across Bastar/Gond regions featuring colorful deity poles (*Anga Deo*) and folk dances. |
| Pola | Bhadrapada Amavasya (Aug/Sep) | Agrarian festival worshipping decorated bulls; children play with clay bull toys (*Nandiya Baila*). |
| Goncha Festival | Ashadha (Jun/Jul) | Bastar's unique **Rath Yatra** famous for playful mock-firing of *Tupki* (bamboo toy guns) loaded with *peng* berries. |
| Teeja (Hartalika) | Bhadrapada (Aug/Sep) | Married women return to their maternal homes and observe a strict fast for marital harmony. |
| Chherchhera | Paush Purnima (January) | Joyous post-harvest sharing festival where children go door-to-door singing for paddy donations. |
| Rajim Kumbh Kalp (Punni Mela) | Magh Purnima (February) | Grand pilgrimage fair at the Triveni Sangam of Mahanadi, Pairi, and Sondur rivers in Rajim. |
| Sirpur National Dance & Music Festival | Jan/Feb | Cultural festival hosted at the ancient Sirpur ruins showcasing classical arts and Buddhism. |
| Bhoramdeo Mahotsav | March | Cultural and heritage festival celebrated at Bhoramdeo Temple, Kawardha. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|----------|
| Gond | Bastar, Dantewada, Dhamtari, Surguja | Largest tribal group of Chhattisgarh (~55% of state's ST population); *Madai* & *Karma* festivals, *Gondwana* heritage. |
| Maria (Abujhmaria & Bison-Horn Maria) | Narayanpur, Bijapur, Bastar, Dantewada | **Abujhmaria** is a **PVTG** living in the remote Abujhmad hills; Bison-Horn Marias are famous for feathered horn headgear and *Gaur* dance. |
| Muria | Kondagaon, Bastar | Renowned for the **Ghotul** (traditional co-educational youth dormitory and cultural learning institution) and *Hulki/Gendi* dances. |
| Baiga | Kabirdham (Kawardha), Bilaspur, GPM | **PVTG**; traditional *Bewar* (slash-and-burn) cultivators, forest herbalists, and extensive **Godna** tattoo art. |
| Pahadi Korwa | Surguja, Jashpur, Balrampur, Korba | **PVTG**; hill hunters skilled in traditional bamboo bow-and-arrow craft. |
| Kamar | Gariaband, Dhamtari | **PVTG**; renowned bamboo basketry artisans who worship *Lohasura*. |
| Birhor | Raigarh, Jashpur, Korba | **PVTG**; semi-nomadic forest community known for crafting ropes from *mahul* creeper fibers. |
| Halba, Bhatra & Dhurva | Bastar, Kanker, Dhamtari | Agrarian and martial tribes closely linked to Kakatiya administration and Bastar Dussehra rituals. |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Raipur & Nava Raipur | **State Capital** (Nava Raipur / Atal Nagar is planned smart city); Swami Vivekananda Sarovar, Purkhouti Muktangan, and nearby Chandkhuri **Mata Kaushalya Temple**. |
| Bhilai | **"Steel City of Chhattisgarh"** (Durg dist); **Bhilai Steel Plant** (established with Soviet/USSR assistance, 1955/1959), Maitri Bagh, IIT Bhilai. |
| Bilaspur | **"Nyayadhani" (Judicial Capital)** housing Chhattisgarh High Court; **SECR (South East Central Railway) HQ**; **"Dhan Ka Katora"** paddy belt; Kopra Jalashay Ramsar site. |
| Korba | **"Power Capital of India"**; major NTPC thermal power stations, SECL coal mines, and BALCO aluminum complex. |
| Jagdalpur | Cultural heart of **Bastar**; Bastar Palace, **Chitrakote Falls** ("Niagara of India" on Indravati River), and 75-day Bastar Dussehra. |
| Champa (Janjgir-Champa) | Premier weaving hub for GI-tagged golden **Champa Kosa Silk** and rice cultivation. |
| Rajim | **"Prayag of Chhattisgarh"** (Gariaband dist); confluence of Mahanadi, Pairi & Sondur rivers; ancient Rajiv Lochan Temple. |
| Ambikapur | Surguja HQ; renowned for municipal waste management, gateway to **Ramgarh Hills** (Jogimara Caves) and **Mainpat** ("Shimla of Chhattisgarh" Tibetan settlement). |
| Dantewada | Houses the revered **Danteshwari Shakti Peetha** and rich **Bailadila** iron ore deposits. |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Lakshmana Temple, Sirpur | Queen Vasata (Panduvamshi Dynasty, 7th c. CE) | One of **India's finest surviving Gupta/post-Gupta red-brick temples** (UNESCO Tentative List), surrounded by Buddhist viharas. |
| Bhoramdeo Temple, Kawardha | Phani Nagavanshi King Gopala Deva (11th c. CE, 1089 CE) | Nagara-style Shiva temple set against the Maikal Hills; famed as the **"Khajuraho of Chhattisgarh"** for intricate stone sculptures. |
| Danteshwari Temple, Dantewada | Kakatiya King Annamdeo (14th c. CE) | Revered as one of the **52 Shakti Peethas** at the confluence of Shankhini and Dankini rivers; focal point of Bastar Dussehra. |
| Deorani-Jethani Temple & Rudra Shiva, Tala | Sarabhapuriya / Panduvamshi Era (5th–6th c. CE) | Bilaspur archaeological site famous for the enigmatic 9-ft zoomorphic composite **Rudra Shiva** statue. |
| Jogimara & Sitabenga Caves, Ramgarh Hills | Mauryan Period (3rd–2nd c. BCE) | Surguja rock-cut caves bearing Prakrit Brahmi inscriptions, considered the **oldest surviving theatre/amphitheatre in Asia/India**. |
| Mahamaya Temple, Ratanpur | Kalachuri King Ratnadeva I (11th c. CE) | Historic temple complex of Ratanpur (ancient capital of Kalachuris). |
| Mata Kaushalya Temple, Chandkhuri | Ancient / Somavanshi origin (modern renovation) | Near Raipur; widely recognized as the **world's only temple dedicated to Mata Kaushalya** (Lord Rama's mother). |
| Rajiv Lochan Temple, Rajim | Nala / Panduvamshi Dynasty (7th–8th c. CE) | Panchayatan-style Vishnu shrine featuring finely sculpted pillar motifs. |
| Pataleshwar & Didineswari Temples, Malhar | Mauryan to Kalachuri Periods | Rich Bilaspur archaeological town yielding ancient coins and stone icons. |
| Battisa Temple & Mama-Bhanja Temple, Barsur | Chhindaka Nagavanshi Dynasty (11th c. CE) | Dantewada twin-sanctum and 32-pillared stone temples built without mortar. |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| None inscribed yet | — | — |

*(Note: **Sirpur Cultural and Archaeological Landscape** and **Kanger Valley National Park** are on UNESCO's Tentative List.)*

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- Known as the **"Rice Bowl of Central India"** (*Dhan Ka Katora*); ~44% of geographical area is under forest cover.
- **1st Jnanpith Winner from CG:** **Vinod Kumar Shukla** (59th Jnanpith Awardee); **Dr. Teejan Bai** (Padma Shri 1988, Padma Bhushan 2003, Padma Vibhushan 2019) took Pandavani worldwide.
- **GI Tags:** **Bastar Dhokra**, **Bastar Iron Craft**, **Bastar Wooden Craft**, **Champa Kosa Silk**, **Jeeraphool Rice** (Surguja), and **Nagri Dubraj Rice** (Dhamtari — "Basmati of Chhattisgarh", 2023).
- **National Parks (3):** **Indravati National Park** (Bijapur — Tiger Reserve & Wild Water Buffalo stronghold), **Kanger Valley National Park** (Bastar — subterranean **Kutumsar** & Kailash limestone caves, habitat of state bird Bastar Hill Myna), **Guru Ghasidas National Park** (Koriya/MCB — combined with Tamor Pingla WLS as **India's 56th Tiger Reserve: Guru Ghasidas–Tamor Pingla Tiger Reserve**).
- **Other Tiger Reserves & Sanctuaries:** **Achanakmar Tiger Reserve** (Mungeli — part of Achanakmar-Amarkantak Biosphere Reserve), **Udanti-Sitanadi Tiger Reserve** (Gariaband/Dhamtari), **Barnawapara WLS**, **Bhoramdeo WLS**.
- **Ramsar Site (1):** **Kopra Jalashay** (Bilaspur district — designated as **Chhattisgarh's first Ramsar site** in 2025).
- **Waterfalls:** **Chitrakote Waterfall** on Indravati River ("Niagara of India" — **widest waterfall in India**); Tirathgarh Falls in Kanger Valley.
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Wild Water Buffalo** (*Van Bhainsa*); State Bird: **Bastar Hill Myna** (*Pahadi Maina*); State Tree: **Sal** (*Sarai*); State Flower: **Foxtail Orchid** (*Rhynchostylis gigantea*); State Song: *Arpa Pairi Ke Dhar*.
