### 6. Goa

**Capital:** Panaji (Panjim)  
**Formation:** 19 December 1961 (Liberated from Portuguese colonial rule via **Operation Vijay** — *Goa Liberation Day*); 30 May 1987 (Conferred full Statehood as 25th State of India under 56th Constitutional Amendment Act, separated from Daman & Diu)  
**Official Language(s):** Konkani (Sole Official Language in Devanagari script under Goa, Daman and Diu Official Language Act, 1987; Eighth Schedule Language via 71st Amendment, 1992; Marathi is also permitted for specified official purposes)  
**Other Languages:** Marathi, English, Portuguese (minority heritage speakers), Hindi, Kannada

#### Brief History
- **Ancient & Medieval Kingdoms:** Known historically as *Gomantak*, *Gopakapattana*, and *Govapuri*. Ruled by the Bhojas, Mauryas, Satavahanas, Western Chalukyas, Shilaharas, and the **Goa Kadambas** (10th–14th c. CE, under Jayakeshi I with capitals at Chandrapur and Govapuri). Later came under the **Vijayanagara Empire** and **Adil Shahi Sultanate of Bijapur**.
- **Portuguese Colonial Rule (1510–1961 — 451 Years):** **Afonso de Albuquerque** captured Goa in **1510** from Yusuf Adil Shah of Bijapur (aided by Vijayanagara commander Timoji). Old Goa (*Velha Goa*) served as the capital of *Estado da Índia* ("Rome of the East") until Panaji became capital in 1843.
- **Resistance & Liberation:** Early revolts included the **Cuncolim Revolt (1583)**, the **Pinto Conspiracy (1787)**, and the **Rane Revolts** of Sattari (19th century). **Tristão de Bragança Cunha** ("Father of Goan Nationalism") founded the Goa Congress Committee (1928). On **18 June 1946** (*Goa Revolution Day*), **Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia** sparked the civil disobedience movement at Margao. Finally, Indian Armed Forces liberated Goa on **19 December 1961** via **Operation Vijay**.
- **Opinion Poll & Statehood:** In the historic **Goa Opinion Poll (16 January 1967)** — India's only official referendum — Goans voted against merging with Maharashtra. Goa gained full statehood on **30 May 1987**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Fugdi | Folk | Energetic Konkan women's circular dance performed during Ganesh Chaturthi (*Chavath*), Shigmo, and *Vrata* vows, clapping and blowing air rhythmically (*foo-foo*). |
| Dhalo | Folk | Sacred nocturnal winter dance performed by women in moonlit courtyards around a *Tulsi Vrindavan* altar, blending hymns and folk legends. |
| Dekhni | Folk | Melodious Indo-Portuguese fusion song-dance performed by Catholic women in traditional *pano-bhaju* holding earthen oil lamps (*samai*). |
| Ghode Modni | Folk | Heroic equestrian warrior dance of Bicholim and Sattari using colorful dummy wooden horses to commemorate Maratha/Rane victories. |
| Goff | Folk | Joyous Shigmo harvest dance where dancers weave and unweave colorful braided ribbons suspended from a central pole. |
| Kunbi Dance | Folk / Tribal | Swift, rhythmic circular dance of Goa's indigenous Kunbi community wearing traditional red-and-white checkered *Kunbi* sarees. |
| Lamp Dance (*Divlyancho Nach*) | Folk | Difficult balancing folk dance performed during Shigmo with lit brass oil lamps balanced on dancers' heads and palms. |
| Tarangamel | Folk | Vibrant Shigmo spring-procession dance accompanied by tall colorful flags (*tarangas*), umbrellas, *dhol*, and *tasha*. |
| Mussal Khel | Folk | Ancient pestle-dance performed by Chandor Christians commemorating Vijayanagara/Chola martial victories. |
| Corridinho | Folk | Lively Portuguese-influenced ballroom couple dance popular at Goan weddings and cultural fests. |
| Talgadi & Tonayamel | Folk | Vigorous Shigmo male folk dances featuring synchronized hand-claps and rhythmic wooden sticks. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Music Traditions:** **Mando** (19th-century romantic Konkani-Portuguese song-dance blending Western harmonies with Konkani lyrics, accompanied by *Ghumot* and violin); **Dulpod** (lively, rhythmic closing verses after Mando); **Suvari** (auspicious temple percussion ensemble); **Ovi** (traditional Hindu wedding and cradle songs); and **Bhajan/Kirtan** (*Damodar Bhajani Saptah*).
- **State Heritage Instrument:** **Ghumot** (notified in **2019** as Goa's **State Heritage Musical Instrument** — an hourglass earthen pot drum covered on one side with stretched membrane, historically monitor lizard skin and now goat skin).
- **Painting & Visual Arts:** **Kaavi Art** (unique indigenous red-laterite *kaav* sgraffito/etching mural art on whitewashed walls of Hindu temples and churches); **Azulejos** (hand-painted blue-and-white Portuguese glazed tiles); and modern masters **Mario Miranda** (legendary Goan cartoonist, Padma Vibhushan), **F.N. Souza** (founder of Progressive Artists' Group), and **V.S. Gaitonde** (Padma Shri abstractionist).
- **Theatre & Handicrafts:** **Tiatr** (distinctive Konkani musical stage drama with satirical social skits and *kantaram* songs, pioneered in 1892 by **Lucasinho Ribeiro**); **Kala / Dashavatara** (folk mythological plays); **Zagor** (nocturnal Gawda/Kunbi syncretic community folk theatre); **Kunbi Saree** (traditional handwoven checkered cotton); Seashell, Coconut-shell, Bamboo, and Bicholim Terracotta crafts.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Shenoi Goembab (Vaman Varde Valaulikar) | *Gomantopanishad*, *Mhogache Lagn* (**"Father of Modern Konkani Literature"**) | Konkani |
| Ravindra Kelekar | *Himalayant* (Sahitya Akademi; **First Konkani Jnanpith Awardee**, 2006) | Konkani / Marathi |
| Damodar Mauzo | *Karmelin* (Sahitya Akademi), *Tsunami Simon* (**57th Jnanpith Awardee**, 2022; 2nd Konkani Jnanpith) | Konkani |
| Pundalik Naik | *Acchev* (*The Upheaval* — first Konkani novel translated into English) | Konkani |
| Bakibab Borkar | *Chaitra*, *Sasay* (lyric poet) | Konkani / Marathi |
| Mahabaleshwar Sail | *Hawthan* (Saraswati Samman, 2016) | Konkani |
| Manohar Rai Sardesai | *Pisollim* (Sahitya Akademi Award) | Konkani |
| Father Thomas Stephens | *Krista Purana* (1616 epic poem) | Marathi / Konkani |
| Francisco Luís Gomes | *Os Brahamanes* (19th-century anti-colonial novel) | Portuguese |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Goa Carnival (*Intruz*) | Feb/Mar (Pre-Lent) | Four-day colorful street festival introduced by Portuguese, led by **"King Momo"** with floats, music, and masquerades. |
| Shigmotsav (Shigmo) | Phalguna (March) | Goa's spring harvest version of Holi featuring grand *Romtamel*, *Ghode Modni*, and *Tarangamel* parades across towns. |
| Feast of St. Francis Xavier (*Goencho Saibache Fest*) | 3 December | Major Christian pilgrimage at **Basilica of Bom Jesus** (Old Goa) honoring Goa's patron saint. |
| São João | 24 June | Feast of St. John the Baptist celebrating the monsoon; revellers wearing floral *kopel* crowns leap joyfully into village wells and ponds. |
| Ganesh Chaturthi (*Chavath*) | Bhadrapada (Aug/Sep) | Goa's grandest Hindu festival, marked by traditional *Matoli* canopies of wild fruits/herbs and *Fugdi* dances. |
| Bonderam Festival | 4th Saturday of August | Held on **Divar Island**, commemorating historical Portuguese-era village boundary disputes via colorful flag parades and mock battles. |
| Chikhal Kalo | Ashadha Ekadashi (Jun/Jul) | Traditional mud-festival in **Marcel** village dedicated to child Krishna's playful games. |
| Lairai Zatra | April/May (Chaitra) | Famous **Shirgao** festival known for *Dhonds* walking barefoot over glowing embers (*Agni Divya*). |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|----------|
| Kunbi | Salcete, Quepem, Canacona (South Goa) | Earliest indigenous tribe; egalitarian community heritage, traditional handwoven **Kunbi Saree**, and fast-paced *Kunbi Dance*. |
| Gawda | Ponda, Tiswadi, Quepem | Indigenous agrarian and salt-pan community; preserves syncretic **Zagor** folk theatre and *Fugdi/Dhalo* rituals. |
| Velip | Canacona, Sanguem, Quepem forests | Forest-dwelling community preserving sacred groves (*Devarai*), medicinal plant knowledge, and seasonal dances. |
| Dhangar *(pastoral community)* | Sattari, Bicholim, Sanguem foothills | Semi-nomadic buffalo-and-goat herders famous for wool-blanket weaving and the vigorous *Gaja* dance. |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Panaji (Panjim) | **State Capital** on the Mandovi River; colorful **Fontainhas Latin Quarter**, Our Lady of the Immaculate Conception Church, Miramar Beach, Dona Paula. |
| Old Goa (*Velha Goa*) | Former Portuguese capital ("Rome of the East"); houses the **UNESCO Churches and Convents of Goa**. |
| Margao (Madgaon) | Commercial and cultural capital of South Goa; **Lohia Maidan** (1946 Revolution site), Monte Hill, and heritage Indo-Portuguese mansions at Chandor/Loutolim. |
| Vasco da Gama & Mormugao | Major industrial/port hub; **Mormugao Port** (natural iron-ore & cruise port), Dabolim Airport, and Indian Naval Aviation Museum. |
| Mapusa | Commercial hub of North Goa famous for its vibrant **Friday Market** and gateway to Calangute, Baga, and Anjuna beaches. |
| Ponda | **"Temple Town of Goa"** housing Shri Mangueshi, Shantadurga, Mahalakshmi, Nagueshi temples, and Safa Shahouri Masjid. |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Basilica of Bom Jesus, Old Goa | Jesuits (1594–1605 CE) | Baroque masterpiece holding the incorrupt mortal remains of **St. Francis Xavier** in a silver casket (UNESCO Site). |
| Sé Cathedral (*Se de Santa Catarina*), Old Goa | Portuguese (1562–1619 CE) | One of the largest churches in Asia, built in Portuguese-Manueline style; houses the renowned **"Golden Bell."** |
| Church of St. Cajetan & Church of St. Francis of Assisi, Old Goa | Portuguese Era (16th–17th c. CE) | St. Cajetan is modeled on **St. Peter's Basilica** in Rome; St. Francis features Manueline doorway and *Kaavi*-like murals (UNESCO Site). |
| Fort Aguada, Sinquerim | Portuguese (1612 CE) | Strategic Mandovi estuary sea-fort built with a freshwater spring (*agua*) and **Asia's oldest lighthouse (1864)**. |
| Chapora Fort, Reis Magos Fort & Cabo de Rama Fort | Portuguese / Adil Shahi Eras | Historic coastal defense bastions overlooking the Arabian Sea and Chapora/Mandovi rivers. |
| Mahadev Temple, Tambdi Surla | **Goa Kadamba Dynasty** (12th–13th c. CE) | **Oldest surviving temple in Goa**, carved out of black basalt in Hemadpanti/Kadamba style inside Bhagwan Mahaveer Sanctuary. |
| Shri Mangueshi & Shri Shantadurga Temples, Ponda | Rebuilt 16th–18th c. CE | Showcase distinct **Goan Temple Architecture** featuring tall octagonal **Deepastambha** (lamp towers) and European-influenced domes. |
| Safa Shahouri Masjid, Ponda | Ibrahim Adil Shah I of Bijapur (1560 CE) | Historic surviving Bijapuri-style mosque featuring a sculpted masonry water tank. |
| Arvalem (Harvalem) Rock-Cut Caves, Bicholim | 5th–6th c. CE (Bhoja/Chalukya period) | Ancient five-compartment laterite rock-cut shrines near Arvalem Waterfalls. |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| Churches and Convents of Goa | Cultural | 1986 |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Smallest state of India by area** (**3,702 sq. km**) and **4th smallest by population**.
- **First 100% Har Ghar Jal State:** Goa became the **first state in India certified 100% Har Ghar Jal** under the Jal Jeevan Mission.
- **Uniform Civil Code:** Historically the **first state in India to have a Uniform Civil Code** (Portuguese Civil Code of 1867).
- **Highest Per Capita GDP:** Consistently ranks **1st among Indian states in per capita income/GSDP**.
- **GI Tags:** **Goan Cashew Feni**, **Khola Chilli** (Canacona), **Harmal Chilli**, **Myndoli (Moira) Banana**, **Goan Khaje** (sweet), **Goa Mankurad Mango**, **Goa Mussarad Mango**, **Goa Cashew**, **Agassaim Brinjal** (*Agsechi Vayingim*), **Sat Shiro Bheno** (7-ridged okra), **Goa Korgut Rice**, and **Goan Bebinca**.
- **National Park (1):** **Mollem National Park** (embedded within **Bhagwan Mahaveer Wildlife Sanctuary**, famous for **Dudhsagar Falls** on the Mandovi River and Tambdi Surla Temple).
- **Notable Wildlife Sanctuaries:** Bhagwan Mahaveer WLS, **Dr. Salim Ali Bird Sanctuary** (Chorao Island — estuarine mangrove sanctuary), Cotigao WLS, Netravali WLS, Mhadei WLS, Bondla WLS.
- **Ramsar Site (1):** **Nanda Lake** (Curchorem, Quepem — Goa's first Ramsar site, designated **2022**).
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Gaur** (Indian Bison); State Bird: **Flame-throated Bulbul** (*Ruby-throated Yellow Bulbul*); State Tree: **Matti** (*Terminalia crenulata*); State Flower: **Red Frangipani** (*Plumeria rubra*); State Heritage Instrument: **Ghumot**.

### 7. Gujarat

**Capital:** Gandhinagar (planned city; largest city: Ahmedabad)  
**Formation:** 1 May 1960 (bifurcated from the bilingual Bombay State under the Bombay Reorganisation Act, 1960 — *Gujarat Sthapana Diwas*)  
**Official Language(s):** Gujarati (Primary Official Language; Eighth Schedule Language)  
**Other Languages:** Hindi, English, Kutchi, Bhili, Sindhi, Urdu, Marathi

#### Brief History
- **Indus Valley Civilization (IVC):** Houses India's highest concentration of Harappan sites — **Lothal** (world's oldest known artificial tidal dockyard), **Dholavira** (UNESCO site with 3-tier urban grid & water reservoirs), **Surkotada** (horse bone remains), **Rangpur**, and **Rojdi**.
- **Ancient & Early Medieval:** Ruled by Mauryas (**Ashokan Rock Edicts** at **Girnar/Junagadh**; Pushyagupta built **Sudarshana Lake**), Western Kshatrapas (**Rudradaman I's Junagadh Inscription** — first long chaste **Sanskrit** rock inscription, 150 CE), Guptas (Skandagupta), and **Maitrakas of Vallabhi** (5th–8th c. CE; 2nd Jain Council). The **Chaulukya (Solanki) Dynasty** of Anhilwad Patan (940–1244 CE; Bhima I, Siddharaja Jayasimha, Kumarapala) ushered in Gujarat's architectural golden age (Modhera Sun Temple, Rani ki Vav).
- **Sultanate & Mughal Era:** **Muzaffarid Gujarat Sultanate** (1407–1573) saw **Sultan Ahmed Shah I** found **Ahmedabad (1411)** and **Mahmud Begada** build Champaner. **Akbar** conquered Gujarat in 1572–73 (commemorated by Buland Darwaza at Fatehpur Sikri). **Surat** served as the Mughal Empire's chief port (*Bab-ul-Makkah*), where the British East India Company established its **first permanent Indian factory in 1613**.
- **Freedom Movement:** Birthplace of **Mahatma Gandhi** (Porbandar) and **Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel** (Nadiad/Karamsad). Major milestones include **Sabarmati Ashram (1917)**, **Ahmedabad Mill Strike (1918)**, **Kheda Satyagraha (1918)**, **Bardoli Satyagraha (1928)** (Patel earned the title *Sardar*), the historic **Dandi March / Salt Satyagraha (12 Mar–6 Apr 1930)**, **Dharasana Satyagraha (1930)**, and the **Haripura INC Session (1938)** presided by Subhas Chandra Bose.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Garba | Folk | **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage (December 2023)**; circular Navratri dance performed around a perforated earthen pot (*Garbi*) holding an eternal flame symbolizing *Shakti* and the cycle of life. |
| Dandiya Raas | Folk | High-energy Navratri partner dance using decorated wooden sticks (*dandiyas*) symbolizing the mock battle between Goddess Durga and Mahishasura. |
| Bhavai | Folk Theatre-Dance | 14th-century traditional dramatic dance created by **Asait Thakar** combining social satire, music, and balancing stacked brass pots (*Matkas*) on the dancer's head. |
| Tippani | Folk | Energetic Saurashtra (Chorwad/Veraval) women labourers' dance using long wooden *tippani* poles originally used to compact lime floors. |
| Padhar | Folk | Rhythmic dance of the Padhar fishermen community around Nalsarovar mimicking rowing boats and surging waves. |
| Siddi Dhamal | Folk / Tribal | Vigorous African-rooted drum dance of the **Siddi** community of Jambur (Gir) wearing peacock feathers and beating *Mushira* drums. |
| Dangi Nritya | Folk / Tribal | Fast-paced circular and human-pyramid dance of the **Dangs** tribes accompanied by *Kahalia* and *Tadpu* wind instruments. |
| Hudo | Folk | Shepherd community dance of Panchal/Surendranagar (Tarnetar Fair) mimicking ram-fighting leaps. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Music Traditions:** *Sugam Sangeet*, *Dayro* and *Lokvarta* (Saurashtra oral storytelling and humor), *Charani Sahitya* (*Duha-Chhand* bardic poetry), *Bhajan* & *Prabhatiya* (*Vaishnava Jana To* by Narsinh Mehta), and *Santvani* devotional music.
- **Painting Styles (GI Tagged & Traditional):** **Mata ni Pachedi** (**GI Tag, 2023** — Ahmedabad Vaghri/Chitara ritual Mother Goddess cloth shrines dubbed the **"Kalamkari of Gujarat"**); **Kutch Rogan Art** (**GI Tag** — Nirona village art using thick boiled castor-oil pigment applied with a metal stylus); **Pithora Painting** (sacred horse-deity wall murals of **Rathwa** tribes in Chhota Udepur); **Lippan Kaam** (Kutch mud-and-mirror relief art on *Bhunga* huts); and **Warli** wall art in South Gujarat.
- **Textiles & Block Prints (GI Tagged):** **Patan Patola** (iconic double-ikat silk saree where both warp and weft are tie-dyed), **Rajkot Patola** (single-ikat, **GI Tag**), **Gharchola** (**GI Tag, 2024** — auspicious red checkered zari-Bandhani wedding textile), **Kutch Ajrakh Print** (**GI Tag, 2024** — natural indigo & madder resist block print of Ajrakhpur/Dhamadka), **Ahmedabad Saudagari Block Print** (**GI Tag, 2025**), **Pethapur Printing Blocks** (carved teakwood blocks — **GI Tag**), **Jamnagari Bandhani** (tie-dye), **Kutch Embroidery & Banni Embroidery**, **Tangaliya Shawl** (Surendranagar dotted weave), and **Surat Zari Craft** (gold/silver metallic embroidery).
- **Other Handicrafts (GI Tagged):** **Sankheda Furniture** (lacquered turned-wood furniture, Chhota Udepur), **Agates of Cambay (Khambhat)** (semi-precious stone polishing), and **Ambaji White Marble** (Banaskantha — **GI Tag, Nov 2025**).

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Narsinh Mehta (*Adi Kavi*) | *Vaishnava Jana To Tene Kahiye*, *Prabhatiya* (15th c.) | Gujarati |

| Premanand Bhatt (*Mahakavi*) | *Akhyanas* (*Sudama Charitra*, *Kunwarbai nu Mameru*, 17th c.) | Gujarati |
| Narmad (Narmadashankar Dave) | *Jai Jai Garavi Gujarat* (**State Song**; "Father of Modern Gujarati Literature") | Gujarati |
| Govardhanram Tripathi | *Saraswatichandra* (landmark 4-volume classic novel) | Gujarati |
| Zaverchand Meghani (*Rashtriya Shayar*) | *Saurashtra ni Rasdhar*, *Kasumbi no Rang* | Gujarati |
| Kanaiyalal Maneklal Munshi (K.M. Munshi) | *Patan ni Prabhuta*, *Gujarat no Nath* (founded Bharatiya Vidya Bhavan) | Gujarati |
| Mahatma Gandhi | *Satyana Prayogo* (*The Story of My Experiments with Truth*), *Hind Swaraj* | Gujarati |
| Umashankar Joshi | *Nishith* (**First Gujarati Jnanpith Awardee**, 1967) | Gujarati |
| Pannalal Patel | *Manvini Bhavai* (Jnanpith Awardee, 1985) | Gujarati |
| Rajendra Shah | *Dhvani* (Jnanpith Awardee, 2001) | Gujarati |
| Raghuveer Chaudhari | *Amrita* (Jnanpith Awardee, 2015) | Gujarati |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Navratri | Ashwin Shukla 1–9 (Sep/Oct) | **World's longest dance festival**; nine nights of state-wide **Garba** and **Dandiya Raas** celebrating Goddess Amba. |
| Uttarayan (International Kite Festival) | 14–15 January | Celebrated on Makar Sankranti with vibrant sky-filled kites across Ahmedabad and Gujarat, featuring traditional *Undhiyu* & *Chikki*. |
| Rann Utsav | November–February | Winter cultural and tent-city festival at **Dhordo** (Great Rann of Kutch) showcasing White Desert landscapes, crafts, and folk music. |
| Modhera Dance Festival (*Uttarardh Mahotsav*) | January (post-Uttarayan) | Three-day annual classical dance festival hosted at the illuminated **Sun Temple of Modhera**. |
| Tarnetar Fair | Bhadrapada Shukla 4–6 (Aug/Sep) | Celebrated at Trinetreshwar Mahadev Temple (Surendranagar), famed for colorful embroidered *Chhatri* umbrellas, folk dances, and traditional matchmaking. |
| Bhavnath Mahadev Fair | Maha Shivaratri (Feb/Mar) | Five-day fair at Girnar foothills (Junagadh) famous for the procession of Naga Sadhus and midnight bath in *Mrigi Kund*. |
| Vautha Mela | Kartik Purnima (November) | Held at the 7-river confluence (*Saptasangam*) near Dholka; renowned for traditional donkey and camel trading. |
| Madhavpur Ghed Mela | Ram Navami (Mar/Apr) | Coastal fair in Porbandar celebrating Lord Krishna's marriage to Princess Rukmini (cultural bridge between **Gujarat and Arunachal Pradesh**). |
| Shamlaji & Chitra Vichitra Fairs | Kartik Purnima / Post-Holi | Major tribal fairs in Aravalli and Sabarkantha districts marked by folk music and river rituals. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|----------|
| Bhil | Banaskantha, Sabarkantha, Dahod, Panchmahal, Narmada | Largest tribe in Gujarat; traditional archers, *Chitra Vichitra* fair, *Gavari* drama, and *Bhilili* oral folklore. |
| Rathwa (Bhilala) | Chhota Udepur | Renowned for sacred **Pithora Wall Paintings** invoking *Baba Pithora* for prosperity. |
| Rabari & Bharwad | Kutch, Saurashtra, North Gujarat | Pastoralist communities famous for intricate mirror-work embroidery, camel/sheep herding, and distinctive silver jewellery. |
| Siddi | Jambur (Gir Somnath / Junagadh) | Afro-Indian community descended from East African Bantu migrants; known for **Siddi Dhamal** dance. |
| Dhodia, Halpati, Gamit, Kukna, Warli | Valsad, Dang, Tapi, Surat | South Gujarat forest-and-agrarian tribes; **Dangi Nritya**, bamboo crafts, and **Warli** geometric paintings. |
| Padhar | Surendranagar (Nalsarovar) | Semi-aquatic lake community harvesting *bid* (water reeds) and performing the **Padhar** boat-rowing dance. |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Gandhinagar | **State Capital** ("Green City"); **Akshardham Temple**, Mahatma Mandir, **GIFT City** (India's 1st operational IFSC smart city), Adalaj Stepwell nearby. |
| Ahmedabad | **India's 1st UNESCO World Heritage City (2017)**; **"Manchester of India"**; Sabarmati Ashram, Sidi Saiyyed Mosque, **Narendra Modi Stadium** (world's largest cricket stadium), IIM-A, ISRO SAC. |
| Surat | **"Diamond City of India"** (~90% of global diamonds cut & polished) and **"Silk City"**; **Surat Zari**; terminal of India's 1st Bullet Train corridor; 1st permanent British factory (1613). |
| Vadodara (Baroda) | **"Cultural Capital / Sanskari Nagari"** of Gujarat; Laxmi Vilas Palace, Maharaja Sayajirao University, Sayaji Baug. |
| Anand | **"Milk Capital of India"** — birthplace of **Amul Dairy** and the **White Revolution (Operation Flood)** led by **Dr. Verghese Kurien**; NDDB HQ. |
| Jamnagar | **"Oil City / Brass City"**; world's largest oil refining complex, **Jamnagari Bandhani**, Lakhota Palace, and **Marine National Park**. |
| Rajkot | Major engineering, goldsmith & **Rajkot Patola** hub; Mahatma Gandhi's childhood home (Kaba Gandhi No Delo) and Alfred High School. |
| Bhavnagar & Alang | **Alang** hosts the **world's largest ship-breaking yard**; nearby Palitana Shatrunjaya Jain temples and Velavadar Blackbuck NP. |
| Morbi | **"Ceramic & Wall-Clock Capital of India"** (produces >70% of India's ceramic tiles & clocks). |
| Bhuj & Dhordo | Heart of **Kutch**; Aina Mahal, Prag Mahal, **Smritivan Earthquake Memorial**, and **White Rann** (*Rann Utsav*). |
| Dwarka & Somnath | Sacred Hindu pilgrimages — **Dwarkadhish Temple** (*Char Dham* site) and **Somnath** (1st Jyotirlinga). |
| Ekta Nagar (Kevadia) | Narmada district; site of Sardar Sarovar Dam and **Statue of Unity** (**world's tallest statue**, 182 m). |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Champaner-Pavagadh Archaeological Park | Chauhan Rajputs & Sultan Mahmud Begada (8th–16th c. CE) | Blend of Hindu-Jain-Islamic architecture; Kalika Mata Temple on Pavagadh hill & Jami Masjid (UNESCO Site, 2004). |
| Rani ki Vav (Queen's Stepwell), Patan | Queen **Udayamati** in memory of Solanki King Bhima I (11th c. CE) | 7-storeyed subterranean inverted water temple in Maru-Gurjara style on Saraswati River; **featured on India's ₹100 currency note** (UNESCO Site, 2014). |
| Historic City of Ahmadabad | Sultan **Ahmed Shah I** (1411 CE) | Walled city famed for unique residential **Pols**, Bhadra Fort, Jama Masjid, and Indo-Islamic wood-stone carvings (UNESCO Site, 2017). |
| Dholavira: A Harappan City | Indus Valley Civilization (~3000–1500 BCE) | Situated on Khadir Bet (Kutch); renowned for 3-tier urban citadel, rock-cut reservoirs, and **10-character Harappan signboard** (UNESCO Site, 2021). |
| Sun Temple, Modhera | Solanki King **Bhima I** (1026–27 CE) | Maru-Gurjara masterpiece on Pushpavati River with **Surya Kund** (108 miniature shrines) aligned to equinox sunrise (UNESCO Tentative List). |
| Somnath Temple, Prabhas Patan | Ancient origin; rebuilt in Chalukya style in 1951 (Sardar Patel initiative) | Revered as the **1st of the 12 Jyotirlingas**; historic site of Mahmud of Ghazni's raid (1025–26 CE). |
| Dwarkadhish Temple (Jagat Mandir), Dwarka | Chalukya style (16th c. CE on ancient site) | Five-storeyed limestone temple on Gomti Creek; one of the four sacred Hindu **Char Dham** sites. |
| Lothal, Ahmedabad District | Indus Valley Civilization (~2400 BCE; excavated by S.R. Rao) | Contains the **world's oldest known artificial tidal dockyard**, bead factory, and rice-husk evidence. |
| Adalaj Stepwell (Rudabai ni Vav), Gandhinagar | Queen Rudabai / Vaghela-Mahmud Begada era (1499 CE) | Intricate five-storeyed octagonal Indo-Islamic sandstone *Vav*. |
| Palitana Jain Temples, Shatrunjaya Hills | Solanki & later patrons (9th–20th c. CE) | Cluster of **863+ marble temples** atop Shatrunjaya Hill (Bhavnagar); holiest Shwetambara Jain pilgrimage. |
| Sidi Saiyyed Mosque, Ahmedabad | Sidi Saiyyed (1572–73 CE, Sultanate era) | World-famous for its intricately carved stone lattice windows (*Jalis*), notably the iconic **"Tree of Life."** |
| Laxmi Vilas Palace, Vadodara | Maharaja **Sayajirao Gaekwad III** (1890 CE; Arch: Major Charles Mant) | Indo-Saracenic royal residence spanning **four times the size of Buckingham Palace**. |
| Uparkot Fort & Girnar Ashokan Edicts, Junagadh | Mauryan / Kshatrapa / Chudasama Periods | Houses **14 Ashokan Major Rock Edicts**, Rudradaman's Sanskrit inscription, Buddhist caves, and *Adi Kadi Vav*. |
| Statue of Unity, Ekta Nagar | Sculpted by **Ram V. Sutar** (Inaugurated 31 Oct 2018) | Tribute to Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel; **world's tallest statue at 182 meters** (240 m including base) on Sadhu Bet island. |

|------|------|-------------------|

| Rani-ki-Vav (The Queen's Stepwell) at Patan | Cultural | 2014 |
| Historic City of Ahmadabad | Cultural | 2017 |

*(Note: Gujarat also holds **1 UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage** inscription — **Garba**, inscribed in **December 2023** — while **Modhera Sun Temple** and the historic town of **Vadnagar** are on UNESCO's Tentative List.)*

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Longest Coastline in India:** Gujarat possesses the **longest coastline among all Indian states** (~1,600 km traditional figure).
- **1st Operational Bullet Train / IFSC / Heritage City:** Houses India's **1st UNESCO World Heritage City (Ahmedabad)**, **1st operational International Financial Services Centre (GIFT City)**, and **1st Marine National Park** (Gulf of Kutch).
- **GI Tags:** **Patan Patola**, **Rajkot Patola**, **Gharchola** (2024), **Kutch Ajrakh Print** (2024), **Mata ni Pachedi** (2023), **Kutch Rogan Craft**, **Pethapur Printing Blocks** (2023), **Ahmedabad Saudagari Block Print** (2025), **Jamnagari Bandhani**, **Kutch Embroidery**, **Tangaliya Shawl**, **Surat Zari Craft**, **Sankheda Furniture**, **Agates of Cambay**, **Ambaji White Marble** (Nov 2025), **Gir Kesar Mango**, **Bhalia Wheat**, and **Kachchhi Kharek** (Dry Dates).
- **National Parks (4):** **Gir National Park** (Junagadh/Gir Somnath — **only natural abode of the Asiatic Lion** in the world), **Velavadar Blackbuck National Park** (Bhavnagar), **Marine National Park** (Gulf of Kutch, Jamnagar — **India's first Marine NP**), **Vansda National Park** (Navsari).
- **Notable Wildlife Sanctuaries:** **Indian Wild Ass Sanctuary** (Little Rann of Kutch — **largest wildlife sanctuary in India** & sole habitat of *Ghudkhar*), **Kutch Desert WLS** (Flamingo City), **Barda WLS** (2nd proposed lion home), **Narayan Sarovar WLS**, **Shoolpaneshwar WLS**, **Jessore Sloth Bear Sanctuary**.
- **Ramsar Sites (5):** **Nalsarovar Bird Sanctuary** (2012), **Thol Lake WLS** (2021), **Wadhvana Wetland** (2021), **Khijadia WLS** (2021), and **Chhari Dhand** (Kutch — designated **2025**).
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Asiatic Lion**; State Bird: **Greater Flamingo**; State Tree: **Banyan**; State Flower: **Marigold** (*Galgalota*); State Fruit: **Mango** (*Kesar*); State Song: *Jai Jai Garavi Gujarat*.

### 8. Haryana

**Capital:** Chandigarh (shared with Punjab; administered as a Union Territory)  
**Formation:** November 1, 1966 (carved out of Punjab as India's 17th state under the Punjab Reorganisation Act, 1966, based on the Shah Commission recommendation)  
**Official Language(s):** Hindi (Primary Official Language); Punjabi (Second Official Language since 2010, replacing Tamil which held symbolic second-language status from 1969–2010)  
**Other Languages:** Haryanvi (Bangru), Bagri, Mewati, Ahirwati, Braj, Deswali, Kauravi, English

#### Brief History
- **Indus Valley Civilization Cradle:** Haryana holds some of the earliest and largest Harappan settlements, including **Rakhigarhi** (Hisar — largest Harappan site in India), **Banawali** (Fatehabad), **Bhirrana** (Fatehabad — among the earliest Pre-Harappan/Neolithic sites), **Kunal** (Fatehabad), **Mitathal** (Bhiwani), and **Siswal** (Hisar).
- **Vedic & Epic Era:** Known as *Brahmavarta* and *Aryavarta*, situated along the ancient Saraswati and Drishadvati rivers. **Kurukshetra** was the battlefield of the *Mahabharata*, where Lord Krishna delivered the *Bhagavad Gita* to Arjuna at **Jyotisar**.
- **Ancient Dynasties:** Ruled by the **Yaudheyas** (tribal republic centered around Rohtak/Hisar), **Mauryas** (Topra Kalan in Yamunanagar originally housed an Ashokan Pillar later shifted to Delhi by Firoz Shah Tughlaq; Sugh Buddhist Stupa), **Kushans**, and **Guptas**.
- **Pushyabhuti (Vardhana) Dynasty (6th–7th Century CE):** Centered at **Thanesar (Sthaneshwar)** under Prabhakaravardhana and **Emperor Harshavardhana** (606–647 CE), vividly described by Chinese pilgrim **Xuanzang (Hiuen Tsang)** and court poet **Banabhatta**.
- **Tomaras & Chahamanas (Chauhans):** Tomaras founded Surajkund and Anangpur Dam. **Prithviraj Chauhan** defeated Muhammad Ghori in the **First Battle of Tarain (1191)** and lost in the **Second Battle of Tarain (1192)** at Taraori (Karnal), altering North Indian history.
- **Medieval & Mughal Era (Three Battles of Panipat & Karnal):**
  - **First Battle of Panipat (1526):** Babur defeated Ibrahim Lodi, establishing the Mughal Empire.
  - **Second Battle of Panipat (1556):** Akbar's regent Bairam Khan defeated **Hemu (Hemchandra Vikramaditya)** of Rewari.
  - **Battle of Karnal (1739):** Persian invader Nadir Shah defeated Mughal Emperor Muhammad Shah.
  - **Third Battle of Panipat (1761):** Ahmad Shah Abdali defeated the Marathas (led by Sadashivrao Bhau).
- **1857 Revolt & Freedom Struggle:** The Revolt of 1857 erupted at Ambala Cantonment (May 10, 1857) hours before Meerut. Key leaders included **Rao Tula Ram** (Ahirwal/Narnaul — Battle of Nasibpur), **Raja Nahar Singh** (Ballabhgarh), **Nawab Abdur Rahman Khan** (Jhajjar), and **Hasan Ali Khan** (Mewat). Later **Lala Lajpat Rai** practiced law and organized Swadeshi in Hisar, **Sir Chhotu Ram** championed agrarian reform, and **Pt. Neki Ram Sharma** ("Haryana Kesari") led the Home Rule and Non-Cooperation movements.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Phag | Folk | Joyous harvest dance performed by farmers (men and women) during the month of Phalgun (Holi season) in colorful attire |
| Khoria | Folk | Energetic all-women circle dance (a variant of Jhumar) performed exclusively during weddings after the groom's procession leaves |
| Loor | Folk | Performed by women during Holi; features witty, satirical question-and-answer folk songs |
| Dhamal | Folk | Ancient men's celebratory dance dating to Mahabharata times; performed with *dhol*, *been*, and *nagada* during spring/harvest |
| Jhumar | Folk | Known as "Haryanvi Gidda"; rhythmic women's dance named after a traditional forehead ornament |
| Gugga | Folk | Male devotional dance honoring Saint Gugga Peer, accompanied by *deru*, *dholak*, and *khadtal* |
| Chhathi | Folk | Ritual celebratory dance performed by women on the sixth night after the birth of a child |
| Daph / Daf | Folk | Seasonal spring dance performed when crops are ready for harvest, named after the *daph* percussion drum |
| Saang / Swang | Folk Dance-Drama | Traditional open-air musical theatre combining dialogue, *ragni* singing, and dance, historically with male actors playing female roles |
| Ras Leela | Folk | Devotional dance depicting Krishna's life, popular in the Braj-influenced Faridabad–Palwal belt |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Folk Music & Ballads:** **Ragni** (narrative folk songs central to *Saang* theatre), **Alha-Udal** heroic ballads, **Barahmasa** (songs of twelve seasons), **Been-Jogi** (snake-charmer *been* melodies), **Bhat** genealogical songs, and Sufi qawwali traditions associated with **Bu Ali Shah Qalandar** (Panipat).
- **Painting Styles:** **Sanjhi** (auspicious decorative clay and cow-dung wall relief art crafted by women during Navratri/Dussehra) and **Bhitti Chitra** (traditional haveli murals seen in Rewari, Bhiwani, Rohtak, and Jind).
- **Handicrafts & Textiles:** **Phulkari** (GI-tagged shared floral embroidery on *odhnis*/shawls), **Panja Durries** (handwoven cotton durries of Panipat, India's "City of Weavers"), **Tilla Jutti** (gold/silver-thread embroidered leather footwear of Rewari/Rohtak), **Jhajjar Surahi** (long-necked clay water pitchers), **Sarkanda (Reed) Basketry & Mudha** stools, and **Rewari Brassware**.

| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|

| Surdas | *Sur Sagar*, *Sur Saravali*, *Sahitya Lahiri* (born at Sihi, Faridabad) | Braj Bhasha (Hindi) |

| Babu Balmukund Gupta | *Shivshambhu ke Chitte*, *Akhabar-e-Chunar* (Rewari) | Hindi |
| Raja Ram Shastri | *Jhadu Phiri* (First Haryanvi novel) | Haryanvi |
| Uday Bhanu Hans | *Desan Mein Des Haryana* (First State Poet / Rajya Kavi of Haryana) | Hindi / Haryanvi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Surajkund International Crafts Mela | February (15 days) | World's largest crafts fair held in Faridabad showcasing global/national handicrafts, handlooms, and folk arts with a Partner Nation and Theme State |
| International Gita Mahotsav | November–December (Margashirsha Shukla Ekadashi) | Celebrated in Kurukshetra around Brahma Sarovar to mark the birth of the *Bhagavad Gita* |
| Pinjore Heritage Festival | December | Celebrated at Yadavindra Gardens (Pinjore) showcasing classical music, folk dance, and heritage crafts |
| Gugga Navami | August–September (Bhadrapada) | Honors Gugga Peer (Jahar Veer) as protector against snake bites; processions with blue banners (*chhari*) |
| Teej (Haryali Teej) | July–August (Shravan) | Monsoon festival of women featuring swings, henna, *ghevar* sweets, and folk songs |
| Mango Mela | July | Annual horticulture and cultural festival held at Pinjore Gardens showcasing hundreds of mango varieties |
| Baisakhi | April 13–14 | Harvest and solar New Year celebration across agricultural districts |
| Kapal Mochan Mela | November (Kartik Purnima) | Sacred bathing fair in Yamunanagar associated with Shiva, Krishna, and Guru Gobind Singh |
| Mata Mansa Devi & Sheetala Mata Mela | March–April & Sep–Oct (Navratri) | Major pilgrimage fairs at Panchkula (Mansa Devi) and Gurugram (Sheetala Mata) |
| Phalgu Mela | September–October (Somvati Amavasya) | Ancient ritual fair held at Pharal village in Kaithal district |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| **No Scheduled Tribes (ST) Notified** | Statewide | Under the Constitution of India and Census 2011, Haryana has **0% Scheduled Tribe population** (no notified ST communities, along with Punjab, Delhi, Chandigarh, and Puducherry) |
| Nomadic & Pastoral Folk Communities (Banjara, Gadaria, Sapera/Jogi, Meo, Gujjar, Ahir) | Nuh (Mewat), Ahirwal (Rewari/Mahendragarh), Rural Plains | Traditional *been* snake-charmer music (Sapera), *Mewati* folk ballads & *Ratwai* dance (Meo), pastoral herding, and martial/agrarian heritage |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Chandigarh | Shared Capital; planned by French-Swiss architect **Le Corbusier**; Rock Garden (Nek Chand), Sukhna Lake |
| Gurugram (Gurgaon) | "Cyber City / Millennium City"; IT & BPO hub; Headquarters of **International Solar Alliance (ISA)**; Sultanpur National Park; National Brain Research Centre (Manesar) |
| Faridabad | Largest industrial hub of Haryana; Surajkund International Crafts Mela; Badkhal Lake; birthplace of Bhakti poet Surdas (Sihi) |
| Panipat | "City of Weavers" (*Panja Durries*, handlooms, blankets); site of Three Battles of Panipat (1526, 1556, 1761); IOCL Oil Refinery; launch site of *Beti Bachao Beti Padhao* (Jan 22, 2015) |
| Kurukshetra | "Dharmakshetra" (Mahabharata War & *Bhagavad Gita* sermon); Brahma Sarovar; Gita Mahotsav; NIT Kurukshetra; Kalpana Chawla Planetarium |
| Karnal | "Rice Bowl of India" / "Paris of Haryana"; **National Dairy Research Institute (NDRI)**; CSSRI; birthplace of astronaut **Kalpana Chawla**; Battle of Karnal (1739) |
| Hisar | "Steel City" (Jindal Steel); Central Institute for Research on Buffaloes (CIRB) & National Research Centre on Equines (NRCE); **Rakhigarhi** & **Agroha** archaeological sites |
| Ambala | "Science City" (scientific instruments manufacturing); major Indian Air Force base; first spark of 1857 revolt in Haryana |
| Rohtak | Cloth market (Asia's notable Shori Wholesale Cloth Market); education hub (MDU, IIM Rohtak); Tilyar Lake |
| Yamunanagar | Plywood, paper (Ballarpur), and sugar industries; **Kalesar National Park**; origin of the Topra Ashokan Pillar |
| Rewari | Brassware & *Tilla Jutti* crafts; **Rewari Railway Heritage Museum** (oldest surviving steam locomotives including *Fairy Queen* runs) |
| Panchkula | Yadavindra (Pinjore) Gardens; Mata Mansa Devi Temple; Morni Hills (Haryana's only hill station, highest peak **Karoh Peak** at 1,467 m) |
| Sirsa | Cotton belt; **Sirsa Kinnow** (Haryana's first GI-tagged fruit); Indo-Israel Centre of Excellence for Fruits (Mangiana) |
| Jhajjar | Traditional clay *Surahi* pottery; **Bhindawas Wildlife Sanctuary** (Ramsar site) |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Rakhigarhi Archaeological Site | Indus Valley Civilization (~3300–1900 BCE) | Largest Harappan site in India (Hisar); excavated planned brick houses, drainage, seals, and burials |
| Sheikh Chilli's Tomb | Mughal Era (17th Century CE; Dara Shikoh period) | Known as the **"Taj Mahal of Haryana"** (Thanesar, Kurukshetra); marble mausoleum, madrasa, and Pathar Masjid |
| Kabuli Bagh Mosque & Ibrahim Lodi's Tomb | Babur (1527 CE) / Lodi Era | Built in Panipat to commemorate Babur's victory over Ibrahim Lodi in the 1st Battle of Panipat |
| Yadavindra Gardens (Pinjore Gardens) | Fidai Khan Koka (17th Century, Aurangzeb's reign) | Classic 7-terrace Mughal garden at Pinjore, Panchkula |
| Surajkund Reservoir | King Surajpal of Tomara Dynasty (10th Century CE) | Ancient stepped semi-circular amphitheatre-shaped water tank in Faridabad |
| Bhima Devi Temple Complex | Gurjara-Pratihara Dynasty (8th–11th Century CE) | Open-air archaeological site in Pinjore often called the **"Khajuraho of North India"** |
| Brahma Sarovar & Jyotisar | Ancient / Mahabharata Tradition | Asia's largest man-made sacred water tank (Brahma Sarovar) and site where Krishna preached the *Gita* (Jyotisar) |
| Firoz Shah Palace & Gujari Mahal | Firoz Shah Tughlaq (1354 CE) | Historic fortified Tughlaq palace complex in Hisar with Lat-ki-Masjid |
| Asigarh Fort (Hansi) | Prithviraj Chauhan / Tomaras (12th Century CE) | Famous medieval fort housing the ancient Baradari and George Thomas's 18th-century mint |
| Jal Mahal & Birbal's Chhatta | Shah Quli Khan / Rai Mukund Das (16th–17th Century CE) | Mughal-era water palace and 5-storeyed stone mansion in Narnaul (Mahendragarh) |
| Kos Minars | Sher Shah Suri / Mughals (16th–17th Century CE) | Medieval brick distance markers surviving along the Grand Trunk Road across Haryana |
| Agroha Mound & Sthaneshwar Mahadev Temple | Pre-Mauryan / Vardhana Era | Ancient capital of King Agrasen (Hisar) and historic Shiva temple worshipped by Harshavardhana (Thanesar) |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **GI Tags:** **Basmati Rice** (shared agricultural GI), **Phulkari** (shared handicraft GI), and **Sirsa Kinnow** (Haryana's first GI-tagged fruit, GI App No. 1101). Also famous for the **Murrah Buffalo** ("Black Gold"), though native livestock breed rather than a conventional crop GI.
- **National Parks (2):** **Sultanpur National Park** (Gurugram — famous for migratory birds like Siberian cranes/flamingos) and **Kalesar National Park** (Yamunanagar — Shivalik foothills, sal forests, barking deer).
- **Ramsar Sites (2):** **Sultanpur National Park** (2021) and **Bhindawas Wildlife Sanctuary** (Jhajjar, 2021 — Haryana's largest man-made freshwater wetland).
- **Wildlife Sanctuaries (8):** Bhindawas (Jhajjar), Khaparwas (Jhajjar), Nahar (Rewari), Abubshahar (Sirsa), Chhilchhila Lake (Kurukshetra), Bir Shikargarh (Panchkula), Khol-Hi-Raitan / Morni Hills (Panchkula), and Saraswati (Kaithal/Kurukshetra).
- **State Symbols:** Animal: **Blackbuck**; Bird: **Black Francolin (Black Partridge)**; Flower: **Lotus**; Tree: **Peepal (Sacred Fig)**.
- **Highest Peak:** **Karoh Peak** (1,467 m) in the Morni Hills (Shivalik range), Panchkula.
- **Key National/International Institutions:** **International Solar Alliance (ISA)** HQ in Gurugram; **National Dairy Research Institute (NDRI)** in Karnal; **Central Institute for Research on Buffaloes (CIRB)** in Hisar.
- **Exam Firsts & Records:** Lowest forest cover percentage among Indian states (alongside Punjab/Rajasthan variations — very low ~3.6% forest cover per ISFR); lowest sex ratio among Indian states in Census 2011 (879 per 1,000), prompting the launch of **Beti Bachao Beti Padhao** from **Panipat** on Jan 22, 2015; **0% ST population**.

### 9. Himachal Pradesh

**Capital:** Shimla (Summer Capital); Dharamshala (Winter Capital, designated in 2017)  
**Formation:** April 15, 1948 (Chief Commissioner's Province formed by merging 30 princely hill states — *Himachal Day*); Union Territory on November 1, 1956; Full Statehood on **January 25, 1971** (18th state of India)  
**Official Language(s):** Hindi (Primary Official Language); **Sanskrit** (Second Official Language since February 2019 — second state in India after Uttarakhand)  
**Other Languages:** Western Pahari / Himachali dialects (Kangri, Mandeali, Kulvi, Chambeali, Mahasu Pahari, Sirmauri, Bilaspuri/Kahluri, Kinnauri, Lahauli/Bhoti, Pangwali, Dogri, English)

#### Brief History
- **Ancient Janapadas & Tribes:** Mentioned in the *Mahabharata*, *Vedas*, and Panini's *Ashtadhyayi*; inhabited by ancient republics (*Janapadas*) of the **Trigartas** (Kangra — oldest surviving hill dynasty), **Audumbaras**, **Kulutas** (Kullu), and **Kunindas**.
- **Imperial Empires & Hill Rajput States:** Came under the **Mauryas** (Ashokan stupas near Kangra), **Kushans**, **Guptas**, and **Harshavardhana**. In medieval times, powerful Rajput hill principalities flourished: **Katoch dynasty of Kangra**, **Chamba** (founded by Maru; Chamba town established by **Sahil Varman** in 920 CE; Bharmaur temples by Meru Varman), **Kullu**, **Mandi**, **Bushahr**, **Bilaspur (Kahlur)**, and **Sirmaur**.
- **Gurkha Invasion & Sikh Rule:** **Maharaja Ranjit Singh** annexed Kangra Fort in 1809 following the **Treaty of Jawalamukhi** with Raja Sansar Chand II. Meanwhile, Gurkhas under Amar Singh Thapa invaded southern hill states (1804–1815) until defeated by the British in the **Anglo-Gurkha War (Treaty of Sugauli, 1815)**.
- **Colonial Era:** British established **Shimla** as the **Summer Capital of British India** in 1864; the Kalka–Shimla Railway opened in 1903; **Simla Conference (1945)** held at Viceregal Lodge.
- **Freedom Movement:** Marked by the **Prajamandal Movement**, **Dhami Firing Tragedy (July 16, 1939)**, **Pajhota Movement (1942, Sirmaur — part of Quit India)**, **Suket Satyagraha (1948)**, revolutionary poet **Baba Kanshi Ram ("Pahari Gandhi")**, and **Dr. Yashwant Singh Parmar** (first Chief Minister and "Architect of Himachal Pradesh").

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Nati | Folk | Iconic group dance of Kullu, Shimla, Mandi, and Sirmaur with rhythmic slow-to-fast steps; **Kulluvi Nati** holds a Guinness World Record for largest folk dance performance |
| Kayang, Bakayang & Banyangchu | Folk | Graceful circle and linear dances of **Kinnaur** performed in semi-circles to *dhole* and *shahnai* |
| Chham (Devil/Mask Dance) | Folk / Ritual | Sacred Tibetan Buddhist monastery mask dance performed by Lamas in Lahaul-Spiti and Kinnaur to ward off evil spirits |
| Jhamakra & Dangi | Folk | Traditional women's dances of **Chamba** and **Kangra**, accompanied by folk ballads (*Jhanjhoti*) |
| Thoda | Folk / Martial | Ancient martial archery dance of Sirmaur, Shimla, and Solan symbolizing the Pandava-Kaurava battles |
| Shand & Shabu | Folk | Devotional harvest dances of **Lahaul Valley** performed at Buddhist gompas |
| Mala (Garland) Dance | Folk | Kinnauri dance where dancers weave arms together like a floral garland (*Phulaich* festival) |
| Rakshas (Demon) & Losar Shona Chuksam | Folk | Traditional harvest and New Year dances of Kinnaur and Lahaul-Spiti |
| Karyala & Banthra | Folk Theatre-Dance | Satirical overnight folk drama-dance of Shimla/Solan/Sirmaur (Karyala) and Mandi (Banthra) |
| Gugga & Chhohara | Folk | Ritual/ceremonial dances performed in Bilaspur, Kangra, and Mahasu regions |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Traditional Music:** Pahari vocal styles including **Laman** (Kullu love songs), **Jhuri** (Sirmaur/Mahasu), **Mohana** (Bilaspur), **Jhanjhoti**, **Chhinj**, **Gangi**, and **Bara-Mah**; traditional mountain wind/brass instruments like the giant curved **Ransingha** (GI-tagged), **Karnal**, **Narsingha**, and **Dhol-Damama**.
- **Painting Styles:** **Pahari Miniature Paintings** — notably the **Kangra School** (world-famous lyrical *Radha-Krishna* and *Gita Govinda* themes patronized by **Raja Sansar Chand** of Kangra), **Guler School** (earliest Pahari style), **Chamba School**, **Mandi School**, and **Nurpur School**. Also **Thangka Painting** (Tibetan Buddhist scroll art on cotton/silk in Spiti, Lahaul, and Kinnaur).
- **Handicrafts & Textiles:** **Chamba Rumal** (GI-tagged "needle wonder" double-sided *dorukha* silk embroidery depicting Krishna Ras Leela), **Kullu Shawl** & **Kinnauri Shawl** (GI-tagged geometric wool weaving), **Himachali Topi** (Kullu, Kinnauri, Bushahri caps), **Chamba Chappal** (GI), **Lahauli Socks & Gloves** (GI), **Chamba Metal Art** (*mohras* and brass idols — GI), **Kath-Kuni** timber-and-stone earthquake-resistant architecture and **Wood Carving** (GI), **Kangra Handmade Carpets (Galicha)**, and **Gudma** blankets.

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| International Kullu Dussehra | October (begins on Vijayadashami for 7 days) | Begins when Dussehra ends elsewhere; 200+ village *Devtas* gather at Dhalpur Maidan to honor **Lord Raghunath** |
| International Mandi Shivratri | February–March (7 days) | Grand gathering of over 200 hill deities (*Devtas*) around Bhootnath Temple, Mandi |
| International Minjar Fair | July–August (Shravan) | Week-long Chamba festival celebrating maize harvest; golden silk tassels (*Minjar*) offered to the **Ravi River** |
| International Lavi Fair | November | Over 300-year-old historic Indo-Tibetan trade fair held at **Rampur Bushahr** (Shimla) |
| Renuka Ji Fair | November (Kartik Ekadashi) | Celebrates the annual reunion of Sage Parashurama with his mother **Renuka** at Renuka Lake (Sirmaur) |
| Phulaich (Ukhyang) | September | Unique Kinnaur flower-and-ancestor festival honoring departed souls with mountain blossoms |
| Losar | February–March (Tibetan New Year) | Grand Buddhist New Year celebrated across Lahaul-Spiti and Kinnaur with *Chham* dances |
| Halda | January | Festival of lights and bonfires in Lahaul honoring **Shashikar Apa**, goddess of wealth |
| Gochi | February | Celebrated in Bhaga Valley (Lahaul) to mark the birth of male children in the past year |
| Sui Mata Fair | April (Chaitra) | Exclusive Chamba fair for women and children honoring **Rani Naina Devi**, who sacrificed her life for water supply |
| Nalwari Fair | March–April | Historic cattle trade fair held in **Bilaspur**, started by W. Goldstein in 1889 |
| Ladarcha & Pori Fair | July–August | Trans-Himalayan trade fair in Kaza (Spiti) and Triloknath temple fair (Lahaul) |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Gaddi | Chamba (Bharmaur) & Kangra | Transhumant shepherds wearing *Chola-Dora* woollen robes; devotees of Shiva; perform *Gaddi Nati* and *Nawala* ritual |
| Kinnaura / Kanaura | Kinnaur | Skilled orchardists, weavers of **Kinnauri Shawls**, syncretic Hindu-Buddhist traditions, *Phulaich* & *Sazo* festivals |
| Lahaula, Swangla & Bhot / Bodh | Lahaul & Spiti | High-altitude agro-pastoralists; Buddhist monasteries, *Losar*, *Halda*, *Gochi*, and *Chham* dances |
| Gujjar | Chamba, Sirmaur, Mandi, Kangra | Semi-nomadic pastoralist buffalo herders (*Gujjar* dera culture) |
| Pangwala | Pangi Valley (Chamba) | Isolated alpine valley dwellers known for wooden architecture, *Jukar*/*Phul Jatra* festivals, and wool craft |
| Jad / Lamba / Khampa | Kinnaur, Kullu, Lahaul | Tibetan-origin trading and pastoral communities skilled in carpet weaving and *Thangka* art |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For

|------|-----------|
| Shimla | Summer Capital; colonial heritage (Viceregal Lodge/IIAS, Mall Road, Gaiety Theatre); **Kalka–Shimla UNESCO Railway**; Central Potato Research Institute (Kufri) |
| Dharamshala (McLeod Ganj) | Winter Capital; residence of the **14th Dalai Lama** & Tibetan Government-in-Exile; HPCA International Cricket Stadium; Kangra Tea |

| Chamba | **Chamba Rumal**; Chaurasi & Laxmi Narayan Temples; Minjar Fair; Khajjiar ("Mini Switzerland of India") nearby |
| Kangra | **Kangra Fort**; Kangra Miniature Paintings; Masroor Rock-Cut Temple; Jawalamukhi & Brajeshwari Shakti Peethas |
| Mandi | "Varanasi of the Hills" / "Chhoti Kashi" (81 ancient stone temples); International Shivratri Fair; IIT Mandi; **Sepu Vadi** |

| Rampur Bushahr | Historic **Lavi Trade Fair**; Padam Palace; gateway to Kinnaur |
| Kaza (Spiti Valley) | Cold Desert Biosphere Reserve; **Key Monastery**; **Tabo Monastery**; **Hikkim** (world's highest post office); **Tashigang** (world's highest polling station) |
| Bilaspur | **Gobind Sagar** reservoir (Bhakra Dam); Naina Devi Shakti Peetha; Nalwari Fair |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Kangra Fort | Katoch Dynasty / Trigarta Kingdom (Ancient, pre-11th c. records) | Largest and oldest fort in the Himalayas; mentioned in Alexander's and Mahabharata accounts; raided by Mahmud of Ghazni (1009) |
| Masroor Rock-Cut Temples | Unknown / 8th Century CE (Nagara Style) | Monolithic 15-shrine rock-cut Hindu temple complex in Kangra, famed as the **"Ellora of Himachal"** |
| Tabo Monastery | Lotsawa Rinchen Zangpo (996 CE) | Oldest continuously functioning Buddhist monastery in the Himalayas (Spiti); famed as the **"Ajanta of the Himalayas"** for ancient murals |
| Key (Ki) Monastery | Dromton / 11th Century CE | Largest Buddhist monastery in Spiti Valley, perched at 4,166 m |
| Hadimba Devi Temple | Maharaja Bahadur Singh (1553 CE) | Unique 4-tiered wooden pagoda-style temple in Manali dedicated to Bhima's wife Hidimba |
| Baijnath Shiva Temple | Merchants Ahuka & Manyuka (1204 CE) | Classic Nagara-style stone temple in Kangra, revered for its architectural symmetry |
| Chaurasi Temple Complex | Sahil Varman / Meru Varman (7th–10th Century CE) | Cluster of 84 shrines in Bharmaur (Chamba), including Manimahesh & Lakshana Devi temples |
| Laxmi Narayan Temple Complex | Raja Sahil Varman (10th Century CE) | Group of 6 Shikhara-style temples in Chamba town built with white marble/stone |
| Shakti Peethas (Jawalamukhi, Chintpurni, Naina Devi, Chamunda) | Ancient / Medieval Reconstructions | Four revered *Shakti Peethas*; Jawalamukhi is famous for eternal natural blue flames without idols |
| Triloknath Temple | Ancient / Medieval (Lahaul) | Unique shrine revered simultaneously by both **Hindus (as Shiva)** and **Buddhists (as Avalokiteshvara)** |
| Viceregal Lodge (IIAS) | Designed by Henry Irwin (1888 CE) | Scottish Baronial building in Shimla; venue of the **1945 Simla Conference**; now Indian Institute of Advanced Study |
| Christ Church | British Era (1857 CE) | Second oldest church in North India (on Shimla Ridge) |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| Mountain Railways of India — **Kalka–Shimla Railway** | Cultural | **2008** (Built 1903; 96.6 km narrow-gauge engineering marvel with 102 operational/historic tunnels & 800+ bridges) |
| **Great Himalayan National Park Conservation Area (GHNPCA)** | Natural | **2014** (Kullu district; protects snow leopards, Western Tragopan, Himalayan tahr, and alpine meadows) |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **GI Tags (24 registered items):** Traditional/older tags: **Kullu Shawl**, **Kangra Tea**, **Chamba Rumal**, **Kinnauri Shawl**, **Kangra Paintings**, **Chamba Chappal**, **Himachali Kala Zeera**, **Himachali Chulli Oil**, **Lahauli Socks & Gloves**, and shared **Basmati**. Major 2024–2026 expansions: **Spiti Chharma (Seabuckthorn)**, **Salooni Safed Makka**, **Kinnauri Apple**, **Sepu Vadi of Mandi**, **Himachal Lingchay**, **Himachal Gudma**, **Himachal Ransingha**, **Himachal Wood Carving Craft**, **Himachal Handmade Galicha (Carpet)**, **Chamba Metal Art**, **Sirmouri Loiya**, **Kinnauri Topi**, and **Kinnauri Jewellery**.
- **National Parks (5):** **Great Himalayan NP** (Kullu — UNESCO), **Pin Valley NP** (Lahaul-Spiti — cold desert), **Khirganga NP** (Kullu), **Inderkilla NP** (Kullu), and **Simbalbara NP** (Sirmaur).
- **Ramsar Sites (3):** **Pong Dam Lake** (Maharana Pratap Sagar, Kangra, 2002), **Chandertal Wetland** (Lahaul-Spiti, 2005), and **Renuka Wetland** (Sirmaur, 2005 — **India's smallest Ramsar site at 20 hectares**).
- **UNESCO MAB Biosphere Reserve:** **Cold Desert Biosphere Reserve** (Lahaul-Spiti, covering Pin Valley NP & Kibber/Chandratal/Sarchu sanctuaries) was inducted into UNESCO's **World Network of Biosphere Reserves (WNBR) in 2025** as India's **13th WNBR** and first high-altitude cold desert biosphere reserve.
- **Notable Wildlife Sanctuaries (~26–28):** Kibber (highest/largest in HP, snow leopards), Kalatop-Khajjiar, Chail, Gobind Sagar, Naina Devi, Renuka, Shikari Devi, Kugti, Dhauladhar, Tundah, Lippa Asrang, Rupi Bhaba, Rakchham Chitkul.
- **State Symbols:** Animal: **Snow Leopard**; Bird: **Western Tragopan (*Jujurana*)**; Flower: **Pink Rhododendron**; Tree: **Deodar Cedar**.
- **Records & Firsts:** **Atal Tunnel (Rohtang)** (9.02 km, world's longest highway single-tube tunnel above 10,000 ft); **Hikkim** (world's highest post office); **Tashigang** (world's highest polling station at 15,256 ft); **Chail** (world's highest cricket ground); India's **first smoke-free state** (2013) and **first 100% LPG-covered state**.

---

### 10. Jharkhand

**Capital:** Ranchi (Sub-capital: Dumka)  
**Formation:** **November 15, 2000** (28th state of India, carved out of southern Bihar under the Bihar Reorganisation Act, 2000, on **Birsa Munda's** birth anniversary)  
**Official Language(s):** Hindi (Primary Official Language); **16 Additional Official Languages:** Urdu, **Santali** (8th Schedule), **Bengali** (8th Schedule), **Odia** (8th Schedule), **Maithili** (8th Schedule), Mundari, Ho, Kharia, Kurukh (Oraon), Khortha, Kurmali, Nagpuri (Sadri), Panchpargania, Magahi, Bhojpuri, and Angika  
**Other Languages:** Sadani, Birhori, Asuri, Malto, English

#### Brief History
- **Ancient & Sacred Geography:** Historically known as *Kukkut Desh*, *Atavi*, *Kark Khand*, and *Jharkhand* ("Land of Forests"). **Parasnath Hill (Shikharji)** in Giridih is the holiest Jain pilgrimage site where **20 of 24 Tirthankaras** (including 23rd Tirthankara Parshvanatha) attained Moksha.
- **Ancient & Regional Dynasties:** Ruled by the **Mauryas**, **Guptas**, **Shashanka of Gauda**, **Nagvanshi Dynasty** of Chotanagpur (founded by **Phani Mukut Rai**), **Chero Dynasty** of Palamu (**Medini Rai**), **Raksels**, **Ramgarh Raj**, and **Dhalbhum/Singhbhum** chiefs.
- **Landmark Anti-British Tribal Uprisings (Most Asked in Exams):**
  - **Tilka Majhi Revolt (1784–85):** One of India's earliest tribal revolts against British rule (killed Augustus Cleveland at Bhagalpur).
  - **Kol Uprising (1831–32):** Led by **Budhu Bhagat** and Ganga Narain against British land policies and *dikus* (outsiders).
  - **Bhumij Revolt (1832–33):** Led by **Ganga Narain Singh** ("Ganga Narain's Hungama").
  - **Santhal Hul (1855–56):** Massive uprising of ~60,000 Santhals at Bhognadih led by brothers **Sidho, Kanhu, Chand, and Bhairav** and sisters **Phulo and Jhano** (commemorated every June 30 as *Hul Diwas*).
  - **1857 Revolt in Jharkhand:** Led by **Thakur Vishwanath Shahdeo**, **Pandey Ganpat Rai**, **Sheikh Bhikhari**, **Tikait Umrao Singh**, and **Nilambar–Pitambar** (Palamu).
  - **Ulgulan / Munda Rebellion (1899–1900):** Led by **Birsa Munda ("Dharti Aba")** against the *zamindari* exploitation and erosion of the *Khuntkatti* communal land system, resulting in the **Chotanagpur Tenancy (CNT) Act, 1908**.
  - **Tana Bhagat Movement (1914):** Purely non-violent, Gandhian tribal reform and freedom movement led by **Jatra Oraon (Jatra Bhagat)**.
- **Statehood Movement:** Spearheaded by **Jaipal Singh Munda** (1928 Olympic Hockey Gold Captain, Constituent Assembly member, founder of *Adivasi Mahasabha* / *Jharkhand Party*), later carried forward by **Shibu Soren** and **Binod Bihari Mahato** (JMM, 1973), culminating in statehood on **Nov 15, 2000** with **Babulal Marandi** as the first CM.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| **Seraikela Chhau** | Folk / Semi-Classical Martial | Mask-based dance-drama using stylized wooden/clay masks to express mythological and heroic themes; inscribed in **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage (2010)** |
| Paika | Folk / Martial | Energetic men's war dance of the Munda community performed with swords, shields, and colourful plumed turbans |
| Karma / Karam | Folk | Harvest and fertility dance performed around a sacred *Karam* tree branch during the Karma festival |
| Jhumair (Mardani & Janani) | Folk | Harvest and festival circle dance with separate male (*Mardani*) and female (*Janani*) variations |
| Domkach | Folk | Playful, satirical all-women dance performed indoors/courtyards during weddings after the *baraat* departs |
| Fagua | Folk | Vigorous springtime dance performed by men and women during Holi |
| Baha / Dong | Folk | Santhali seasonal dances celebrating spring blossoms (*Baha*) and marriage rituals (*Dong*) |
| Jadur | Folk | Oraon/Munda dance celebrating the Sarhul spring festival around the *Sarna* sacred grove |
| Hunta | Folk | Santhal hunting-themed group dance performed with bows and arrows |
| Nachni & Natua | Folk | Traditional acrobatics-infused duet and courtly folk entertainment |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Traditional Music & Instruments:** Rich tribal song cycles tied to seasons and agriculture (*Sarhul, Karam, Sohrai, Baha* songs) and **Nagpuri/Khortha** folk songs. Signature instruments: **Mandar** (two-headed terracotta/wood drum — heartbeat of tribal music), **Nagara**, **Banam** (Santhali bowed single-string fiddle carved with human/animal motifs), **Kendra**, **Bansuri**, **Dhak**, **Singa** (horn), and **Turi**.
- **Painting Styles:**
  - **Sohrai-Khovar Painting (GI-tagged, Hazaribagh):** Traditional mud-wall mural art painted by tribal women using natural red, black, yellow, and white earth pigments (*Sohrai* for harvest/cattle; *Khovar* using comb-cut technique for weddings).
  - **Jadopatia Painting (GI-tagged, 2026):** Scroll paintings created by Santhal *Jado* (magic-painter) artists narrating myths, afterlife journeys, and tiger legends.
  - **Paitkar Painting:** Ancient folk scroll painting from **Amadubi village (East Singhbhum)** — one of India's oldest surviving tribal scroll art traditions.
  - **Baroni Paintings (GI-tagged, 2026):** Traditional folk decorative art of the region.
- **Handicrafts & Textiles:** **Dokra Craft** (GI-tagged 2026 — ancient non-ferrous lost-wax metal casting), **Jharkhand Bamboo Craft** (GI-tagged 2026), **Kuchai Silk & Jharkhand Tussar Silk** (GI-tagged 2026 — Jharkhand is India's largest producer of *Tussar silk*), **Munda Jewellery** (GI-tagged 2026), **Terracotta & Maluti-style clay work**, and **Lac Crafts/Bangles**.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Pandit Raghunath Murmu | Creator of **Ol Chiki** script for Santali; *Bidu Chandan*, *Kherwal Bir* (born in Odisha, cornerstone of Jharkhand Santali literature) | Santali |
| Lako Bodra | Creator of **Warang Chiti** script for Ho; *Sahar Hora* | Ho |
| Dr. Ram Dayal Munda | *Adi Dharam*, *The Jharkhand Movement*, Mundari songs (Padma Shri) | Mundari / Hindi / English |
| Father Camille Bulcke | *Ramkatha: Utpatti aur Vikas*, authoritative *English–Hindi Dictionary* (Ranchi scholar) | Hindi / English |
| Jaipal Singh Munda | *Lo Bir Sendra* and landmark Constituent Assembly speeches on Adivasi rights | English / Mundari |
| Hansda Sowvendra Shekhar | *The Adivasi Will Not Dance*, *The Mysterious Ailment of Rupi Baskey* (Sahitya Akademi Yuva Puraskar) | English / Santali |
| Jacinta Kerketta | *Angor*, *Jadon Ki Zameen* (internationally acclaimed Adivasi poet) | Hindi / Kurukh |
| Bulu Imam | *The Tribal Art of Jharkhand*, *Relics of the Stone Age* (Hazaribagh rock art activist) | English |
| Mahasweta Devi | *Aranyer Adhikar* (Sahitya Akademi Award novel on **Birsa Munda**) | Bengali |
| Radhakrishna | *Rupantara*, *Genda* (pioneer Hindi novelist/storyteller of Jharkhand) | Hindi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| **Sarhul** | March–April (Chaitra Shukla Tritiya) | Greatest tribal festival celebrating the **New Year** and worship of the blooming **Sal tree** at the *Sarna* sacred grove |
| **Karma / Karam** | August–September (Bhadrapada Shukla Ekadashi) | Celebrates nature, fertility, and brother–sister bond around a sacred *Karam* tree branch |
| **Sohrai** | October–November (Kartik Amavasya, after Diwali) | Major harvest and cattle-worship festival featuring elaborate *Sohrai* wall murals |
| **Tusu Parab (Makar)** | January (Makar Sankranti) | Harvest festival predominantly celebrated by unmarried girls carrying decorated bamboo-paper *Chaudals* |
| **Baha** | February–March | Santhali festival of flowers welcoming spring without animal sacrifice, using pure Sal and Mahua blossoms |
| **Manda** | April–May (Vaishakh) | Rigorous Shiva-worship festival involving barefoot fire-walking (*Phulkundi*) and swinging (*Charkha Puja*) |
| **Jani Shikar** | Once every **12 years** (May–June) | Unique Oraon festival where women dress in male attire and go on a ceremonial hunt, commemorating their victory over invaders at Rohtasgarh |
| **Hal Punhya & Rohini** | January & May | Agricultural festivals marking the ceremonial start of ploughing (first day of Magh) and seed sowing (*Rohini*) |
| **Shravani Mela** | July–August (Shravan) | Month-long *Kanwar Yatra* pilgrimage to **Baba Baidyanath Dham** in Deoghar |
| **Bhagta Parab & Hijla Mela** | Spring (Feb–Mar) | Worship of Budha Baba (Tamar) and iconic Santhal tribal cultural fair along the Mayurakshi River in Dumka |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| **Santhal** | Santhal Pargana (Dumka, Deoghar, Jamtara, Sahibganj, Pakur, Godda) | **Largest tribe in Jharkhand**; *Ol Chiki* script; *Bitlaha* social code; *Manjhi* village council; *Sohrai* & *Baha* festivals |
| **Oraon (Kurukh)** | Ranchi, Gumla, Lohardaga, Latehar | Speak Dravidian *Kurukh*; **Dhumkuria** youth dormitory; *Parha* Panchayat system; *Jani Shikar*; *Tana Bhagat* movement |
| **Munda** | Ranchi, Khunti, West Singhbhum | **Khuntkatti** communal land system; **Gitiora** youth dormitory; *Sarna* worship; home to Birsa Munda |
| **Ho** | Kolhan (East & West Singhbhum, Seraikela) | *Warang Chiti* script; traditional **Manki–Munda** self-governance; *Mage Parab* festival |
| **Kharia** | Gumla, Simdega | Divided into Hill, Dudh, and Dhelki Kharias; speak Austroasiatic Kharia language |
| **Asur** *(PVTG)* | Netarhat Plateau (Gumla, Latehar, Lohardaga) | One of India's oldest **iron-smelting** tribal communities; *Sarhul* & *Sohrai* traditions |
| **Birhor** *(PVTG)* | Hazaribagh, Chatra, Giridih, Gumla | Semi-nomadic "forest people" famed for rope-making from *chop* creeper fibers and monkey hunting |
| **Sauria Paharia & Mal Paharia** *(PVTG)* | Rajmahal Hills (Sahibganj, Pakur, Godda) | Speak Dravidian *Malto*; practice traditional *Kurao* shifting cultivation on hill slopes |
| **Korwa, Parhaiya, Birjia, Savar** *(PVTGs)* | Palamu, Garhwa, Latehar, East Singhbhum | Remaining 4 of Jharkhand's **8 Particularly Vulnerable Tribal Groups (PVTGs)** |
| **Chik Baraik, Mahli & Lohra** | Chotanagpur Plateau | Traditional artisan tribes: weavers (*Chik Baraik*), bamboo craftsmen (*Mahli*), and blacksmiths (*Lohra*) |
| **Puran** | Singhbhum / Kolhan | Added as the **33rd Scheduled Tribe** of Jharkhand (taking total STs from 32 to 33) |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| **Ranchi** | Capital; **"City of Waterfalls"** (Hundru, Dassam, Jonha, Hirni); Heavy Engineering Corporation (HEC); IIM Ranchi; BIT Mesra; MS Dhoni's hometown |
| **Jamshedpur** | **"Steel City of India"** & **"Pittsburgh of India"**; site of **TISCO (Tata Steel, est. 1907)** at Sakchi — India's first planned industrial city; Jubilee Park; XLRI |
| **Dhanbad** | **"Coal Capital of India"**; Jharia Coalfields; IIT (ISM) Dhanbad; Topchanchi Lake |
| **Bokaro** | **Bokaro Steel Plant** (established during 3rd Five-Year Plan with Soviet Union assistance) |
| **Deoghar** | **Baba Baidyanath Dham** (one of 12 Jyotirlingas and a Shakti Peetha); Trikut Hills; Shravani Mela |
| **Hazaribagh** | **Sohrai-Khovar GI painting**; Isko Rock Art; Hazaribagh Wildlife Sanctuary; Canary Hill |
| **Giridih** | **Parasnath Hill (Shikharji)** — highest peak of Jharkhand (1,365 m) and supreme Jain pilgrimage center |
| **Dumka** | Sub-capital of Jharkhand; **Maluti Terracotta Temples**; Massanjore Dam; Hijla Mela |
| **Koderma** | **"Mica Capital of India"** (world-famous Ruby Mica reserves); Tilaiya Dam |
| **Jaduguda** (East Singhbhum) | **India's first and primary Uranium mine** (UCIL) |
| **Noamundi / Saranda** (West Singhbhum) | **Saranda Forest ("Land of 700 Hills")** — Asia's largest Sal forest; major Iron Ore mines (Noamundi, Chiria) |
| **Sindri** (Dhanbad) | Site of independent India's first major public-sector fertilizer factory (FCI, 1951) |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Baba Baidyanath Dham (Deoghar) | Rebuilt by Raja Puran Mal of Gidhaur (1596 CE) | One of the **12 Jyotirlingas** and **51 Shakti Peethas** (*Kamna Linga*) |
| Parasnath / Sammed Shikharji | Ancient Jain Tradition | Supreme Jain pilgrimage in Giridih where 20 of 24 Tirthankaras attained Moksha |
| Maluti Terracotta Temples | Baj Basanta Rulers (17th–19th Century CE) | Cluster of 72 surviving (originally 108) miniature terracotta-carved temples in Dumka depicting Ramayana/Mahabharata scenes |
| Chhinnamastika Temple (Rajrappa) | Ancient Shakta Tradition | Famous Tantric Shakti Peetha located at the confluence of the **Damodar and Bhairavi (Bhera) rivers** in Ramgarh |
| Palamu Forts (Old & New) | Chero King Medini Rai (17th Century CE) | Twin historic forest forts located inside Betla National Park / Palamu Tiger Reserve |
| Jagannath Temple (Ranchi) | Thakur Ani Nath Shahdeo (1691 CE) | Built on a hillock in Puri temple style; hosts annual Rath Yatra |
| Bhadrakali Temple (Itkhori) | Pala Period (9th–10th Century CE) | Unique sacred confluence site in Chatra revered by **Hindus, Buddhists, and Jains** |
| Navratnagarh (Doisa) Fort | Nagvanshi King Durjan Sal (17th Century CE) | 5-storeyed stone palace-fortress in Gumla (protected ASI monument) |
| Sun Temple (Bundu) | Sanskriti Vihar (Modern Architectural Tribute) | Granite temple shaped as an 18-wheeled chariot drawn by 7 horses near Ranchi |
| Isko Rock Art & Megaliths | Mesolithic–Chalcolithic Era | Prehistoric cave paintings in Hazaribagh featuring geometric, animal, and *Sohrai*-precursor motifs |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| *None on the main UNESCO World Heritage List* | — | No World Heritage Site is inscribed in Jharkhand as of 2026 |
| *(Intangible Heritage Note)* **Chhau Dance (Seraikela Chhau)** | UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage | **2010** (Inscribed alongside Purulia and Mayurbhanj Chhau) |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **GI Tags:** **Sohrai Khovar Painting** (2020 — Jharkhand's 1st GI tag) plus **11 newly granted GI tags in 2026**: **Dokra Craft**, **Jadopatia Painting**, **Kuchai Silk Saree**, **Jharkhand Tussar Silk and Sarees**, **Bhoya Saree and Fabric**, **Pancho Saree and Fabric**, **Munda Jewellery**, **Jharkhand Bamboo Craft**, **Kesaria Kalakand**, **Tumka Chadar**, and **Baroni Paintings**.
- **National Parks (1) & Tiger Reserves (1):** **Betla National Park** (Latehar/Palamu — core of **Palamu Tiger Reserve**, historically the site of the **world's first tiger census using pugmarks in 1932** under J.W. Nicholson!).
- **Ramsar Sites (1):** **Udhwa Lake Bird Sanctuary** (Sahibganj) — designated as **Jharkhand's first Ramsar site in early 2025**.
- **Notable Wildlife Sanctuaries (11):** **Mahuadanr Wolf Wildlife Sanctuary** (Latehar — **India's only sanctuary dedicated to Indian Wolves**), **Dalma WLS** (East Singhbhum — famous for Asian elephants), Hazaribagh WLS, Parasnath WLS, Topchanchi WLS, Lawalong WLS, Koderma WLS, Gautam Budha WLS, Palkot WLS, Palamu WLS, and Udhwa Lake.
- **Tribal Statistics:** **33 Scheduled Tribes** (after addition of **Puran**), including **8 PVTGs**. **Birsa Munda's** birthday (**November 15**) is celebrated across India as **Janjatiya Gaurav Divas**.
- **Mineral & Industrial Powerhouse:** Holds ~40% of India's mineral wealth (Coal in Jharia/Bokaro/Karanpura; Copper in Ghatshila; Uranium in Jaduguda; Mica in Koderma; Iron in Noamundi/Chiria). **Damodar Valley Corporation (DVC, 1948)** — India's first multipurpose river valley project — operates dams at Tilaiya, Konar, Maithon, and Panchet.
- **State Symbols:** Animal: **Asian Elephant**; Bird: **Asian Koel**; Flower: **Palash (Flame of the Forest)**; Tree: **Sal**.

### 11. Karnataka

**Capital:** Bengaluru (Winter Legislature Session held at Suvarna Vidhana Soudha, Belagavi)  
**Formation:** **November 1, 1956** (formed as Mysore State under the States Reorganisation Act; renamed **Karnataka** on **November 1, 1973** during CM D. Devaraj Urs's tenure — *Karnataka Rajyotsava*)  
**Official Language(s):** **Kannada** (Recognized as a **Classical Language of India** in **2008**)  
**Other Languages:** Tulu, Konkani (8th Schedule), Kodava Takk, Beary, Lambani, Marathi, Urdu, Telugu, Tamil, Hindi, English

#### Brief History
- **Mauryas & Jain Heritage:** **Chandragupta Maurya** renounced his throne and migrated with Jain saint **Bhadrabahu** to **Shravanabelagola** (~300 BCE). Numerous Ashokan edicts exist here, notably the **Maski Rock Edict (Raichur)** — the *first edict discovered bearing the personal name "Ashoka"*.
- **Early Indigenous Dynasties:** Ruled by **Satavahanas**, **Kadambas of Banavasi** (founded by **Mayurasharma** in 345 CE — first native Kannada kingdom), and **Western Gangas of Talakad** (who erected the colossal Gommateshwara statue).
- **Imperial Deccan Dynasties (Golden Ages of Architecture):**
  - **Chalukyas of Badami (6th–8th c.):** **Pulakeshin II** defeated Harshavardhana on the Narmada (Aihole inscription by Ravikirti). **Aihole** is dubbed the *"Cradle of Indian Temple Architecture"*.
  - **Rashtrakutas of Manyakheta (8th–10th c.):** **Amoghavarsha I** co-authored *Kavirajamarga*; Krishna I built the Ellora Kailasa Temple.
  - **Western Chalukyas of Kalyani (10th–12th c.):** Witnessed the 12th-century **Virashaiva / Lingayat Bhakti movement** led by **Basaveshwara (Basavanna)** and *Anubhava Mantapa* at Basavakalyan.
  - **Hoysalas (11th–14th c.):** **Vishnuvardhana** & Ballala II built iconic star-shaped soapstone temples at Belur, Halebidu, and Somnathapura.
- **Vijayanagara Empire (1336–1565):** Founded by **Harihara I and Bukka Raya I** at **Hampi**; reached its zenith under **Krishnadevaraya** (*Ashtadiggajas*); declined after the **Battle of Talikota (1565)**.
- **Deccan Sultanates & Mysore Kingdom:** **Adil Shahis of Bijapur** and **Bahmanis of Bidar/Gulbarga** shaped Indo-Islamic Deccani architecture. **Wodeyars of Mysore** rose after 1399, interrupted by **Hyder Ali** and **Tipu Sultan ("Tiger of Mysore")** who fought **four Anglo-Mysore Wars** (1767–1799), introducing rocketry warfare before falling at Srirangapatna (1799).
- **Freedom Movement:** **Kittur Rani Chennamma (1824)** led one of India's earliest armed rebellions against the British Doctrine of Lapse alongside **Sangolli Rayanna**. Belagavi hosted the **1924 INC Session** — the *only Congress session presided over by Mahatma Gandhi*. Other milestones include the **Vidurashwatha Massacre (1938 — "Jallianwala Bagh of South India")**, **Shivapura Flag Satyagraha (1938)**, and **Isuru Village Revolt (1942)**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| **Bharatanatyam (Mysore School)** | Classical | Flourished under royal Wodeyar patronage; distinct lyrical *abhinaya* and graceful footwork alongside Kuchipudi traditions |
| **Yakshagana** | Folk / Traditional Dance-Drama | Iconic coastal and Malnad night-long theatre combining vigorous dance, colourful headgear (*Mundasu*), dialogue, and *Chande/Maddale* percussion led by a *Bhagavata* narrator |
| Dollu Kunitha | Folk | Vigorous, athletic drum dance of the Kuruba shepherd community using heavy cylindrical *dollu* drums |
| Kamsale | Folk | Male devotional dance of the Mysuru region praising Lord Male Mahadeshwara with synchronized brass cymbals |
| Veeragase | Folk | Intense Shaivite heroic dance performed during Dasara and temple processions depicting Daksha Yajna |
| Bhootha Kola / Bhuta Aradhane | Folk / Ritual | Spirit-worship ritual dance of Tulunadu (Dakshina Kannada/Udupi) featuring ornate costumes and fire-torches |
| Pata Kunitha & Puja Kunitha | Folk | Processional dances of Mysuru/Mandya/Bengaluru carrying decorated bamboo poles (*Pata*) or sacred deity frameworks (*Puja*) on the head |
| Karaga | Folk | Historic Bengaluru festival dance led by a Thigala priest balancing an earthen floral pot on his head without touching it |
| Suggi Kunitha | Folk | Harvest dance performed by the Halakki Vokkaliga community of Uttara Kannada during Holi |
| Ummathat & Bolakat | Folk | Traditional circular dances of **Kodava** women (*Ummathat* with cymbals) and men (*Bolakat*) of Coorg |
| Siddi Dhamam | Folk | Rhythmic Afro-Indian tribal dance performed by the Siddis of Uttara Kannada |
| Gaarudi Gombe | Folk | Giant bamboo-paper puppet dance performed during Mysuru Dasara and major processions |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Classical Music Heritage:** Karnataka is the cradle of **Carnatic Music**, whose "Pitamaha" (Grandfather) **Purandara Dasa** systematized Carnatic pedagogy here (*Mayamalavagowla* scales), alongside **Kanakadasa** (*Dasa Sahitya*). Remarkably, northern Karnataka (**Hubballi–Dharwad**) is equally a powerhouse of **Hindustani Classical Music**, producing Bharat Ratna **Pt. Bhimsen Joshi**, **Gangubai Hangal**, **Mallikarjun Mansur**, **Basavaraj Rajguru**, and **Kumar Gandharva**.
- **Painting Styles:** **Mysore Traditional Painting** (GI-tagged — characterized by delicate lines, muted colours, and raised *gesso* work covered with pure gold leaf), **Chittara** (intricate Malnad geometric folk floor/wall murals drawn with rice paste), **Surpur Paintings** (18th–19th c. miniature style), and **Kawandi** (Siddi patchwork quilting art).
- **Handicrafts & Textiles:** **Bidriware** (Bidar — GI-tagged blackened zinc-copper alloy inlaid with pure silver), **Channapatna Toys** (GI-tagged lacquered ivory-wood toys, "Toy Town"), **Kinhal Toys** (Koppal — GI-tagged lightweight wooden craft), **Mysore Silk** (GI), **Mysore Rosewood Inlay** (GI), **Sandalwood Carving**, **Ilkal Saree** (GI), **Molakalmuru Saree** (GI), **Kasuti Embroidery** (Dharwad — GI), **Sandur Lambani Embroidery** (Ballari — GI), **Navalgund Durries** (GI), and **Kolhapuri Chappal** (shared GI).
