#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| **Mysuru Dasara (*Nadahabba*)** | September–October (Navratri, 10 days) | State festival started by Vijayanagara/Wodeyar kings; culminates in the **Jumbo Savari** procession from illuminated Mysuru Palace |
| **Mahamastakabhisheka** | Once every **12 years** (Feb, last in 2018; next 2030) | Sacred anointing of the 57-ft **Gommateshwara (Bahubali)** monolith at Shravanabelagola with milk, saffron, and ghee |
| **Kambala** | November–March | Traditional annual slush-track **buffalo race** held across coastal Dakshina Kannada and Udupi |
| **Ugadi** | March–April (Chaitra Shukla Pratipada) | Kannada New Year celebrated with *Bevu-Bella* (neem and jaggery) symbolizing life's sweet and bitter moments |
| **Hampi Utsav (Vijaya Utsav)** | November / January | Cultural festival celebrating the grandeur of the Vijayanagara Empire amid Hampi's ruins |
| **Bengaluru Karaga** | March–April (Chaitra Purnima) | Historic 9-day night festival of the Thigala community at Dharmaraya Swamy Temple |
| **Pattadakal Dance Festival (Chalukya Utsav)** | January–February | Classical dance festival organized amidst UNESCO-listed Chalukyan temples |
| **Vairamudi Brahmotsava** | March–April | Grand festival at **Melukote** Cheluvanarayana Swamy temple where the deity wears a sacred diamond crown |
| **Kailpodh, Kaveri Sankramana & Puthari** | Sep, Oct & Nov–Dec | Signature **Kodava** festivals celebrating weapons (Kailpodh), River Kaveri's sacred birth (Oct), and rice harvest (Puthari) |
| **Makara Sankranti** | January 14–15 | Harvest festival marked by exchanging *Yellu-Bella* (sesame-jaggery) and cattle parades (*Kichchu Haisuvudu*) |

|-------|--------|-----------|
| **Soliga** | Biligiri Ranganatha (BRT) Hills, Chamarajanagar | Forest-dwelling conservationists famous for sustainable NTFP harvesting and *Rotti Habba* festival |
| **Jenu Kuruba** *(PVTG)* | Mysuru, Kodagu, Chamarajanagar | Traditional wild honey collectors ("Jenu" = honey) inhabiting Nagarahole and Bandipur borders |
| **Koraga** *(PVTG)* | Dakshina Kannada, Udupi | Traditional bamboo/vine basket weavers known for *Dolu* drumming and *Kambala* folk traditions |
| **Siddi** | Uttara Kannada (Yellapur, Haliyal, Ankola) | Descendants of East African Bantu peoples brought centuries ago; renowned for *Dhamam* dance and *Kawandi* quilts |
| **Kadu Kuruba** | Kodagu, Mysuru (Nagarahole) | Skilled elephant mahouts, forest trackers, and bamboo craftspeople |
| **Hakki Pikki** | Shivamogga, Mysuru, Hassan | Semi-nomadic traditional bird-catchers now famous for herbal hair oils and multilingual dialect skills |
| **Malekudiya & Yerava** | Kudremukh / Dakshina Kannada & Kodagu | Forest-based agriculturalists and honey/bamboo gatherers of the Western Ghats |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| **Group of Monuments at Hampi** | Vijayanagara Kings (14th–16th Century CE) | Virupaksha Temple, **Vittala Temple** (iconic **Stone Chariot** featured on Rs. 50 note & 56 Musical Pillars), Lotus Mahal, Queen's Bath |
| **Group of Monuments at Pattadakal** | Badami Chalukyas (7th–8th Century CE) | Coronation capital; showcases fusion of **Nagara** and **Dravidian** (Vesara) styles (Virupaksha & Mallikarjuna Temples) |
| **Badami Cave Temples & Aihole** | Badami Chalukyas (6th–8th Century CE) | Four rock-cut caves at Badami (Hindu & Jain); **Aihole** ("Cradle of Indian Temple Architecture" — Durga & Lad Khan temples) |
| **Chennakeshava (Belur), Hoysaleswara (Halebidu) & Keshava (Somnathapura)** | Hoysala Empire (12th–13th Century CE) | Star-shaped (*stellate*) soapstone Vesara architecture; **UNESCO Sacred Ensembles of the Hoysalas (2023)** |
| **Gommateshwara (Bahubali) Statue** | Commissioned by **Chavundaraya** under Ganga King Rachamalla IV (981 CE) | 57-ft monolithic granite Jain statue at Shravanabelagola; one of the largest freestanding statues in the ancient world |
| **Gol Gumbaz (Vijayapura)** | Mohammed Adil Shah (1656 CE) | Features one of the world's largest pre-modern unsupported domes and a famous **"Whispering Gallery"** |
| **Mysore Palace (Amba Vilas)** | Wodeyars; rebuilt by Henry Irwin (1897–1912) | Iconic Indo-Saracenic palace; India's most visited palace monument |
| **Bidar Fort & Mahmud Gawan Madrasa** | Ahmad Shah Wali Bahmani & Mahmud Gawan (15th Century CE) | Historic Deccan fort and grand Persian-style Islamic university ruins |
| **Chitradurga Fort** | Nayakas of Chitradurga (17th–18th Century CE) | Seven-tiered concentric hill fort (*Yelusuttina Kote*), famed for heroine **Onake Obavva** |
| **Sringeri Sharada Peetham** | Adi Shankaracharya (8th Century CE) | **First of the four cardinal Advaita Mathas** established by Adi Shankaracharya, on the Tunga River |
| **Sri Krishna Matha (Udupi)** | Madhvacharya (13th Century CE) | Center of Dvaita Vedanta; features the famous *Kanakana Kindi* window |
| **Tipu Sultan's Summer Palace & Dariya Daulat Bagh** | Hyder Ali & Tipu Sultan (18th Century CE) | Teakwood palaces in Bengaluru and Srirangapatna with battle murals |
| **Vidhana Soudha (Bengaluru)** | Kengal Hanumanthaiah (1956 CE) | Iconic Neo-Dravidian granite State Secretariat ("Government's Work is God's Work") |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **GI Tags (40+):** **Mysore Silk**, **Mysore Sandalwood Oil**, **Mysore Sandal Soap**, **Mysore Agarbathi**, **Bidriware**, **Channapatna Toys & Dolls**, **Kinhal Toys**, **Mysore Traditional Paintings**, **Mysore Rosewood Inlay**, **Ilkal Saree**, **Molakalmuru Sarees**, **Kasuti Embroidery**, **Sandur Lambani Embroidery**, **Navalgund Durries**, **Coorg Orange**, **Coorg Green Cardamom**, **Byadagi Chilli**, **Nanjanagud Banana**, **Mysore Betel Leaf**, **Appemidi Mango**, **Devanahalli Pomelo**, **Gulbarga Tur Dal**, **Sirsi Supari**, **Mysore/Udupi/Hadagali Malligae (Jasmine)**, **Indi Limbe**, plus shared **Kolhapuri Chappal** (with Maharashtra), **Malabar Pepper** & **Monsooned Malabar Arabica/Robusta Coffee** (with Kerala).
- **National Parks (5):** **Bandipur NP**, **Nagarahole (Rajiv Gandhi) NP**, **Bannerghatta NP** (Bengaluru), **Kudremukh NP**, and **Anshi (Kali) NP**.
- **Tiger Reserves (5):** **Bandipur TR**, **Nagarahole TR**, **Bhadra TR**, **Kali (Dandeli–Anshi) TR**, and **Biligiri Ranganatha Temple (BRT) TR**.
- **Ramsar Sites (4):** **Ranganathittu Bird Sanctuary** (2022) and three designated in **2024**: **Ankasamudra Bird Conservation Reserve**, **Aghanashini Estuary**, and **Magadi Kere Conservation Reserve**.
- **Biosphere Reserve:** Part of the **Nilgiri Biosphere Reserve** (India's 1st Biosphere Reserve, est. 1986, WNBR 2000).
- **Literary & Infrastructure Milestones:** **8 Jnanpith Awards in Kannada** (highest among South Indian languages); **Hubballi Junction** has the **world's longest railway platform (1,507 m)**; **Maski** (first edict naming Ashoka); **Jog Falls** on Sharavathi River; **Kunchikal Falls** (Varahi River — highest tiered waterfall in India at ~455 m).
- **State Symbols:** Animal: **Asian Elephant**; Bird: **Indian Roller (Blue Jay)**; Flower: **Lotus**; Tree: **Sandalwood**; Butterfly: **Southern Birdwing**; Emblem: **Gandaberunda** (mythical two-headed bird).

### 12. Kerala

**Capital:** Thiruvananthapuram  
**Formation:** **November 1, 1956** (formed by merging Travancore–Cochin with the Malabar and Kasaragod regions of Madras State under the States Reorganisation Act, 1956 — *Kerala Piravi*)  
**Official Language(s):** **Malayalam** (Recognized as a **Classical Language of India** in **2013**); **English** (Additional Official Language)  
**Other Languages:** Tamil, Tulu, Konkani, Kannada, tribal languages (Kurumba, Irula, Paniya, Muduga)

#### Brief History
- **Sangam Age & Spice Trade:** Ancient **Chera Dynasty** ruled from Vanchi/Muziris (Kodungallur), trading black pepper and spices with Phoenicians, Greeks, and Rome.
- **Early Religious Arrival & Advaita:** Christianity arrived traditionally with **St. Thomas (52 CE)**; **Cheraman Juma Mosque** at Kodungallur (629 CE) is **India's oldest mosque**; **Paradesi Synagogue** (1568) in Kochi is the oldest active synagogue in the Commonwealth. **Adi Shankaracharya** (born at **Kalady**, 8th c.) propounded **Advaita Vedanta**.
- **Medieval Kingdoms & European Contact:** Under the **Kulasekharas (Second Cheras)**, Malayalam crystallized as a distinct language. Later divided among the **Zamorin of Kozhikode**, **Kingdom of Kochi**, and **Travancore**. **Vasco da Gama** landed at **Kappad Beach (near Kozhikode) on May 20, 1498**, inaugurating European maritime colonialism.
- **Resistance Against Colonial Powers:** **Travancore King Marthanda Varma** defeated the Dutch East India Company at the **Battle of Colachel (1741)** — the *first decisive defeat of a European naval power by an Asian kingdom*. Early anti-British resistance was led by **Kerala Varma Pazhassi Raja** ("Lion of Kerala", 1790s–1805) and **Velu Thampi Dalawa** (*Kundara Proclamation*, 1809).
- **Renaissance, Social Reforms & Freedom Struggle:** Spearheaded by **Sree Narayana Guru** (*"One Caste, One Religion, One God for Mankind"*, SNDP Yogam 1903), **Ayyankali** (*Villuvandi* cart protest 1893), **Chattambi Swamikal**, **Vaikom Satyagraha (1924–25)**, **Guruvayur Satyagraha (1931–32)**, **Temple Entry Proclamation (1936)** by Chithira Thirunal Balarama Varma, **Malabar (Moplah) Rebellion (1921)**, and **Punnapra–Vayalar Uprising (1946)**. In **1957**, Kerala elected the world's first democratically elected Communist ministry under **E.M.S. Namboodiripad**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| **Kathakali** | **Classical** | Renowned story-play (*attakatha*) featuring towering crowns (*kiritam*), elaborate *pacha/kathi/thadi* facial painting, intricate *mudras*, and *chenda/maddalam* percussion; revived by **Vallathol Narayana Menon** at Kerala Kalamandalam |
| **Mohiniyattam** | **Classical** | "Dance of the Enchantress" — graceful solo female *lasya* dance in white-and-gold *kasavu* saree with swaying *atavus* |
| **Koodiyattam** | Classical Sanskrit Theatre | Oldest surviving Sanskrit theatre (Chakyar community, *Mizhavu* drum); proclaimed **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage (2008)** |
| **Mudiyettu** | Folk / Ritual Dance-Drama | Ritual Bhadrakali–Darika battle dance of Central Kerala; proclaimed **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage (2010)** |
| Theyyam (Kaliyattam) | Folk / Ritual | Vibrant North Malabar "Dance of the Gods" with towering headpieces (*mudi*), red-orange paints, and oracle trance |
| Ottamthullal | Folk | Witty, satirical solo narrative dance-poetry founded in the 18th century by **Kunchan Nambiar** ("poor man's Kathakali") |
| Thiruvathirakali (Kaikottikali) | Folk | Graceful circular clapping dance performed by women in *kasavu* sarees around a *nilavilakku* during Onam & Thiruvathira |
| Oppana | Folk | Joyful Mappila Muslim bridal group dance with rhythmic hand-clapping |
| Margamkali | Folk | Traditional Syrian Christian women's circle dance celebrating St. Thomas's legacy |
| Pulikali & Kummattikali | Folk | Thrissur's colorful "Tiger Dance" (body-painted dancers during Onam) and wooden-mask folk dance (*Kummattikali*) |
| Padayani & Chakyar Koothu | Folk / Ritual | Southern Travancore painted areca-spathe mask dance (*Padayani*) and witty solo Chakyar storytelling (*Chakyar Koothu*) |
| Kolkali & Parichamuttukali | Folk / Martial | Energetic stick dance (*Kolkali*) and sword-and-shield martial folk dance linked to **Kalaripayattu** |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Traditional Music:** **Sopana Sangeetham** (devotional temple vocal style sung beside the sanctum steps accompanied by the hourglass *idakka* drum), **Carnatic Music** (patronized brilliantly by Travancore King **Swathi Thirunal**), **Mappila Pattu** (Malabar Muslim melodic ballads), **Nadan Pattu** (folk songs), and monumental temple percussion ensembles **Panchavadyam** and **Chenda Melam / Pandi Melam** (*chenda, maddalam, ilathalam, kombu, timila*).
- **Painting & Floor Art:** **Kerala Mural Paintings** (bold vibrant frescoes using five natural pigments — *Panchavarna* — adorning Mattancherry Palace, Padmanabhapuram Palace, Guruvayur, and Ettumanoor) and **Kalamezhuthu** (sacred temporary floor drawings of deities using five natural powders before ritual erasure).
- **Handicrafts & Textiles:** **Aranmula Kannadi** (GI-tagged unique front-surface high-tin bronze metal mirror), **Kannadippaya** ("Mirror Mat" — **GI-tagged April 2025**, **Kerala's first tribal handicraft GI**, woven from inner bamboo reeds by tribal artisans), **Alleppey Coir** (GI), **Kuthampully, Balaramapuram, Chendamangalam & Kasaragod Kasavu Handlooms** (GI), **Payyannur Pavithra Mothiram** (sacred gold/silver ring — GI), **Nettur Petti** (ornate Malabar jewellery box), **Screw Pine Mats**, and **Maddalam of Palakkad** (GI).

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| **Onam** | August–September (Chingam, 10 days) | **State & Harvest Festival** welcoming mythical King **Mahabali**; marked by *Athappookalam* floral carpets, *Onasadya* feast, *Pulikali*, and **Vallam Kali** (snake-boat races) |
| **Thrissur Pooram** | April–May (Medam) | Grandest temple festival at **Vadakkunnathan Temple** instituted by **Sakthan Thampuran**; features *Kudamattam* (umbrella exchange), 30+ caparisoned elephants, and *Ilanjithara Melam* |
| **Vishu** | April 14/15 (Medam 1) | Malayalam Astronomical New Year featuring auspicious dawn viewing of **Vishukkani** and gifting **Vishukkaineetam** |
| **Makaravilakku** | January 14 (Makar Sankranti) | Sacred annual pilgrimage culmination at **Sabarimala Sree Dharma Sastha Temple** |
| **Attukal Pongala** | February–March | Held at Attukal Bhagavathy Temple, Thiruvananthapuram; Guinness World Record for the **largest annual gathering of women** |
| **Nehru Trophy Boat Race** | 2nd Saturday of August | Iconic *Chundan Vallam* (snake-boat) race on **Punnamada Lake**, Alappuzha |
| **Kalpathy Ratholsavam** | November | Grand 7-day Vedic chariot festival in Palakkad |
| **Theyyam Season** | November–May | Month-long *Kaliyattam* shrine ceremonies across Kannur and Kasaragod |
| **Maramon Convention** | February | Asia's largest annual Christian religious convention on the Pampa riverbed (Pathanamthitta) |

| **Kurichiyan** | Wayanad, Kannur | Famous traditional **archers** who fought alongside Pazhassi Raja against the British; matrilineal joint-family culture |
| **Cholanaikkan** *(PVTG)* | Nilambur Forests (Malappuram) | One of Asia's last surviving rock-cave dwelling hunter-gatherer communities (5 PVTGs in Kerala total) |
| **Kadar, Kattunayakan, Koraga & Kurumba** *(PVTGs)* | Palakkad (Silent Valley/Parambikulam), Wayanad, Kasaragod, Attappady | Remaining **4 of Kerala's 5 PVTGs** — wild honey gatherers (*Kattunayakan*), bamboo reed artisans (*Kadar*), basket weavers (*Koraga*), and pastoral hill tribes (*Kurumba*) |
| **Muthuvan, Mannan & Oorali** | Idukki, Ernakulam, Thrissur | Key artisans behind **Kannadippaya** (GI-tagged bamboo mirror mat); *Mannan Koothu* tribal dance |

| **Irula & Mudugar** | Attappady (Palakkad) | Traditional millet cultivators (*Attappady Thuvara* / *Attappady Aattukombu Avara* GI crops) |

| **Kochi (Ernakulam)** | **"Queen of the Arabian Sea"**; Commercial Capital; **India's first Water Metro** (2023); **Cochin Shipyard** (built **INS Vikrant**); Fort Kochi; Mattancherry; International Spice Trade |

| **Thrissur** | **"Cultural Capital of Kerala"**; Thrissur Pooram; **Kerala Kalamandalam** (Cheruthuruthy); **Guruvayur** Sri Krishna Temple; oldest Cheraman Mosque nearby |

| **Kasaragod** | **Bekal Fort**; *Sapta Bhasha Sangama Bhoomi* (7-language confluence); Kasaragod Sarees |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| **Sree Padmanabhaswamy Temple** | Travancore Kings / Marthanda Varma (18th c. renovation) | Iconic Kerala–Dravidian temple in Thiruvananthapuram; world's richest temple; reclining Vishnu on Ananta |
| **Vadakkunnathan Temple** | Ancient Chera / Kerala Architecture | Thrissur's grand timber-and-laterite Shiva temple (UNESCO Asia-Pacific Heritage Award); venue of Thrissur Pooram |
| **Guruvayur Sri Krishna Temple & Sabarimala** | Ancient Pilgrimage Traditions | Major pilgrimage hubs (*Bhuloka Vaikunta* at Guruvayur; forest shrine of Lord Ayyappa at Sabarimala) |
| **Cheraman Juma Mosque (Kodungallur)** | Built by Malik Dinar (629 CE) | **Oldest mosque in India** (and second oldest Juma mosque globally), constructed in traditional Kerala temple architecture |
| **Paradesi Synagogue (Mattancherry)** | Malabari & Sephardic Jews (1568 CE) | **Oldest active synagogue in the Commonwealth of Nations**, featuring hand-painted Chinese tiles and Belgian chandeliers |
| **St. Francis Church (Fort Kochi)** | Portuguese Friars (1503 CE) | **Oldest European church in India**; Vasco da Gama was originally buried here in 1524 before transfer to Lisbon |
| **Bekal Fort** | Shivappa Nayaka of Keladi (1650 CE) | Largest and best-preserved keyhole-shaped coastal fort in Kerala (Kasaragod) |
| **Mattancherry Palace (Dutch Palace)** | Built by Portuguese (1555 CE), renovated by Dutch (1663) | Royal museum in Kochi famous for extraordinary *Ramayana* and *Puranic* Kerala murals |
| **Krishnapuram Palace** | Marthanda Varma (18th Century CE) | Kayamkulam palace preserving *Gajendra Moksham*, Kerala's largest single-panel mural |
| **Padmanabhapuram Palace** | Iravi Varma Kulasekhara Perumal (1601 CE) | Asia's largest traditional **wooden palace** (located in Kanyakumari district, TN, yet owned and maintained by the Govt. of Kerala) |
| **Edakkal Caves (Wayanad)** | Neolithic–Megalithic Cultures (~6000–1000 BCE) | Natural rock shelter in Ambukuthi Hills featuring ancient prehistoric petroglyphs |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **GI Tags (35+):** Includes **Kannadippaya (April 2025 — Kerala's 1st tribal GI tag)**, **Aranmula Kannadi**, **Alleppey Coir**, **Malabar Pepper**, **Alleppey Green Cardamom**, **Palakkadan Matta Rice**, **Pokkali Rice** (salt-tolerant paddy), **Navara Rice**, **Kaipad Rice**, **Wayanad Jeerakasala & Gandhakasala Rice**, **Wayanad Robusta Coffee**, **Tirur Betel Leaf**, **Marayoor Jaggery**, **Central Travancore Jaggery**, **Nilambur Teak**, **Chengalikodan Nendran Banana**, **Attappady Thuvara**, **Kuthampully, Balaramapuram, Chendamangalam & Kasaragod Handlooms**, **Payyannur Pavithra Ring**, **Maddalam of Palakkad**, and shared **Monsooned Malabar Arabica/Robusta Coffee**.
- **National Parks (6):** **Eravikulam NP** (Nilgiri Tahr & *Neelakurinji* flower blooming once every 12 years), **Periyar NP**, **Silent Valley NP** (Lion-tailed Macaque; evergreen tropical rainforest), **Anamudi Shola NP**, **Mathikettan Shola NP**, and **Pambadum Shola NP**.
- **Tiger Reserves (2):** **Periyar TR** (Thekkady) and **Parambikulam TR**.
- **Ramsar Sites (3):** **Vembanad-Kol Wetland** (**India's longest lake** at 96.5 km), **Ashtamudi Wetland**, and **Sasthamkotta Lake** (Kerala's largest freshwater lake) — all designated in 2002.
- **Biosphere Reserves (2 in UNESCO MAB WNBR):** **Nilgiri Biosphere Reserve** and **Agasthyamalai Biosphere Reserve** (shared with Tamil Nadu).
- **Highest Peak in South India:** **Anamudi** (2,695 m) in Eravikulam National Park, Idukki.
- **Demographic & Cultural Firsts:** Highest sex ratio among states in Census 2011 (**1,084 per 1,000**); highest literacy rate (**94%** in 2011; first 100% literate state in 1991); **India's first Water Metro** (Kochi, 2023); **India's first UNESCO City of Literature** (Kozhikode, 2023); **6 Jnanpith Awards** (including India's 1st in 1965 to G. Sankara Kurup).
- **State Symbols:** Animal: **Indian Elephant**; Bird: **Great Indian Hornbill**; Flower: **Kanikonna (Golden Shower)**; Tree: **Coconut Tree**.

### 13. Madhya Pradesh

**Capital:** Bhopal  
**Formation:** **November 1, 1956** (formed by merging Madhya Bharat, Vindhya Pradesh, Bhopal State, and Mahakoshal); reorganized on **November 1, 2000** when Chhattisgarh was carved out  
**Official Language(s):** Hindi  
**Other Languages:** Bundeli, Bagheli, Malvi, Nimadi, Gondi, Bhili, Korku, Urdu, Marathi

#### Brief History
- **Prehistoric & Chalcolithic Cradle:** **Bhimbetka Rock Shelters** preserve Paleolithic/Mesolithic cave art; **Hathnora** (Sehore, Narmada Valley) yielded South Asia's earliest fossilized hominid cranium (*Narmada Man*).
- **Mahajanapadas & Maurya–Shunga–Gupta Era:** **Avanti** (capitals Ujjain & Mahishmati) and **Chedi** were major Mahajanapadas. **Ashoka** served as Viceroy of Ujjain and built the **Sanchi Stupa**; the Shungas built **Bharhut Stupa**, and Greek ambassador Heliodorus erected the **Besnagar Garuda Pillar** (Vidisha). Under the **Guptas**, Ujjain became India's cultural and scientific capital (Kalidasa; Udayagiri & Bagh Caves; **Eran Inscription of 510 CE** — the *first epigraphic record of Sati in India*).
- **Medieval Dynasties:** **Gurjara-Pratiharas**, **Paramaras of Malwa** (**Raja Bhoj** of Dhar — patron of arts, *Bhojshala*, Bhojpur), **Chandelas of Jejakabhukti** (built Khajuraho Temples, **Vidyadhara**), **Kalachuris of Tripuri**, **Tomaras of Gwalior** (**Man Singh Tomar**), and the **Malwa Sultanate** at **Mandu** (Hoshang Shah, Baz Bahadur & Rani Roopmati).
- **Gondwana, Marathas & Begums:** **Rani Durgavati** of Garha-Katanga (Gondwana) valiantly resisted Akbar's army led by Asaf Khan in **1564**. Later ruled by **Bundelas** (Orchha/Chhatrasal), Maratha confederates (**Scindias of Gwalior**, **Holkars of Indore** led by **Devi Ahilyabai Holkar**), and the **Begums of Bhopal** (four progressive female rulers: Qudsia, Sikandar, Shah Jahan, and Sultan Jahan Begum).
- **Freedom Movement:** **Rani Avantibai Lodhi (1857, Ramgarh)**, **Tatya Tope** (betrayed and hanged at Shivpuri, 1859), **Tantya Bhil** ("Indian Robin Hood"), **Bhima Nayak**, **Chandra Shekhar Azad** (born at Bhavra, Alirajpur), **Jhanda Satyagraha (1923, Jabalpur)**, and **Charan Paduka Massacre (1931, Chhatarpur — "Jallianwala Bagh of MP")**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Matki | Folk | Signature **Malwa** women's solo/group wedding dance balancing multiple earthen pots (*matkis*) on the head to *dhol* beats |
| Jawara | Folk | **Bundelkhand** harvest celebration where women balance baskets of germinating wheat seedlings (*jawara*) on their heads |
| Rai | Folk | Famous **Bundelkhand** auspicious festival dance performed to the vigorous rhythm of *mridang* and *dholak* |
| Tertali | Folk | Unique **Kamar** tribal women's devotional dance performed seated, striking 13 small brass cymbals (*manjiras*) tied to the body |
| Karma & Saila | Folk | Iconic **Gond and Baiga** harvest/festival dances (*Karma* honors Karma Devta; *Saila* uses bamboo sticks) |
| Bhagoria | Folk | Vibrant **Bhil and Bhilala** youth courtship and harvest dance performed before Holi in Jhabua/Alirajpur |
| Ahiri / Baredi | Folk | Cattle-herder celebration dance performed by the Yadav community of Bundelkhand/Gwalior during Diwali |
| Phulpati & Grida | Folk | Malwa unmarried girls' dance (*Phulpati*) and winter rabi harvest celebration (*Grida*) |
| Gangaur & Kathi | Folk | Popular Nimad region devotional folk dances |
| Maanch | Folk Theatre-Dance | Traditional **Malwa** lyrical folk drama — officially recognized as the **State Drama of Madhya Pradesh** |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Hindustani Classical Gharanas:**
  - **Gwalior Gharana:** The **oldest Khayal gharana** of Hindustani music; associated with **Mian Tansen** (born Behat near Gwalior) and Baiju Bawra; Gwalior is a **UNESCO Creative City of Music (2023)**.
  - **Maihar Gharana:** Founded by **Ustad Allauddin Khan** (Satna/Maihar), guru of **Pt. Ravi Shankar**, **Ustad Ali Akbar Khan**, and **Annapurna Devi**.
  - **Indore Gharana:** Founded by **Ustad Amir Khan**; **Senia Gharana** flourished at Rewa.
- **Folk Songs:** **Pandavani**, **Alha-Udal** (Bundelkhand heroic epic), **Bharthari** (Malwa), **Kalgi-Turra** (Nimad competitive singing), **Biraha**, and **Hardol ki Manauti**.
- **Painting Styles:** **Gond Painting** (GI-tagged intricate dot-and-line tribal art popularized globally by **Jangarh Singh Shyam**), **Pithora Painting** (sacred horse mural art of Bhils/Bhilalas), **Mandana** (floor/wall white-chalk geometric folk art), **Bagh Print** (Dhar — GI natural-dye hand block print), and **Ujjain Batik Print** (GI).
- **Handicrafts & Textiles:** **Chanderi Saree** & **Maheshwari Saree** (both GI-tagged delicate silk-cotton weaves; Maheshwari revived by Devi Ahilyabai Holkar), **Waraseoni Handloom Saree** (GI), **Bell Metal Ware (Dokra) of Datia & Tikamgarh** (GI), **Betul Bharewa Metal Craft** (GI), **Jabalpur & Khajuraho Stone Crafts** (GI), **Leather Toys of Indore** (GI), **Gwalior Handmade Carpet & Paper Mache** (GI), and **Bhopal Zari-Zardozi**.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| **Kalidasa** | *Abhijnanasakuntalam*, *Meghaduta*, *Raghuvamsha*, *Kumarasambhava* (Ujjain court) | Sanskrit |
| Bhavabhuti | *Malatimadhava*, *Uttararamacharita*, *Mahaviracharita* (Padmavati/Vidisha region) | Sanskrit |
| Raja Bhoj | *Samarangana Sutradhara* (architecture), *Saraswati Kanthabharana* (Paramara King) | Sanskrit |
| Keshavdas | *Ramchandrika*, *Rasikapriya*, *Kavipriya* (Orchha, Ritikal pioneer) | Hindi (Braj) |
| Jagnik | *Alha Khand* (epic ballad of Bundela heroes Alha and Udal) | Bundeli / Hindi |
| **Makhanlal Chaturvedi** | *Him Tarangini* (**India's VERY FIRST Hindi Sahitya Akademi Award, 1955**), *Pushp ki Abhilasha* | Hindi |
| Subhadra Kumari Chauhan | *Jhansi Ki Rani*, *Bikhare Moti* (active in Jabalpur) | Hindi |
| Gajanan Madhav Muktibodh | *Chand Ka Muh Tedha Hai* (Sheopur, New Poetry pioneer) | Hindi |
| Harishankar Parsai | *Viklang Shraddha Ka Daur* (Sahitya Akademi; master of Hindi satire, Jabalpur) | Hindi |
| Bhawani Prasad Mishra & Sharad Joshi | *Buni Hui Rassi* (Mishra); *Andhon Ka Hathi* (Joshi — Ujjain) | Hindi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| **Simhastha Kumbh Mela** | Once every **12 years** (April–May; next in **2028**) | Sacred Kumbh bathing festival held on the **Kshipra River** at **Ujjain** when Jupiter enters Leo (*Simha*) |
| **Khajuraho Dance Festival** | February (7 days) | Premier national classical dance festival set against the illuminated Chandela temples |
| **Tansen Samaroh** | December (4 days) | Prestigious Hindustani classical music festival held at Tansen's tomb in **Gwalior** |
| **Bhagoria Haat** | March (week before Holi) | Traditional **Bhil & Bhilala** harvest, music, and courtship fair in Jhabua, Alirajpur, and Dhar |
| **Lokrang Festival** | January (Republic Day week) | Major tribal and folk arts festival hosted at Ravindra Bhavan/Bhopal |
| **Malwa Utsav & Kalidas Samaroh** | May & November | Festivals in Indore and Ujjain celebrating Malwa traditions and Sanskrit poet Kalidasa |
| **Allauddin Khan Music Festival** | March / Spring | Classical music gathering in **Maihar** honoring the founder of the Maihar Gharana |
| **Mandu Utsav & Orchha Utsav** | Dec–Jan & March | Heritage and cultural festivals celebrating medieval architecture at Mandu and Orchha |
| **Madai Festival** | Jan–March | Traditional tribal gathering and deity procession of the Gond community |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| **Bhil (incl. Bhilala)** | Jhabua, Alirajpur, Dhar, Khargone, Barwani | **Largest tribe in MP (and in India)**; celebrate **Bhagoria Haat**; famous for **Pithora** wall paintings and *Ghoomar/Dhol* dance |
| **Gond** | Mandla, Dindori, Chhindwara, Betul, Shahdol | **2nd largest tribe in MP**; creators of GI-tagged **Gond Painting**; *Karma/Saila* dances; *Madai* festival; legacy of **Rani Durgavati** |
| **Baiga** *(PVTG)* | Mandla, Dindori, Balaghat, Umaria | **National Human of the Forest** heritage; intricate ***Godna* (body tattooing)**, *Bewar* shifting cultivation, *Karma* & *Dadaria* songs |
| **Sahariya** *(PVTG)* | Shivpuri, Sheopur, Guna, Gwalior, Morena | Renowned knowledge of forest medicinal herbs; *Lahangi* & *Duldul Ghodi* folk dances |
| **Bharia** *(PVTG)* | **Patalkot Valley** (Chhindwara) | Live in the isolated deep horse-shoe gorge of **Patalkot**; skilled herbal healers (*Bhumka*) |
| **Korku** | Betul, Harda, Narmadapuram, Khandwa | Celebrate *Sidoli* memorial pillar rituals and perform *Khamb Swang* theatre |
| **Kol & Panika** | Rewa, Satna, Sidhi, Shahdol | *Kol Dahaka* dance (Kol); traditional weaving and followers of Kabirpanth (Panika) |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| **Bhopal** | Capital; **"City of Lakes"**; Bhoj Wetland; Bharat Bhavan; Indira Gandhi Rashtriya Manav Sangrahalaya; Taj-ul-Masajid; **1984 Bhopal Gas Tragedy** |
| **Indore** | Commercial capital; **"Mini Mumbai"**; **Cleanest City of India** (record 7+ consecutive Swachh Survekshan #1 ranks); **ONLY city in India with both an IIT and an IIM**; Rajwada; Leather Toys (GI) |
| **Ujjain** | **"City of Temples"**; **Mahakaleshwar Jyotirlinga**; **Simhastha Kumbh Mela**; ancient **Greenwich of India** (Tropic of Cancer & Vedh Shala / Jantar Mantar); Sandipani Ashram |
| **Gwalior** | **UNESCO Creative City of Music (2023)**; **Gwalior Fort**; Tansen Tomb; Jai Vilas Palace; birthplace of Atal Bihari Vajpayee |
| **Jabalpur** | **"Marble City of India"**; **Bhedaghat & Dhuandhar Falls** on Narmada; MP High Court; Ordinance Factory; **West Central Railway HQ** |
| **Khajuraho** (Chhatarpur) | UNESCO World Heritage Chandela Temples; annual Khajuraho Dance Festival |
| **Sanchi** (Raisen) | UNESCO World Heritage **Great Stupa** built by Emperor Ashoka; *India's first Solar City* (2023) |
| **Panna** | **"Diamond City of India"** (Majhgawan diamond mines — India's only mechanized diamond mine); Panna Tiger Reserve |
| **Orchha** (Niwari) | **Ram Raja Temple** (only temple in India where Lord Rama is worshipped as a King with police guard of honour); Bundela heritage |
| **Mandu (Mandavgad)** | **"City of Joy"**; Jahaz Mahal, Rani Roopmati Pavilion, Hoshang Shah's Tomb |
| **Pithampur** (Dhar) | **"Detroit of India"** (major automobile & pharma manufacturing hub) |
| **Nepanagar & Singrauli** | Newsprint paper mill (Nepanagar — 1st in India); **"Energy Capital of MP"** (Singrauli coal & thermal power plants) |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| **Khajuraho Group of Monuments** | Chandela Dynasty (950–1050 CE) | Nagara-style Hindu and Jain temples (**Kandariya Mahadeva**, Lakshmana, Vishwanath, Parsvanath); world-famous stone sculptures |
| **Great Stupa at Sanchi** | Mauryan Emperor Ashoka (3rd c. BCE); expanded by Shungas & Satavahanas | Oldest stone structure in India; exquisitely carved *Toranas* (gateways) depicting Jataka tales |
| **Rock Shelters of Bhimbetka** | Prehistoric (Paleolithic–Mesolithic) | ~750 rock shelters in Raisen (Ratapani landscape) preserving human cave paintings dating over 10,000–30,000 years |
| **Gwalior Fort** | Suraj Sen / Man Singh Tomar (6th–16th Century CE) | Called **"Pearl amongst fortresses in India"** by Babur; houses Gujari Mahal, Teli ka Mandir, Sas-Bahu Temple, and **Chaturbhuj Temple (876 CE inscription bearing one of the world's oldest engraved "0" symbols)** |
| **Chausath Yogini Temple (Mitaoli, Morena)** | Kachchhapaghata King Devapala (11th Century CE) | Circular hypaethral 64-pillared temple that famously **inspired the design of India's Old Parliament House (Sansad Bhavan)** |
| **Mahakaleshwar & Omkareshwar** | Ancient / Rebuilt by Marathas | **Two of India's 12 Jyotirlingas**; Mahakaleshwar (Ujjain) is the *only South-facing (Dakshinamukhi) Jyotirlinga* |
| **Mandu Monuments** | Malwa Sultans (15th–16th Century CE) | **Jahaz Mahal** ("Ship Palace"), Hindola Mahal, and **Hoshang Shah's Tomb** (**India's first marble mausoleum**, inspiring the Taj Mahal) |
| **Bhojeshwar Temple (Bhojpur)** | Paramara King Raja Bhoj (11th Century CE) | Houses one of the largest monolithic Shiva Lingams in India inside an unfinished royal temple |
| **Udayagiri Caves & Heliodorus Pillar** | Gupta Dynasty (5th c. CE) & Heliodorus (~110 BCE) | Vidisha complex: Udayagiri depicts the colossal **Varaha Avatar**, while **Khamba Baba** (Heliodorus Pillar) is early Bhagavata Vaishnavite evidence |
| **Bagh Caves** | Gupta / Valkha Period (5th–6th Century CE) | Nine Buddhist rock-cut caves in Dhar district famed for tempera murals akin to Ajanta |
| **Taj-ul-Masajid (Bhopal)** | Begums of Bhopal (started 1887 by Shah Jahan Begum) | One of the largest mosques in Asia |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| **Khajuraho Group of Monuments** | Cultural | **1986** |
| **Buddhist Monuments at Sanchi** | Cultural | **1989** |
| **Rock Shelters of Bhimbetka** | Cultural | **2003** |
| *(Creative City Note)* **Gwalior** | UNESCO Creative City of Music | **2023** (Recognized under UCCN for Tansen & Gwalior Gharana heritage) |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **GI Tags (20+):** **Chanderi Saree**, **Maheshwari Saree**, **Bagh Print**, **Gond Painting**, **Sharbati Wheat**, **Jhabua Kadaknath Black Chicken**, **Ratlami Sev**, **Rewa Sundarja Mango**, **Morena Gajak**, **Balaghat Chinnor Rice**, **Leather Toys of Indore**, **Bell Metal Ware of Datia & Tikamgarh**, **Ujjain Batik Print**, **Jabalpur Stone Craft**, **Khajuraho Stone Craft**, **Gwalior Handmade Carpet & Paper Mache**, **Betul Bharewa Metal Craft**, **Waraseoni Handloom Saree**, plus shared **Nagpur Orange** (with Maharashtra) and **Bundelkhand Kathiya Gehu** (with UP).
- **National Parks (11 — HIGHEST IN INDIA):** **Kanha**, **Bandhavgarh**, **Pench (Priyadarshini)**, **Panna**, **Satpura**, **Sanjay**, **Madhav**, **Van Vihar** (Bhopal), **Mandla Plant Fossils NP**, **Dinosaur Fossils NP** (Dhar), and **Kuno NP** (Sheopur — **site of Project Cheetah**, reintroducing African cheetahs from Namibia & South Africa).
- **"Tiger State of India" (9 Tiger Reserves — HIGHEST IN INDIA):** **Kanha**, **Bandhavgarh**, **Pench**, **Panna**, **Satpura**, **Sanjay–Dubri**, **Veerangana Durgavati TR** (2023), **Ratapani TR** (Dec 2024), and **Madhav TR** (early 2025 — **India's 58th Tiger Reserve**).
- **Ramsar Sites (5):** **Bhoj Wetland** (Bhopal, 2002), **Sakhya Sagar** (Shivpuri, 2022), **Sirpur Lake** (Indore, 2022), **Yashwant Sagar** (Indore, 2022), and **Tawa Reservoir** (Narmadapuram, **2024**).
- **UNESCO MAB Biosphere Reserves (3):** **Pachmarhi**, **Achanakmar–Amarkantak** (shared with Chhattisgarh), and **Panna** (WNBR 2020).
- **Records & Superlatives:** Highest forest area in India (in sq km per ISFR); highest Scheduled Tribe population in India (~1.53 crore); **Sanchi** = India's 1st Solar City (2023); **Indore** = only city with both IIT & IIM; **State Sport: Mallakhamb**.
- **State Symbols:** Animal: **Barasingha (Hard-ground Swamp Deer)**; Bird: **Indian Paradise Flycatcher (*Dudhraj*)**; Flower: **Madonna Lily**; Tree: **Banyan Tree**; Fish: **Mahaseer**.

### 14. Maharashtra

**Capital:** Mumbai (Primary/Summer); Nagpur (Winter Capital)  
**Formation:** **May 1, 1960** (Bombay State bifurcated into Maharashtra and Gujarat under the Bombay Reorganisation Act, 1960, following the Samyukta Maharashtra Movement — celebrated as *Maharashtra Din*)  
**Official Language(s):** **Marathi** (Granted **Classical Language of India** status on **October 3, 2024**!)  
**Other Languages:** Konkani (8th Schedule), Hindi, Gujarati, Urdu, Kannada, Telugu, Ahirani (Khandeshi), Varhadi, Malvani, Gondi, Warli, English

#### Brief History
- **Ancient Era:** Known as *Aparanta* (Konkan) and *Maharashtraka*. **Sopara** (Nala Sopara) was an ancient Mauryan port with Ashokan edicts. **Satavahanas** (Pratishthana/Paithan) ruled under **Gautamiputra Satakarni** and **King Hala** (*Gaha Sattasai*).
- **Rock-Cut Architecture Dynasties:** **Vakatakas** patronized the glorious murals of **Ajanta Caves**; **Kalachuris** sculpted **Elephanta Caves**; **Rashtrakutas** (Krishna I) carved the monolithic **Kailasa Temple at Ellora**; **Yadavas of Devagiri** nurtured early Marathi literature (*Jnaneshwar*).
- **Maratha Empire:** Founded by **Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj** (born 1630 at **Shivneri**, crowned *Chhatrapati* in **1674 at Raigad Fort**, administered via *Ashta Pradhan* council and built a pioneering coastal navy). Later expanded across India under the **Peshwas of Pune** (Bajirao I, Balaji Bajirao, Madhavrao I, Nana Phadnavis) before falling to the British after **Three Anglo-Maratha Wars (1775–1818)**.
- **Social Reform & Dalit Liberation:** Epicenter of modern social reform led by **Mahatma Jyotirao Phule & Savitribai Phule** (1848 girls' school in Pune, *Satyashodhak Samaj* 1873), **Lokhitwadi**, **M.G. Ranade**, **G.G. Agarkar**, **Maharshi D.K. Karve** (SNDT Women's University 1916), **Rajarshi Shahu Maharaj** of Kolhapur, and **Dr. B.R. Ambedkar** (Mahad Satyagraha 1927, Constitutional Architect, historic Buddhist conversion at **Deekshabhoomi**, Nagpur in **1956**).
- **Freedom Struggle:** **Indian National Congress (INC)** held its **1st Session in Bombay (Dec 28, 1885)** at Gokuldas Tejpal Sanskrit College (presided by W.C. Bonnerjee). **Vasudev Balwant Phadke** pioneered armed resistance; **Bal Gangadhar Tilak** ("Lokmanya", *Kesari* & *Maratha*) popularized **Ganeshotsav (1893)** and **Shivaji Jayanti**; **Gopal Krishna Gokhale** founded *Servants of India Society (1905)*; **V.D. Savarkar** formed *Abhinav Bharat (1904)*. Mumbai witnessed the **Quit India Resolution (Aug 8, 1942 at Gowalia Tank / August Kranti Maidan)** and the **Royal Indian Navy Mutiny (1946)**, while **Nana Patil** ran the **Satara Prati Sarkar** (parallel government, 1943–46).

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| **Lavani** | Folk | Signature high-energy Marathi dance performed in 9-yard *nauvari* sarees to fast *dholki* beats; combines sensual grace, expressive *abhinaya*, and social satire |
| **Tamasha** | Folk Theatre-Dance | Maharashtra's premier folk drama blending *Lavani*, *Powada*, comic skits (*Vag*), and devotional *Gan-Gavalan* |
| **Powada** | Folk | Heroic ballad-dance reciting the valour of Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj and Maratha warriors |
| **Koli Dance** | Folk | Celebratory rhythmic dance of Mumbai and Konkan fisherfolk (*Kolis*) imitating rowing boats and casting nets |
| **Lezim** | Folk | Energetic group folk dance named after the wooden jingle-staff (*lezim*) swung in martial formations |
| **Dhangari Gaja** | Folk | Vigorous dance of Konkan/Solapur shepherd (*Dhangar*) communities accompanied by *dhol* drums |
| **Dindi & Kala** | Folk / Devotional | Processional dances performed by **Warkari** devotees on Ekadashi chanting *Vithoba* *abhangs* |
| **Gondhal & Bharud** | Folk | Ritual storytelling dance praising Goddess Tulja Bhavani (*Gondhal*) and witty spiritual folk-drama popularized by **Sant Eknath** (*Bharud*) |
| **Dashavatar** | Folk Theatre | All-night Konkan/Sindhudurg traditional mythological drama depicting Vishnu's 10 avatars |
| **Tarpa & Bohada** | Tribal Folk | Circle dance of the Warli tribe around the *tarpa* windpipe (*Tarpa*), and colourful wooden-mask procession dance of Palghar/Nashik tribes (*Bohada*) |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Music & Musical Theatre:** **Natya Sangeet** (classic Marathi musical drama blending Hindustani classical ragas with theatre — pioneered by Annasaheb Kirloskar and **Bal Gandharva**), **Abhang & Ovi** (devotional poetry and women's grindstone folk songs), **Bhajan**, and **Hindustani Classical Music** (modern notation and music schools pioneered by **Pt. Vishnu Narayan Bhatkhande** and **Pt. Vishnu Digambar Paluskar**; legends like **Lata Mangeshkar**, **Kishori Amonkar**, and Kirana/Jaipur-Atrauli traditions).
- **Painting & Folk Visual Arts:** **Warli Painting** (GI-tagged minimalist white rice-paste geometric tribal murals on red mud walls, elevated globally by **Jivya Soma Mashe**), **Pinguli Chitrakathi & Leather Shadow Puppetry** (storytelling picture scrolls of the Thakar tribe in Sindhudurg), and **Ajanta Fresco/Tempera Heritage**.
- **Handicrafts & Textiles:** **Paithani Saree** (GI-tagged "Queen of Sarees" woven in Yeola & Chhatrapati Sambhajinagar with pure silk, gold zari, and peacock motifs), **Himroo Shawl** (gold-brocade silk-cotton weave of Sambhajinagar), **Kolhapuri Chappal** (GI — shared with Karnataka), **Solapur Chaddar & Terry Towel** (GI), **Puneri Pagadi** (GI-tagged traditional crimson turban), **Sawantwadi Wooden Toys & Ganjifa Cards** (Sindhudurg lacquer craft), **Narayan Peth & Karvat Kathi Sarees**, and **Bidri-style metalcraft**.

| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| King Hala | *Gaha Sattasai* (700 verses anthology, Satavahana era) | Maharashtri Prakrit |
| **Sant Jnaneshwar (Dnyaneshwar)** | *Jnaneshwari (Bhavartha Dipika)*, *Amritanubhav* (13th c. founder of Warkari literature) | Marathi |

| Bal Gangadhar Tilak | *Gita Rahasya* (written in Mandalay Jail), *The Arctic Home in the Vedas* | Marathi / English |

| **Vishnu Sakharam Khandekar** | *Yayati* (**1st Marathi Jnanpith Award, 1974**) | Marathi |
| **V.V. Shirwadkar (Kusumagraj)** | *Natsamrat*, *Vishakha* (**Jnanpith Award, 1987**; birthday Feb 27 = *Marathi Bhasha Din*) | Marathi |

| P.L. Deshpande & Vijay Tendulkar | *Batatyachi Chawl* (P.L.); *Ghashiram Kotwal*, *Sakharam Binder* (Tendulkar) | Marathi |
| Dr. B.R. Ambedkar | *Annihilation of Caste*, *The Buddha and His Dhamma* | English |
| Daya Pawar | *Baluta* (landmark Dalit autobiographical literature) | Marathi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| **Ganesh Chaturthi (Ganeshotsav)** | August–September (Bhadrapada, 10 days) | Revived as a grand public nationalist festival by **Lokmanya Tilak in 1893**; culminates in *Anant Chaturdashi* immersion |
| **Gudi Padwa** | March–April (Chaitra Shukla Pratipada) | Marathi New Year marked by hoisting the silk-and-neem *Gudi* banner outside homes |
| **Ashadhi & Kartiki Ekadashi (Pandharpur Wari)** | June–July & October–November | Massive centuries-old **Warkari** foot-pilgrimage carrying saint *palkhis* (Jnaneshwar & Tukaram) to **Vitthal Temple, Pandharpur** |
| **Kala Ghoda Arts Festival** | February | Premier annual 9-day arts, literature, cinema, and heritage street festival in South Mumbai |
| **Ellora–Ajanta & Elephanta Festivals** | Feb–Mar / Winter | Classical music and dance festivals set beside the UNESCO cave complexes |
| **Pola** | August (Shravan Amavasya) | Agrarian festival in Vidarbha and Marathwada honoring decorated bullocks (*Bail Pola*) |
| **Narali Purnima** | August (Shravan Purnima) | Coconut offering to the sea by Konkan/Mumbai Koli fisherfolk marking the safe reopening of fishing after monsoon |
| **Makar Sankranti** | January 14–15 | Exchanging *Tilgul* (sesame-jaggery sweets) with *"Tilgul ghya, god god bola"* |
| **Marbat Festival** | August–September (Nagpur) | Unique 140-year-old folk festival parading effigies (*Kali* & *Pili Marbat*) mocking social evils and diseases |
| **Nashik–Trimbakeshwar Kumbh Mela** | Every 12 years (next **2027**) | Sacred *Simhastha* Kumbh bath on the **Godavari River** |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| **Bhil** | Nandurbar, Dhule, Jalgaon, Nashik | **Largest Scheduled Tribe in Maharashtra**; traditional bowmen, *Dhol* dances, *Holi* fairs |
| **Gond & Maria Gond** *(Maria = PVTG)* | Gadchiroli, Chandrapur, Yavatmal, Nagpur | **Ghotul** youth dormitory, *Relo* and *Demsa* dances, *Persa Pen* worship |
| **Warli** | Palghar, Thane, Nashik | Creators of globally renowned **Warli Painting**, **Tarpa** dance, **Bohada** mask festival, and *Hirva/Waghoba* nature worship |
| **Katkari** *(PVTG)* | Raigad, Thane, Palghar, Pune | Traditionally extracted *katha* (catechu) from Khair trees; distinct seasonal folk dances |
| **Kolam** *(PVTG)* | Yavatmal, Chandrapur, Nanded | Speak Dravidian *Kolami*; unique *Chawadi* village hall and *Satti* bamboo rituals |
| **Mahadev Koli & Malhar Koli** | Pune, Ahilyanagar, Nashik, Raigad | Sahyadri hill and coastal Koli communities central to Maratha fort-guarding and maritime heritage |
| **Kokna (Kokni) & Pawra** | Nashik, Dhule, Nandurbar | Known for *Bohada* mask-making (Kokna) and Satpura *Indal/Holi* dances (Pawra) |
| **Thakur / Thakar & Korku** | Sindhudurg / Raigad & Melghat (Amravati) | *Pinguli Chitrakathi* visual storytelling (Thakar); Satpura forest culture (Korku) |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| **Mumbai** | Capital; **"Financial Capital of India"** & **"City of Seven Islands"**; HQ of **RBI, SEBI, BSE, NSE**; Bollywood; Gateway of India; CSMT; **Elephanta Caves**; **India's 1st passenger railway (Bombay–Thane, April 16, 1853)** |
| **Pune** | **"Oxford of the East"** & **"Cultural Capital of Maharashtra"**; IT & Automobile hub; Shaniwar Wada; Aga Khan Palace; Bhandarkar Oriental Research Institute; NDA (Khadakwasla); Film & TV Institute (FTII) |
| **Nagpur** | Winter Capital; **"Orange City"**; **"Zero Mile Stone"** (geographic center of India); **Deekshabhoomi**; NEERI; MIHAN; Marbat Festival |
| **Nashik** | **"Wine Capital of India"** (Sula/vineyards); **Kumbh Mela** on Godavari; **Trimbakeshwar Jyotirlinga**; India Security Press & Currency Note Press; Panchavati |
| **Chhatrapati Sambhajinagar** *(formerly Aurangabad)* | **"Tourism Capital of Maharashtra"**; gateway to **Ajanta & Ellora Caves**; Bibi Ka Maqbara; **Paithani Saree** & **Himroo Shawl**; Daulatabad Fort |
| **Kolhapur** | **Kolhapuri Chappal**; **Mahalaxmi (Ambabai) Temple**; **Panhala Fort**; traditional *Kushti* (wrestling) hub; *Kolhapur Jaggery* |
| **Solapur** | **Solapur Chaddar** & **Solapur Terry Towel** (GI); Siddheshwar Temple; Pandharpur Vitthal pilgrimage nearby |

| **Jalgaon** | **"Banana City of India"**; Ajanta gateway; Bhusawal railway junction |
| **Wardha** | **Sevagram Ashram** (Mahatma Gandhi's headquarters 1936–48); **Paunar Ashram** (Acharya Vinoba Bhave's *Bhoodan* heritage) |
| **Nanded** | **Takht Sachkhand Sri Hazur Sahib** (one of the **Five Takhts of Sikhism**, site of Guru Gobind Singh Ji's final abode in 1708) |
| **Shirdi** (Ahilyanagar) | Revered **Sai Baba Samadhi Mandir** pilgrimage center |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| **Ajanta Caves** (Sambhajinagar) | Satavahanas & Vakatakas (2nd c. BCE – 6th c. CE) | 30 rock-cut **Buddhist** caves on Waghora River gorge featuring world-famous Jataka frescoes (*Padmapani, Vajrapani*) |
| **Ellora Caves** (Sambhajinagar) | Kalachuris, Chalukyas, **Rashtrakutas** & Yadavas (6th–10th c. CE) | 34 caves uniting **Hindu, Buddhist, and Jain** monuments; Cave 16 **Kailasa Temple** carved top-down from a single basalt monolith by **Krishna I** |
| **Elephanta (Gharapuri) Caves** | Kalachuris / Konkan Mauryas (5th–7th Century CE) | Island cave complex off Mumbai harbor famed for the monumental 20-ft **Trimurti Sadashiva** sculpture |
| **Karla, Bhaja, Bedsa & Kanheri Caves** | Satavahana Era (2nd c. BCE – 3rd c. CE) | Early Hinayana Buddhist *Chaitya* halls with timber-ribbed vaults (Karla near Lonavala; Kanheri inside Sanjay Gandhi NP, Mumbai) |
| **Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj Terminus (CSMT)** | Frederick William Stevens (1887 CE) | High Victorian Gothic–Indo-Saracenic railway station; Central Railway HQ |
| **Gateway of India (Mumbai)** | George Wittet (1924 CE) | Built to commemorate King George V & Queen Mary's 1911 visit; last British troops exited India from here in **Feb 1948** |
| **Maratha Forts (Raigad, Shivneri, Rajgad, Pratapgad, Sinhagad, Sindhudurg, Vijaydurg, Panhala, Lohagad, Salher, Khanderi, Suvarnadurg)** | Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj & Bahmani/Maratha Eras (17th–18th c.) | Strategic hill and island sea forts (**11 included in 2025 UNESCO inscription**); **Raigad** (coronation capital), **Shivneri** (birthplace), **Sindhudurg** (naval bastion) |
| **Bibi Ka Maqbara** (Sambhajinagar) | Azam Shah / Aurangzeb (1660 CE) | Tomb of Rabia-ul-Daurani; famous as the **"Taj of the Deccan" / "Mini Taj"** |
| **Daulatabad (Devagiri) Fort** | Yadava King Bhillama V (1187 CE) & Tughlaqs | Nearly impregnable hill fort; briefly made capital of Delhi Sultanate by Muhammad bin Tughlaq (1327) |
| **Five Jyotirlingas in Maharashtra** | Ancient / Rebuilt by Ahilyabai Holkar & Marathas | **Trimbakeshwar** (Nashik), **Bhimashankar** (Pune), **Grishneshwar** (Ellora), **Aundha Nagnath** (Hingoli), and **Parli Vaijnath** (Beed) — *highest count in any Indian state* |
| **Aga Khan Palace & Shaniwar Wada** (Pune) | Sultan Muhammad Shah Aga Khan III (1892) / Peshwa Bajirao I (1732) | Aga Khan Palace served as prison for Gandhi, Sarojini Naidu & Kasturba Gandhi (1942–44; houses Kasturba's & Mahadev Desai's samadhis) |
| **Deekshabhoomi (Nagpur)** | Designed by Sheo Dan Mal | Iconic hollow Buddhist stupa marking **Dr. B.R. Ambedkar's** conversion to Buddhism on **Oct 14, 1956** |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| **Ajanta Caves** | Cultural | **1983** *(among India's very first 4 UNESCO sites)* |
| **Ellora Caves** | Cultural | **1983** *(among India's very first 4 UNESCO sites)* |
| **Elephanta Caves** | Cultural | **1987** |

| **Victorian Gothic and Art Deco Ensembles of Mumbai** | Cultural | **2018** |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Highest UNESCO Count in India (7 Sites):** Maharashtra tops India with **7 UNESCO World Heritage Sites**, boosted by the **July 2025** inscription of **Maratha Military Landscapes of India** (Salher, Shivneri, Lohagad, Khanderi, Raigad, Rajgad, Pratapgad, Suvarnadurg, Panhala, Vijaydurg, and Sindhudurg in Maharashtra, plus Gingee in TN).
- **Classical Language Status:** **Marathi** became one of India's **11 Classical Languages** on **October 3, 2024**.
- **GI Tags (30+):** **Paithani Saree**, **Alphonso (Hapus) Mango**, **Warli Painting** (shared), **Kolhapuri Chappal** (shared with Karnataka), **Solapur Chaddar**, **Solapur Terry Towel**, **Puneri Pagadi**, **Nashik Grapes**, **Mahabaleshwar Strawberry**, **Nagpur Orange** (shared with MP), **Lasalgaon Onion**, **Sangli Raisins & Turmeric**, **Ambemohar Rice**, **Ajara Ghansal Rice**, **Jalgaon Banana**, **Dahanu Gholvad Chikoo**, **Jalna Mosambi**, **Mangalwedha & Pandharpur Bhimthadi Jowar**, **Kolhapur Jaggery**, and **Konkan Kokum**.
- **National Parks (6):** **Tadoba NP**, **Sanjay Gandhi (Borivali) NP** (Mumbai — unique urban NP housing Kanheri Caves), **Gugamal NP**, **Navegaon NP**, **Chandoli NP**, and **Pench (Jawaharlal Nehru) NP**.
- **Tiger Reserves (6):** **Tadoba–Andhari TR**, **Melghat TR**, **Pench TR**, **Sahyadri TR**, **Navegaon–Nagzira TR**, and **Bor TR** (**one of India's smallest Tiger Reserves**).
- **Ramsar Sites (3):** **Nandur Madhameshwar** (Nashik, 2019 — "Bharatpur of Maharashtra"), **Lonar Lake** (Buldhana, 2020 — famous **basaltic meteorite impact crater soda lake**), and **Thane Creek Flamingo Sanctuary** (Mumbai Metropolitan Region, 2022).
- **Firsts & Infrastructure Milestones:** **India's 1st passenger train** (April 16, 1853, Bori Bunder to Thane, 34 km); **India's 1st electric train** (1925, Bombay VT to Kurla); **India's 1st nuclear reactor *Apsara*** (1956, BARC Trombay); **India's 1st nuclear power station** (**Tarapur**, 1969); **Bombay High** (India's largest offshore oilfield); **Central & Western Railway HQs** both in Mumbai.
- **State Symbols:** Animal: **Indian Giant Squirrel (*Shekhru*)**; Bird: **Yellow-footed Green Pigeon (*Hariyal*)**; Flower: **Jarul (Pride of India)**; Tree: **Mango Tree**; Butterfly: **Blue Mormon**; Fish: **Silver Pomfret**.

### 15. Manipur

**Capital:** Imphal  
**Formation:** Union Territory on November 1, 1956; attained full statehood on January 21, 1972 (North-Eastern Areas (Reorganisation) Act, 1971)  
**Official Language(s):** Meiteilon / Manipuri (included in the Eighth Schedule via the 71st Constitutional Amendment Act, 1992), English (official/associate administrative use)  
**Other Languages:** Tangkhul, Thadou (Kuki), Rongmei (Kabui), Mao, Poumai, Paite, Hmar, Vaiphei, Zou, Anal, Maring, Hindi, Nepali

#### Brief History
- Ancient kingdom known as **Kangleipak**, ruled continuously by the **Ningthouja dynasty** beginning with **Nongda Lairen Pakhangba** in **33 CE** (recorded in the royal chronicle *Cheitharol Kumbaba*).
- **King Pamheiba (Garib Niwaz, 1709–1748):** Adopted Gaudiya Vaishnavism as the state religion, patronized Manipuri culture, and campaigned deep into Burma (Myanmar) up to Sagaing.
- **Chahi Taret Khuntakpa (Seven Years' Devastation, 1819–1826):** Burmese occupation ended when **Maharaja Gambhir Singh** liberated Manipur with British support; Burmese claims were renounced under the **Treaty of Yandabo (1826)**.
- **Anglo-Manipur War (1891):** Fought at the **Battle of Khongjom** (April 23, 1891 — observed as *Khongjom Day*); **Bir Tikendrajit Singh** and **Thangal General** were executed by the British on August 13, 1891 (*Patriots' Day*).
- **Nupi Lan (Women's Wars, 1904 & 1939):** Iconic agitations led by Manipuri women against British forced labour (*Lalup*) and artificial rice scarcity caused by unrestricted exports.
- **World War II & Freedom Struggle:** Site of the **Battle of Imphal (1944)**; on **April 14, 1944**, Colonel Shaukat Malik of the **Indian National Army (INA)** hoisted the Azad Hind flag for the first time on Indian mainland soil at **Moirang**. **Haipou Jadonang** and **Rani Gaidinliu** led the anti-colonial Heraka movement across the Manipur–Naga hills.
- **Integration:** Maharaja Bodhachandra Singh signed the Manipur Merger Agreement on September 21, 1949 (effective October 15, 1949).

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Manipuri (Jagoi / Rasleela) | Classical | One of India's 8 classical dances; centers on Vaishnavite *Rasleela* (Radha–Krishna) themes with graceful, fluid movements, restrained footwork, and traditional cylindrical *Potloi* (Kumil) skirts accompanied by the *Pung* drum. |
| Pung Cholom | Classical-allied / Folk | Acrobatic male dance where performers play the *Pung* (Manipuri mridanga) while executing high leaps, spins, and synchronized rhythms; integral to *Nata Sankirtana*. |
| Kartal Cholom | Classical-allied / Folk | Male cymbal (*kartal*) dance marked by brisk rhythmic clashing and graceful body movements, traditionally performed during *Yaoshang* and religious ceremonies. |
| Maibi Jagoi | Folk / Ritual | Sacred creation and fertility dance performed by traditional priestesses (*Maibis*) during the *Lai Haraoba* festival to invoke ancestral *Umang Lai* deities. |
| Khamba Thoibi | Folk | Romantic duet dance originating in Moirang depicting the legendary love story of the orphan hero Khamba and Princess Thoibi. |
| Thang-Ta & Sarit Sarak | Folk / Martial Art | Traditional *Huyen Langlon* martial art performed as a rhythmic sword (*Thang*) and spear (*Ta*) dance, alongside unarmed combat choreography (*Sarit Sarak*). |
| Dhol Cholom | Folk | Vigorous large-drum (*dhol*) dance performed predominantly during the spring *Yaoshang* (Holi) celebrations. |
| Thabal Chongba | Folk | "Dancing in the Moonlight" — traditional circle dance performed by youth holding hands under the full moon during *Yaoshang*. |
| Luivat Pheizak | Folk | Popular **Tangkhul Naga** dance depicting traditional stages of cultivation and communal joy. |
| Shim Lam (Kabui / Rongmei Dance) | Folk | Traditional fly-dance of the Rongmei/Kabui Naga community performed during *Gaan-Ngai* and festivals. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Nata Sankirtana:** Sacred Vaishnavite ritual singing, drumming (*Pung*), and cymbal dancing marking life-cycle ceremonies; inscribed on the **UNESCO Representative List of the Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity (2013)**.
- **Traditional Music & Ballads:** **Pena** (ancient single-stringed bowed lute with a coconut-shell resonator and bamboo neck used by bards during *Lai Haraoba*); **Khongjom Parba** (heroic narrative ballad singing accompanied by a *Dholak* recounting the 1891 Battle of Khongjom); **Lai Haraoba** ritual hymns.
- **Traditional Theatre:** **Shumang Leela** (indigenous Manipuri courtyard theatre performed in the open on a square stage surrounded on all sides by spectators, featuring all-male *Nupa Shumang Leela* and all-female *Nupi Shumang Leela* troupes).
- **Handlooms & Textiles (GI Tagged):** **Shaphee Lanphee** (embroidered wool/cotton shawl traditionally gifted to brave warriors), **Wangkhei Phee** (ultra-fine transparent white muslin cloth), and **Moirang Phee** (traditional fabric featuring pointed temple-shaped *Moirangfee* borders).
- **Pottery & Handicrafts:** **Longpi Black Pottery** (*Nungbi* pottery of Ukhrul made without a potter's wheel using weathered serpentine rock and clay), **Kouna Craft** (mats, baskets, and cushions woven from aquatic water reeds), bamboo and cane craft, and traditional cloth dolls (*Laiphadibi*).
- **Painting:** Traditional manuscript illustrations in ancient *Puya* texts (such as *Subika* astrological manuscripts) and contemporary narrative paintings of *Khamba-Thoibi* and *Lai Haraoba*.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Royal Chroniclers | *Cheitharol Kumbaba* (Royal Chronicle of Manipur) | Meitei (Meetei Mayek) |
| Ancient Meitei Scholars | *Puya* texts (*Numit Kappa*, *Wakoklon Heelel Thilen*) | Old Manipuri |
| Hijam Anganghal Singh (*Mahakavi*) | *Khamba Thoibi Sheireng* (~39,000-line epic), *Jahera*, *Ibemma* | Manipuri |
| Lamabam Kamal Singh | *Madhavi* (first modern Manipuri novel, 1930), *Lei Pareng* | Manipuri |
| Khwairakpam Chaoba Singh | *Labangalata* (novel), *Thainagi Leirang* (poetry) | Manipuri |
| Ashangbam Minaketan Singh | *Asheibagi Nitaipod* (Sahitya Akademi Award, 1977) | Manipuri |
| Pacha Meetei | *Imphal Amasung Magi Esing Nungsitki Phibham* (Sahitya Akademi Award, 1973) | Manipuri |
| M.K. Binodini Devi | *Boro Saheb Ongbi Sanatombi* (Sahitya Akademi Award, 1979) | Manipuri |
| Ratan Thiyam | *Chakravyuha*, *Urubhangam* (avant-garde Manipuri theatre & dramaturgy) | Manipuri |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Lai Haraoba | April–May | Meaning "Merrymaking of the Gods"; ancient Meitei festival worshipping *Umang Lai* (forest/ancestral deities) through *Maibi* dances and *Pena* music. |
| Yaoshang | Feb–March (Phalgun) | Five-day spring festival coinciding with Holi, featuring *Thabal Chongba* moonlight dances, *Nakatheng* donations, and community sports. |
| Ningol Chakouba | Oct–Nov (Hiyangei) | Major family festival strengthening bonds as married daughters (*Ningols*) are invited for a grand feast at their parental homes. |
| Cheiraoba (Sajibu Nongma Panba) | March–April | Meitei Lunar New Year celebrated with traditional feasts, offering food at gates, and climbing local hilltops for prosperity. |
| Sangai Festival | November (21–30) | Annual flagship tourism and cultural festival named after the endemic state animal, the *Sangai* deer. |
| Lui-Ngai-Ni | February 15 | Collective seed-sowing festival celebrated by all Naga tribes of Manipur to invoke blessings for a bountiful harvest. |
| Kut (Chavang Kut) | November 1 | Grand post-harvest autumn thanksgiving festival of the Kuki-Chin-Mizo communities. |
| Kang (Rath Yatra) | June–July | Ten-day chariot procession of Lord Jagannath, Balabhadra, and Subhadra centered at Shree Govindajee Temple. |
| Heikru Hidongba | September | Traditional ceremonial boat race held in the moat of Sagolband Bijoy Govinda in Imphal. |
| Shirui Lily Festival | May | State-level cultural festival in Ukhrul celebrating the rare blooming season of the endemic *Shirui Lily*. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
Manipur recognizes **33/34 Scheduled Tribes** belonging predominantly to the **Naga** and **Kuki-Chin-Mizo** groups inhabiting the surrounding hills (note that the valley-dwelling **Meitei** majority and **Meitei Pangal** / Manipuri Muslims are non-ST communities).

| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Tangkhul Naga | Ukhrul & Kamjong | Largest Naga group in Manipur; *Longpi* black pottery, *Luira Phanit* seed-sowing festival, *Luivat* dance, and Sirarakhong chilli cultivation. |
| Thadou (Kuki) | Kangpokpi, Churachandpur, Tengnoupal | Largest ST community; *Chavang Kut* harvest festival, *Sawm* youth dormitory tradition, and *Sagol* / martial folk dances. |
| Rongmei (Kabui) & Zeme / Liangmai (Zeliangrong) | Tamenglong & Noney | *Gaan-Ngai* post-harvest festival (New Year & honoring departed souls), *Shim Lam* dance, and bamboo-cane artistry. |
| Mao & Poumai Naga | Senapati | Terrace rice farming, *Chithuni* festival, and traditional megalithic stone-pulling customs. |
| Maram Naga | Senapati | *Kanghi* & *Punghi* festivals, strict village taboos, and *Willong Khullen* megalithic heritage. |
| Paite, Hmar, Vaiphei, Zou, Simte & Gangte | Churachandpur, Pherzawl & Tengnoupal | *Chavang Kut* and *Sikpui Ruoi* (Hmar) festivals, vibrant handwoven *Puan* shawls, and *Buonzawl* youth dormitories. |
| Anal, Maring, Moyon, Monsang, Lamkang & Chothe | Chandel & Tengnoupal | *Ikam* and *Sangkhuai* festivals, cane-bamboo handicrafts, and traditional village council (*Khullakpa*) governance. |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Imphal | Capital city; houses Kangla Fort, Ima Keithel (world's largest all-women market), Shree Govindajee Temple, and India's first National Sports University. |
| Moirang | Bishnupur district; **INA Memorial Complex** (first Azad Hind flag hoisting on Indian soil on April 14, 1944); legendary home of *Khamba–Thoibi*; gateway to Loktak Lake. |
| Bishnupur | Famous for the 15th-century brick **Vishnu Temple** and GI-tagged *Moirang Phee* handloom weaving. |
| Ukhrul | Highest hill station of Manipur; home to **Shirui Hills** (*Shirui Lily*), *Longpi* black pottery, GI-tagged *Kachai Lemon*, and *Sirarakhong Hathei Chilli*. |
| Thoubal | Agricultural hub known for *Chak-Hao* (black rice), *Shaphee Lanphee* weaving, and the Khongjom War Memorial nearby. |
| Churachandpur (Lamka) | Second-largest town; cultural hub of Kuki-Chin-Mizo communities, Khuga Dam, and scenic hill valleys. |
| Moreh | Tengnoupal district; major international border trade town connecting India with Myanmar along Asian Highway 1 (Tamu–Kalewa road). |
| Tamenglong | Known as the "Orange Bowl of Manipur" (GI-tagged *Tamenglong Orange*), Barak Waterfalls, and Tharon Caves. |
| Senapati | Northern gateway along NH-2; Naga cultural hub hosting *Lui-Ngai-Ni* and access to Dzükou Valley's southern approaches. |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Kangla Fort, Imphal | Ningthouja Kings (Ancient–19th c. CE) | Historic royal seat of Manipur; features the iconic twin *Kangla Sha* dragon-lion statues, coronation site, and Pakhangba shrines. |
| Shree Govindajee Temple, Imphal | Maharaja Nara Singh (1846 CE) | Principal Vaishnavite shrine adjacent to Kangla Palace, built with twin gold-plated domes in traditional Bengali-Manipuri architecture. |
| INA War Memorial & Museum, Moirang | Post-Independence Memorial | Marks the spot where Colonel Shaukat Malik hoisted the INA flag on April 14, 1944; preserves WWII relics and Netaji Subhas Chandra Bose memorabilia. |
| Bishnupur Vishnu Temple | King Kyamba (1467 CE) | Oldest surviving brick temple in Manipur, constructed in Bengali *Chala* style reflecting early cultural ties with Bengal and Burma. |
| Khongjom War Memorial, Thoubal | State Memorial | Honors martyrs of the 1891 Anglo-Manipur War (Paona Brajabasi); features the world's tallest traditional sword statue atop Kheba Ching. |
| Ima Keithel (Mother's Market), Imphal | ~16th Century CE Tradition | Over 500-year-old historic market complex run exclusively by more than 3,000 married women (*Imas*) vendors. |
| Sanamahi Temple, Imphal | Maharaja Kulachandra (1891 CE) | Dedicated to *Lainingthou Sanamahi*, the indigenous supreme household deity of the Meitei faith. |
| Red Hill (Maibam Lokpa Ching) & Imphal Peace Museum | WWII Site / 2019 | Fierce 1944 WWII battleground housing the Japanese-funded Imphal Peace Museum commemorating reconciliation. |
| Willong Khullen Megaliths, Senapati | Prehistoric / Ancestral Naga | Ring of towering carved stone monoliths sometimes called "Manipur's Stonehenge." |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| None inscribed yet on the World Heritage List | — | — *(Note: **Keibul Lamjao Conservation Area** is on the **UNESCO Tentative List [2016]**; **Sankirtana** of Manipur was inscribed on **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage [2013]**)* |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **7 GI Tags:** *Shaphee Lanphee*, *Wangkhei Phee*, *Moirang Phee* (handlooms), *Chak-Hao* (scented Black Rice), *Kachai Lemon*, *Tamenglong Orange*, and *Sirarakhong Hathei Chilli*.
- **National Parks (2):** **Keibul Lamjao National Park** (Bishnupur; the **world's only floating national park** built over *phumdis* [floating biomass] on Loktak Lake, last natural habitat of the endangered *Sangai* deer) and **Shiroi (Sirohi) National Park** (Ukhrul; habitat of the endemic *Shirui Lily*).
- **Wildlife Sanctuaries:** Yangoupokpi-Lokchao, Jiri-Makru, Kailam, Bunning, and Zeilad Wildlife Sanctuaries.
- **Ramsar Site (1):** **Loktak Lake** (designated 1990; largest freshwater lake in Northeast India; placed on the **Montreux Record** in 1993).
- **State Symbols:** Animal — *Sangai* (Brow-antlered / Dancing Deer); Bird — *Mrs. Hume's Pheasant* (*Nongin*); Flower — *Shirui Lily* (*Lilium mackliniae*); Tree — *Uningthou* (*Phoebe hainesiana*).
- **Birthplace of Modern Polo:** Indigenous *Sagol Kangjei* originated here; **Mapal Kangjeibung** in Imphal is the **world's oldest surviving polo ground**.
- **Sports & Defence Milestones:** Birthplace of **MC Mary Kom**, **Saikhom Mirabai Chanu**, and **Dingko Singh**; hosts India's first **National Sports University** (Imphal).

### 16. Meghalaya

**Capital:** Shillong  
**Formation:** Autonomous state within Assam on April 2, 1970; full statehood on January 21, 1972 (North-Eastern Areas (Reorganisation) Act, 1971)  
**Official Language(s):** English, Khasi, and Garo (Khasi and Garo were elevated from associate official status to full official languages alongside English via the *Meghalaya Official Languages Ordinance, 2026*; none are in the Eighth Schedule yet)  
**Other Languages:** Pnar (Jaintia), War, Bhoi, Biate, Lyngngam, Hajong, Koch, Rabha, Bengali, Assamese, Nepali, Hindi

#### Brief History
- Historically organized into autonomous indigenous polities: **Khasi Syiemships** (*Hima*), **Jaintia Dolloiships**, and **Garo Nokmas** (village headmen), uniquely governed under a **matrilineal social system**.
- **Jaintia Kingdom (Sutnga Dynasty):** Controlled the Jaintia Hills and Jaintiapur plains until annexed by the British East India Company in **1835**.
- **Anglo-Khasi War (1829–1833):** **U Tirot Sing Syiem** of Nongkhlaw led fierce guerrilla resistance against British encroachment and road construction across the Khasi Hills.
- **Jaintia Rebellion (1860–1863):** **U Kiang Nangbah** led an armed uprising against British house and income taxes; captured and publicly hanged in Jowai in December 1862.
- **Garo Resistance (1872):** **Pa Togan Nengminja Sangma** resisted British forces at Matchokgre in the Garo Hills.
- **Assam Capital Legacy:** **Shillong** served as the capital of composite Assam from **1874 to 1972**, making it the administrative center of Northeast India until Meghalaya attained full statehood in 1972.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Ka Shad Suk Mynsiem | Folk | "Dance of the Joyful Heart" — Khasi spring thanksgiving dance where unmarried girls dressed in silk *Dhara* and gold crowns glide slowly in an inner circle while men dance protectively around them with swords and whisks. |
| Ka Shad Nongkrem | Folk | Five-day Khasi autumn thanksgiving and fertility dance held at Smit under the *Syiem of Khyrim* accompanied by *Tangmuri* pipes and drums. |
| Wangala Dance | Folk | Energetic Garo post-harvest dance honoring *Misi Saljong* (Sun God) performed to the beat of 100 *Dama* drums and *Adil* buffalo horns. |
| Behdienkhlam | Folk / Ritual | Jaintia (Pnar) monsoon ritual in Jowai where men wade into the muddy *Aitnar* pool immersing tall decorated bamboo *Rots* to ward off plague and evil. |
| Laho Dance | Folk | Lighthearted Jaintia dance where a girl links arms between two boys while a narrator (*Chaphrang*) recites witty couplets. |
| Do·dru-su·a | Folk | Garo folk dance imitating doves pecking grains, performed by women in bright *Dakmanda* wraps. |
| Shad Mastieh | Folk | Traditional Khasi male martial dance performed with swords and shields celebrating bravery. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Traditional Music:** Khasi **Duitara** (4-stringed plucked lute), **Tangmuri** (double-reed wind pipe called "Queen of Musical Instruments"), *Ksing* & *Nakra* drums; Garo **Dama** (long wooden drum), **Adil** (buffalo-horn trumpet), **Bangsi** (bamboo flute), and **Dotrong**. Shillong is popularly called the **"Rock Capital of India."**
- **Handlooms & Textiles (GI Tagged):** **Meghalaya Ryndia Textile** (organic Eri/Ahimsa peace silk naturally dyed with lac, turmeric, and indigo), **Meghalaya Garo Textile** (traditional *Dakmanda* handwoven wrap-around skirt with geometric diamond motifs), and **Meghalaya Khasi Handloom Products** (*Jainsem*, *Dhara*, and *Jainkup* shawls).
- **Pottery & Crafts:** **Meghalaya Lyrnai Pottery** (GI Tagged; black clay pottery of Lyrnai village in West Jaintia Hills crafted by hand without a potter's wheel using black clay and serpentine stone dust); **Meghalaya Chubitchi** (GI Tagged traditional fermented rice beverage); **Khoh** (conical bamboo carrying baskets), **Knup** (woven bamboo-leaf rain shields), and Garo woodcarvings.
- **Sacred Heritage & Architecture:** Traditional Khasi oval stone-and-wood thatch dwellings (*Iing Khasi*) and megalithic ancestral memorial stones (*Mawbynna*).

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|

| Soso Tham | *Ki Sngi Barim U Hynñiew Trep* (*The Old Days of the Khasis*; Poet Laureate of the Khasis) | Khasi |

| Kynpham Sing Nongkynrih | *The Yearning of Seeds*, *Funeral Nights*, *Around the Hearth* | Khasi / English |
