| Desmond L. Kharmawphlang | *Touchstone*, Khasi oral folklore documentation | English / Khasi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Wangala (100 Drums Festival) | Oct–Nov | Premier Garo post-harvest thanksgiving festival dedicated to *Misi Saljong* (Sun God), marked by 100 *Dama* drums. |
| Shad Suk Mynsiem | April | Three-day Khasi spring thanksgiving festival held at Weiking Ground, Shillong, celebrating renewal and gratitude. |
| Ka Pomblang Nongkrem | Oct–Nov | Five-day autumn festival at Smit praying for prosperity, peace, and bountiful crops through goat sacrifice and dances. |
| Behdienkhlam | July | Most important Jaintia (Pnar) festival in Jowai after sowing, driving out pestilence and evil spirits with *Rots* in *Aitnar* pool. |
| Chad Sukra | April–May | Jaintia pre-sowing agricultural festival invoking nature's blessings before planting seeds. |
| Ahaia | December | Garo winter festival celebrating harvest abundance with folk sports and dances. |
| Seng Kut Snem | November 23 | Annual Khasi cultural day marking the 1899 founding of *Seng Khasi* to preserve indigenous faith and heritage. |
| India International Cherry Blossom Festival | November | Annual autumn festival in Shillong when pink Himalayan cherry blossoms bloom across the city. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
Meghalaya's society is renowned for its **matrilineal system**, where clan lineage, surname, and ancestral property pass through women (*Ka Khadduh* — youngest daughter among Khasis; *Nokna* — heiress among Garos).

| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Khasi (including Khynriam, Bhoi, War, Lyngngam) | East & West Khasi Hills, Eastern West Khasi Hills, Ri-Bhoi, South West Khasi Hills | Matrilineal *Ka Khadduh* inheritance, *Seng Khasi* (*Niam Khasi*) indigenous faith, *Shad Suk Mynsiem* & *Nongkrem* festivals, *Jingkieng Jri* living root bridges, and sacred groves (*Law Kyntang*). |
| Garo (A·chik) | West, East, South, North & South West Garo Hills | *Nokpante* (bachelor dormitory), *Wangala* 100-drum festival, *Songsarek* indigenous faith, *Dakmanda* textiles, and *Nokma* chieftaincy. |
| Jaintia (Pnar / Synteng) | East & West Jaintia Hills | *Niamtre* indigenous faith, *Behdienkhlam* & *Chad Sukra* festivals, *Lyrnai* black pottery, and *Dolloiship* governance. |
| Hajong, Koch, Rabha, Biate & Boro | Garo Hills fringes & East Jaintia Hills | Minority plains/border Scheduled Tribes; *Pushna* & *Bastupuja* (Hajong), rice farming, and traditional bamboo weaving. |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Shillong | Capital city; nicknamed **"Scotland of the East"** and **"Rock Capital of India"**; Ward's Lake, Shillong Peak, Don Bosco Museum, headquarters of Assam Rifles and North Eastern Council (NEC). |
| Sohra (Cherrapunji) | Famous for extreme monsoon rainfall, **Nohkalikai Falls** (India's tallest plunge waterfall, 340 m), Mawsmai Cave, and Double-Decker Living Root Bridge at Nongriat. |
| Mawsynram | Holds the world record for the **highest average annual rainfall**; famous for *Mawjymbuin* stalagmite cave. |
| Tura | Largest town in Garo Hills; base for Nokrek Biosphere Reserve, Tura Peak, and Balpakram National Park. |
| Jowai | Headquarters of West Jaintia Hills; famous for *Behdienkhlam* festival, Krang Suri Falls, and nearby Nartiang Monoliths. |
| Mawlynnong | East Khasi Hills; acclaimed as **"Asia's Cleanest Village"**, known for bamboo skywalks and nearby Riwai Living Root Bridge. |
| Dawki (Shnongpdeng) | Border trade town on the Indo-Bangladesh boundary, famous for the crystal-clear **Umngot River**. |
| Nongpoh | Administrative hub of Ri-Bhoi district; gateway to Shillong and center for organic Eri (*Ryndia*) silk weaving. |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Jingkieng Jri (Living Root Bridges) | Khasi & Jaintia Communities (Centuries-old living craft) | Bio-engineered bridges guided from aerial roots of *Ficus elastica* (rubber fig) trees; notable **Umshiang Double-Decker Bridge** at Nongriat. |
| Nartiang Monoliths | Jaintia Kings (c. 16th–18th c. CE) | Largest cluster of menhirs and dolmens in Meghalaya (tallest *Moo Iong Syiem* stands ~8 m high), commemorating royal ceremonies. |
| Nartiang Durga Temple | King Dhan Manik of Jaintia (c. 1500 CE) | Historic 600-year-old Shakti Peetha blending Hindu and indigenous Jaintia ritual traditions. |
| Mawphlang Sacred Grove (*Law Kyntang*) | Ancient Khasi Clans | Century-old biodiversity forest strictly protected by indigenous religious taboos forbidding removal of any leaf or twig. |
| Krem Liat Prah & Siju Caves | Natural Limestone Formations | **Krem Liat Prah** (East Jaintia Hills) is **India's longest cave system** (>30 km); Siju (*Dobakkol*) in Garo Hills is famed for bats and limestone galleries. |
| Don Bosco Centre for Indigenous Cultures, Shillong | Salesians of Don Bosco (2010) | Seven-storey museum housing exhaustive ethnographic collections from all Northeastern states. |
| U Tirot Sing & U Kiang Nangbah Memorials | State Heritage Monuments (Mairang & Jowai) | Honor the 19th-century Khasi and Jaintia freedom heroes who resisted British colonial annexation. |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| None inscribed yet on the World Heritage List | — | — *(Note: **Jingkieng Jri: Living Root Bridge Cultural Landscapes of Meghalaya** is on India's **UNESCO Tentative List [2022]** and nominated for the 2026–27 cycle; **Nokrek Biosphere Reserve** is part of the **UNESCO MAB Network [2009]**)* |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **8 GI Tags:** *Khasi Mandarin*, *Memong Narang* (indigenous wild citrus), *Lakadong Turmeric* (West Jaintia Hills; world's highest curcumin content ~7–12%), *Meghalaya Garo Textile*, *Meghalaya Lyrnai Pottery*, *Meghalaya Chubitchi*, *Meghalaya Ryndia Textile*, and *Meghalaya Khasi Handloom Products*.
- **National Parks (2):** **Nokrek National Park** (West Garo Hills; UNESCO Biosphere Reserve; preserves wild *Citrus indica*) and **Balphakram National Park** (South Garo Hills; canyon ecosystem called "Land of Perpetual Winds").
- **Wildlife Sanctuaries:** Siju, Nongkhyllem, Narpuh, and **Baghmara Pitcher Plant Sanctuary** (preserves the endemic insectivorous pitcher plant *Nepenthes khasiana*).
- **Ramsar Sites:** 0 (none as of 2026).
- **State Symbols:** Animal — *Clouded Leopard*; Bird — *Hill Myna*; Flower — *Lady's Slipper Orchid*; Tree — *Gamhar* (*Gmelina arborea*).
- **Constitutional Provision:** Entire state (barring small Shillong cantonment/municipal pockets) falls under the **Sixth Schedule** with 3 Autonomous District Councils (Khasi Hills, Jaintia Hills, Garo Hills).
- **Record Rainfall & Natural Wonders:** **Mawsynram** receives the world's highest average annual rainfall; **Nohkalikai Falls** is India's tallest plunge waterfall; **Krem Liat Prah** is India's longest natural cave.

---

### 17. Mizoram

**Capital:** Aizawl  
**Formation:** Lushai Hills district of Assam until January 21, 1972 (Union Territory); became India's 23rd state on February 20, 1987 (53rd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1986 following the Mizo Peace Accord)  
**Official Language(s):** Mizo (Duhlian/Lushai, official since 1974), English, Hindi (none currently in the Eighth Schedule; Mizoram Assembly has passed a unanimous resolution for Mizo inclusion)  
**Other Languages:** Mara (Lakher), Lai (Pawi), Hmar, Paite, Chakma, Bru (Reang), Bawm, Pang

#### Brief History
- Mizo ancestors migrated from the Chin Hills (Myanmar) between the 16th and 18th centuries and settled under hereditary village chiefs (**Lal**), prominently led by the **Sailo** clan.
- **Colonial Annexation (1871–1890):** Following British Lushai Expeditions, the region became the **Lushai Hills District** of Assam; resistance leaders like **Pasaltha Khuangchera** and **Queen Ropuiliani** opposed British rule in the 1890s.
- **Mautam Famine (1959):** Cyclical bamboo (*Melocanna baccifera*) flowering triggered massive rat plagues and famine, leading to the formation of the **Mizo National Front (MNF)** under **Laldenga** (1961) and a two-decade insurgency (1966–1986).
- **Mizo Peace Accord (June 30, 1986):** Signed between the Government of India, Mizoram, and MNF, paving the way for statehood on **February 20, 1987** and special constitutional safeguards under **Article 371G**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Cheraw (Bamboo Dance) | Folk | Iconic dance of Mizoram; women step deftly between pairs of long bamboo staves rhythmically clapped by seated men; traditionally linked to comforting a departed mother's spirit. |
| Khuallam | Folk | "Dance of the Guests" — performed by men waving colorful *Puandum* shawls to welcome honored visitors during the *Khuangchawi* feast. |
| Chheih Lam | Folk | Joyful community dance performed in a circle to *Chheih Hla* songs, *Khuang* (drum), and *Mautawng* (bamboo tube) beats. |
| Chai Lam | Folk | Circle dance performed during *Chapchar Kut* with alternating men and women holding waists/shoulders while dancing to mithun-horn (*Seki*) and drum beats. |
| Sarlamkai (Solakia) | Folk | Traditional warrior victory dance of the Mara and Lai (Pawi) tribes performed by men carrying shields and swords. |
| Par Lam | Folk | Graceful dance by young girls wearing flowers in their hair, emphasizing synchronized wrist and hand movements. |
| Zangtalam & Tlanglam | Folk | Group community dances accompanied by singing; *Zangtalam* is a celebrated folk dance of the Paite tribe. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Traditional Instruments:** **Khuang** (hollowed wooden drum covered in animal hide — central to Mizo music), **Darbu** (set of three brass gongs), **Darkhuang** (large gong), **Seki** (mithun horn), **Tumphit** (bamboo panpipe), **Lemlawi** (bamboo jaw harp), **Bengbung** (wooden xylophone), and **Tingtang** (single-string bamboo fiddle).
- **Handlooms & Textiles (GI Tagged):** Famous Mizo *Puan* (traditional woven wrap) craft boasting five GI-tagged textiles — **Mizo Puanchei** (vibrant festive wrap worn during Cheraw), **Tawlhlohpuan** (cloth symbolizing courage and refusal to retreat), **Pawndum** (black-and-red striped cloth of deep social significance), **Ngotekherh** (checked ceremonial cloth), and **Hmaram** (indigo-patterned skirt).
- **Handicrafts:** Intricate **Bamboo & Cane Craft**, including *Dawrawn* (conical carrying baskets), *Thul* (lidded ceremonial storage baskets), *Khumbeu* (waterproof bamboo-leaf ceremonial hats), and *Tuium* (bamboo water containers).
- **Traditional Institution:** **Zawlbuk** (traditional bachelor dormitory in every Mizo village where oral music, folklore, discipline, and craft skills were transmitted).

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| L. Rokunga | *Ro Min Rêlsak Ang Che*, *Ka Pianna Zoram Nuam*, *Raltiang I Kai Ve Ang* ("Poet of the Century"; 120+ songs) | Mizo |
| Dr. James Dokhuma | *Tawng Un Hrilhfiahna* (dictionary), *Rinawmin*, *Thla Hleinga Zan* (*Rambuai* era fiction; Padma Shri & Bhasha Samman) | Mizo |
| Lalsangzuali Sailo | *Tlawm Ve Lo Lalnu Ropuiliani* (Padma Shri; prolific poet, singer & historian) | Mizo |
| Prof. Laltluangliana Khiangte | *Pasaltha Khuangchera*, *Mizo Drama* (Padma Shri; folklorist & playwright) | Mizo |
| K.C. Lalvunga (*Zikpuii Pa*) | *Nunna Kawngthuampuiah* (landmark modern Mizo novel) | Mizo |
| Saihnuna & P.S. Chawngthu | Classic early Mizo lyric poetry and Christian hymns | Mizo |
| Malsawmi Jacob | *Zorami: A Redemption Song* (first English novel by a Mizo writer) | English |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Chapchar Kut | Early March | Mizoram's grandest spring festival celebrated after clearing bamboo/forests for *Jhum* (shifting) cultivation; showcases *Cheraw* and *Chai* dances. |
| Mim Kut | Aug–Sept | Traditional festival honoring deceased ancestors through offerings of fresh maize, *Job's tears*, and rice beer (*Zu*). |
| Pawl Kut | December | Cheerful winter post-harvest festival centered on feasting, especially celebrating children with traditional meat and egg dishes. |
| Anthurium Festival | September | State tourism and floriculture festival held at Reiek Heritage Village celebrating Mizo culture and Anthurium cultivation. |
| Thalfavang Kut | November | Autumn festival marking the completion of harvesting and celebrating rural sports and music. |
| Lyuva Khutla | March–April | Annual spring/harvest festival of the **Mara** tribe celebrated in Siaha district. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
The overarching **Mizo** Scheduled Tribe identity encompasses closely related ethnic clans alongside distinct minority tribes; Mizoram houses **3 Sixth Schedule Autonomous District Councils** (Chakma, Lai, Mara).

| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Lusei (Lushai) & Ralte | Central & Northern Mizoram (Aizawl, Serchhip, Kolasib) | Core *Mizo (Duhlian)* language; *Tlawmngaihna* ethical code (selfless community service), *Zawlbuk* dormitories, and *Chapchar Kut*. |
| Hmar | North-Eastern Mizoram (Champhai, Saitual) | *Sikpui Ruoi* festival, *Buonzawl* youth dormitories, and distinctive *Hmaram* / *Thangsuo Puan* weaving. |
| Lai (Pawi) | Lawngtlai (Lai Autonomous District Council) | *Sarlamkai* warrior dance, *Khuangchawi* feast of merit, and *Phawngpui* mountain traditions. |
| Mara (Lakher) | Siaha (Mara Autonomous District Council) | *Lyuva Khutla* festival, *Solakia* war dance, and guardianship of *Pala Wetland*. |
| Paite | Champhai & Khawzawl border | *Zangtalam* dance, *Ngaidam* traditions, and distinctive striped *Paite Puan*. |
| Chakma | Lawngtlai & Lunglei (Chakma Autonomous District Council) | Predominantly Theravada Buddhist community; *Biju* New Year festival and *Pinon-Hadi* weaving. |
| Bru (Reang) | Mamit & Kolasib | *Hojagiri* folk dance, *Buisu* festival, and traditional jhum cultivation. |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Aizawl | Capital city perched on steep ridges; houses Solomon's Temple, Mizoram State Museum, Durtlang Hills, and nearby Falkawn Mizo Village. |
| Lunglei | Second-largest city; named after a bridge-like rock ("Bridge of Rock"); southern commercial hub near Khawnglung Wildlife Sanctuary. |
| Champhai | Known as the **"Rice Bowl of Mizoram"**; Indo-Myanmar border trade hub (Zokhawthar), gateway to Murlen National Park and *Vangchhia* megaliths. |
| Serchhip | Recorded one of **India's highest district literacy rates**; famous for **Vantawng Falls** (Mizoram's highest waterfall, 229 m) and **Thenzawl** handloom hub. |
| Siaha (Saiha) | Headquarters of the Mara Autonomous District Council; gateway to **Pala Wetland (Palak Dil)** Ramsar site. |
| Lawngtlai | Headquarters of the Lai Autonomous District Council; base for **Phawngpui (Blue Mountain)** National Park. |
| Kolasib | Northern gateway from Assam; houses Bairabi railway terminal and Serlui B Dam. |
| Mamit | Western district bordering Tripura and Bangladesh; home to **Dampa Tiger Reserve**. |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Vangchhia Megaliths (*Kawtchhuah Ropui*) | Prehistoric / Early Historic Civilization | ASI-protected archaeological site in Champhai featuring over 170 carved stone slabs depicting warriors, animals, and rituals ("Great Stone Highway"). |
| Solomon's Temple, Aizawl | Kohhran Thianghlim (Completed 2017) | Monumental white marble church in Chawlhhmun featuring four towering corner spires and Biblical carvings. |
| Falkawn Mizo Village | State Heritage Recreation | Living heritage village near Aizawl showcasing authentic *Zawlbuk* (bachelor house), chief's residence, and traditional huts. |
| Sibuta Lung, Tachhip | Chief Sibuta (c. 18th c. CE) | Famous 3.5-meter memorial monolith tied to Mizo oral tragedy and chieftainship lore. |
| Mizo Hlakungpui Mual (Poets' Square) | Khawbung Community (1986) | Open-air hill memorial in Champhai honoring towering Mizo poets including Rokunga and P.S. Chawngthu. |
| Lianchhiari Lunglen Tlang & Kungawrhi Puk | Natural Folklore Landmarks (Champhai) | Cliff ledge and cave steeped in legendary Mizo folk ballads and historical chiefdom tales. |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| None inscribed yet | — | — |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **7 GI Tags:** *Mizo Puanchei*, *Tawlhlohpuan*, *Pawndum*, *Ngotekherh*, *Hmaram* (traditional handlooms), *Mizo Chilli (Bird's Eye Chilli / Hmar-Am)*, and *Mizo Ginger*.
- **National Parks (2):** **Phawngpui (Blue Mountain) National Park** (Lawngtlai; contains Mizoram's highest peak, *Phawngpui* at 2,157 m) and **Murlen National Park** (Champhai; dense forest dubbed the "Storehouse of Nature").
- **Tiger Reserve & Wildlife Sanctuaries:** **Dampa Tiger Reserve** (largest wildlife sanctuary in Mizoram, Mamit), **Lengteng**, **Ngengpui**, **Khawnglung**, **Tawi**, **Thorangtlang**, **Pualreng**, and **Tokalo**.
- **Ramsar Site (1):** **Pala Wetland (Palak Dil)** (Siaha district; Mizoram's largest natural lake, designated 2021/2022).
- **State Symbols:** Animal — *Himalayan Serow* (*Saza*); Bird — *Mrs. Hume's Pheasant* (*Vavu*); Flower — *Red Vanda*; Tree — *Indian Rose Chestnut / Ceylon Ironwood* (*Herhse*).
- **High Literacy & Geography:** Consistently among India's top literate states (~91.33%, 2011 Census); **Tropic of Cancer** passes directly across central Mizoram.
- **Constitutional Safeguards:** Protected under **Article 371G** (parliamentary acts on Mizo religious/social practices, customary law, and land ownership do not apply without Assembly resolution) and contains 3 **Sixth Schedule** Autonomous District Councils.

---

### 18. Nagaland

**Capital:** Kohima  
**Formation:** Became India's 16th state on December 1, 1963 (State of Nagaland Act, 1962, following the 16-Point Agreement of 1960; special provisions under Article 371A)  
**Official Language(s):** English (sole official language; no Naga language is currently in the Eighth Schedule)  
**Other Languages:** Nagamese (wide lingua franca), Ao, Angami, Sumi (Sema), Lotha, Konyak, Chakhesang, Chang, Sangtam, Rengma, Phom, Zeme, Liangmai, Khiamniungan, Yimkhiung, Pochury, Kuki, Tikhir

#### Brief History
- **Kachari Kingdom (c. 10th–13th c. CE):** Medieval Dimasa Kachari rulers established their capital at Dimapur, leaving behind the iconic monolithic Kachari Ruins before moving to Maibang under Ahom pressure.
- **Autonomous Village Republics:** Naga tribes historically functioned as self-governing village republics organized around the **Morung** (youth dormitory/training center) and **Putu Menden** (tribal councils).
- **British Contact (1832–1880s):** Fierce resistance culminated in the **Battle of Khonoma (1879–1880)** led by Angami warriors before the Naga Hills District was consolidated under Assam.
- **World War II — Battle of Kohima (April–June 1944):** Turning point of the Burma Campaign halting the Japanese-INA advance into India; famous for the *Battle of the Tennis Court* and the **Kohima Epitaph**.
- **Freedom & Statehood Movement:** The **Naga Club** (1918) petitioned the Simon Commission (1929); **Haipou Jadonang** and **Rani Gaidinliu** fought colonial rule; post-independence agitation led by **A.Z. Phizo** was addressed through the **16-Point Agreement (1960)** signed with the Naga People's Convention, creating Nagaland on **December 1, 1963** under **Article 371A**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Naga War Dance (Multi-tribal) | Folk | Vigorous synchronized warrior dance performed with spears, *daos* (machetes), shields, mock battle formations, and rhythmic war cries. |
| Chang Lo (Sua Lua) | Folk | Traditional **Chang** tribe victory dance performed in dramatic hornbill-feather headgear; originally celebrated triumph in battle, now staged at *Poanglem*. |
| Zeliang Dance | Folk | High-energy circular and linear dance of the Zeme and Liangmai tribes marked by synchronized stomping and swaying. |
| Aoling Dance | Folk | Celebratory spring New Year dance of the **Konyak** tribe performed with feathered hats, brass ornaments, and *dao* movements. |
| Modse / Moatsu Dance | Folk | Communal dance of the **Ao** tribe celebrating the end of sowing in vivid *Tsungkotepsu* shawls. |
| Melophita & Khamba Lim | Folk | Ceremonial folk dances of the **Angami** and **Ao** communities performed in two alternating rows. |
| Leshalaptu & Sadal Kekai | Folk | Traditional ceremonial dances of the **Chakhesang** and **Kuki** tribes. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Traditional Music & Instruments:** Unison and polyphonic tribal folk songs (*Li* songs); giant hollowed **Log Drums** (*Kham / Songkong*) kept in *Morungs*; **Tati** (single-stringed fiddle of Angamis and Chakhesangs); **Bamhum** (bamboo wind pipe); **Phetku** (bamboo jaw harp); buffalo-horn trumpets; and brass gongs.
- **Handlooms & Textiles (GI Tagged):** Symbolic handwoven shawls denoting social status, tribe, and warrior feats (e.g., Ao *Tsungkotepsu* warrior shawl, Angami *Lohe*, and GI-tagged **Chakhesang Shawls**).
- **Handicrafts:** Intricate **Woodcarving** (mithun heads, hornbills, human figures on *Morung* pillars and village gates), **Phom Black Pottery** (Longleng), **Bamboo & Cane Basketry**, **Traditional Blacksmithing** (*Dao* blades and spears), and colorful **Beadwork** (carnelian, cowrie shells, ivory/horn armlets).
- **Oral & Living Heritage:** The **Morung** served as the traditional academy of Naga music, woodcraft, storytelling, and community ethics.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Oral Folk Bards | *Morung* epics, creation myths & folk songs (Roman script introduced by missionaries like Rev. E.W. Clark for Ao) | Ao, Angami, Sumi, Lotha, etc. |
| Easterine Kire | *A Naga Village Remembered* (first Naga English novel, 2003), *When the River Sleeps* (Hindu Literary Prize), *A Terrible Matriarchy* | English |
| Temsula Ao | *These Hills Called Home: Stories from a War Zone*, *Laburnum for My Head* (Sahitya Akademi Award, 2013; Padma Shri) | English |
| Nini Lungalang | *The Morning Years* (collected poetry) | English |
| Monalisa Changkija | *Weapons of Words on Pages of Pain* (poetry & journalism) | English |
| Avinuo Kire | *The Power to Forgive and Other Stories*, *Where the Wildflowers Grow* | English |

#### Festivals
Nagaland is widely called the **"Land of Festivals"** as each major tribe celebrates distinct agrarian thanksgiving festivals:

| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Hornbill Festival | December 1–10 | State's flagship **"Festival of Festivals"** at **Kisama Heritage Village** near Kohima, coinciding with Statehood Day (Dec 1) and uniting all Naga tribes. |
| Sekrenyi (Phousanyi) | February | Ten-day purification and sanctification festival of the **Angami** tribe before the agricultural year. |
| Aoling (Aoleang Monyu) | April 1–6 | Six-day spring and **New Year festival of the Konyak** tribe invoking blessings for newly sown seeds. |
| Moatsu Mong & Tsungremmong | May 1–3 & August | **Ao** tribe's post-sowing (*Moatsu*) and pre-harvest (*Tsungremmong*) thanksgiving festivals. |
| Tokhü Emong | November 1–9 | Post-harvest thanksgiving and reconciliation festival of the **Lotha** tribe. |
| Tuluni & Ahuna | July 8 & November | **Sumi** tribe festivals (*Tuluni* mid-year rice-beer feast; *Ahuna* celebrating first harvest cooked inside bamboo tubes). |
| Ngada | Late November | Grand post-harvest festival of the **Rengma** tribe marking completion of the agricultural year. |
| Naknyulum & Poanglem | July & January | **Chang** tribe's festival of overcoming darkness (*Naknyulum*) and harvest celebration (*Poanglem*). |
| Monyu | April | Spring festival of the **Phom** tribe marking the transition from winter to sowing season. |
| Metumniu (Metemneo) | August | Five-day post-harvest millet festival of the **Yimkhiung** tribe honoring departed souls. |
| Amongmong | September | Harvest thanksgiving festival of the **Sangtam** tribe. |
| Yemshe & Nazu | October & February | Pre-harvest (*Yemshe*) and sowing (*Nazu*) festivals of the **Pochury** tribe. |
| Tsukhenyie & Sükrünye | March & January | Major agricultural and purification festivals of the **Chakhesang** tribe. |
| Hega & Mileinyi | February & March | Cultural and New Year festivals of the **Zeliang** community. |
| Tsonglaknyi | October | Traditional purification and shield-blessing festival of the **Tikhir** tribe. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
Nagaland officially recognizes **17 major indigenous tribes** (the 16 longstanding tribes plus **Tikhir**, recognized as the 17th tribe in 2022):

| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Angami | Kohima & Chümoukedima | Elaborate terraced wet-rice cultivation, woodcraft, *Sekrenyi* festival, and Khonoma Green Village heritage. |
| Ao | Mokokchung | Earliest tribe to adopt written literature (Roman script), *Moatsu* festival, *Tsungkotepsu* shawl, and *Putu Menden* councils. |
| Konyak | Mon | Hereditary *Angh* (chiefs), iconic facial tattoos (former headhunter heritage), *Aoling* festival, and muzzle-loading gun craftsmanship. |
| Sumi (Sema) | Zunheboto | Martial traditions, *Tuluni* and *Ahuna* festivals, and grand chieftaincy feasts. |
| Chakhesang | Phek | GI-tagged **Chakhesang Shawls**, terraced agriculture, *Tsukhenyie* festival, and *Khezhakeno* origin legend. |
| Lotha | Wokha | *Tokhü Emong* festival, *Doyang* Amur Falcon conservation (*Pangti*), and rich oral ballads. |
| Chang | Tuensang | *Chang Lo* warrior dance, *Naknyulum* festival, and giant *Morung* log drums. |
| Khiamniungan | Noklak & Tuensang | Cliff-face wild honey hunting, brass-wood ornaments, and *Miu* / *Tsokum* festivals. |
| Phom | Longleng | Traditional **Black Pottery**, fine cane craft, and *Monyu* festival. |
| Sangtam | Kiphire & Tuensang | *Amongmong* festival, bamboo crafts, and high-altitude millet farming. |
| Yimkhiung (Yimchunger) | Shamator & Kiphire | *Metumniu* millet harvest festival and border-ridge settlements near Saramati. |
| Pochury | Phek (Meluri) | *Yemshe* and *Nazu* festivals, salt-brine wells of Meluri, and colorful woven shawls. |
| Rengma | Tseminyü & Kohima | *Ngada* harvest festival and distinctive warrior shawls. |
| Zeliang (Zeme & Liangmai) | Peren | Synchronized *Zeliang* group dances, *Hega* festival, and Heraka movement legacy. |
| Tikhir | Kiphire & Shamator | Recognized as Nagaland's **17th major tribe (2022)**; *Tsonglaknyi* festival. |
| Kachari & Kuki | Dimapur & Peren | Plains/hill minority recognized tribes; *Bushu* (Kachari) and *Mimkut* (Kuki) festivals, bamboo dances. |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Kohima | Capital city; **Battle of Kohima WWII Cemetery**, **Kisama Heritage Village** (Hornbill venue), Japfü Peak, and gateway to Dzükou Valley. |
| Dimapur | Commercial capital & only rail/major air hub; **Kachari Ruins**, Rangapahar Wildlife Sanctuary, and Diezephe Craft Village. |
| Mokokchung | Intellectual and cultural hub of the Ao Nagas; historic villages of **Ungma** and **Mopungchuket**. |
| Mon | Land of the Konyak Nagas; home to **Longwa Village**, where the *Angh's* house straddles the **India–Myanmar international border**. |
| Wokha | Lotha Naga heartland; home to **Doyang Reservoir** (world's largest roosting site for migrating **Amur Falcons**). |
| Zunheboto | Sumi Naga hub; houses the iconic **Sumi Baptist Church Zunheboto** (largest Baptist church building in Asia). |
| Tuensang & Noklak | Eastern Naga cultural hubs; Noklak is famous for Khiamniungan cliff-honey harvesters and border trade. |
| Phek & Pfutsero | Chakhesang heartland; **Pfutsero** is Nagaland's highest & coldest town; nearby Shilloi Lake and Khezhakeno megalith. |
| Khonoma | Near Kohima; acclaimed as **Asia's First Green Village** for community-led ban on hunting/logging and historic 1879 anti-British fort. |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Kohima War Cemetery | Commonwealth War Graves Commission (1944 Battle) | Terraced WWII memorial on Garrison Hill featuring the renowned **Kohima Epitaph** ("For Your Tomorrow, We Gave Our Today"). |
| Kachari Ruins, Dimapur | Dimasa Kachari Kingdom (10th–13th c. CE) | Mysterious mushroom-shaped and V-shaped carved sandstone monoliths representing an ancient pre-Ahom urban center. |
| Kisama Heritage Village | Govt of Nagaland (Near Kohima) | Living cultural complex featuring 17 traditional tribal *Morungs*; annual venue of the Hornbill Festival. |
| Khonoma Fort & Ceremonial Gates (*Kharu*) | Angami Warriors (19th c. CE) | Historic dry-stone battlements and carved wooden village gates central to the 1879 Anglo-Naga War. |
| Khezhakeno Megalith (*Tsokho*) | Ancestral Naga Folklore | Legendary flat stone slab in Phek revered as the dispersal point of early Naga ancestors. |
| Longwa *Angh's* House, Mon | Konyak Chieftaincy | Unique royal residence bisected directly by the international boundary between India and Myanmar. |
| Kohima Cathedral (Mary Help of Christians) | Completed 1991 (Indo-Japanese Goodwill) | Iconic hillside cathedral combining Naga architectural motifs with WWII reconciliation memory. |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| None inscribed yet | — | — |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **4 GI Tags:** *Naga Mircha (King Chilli / Bhut Jolokia)*, *Naga Tree Tomato*, *Naga Cucumber*, and *Chakhesang Shawls*.
- **National Park (1):** **Intanki (Ntangki) National Park** (Peren district; protects Hoolock Gibbons, Asian elephants, tigers, and sloth bears).
- **Wildlife Sanctuaries (4):** **Fakim** (Kiphire, near Myanmar border), **Puliebadze** (Kohima), **Rangapahar** (Dimapur), and **Singphan** (Mon — also an Elephant Reserve).
- **Ramsar Sites:** 0 (none as of 2026).
- **State Symbols:** Animal — *Mithun* (*Bos frontalis*); Bird — *Blyth's Tragopan*; Flower — *Tree Rhododendron*; Tree — *Alder* (*Alnus nepalensis*).
- **Falcon Capital of the World:** **Doyang Reservoir (Wokha)** hosts massive annual roosts of migrating **Amur Falcons**, protected by local Naga communities (especially Pangti village).
- **Constitutional & Demographic Distinctions:** **Article 371A** safeguards Naga customary law, religion, and land/resource ownership; **Mount Saramati** (3,826 m, Kiphire) is Nagaland's highest peak; Nagaland was the **only Indian state to record negative decadal population growth (-0.58%)** in the 2011 Census.

### 19. Odisha

**Capital:** Bhubaneswar  
**Formation:** Formed as a separate linguistic province on April 1, 1936 (**Utkala Dibasa** / Odisha Day); princely states merged in 1948–49; officially renamed from Orissa to **Odisha** (and Oriya to **Odia**) in 2011  
**Official Language(s):** Odia (Eighth Schedule; **6th Classical Language of India**, designated in **2014**), English (additional official use); **Santali** (spoken widely in northern Odisha, is also an Eighth Schedule language)  
**Other Languages:** Sambalpuri (Kosli), Kui, Mundari, Ho, Saora, Desiya, Bengali, Telugu, Hindi

#### Brief History
- Ancient **Kalinga**, **Utkala**, and **Odra** regions; the bloody **Kalinga War (261 BCE)** at **Dhauli** transformed Mauryan Emperor **Ashoka** toward Buddhism and *Dhamma* (recorded in Rock Edicts at **Dhauli** and **Jaugada**).
- **Mahameghavahana (Chedi) Dynasty:** **Emperor Kharavela** (1st century BCE) patronized Jainism and inscribed his achievements in the **Hathigumpha Inscription** at Udayagiri.
- **Medieval Temple-Building Dynasties:** **Shailodbhavas**, **Bhauma-Karas** (noted for queens like Tribhuvana Mahadevi I), **Somavamshis** (built Lingaraj and Mukteswara temples), **Eastern Gangas** (**Anantavarman Chodaganga Deva** began Jagannath Puri; **Narasimhadeva I** built Konark Sun Temple), and **Suryavamshi Gajapatis** (**Kapilendra Deva**, **Prataparudra Deva**).
- **Maritime & Colonial Resistance:** Ancient *Sadhaba* merchants sailed to Bali, Java, and Sumatra. Anti-British struggles include **Jayee Rajaguru** (1804), the **Paika Rebellion (1817)** led by **Bakshi Jagabandhu** in Khordha, **Veer Surendra Sai** (Sambalpur), **Laxman Naik** (Koraput), and **Baji Rout** (youngest freedom martyr, 1938).
- **Odisha Unification:** **Utkala Sammilani (1903)** led by **Madhusudan Das** (*Utkala Gouraba*) and **Gopabandhu Das** (*Utkala Mani*) achieved India's first linguistic province in **1936**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Odissi | Classical | One of India's 8 classical dances; rooted in *Natya Shastra* and *Mahari*/*Gotipua* temple traditions; defined by lyrical grace, *Tribhanga* (three-bend) and *Chauka* postures; revived by Guru Kelucharan Mohapatra and Sanjukta Panigrahi. |
| Mayurbhanj Chhau | Semi-classical / Folk | Vigorous martial dance performed **without masks** (unlike Seraikela and Purulia Chhau); Chhau is inscribed on **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage (2010)**. |
| Gotipua | Folk / Pre-classical | Acrobatic devotional dance performed by young boys dressed as girls praising Radha–Krishna; historical precursor to modern Odissi. |
| Dalkhai | Folk | Lively Western Odisha women's dance performed during *Bhaijiuntia* and Dussehra, accompanied by *Dhol*, *Nisan*, and *Mahuri* with refrains of *"Dalkhai Bo"*. |
| Ghumura | Folk | Martial-rooted folk dance of Kalahandi performed with pitcher-shaped *Ghumura* drums, recalling ancient battle energy. |
| Ranapa | Folk | Traditional stilt dance of Ganjam where dancers walk and perform on bamboo stilts while singing Krishna legends. |
| Paika Akhada / Paika Nrutya | Folk / Martial | Sword-and-shield battle dance preserving the martial discipline of Odisha's peasant militia (*Paikas*). |
| Danda Nata (Danda Jatra) | Folk / Ritual | Ancient penance dance-drama of Southern Odisha performed in *Chaitra* in devotion to Shiva–Shakti. |
| Karma Naach & Dhemsa | Folk (Tribal) | *Karma* celebrates *Karam Rani* tree worship (Gond/Oraon/Binjhal); *Dhemsa* is the iconic communal circle dance of Koraput tribes. |
| Sambalpuri (Rasarkeli / Mailajada) | Folk | Rhythmic group folk dances of Western Odisha driven by energetic drum patterns. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Odissi Music:** Distinct classical vocal-instrumental tradition (*Raganga*, *Bhabanga*, *Natyanga*, *Dhrubapadanga*) using **Mardala** (traditional barrel drum), *Gini* (cymbals), *Bansi* (flute), and *Kendera*; complemented by vibrant **Sambalpuri folk music** (e.g., *Rangabati*).
- **Painting Styles:** **Odisha Pattachitra** (GI Tagged; traditional cloth-based scrolls painted with natural mineral/vegetable pigments depicting Lord Jagannath and epics, centered at **Raghurajpur** Heritage Craft Village); **Talapatra Chitra** (intricate palm-leaf etchings); and **Lanjia Saora Painting / Idital** (GI Tagged Jan 2024; white geometric tribal wall murals on crimson-ochre mud walls).
- **Handicrafts & Textiles (GI Tagged):** **Cuttack Silver Filigree (*Tarakasi*)**; **Pipili Applique (*Chandua*)** (GI Tagged colorful patchwork canopies); **Konark Stone Carving** (GI Tagged); **Kapdaganda Shawl** (GI Tagged Jan 2024; embroidered shawl of Dongria Kondh women); GI-tagged handlooms including **Kotpad Handloom**, **Orissa Ikat / Sambalpuri Bandha**, **Bomkai**, **Khandua**, **Habaspuri**, **Berhampur Patta (Phoda Kumbha)**, **Gopalpur Tussar**, and **Dhalapathar Parda**; **Dhokra** (lost-wax brass metalcraft); **Horn Craft** (Paralakhemundi); and **Kaincha** (golden grass craft of Kendrapara).
- **Folk Theatre:** **Dhanu Jatra** (Bargarh; **world's largest open-air theatre** spanning 11 days), **Pala** and **Daskathia** (literary ballad performances), **Prahallada Nataka** (Ganjam), and **Ravana Chhaya** (traditional leather shadow puppetry).

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Rath Yatra (Car Festival) | June–July (Ashadha) | World-famous chariot journey of Lord Jagannath, Balabhadra, and Subhadra in Puri (*Gundicha Yatra*); *Nabakalebara* occurs every 8, 12, or 19 years. |
| Raja Parba | Mid-June | Unique 3-day festival celebrating womanhood, menstruation, and Mother Earth's fertility; girls wear new attire, swing on *Doli*, and avoid cutting/ploughing. |
| Nuakhai | Aug–Sept | Biggest agricultural harvest festival of Western Odisha celebrating the season's first rice (*Nabanna*) offered to Goddess Samaleswari. |
| Bali Jatra & Boita Bandana | Oct–Nov (Kartik Purnima) | Asia's largest open trade fair on Mahanadi banks in Cuttack honoring ancient *Sadhaba* maritime voyages to Bali, Java, and Sumatra; devotees float miniature boats (*Boita*). |
| Dhanu Jatra | Dec–Jan | 11-day open-air folk drama in Bargarh depicting Krishna–Kansa episodes across an ~8 km radius (**world's largest open-air theatre**). |
| Konark Dance & Music Festival | December 1–5 | Annual classical dance extravaganza held in the open-air amphitheater overlooking Konark Sun Temple. |
| Sital Sasthi | May–June | Grand week-long carnival celebrating the celestial marriage of Shiva and Parvati, especially famous in Sambalpur. |
| Chandan Yatra | April–May | 42-day summer ritual in Puri where deities are anointed with cooling sandalwood paste and floated in Narendra Tank. |
| Chhau Festival (Chaitra Parba) | April | Traditional spring festival in Mayurbhanj celebrating *Chhau* dance and Shiva worship. |
| Kalinga Mahotsav | Jan–Feb | Classical and martial arts festival held at Dhauli Shanti Stupa promoting global peace. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
Odisha houses **62 Scheduled Tribes** (~22.85% of the state population) and **13 Particularly Vulnerable Tribal Groups (PVTGs)** — the **highest number of PVTGs in India**.

| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Kondh (including Dongria & Kutia Kondh — PVTGs) | Kandhamal, Rayagada, Kalahandi (Niyamgiri) | *Kapdaganda* embroidered shawls (GI Tagged), *Kandhamal Haladi*, worship of Earth Goddess *Dharani Penu*, and *Meriah* cultural past. |
| Santal | Mayurbhanj, Keonjhar, Balasore | **Ol Chiki** script invented by Pandit Raghunath Murmu (1925), *Sohrae* & *Baha* festivals, and rich mud-wall artistry. |
| Bonda (Bondo — PVTG) | Malkangiri (Bonda Hills) | One of India's oldest primitive tribes; distinct beaded jewelry, brass necklaces (*Khagla*), shaven heads, and *Patkhanda* festival. |
| Saora / Lanjia Saora (PVTG) | Rayagada & Gajapati | GI-tagged **Idital (Lanjia Saora) wall murals** painted in white rice paste on red ochre, and terraced hill farming. |
| Juang (PVTG) | Keonjhar & Dhenkanal | *Changu* tambourine folk dance, *Mandaghar* youth dormitory, and leaf/bead traditions. |
| Gadaba & Koya | Koraput & Malkangiri | *Dhemsa* dance and *Kereng* bark weaving (Gadaba); iconic bison-horn dance headgear (Koya). |
| Gond, Munda & Oraon | Sundargarh, Sambalpur, Mayurbhanj, Keonjhar | *Karma* harvest festival, *Sarhul* (Oraon/Munda), *Ol* metal/wood craft, and vibrant community dances. |
| Paudi Bhuyan, Lodha, Mankidia, Birhor, Didayi & Chuktia Bhunjia (PVTGs) | Keonjhar, Mayurbhanj, Malkangiri, Nuapada | Forest-based foraging, *Similipal Kai Chutney* (Mayurbhanj tribes), rope making from *Siali* fiber (*Mankidia*), and *Chaita* festivals. |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Bhubaneswar | Capital city; **"Temple City of India"** (*Ekamra Kshetra*); Lingaraj Temple, Udayagiri–Khandagiri Caves, Dhauli, and Nandankanan Zoological Park. |
| Puri | One of the **Char Dham** pilgrimage centers; Shree Jagannath Temple, Golden Beach (Blue Flag certified), and **Raghurajpur** Heritage Craft Village. |
| Cuttack | Former capital; **"Silver City"** (*Tarakasi* silver filigree), Barabati Fort, Bali Jatra, birthplace of Netaji Subhas Chandra Bose, and Odisha High Court. |
| Konark | World-famous **Sun Temple ("Black Pagoda")** and Chandrabhaga Beach. |
| Rourkela | **"Steel City"** in Sundargarh; India's first public-sector integrated steel plant (1959, German aid) and the **Birsa Munda International Hockey Stadium**. |
| Sambalpur | **Hirakud Dam** (world's longest major earthen/mainstream dam), Sambalpuri handloom (*Ikat*), Samaleswari Temple, and *Nuakhai* center. |
| Berhampur (Brahmapur) | **"Silk City"** (*Berhampur Patta* sarees), Gopalpur-on-Sea, *Tara Tarini* Shakti Peetha, and *Ganjam Kewda* perfume extracts. |
| Balasore (Baleswar) | Chandipur ("Vanishing Sea" tidal beach) and **DRDO Integrated Test Range** at Abdul Kalam (Wheeler) Island. |
| Koraput | Tribal Heritage Museum, **Deomali** (highest peak of Odisha at 1,672 m), Araku/Koraput Coffee, and *Kalajeera* rice. |
| Baripada | Mayurbhanj headquarters; "Dwitiya Srikshetra" (famous Rath Yatra where **women pull Subhadra's chariot**) and gateway to Similipal. |
| Paradip | Major deep-water maritime port on the Bay of Bengal coast. |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Sun Temple, Konark | King Narasimhadeva I, Eastern Ganga (c. 1250 CE) | Known as the **"Black Pagoda"**; colossal stone chariot of Surya with 24 intricately carved wheels pulled by 7 horses. |
| Shree Jagannath Temple, Puri | Anantavarman Chodaganga Deva (12th c. CE) | Known as the **"White Pagoda"**; major *Char Dham* Vaishnavite shrine showcasing soaring *Kalinga Rekha Deula* architecture. |
| Lingaraj Temple, Bhubaneswar | Somavamshi Dynasty (11th c. CE) | Largest and finest pinnacle of *Kalinga* temple architecture dedicated to *Harihara* (Shiva–Vishnu). |
| Mukteswara & Rajarani Temples, Bhubaneswar | Somavamshi Dynasty (10th–11th c. CE) | Mukteswara's arched *Torana* gateway is famed as the **"Gem of Odisha Architecture"**; Rajarani is celebrated for ornate sculptures. |
| Udayagiri & Khandagiri Caves | King Kharavela, Chedi Dynasty (1st–2nd c. BCE) | Rock-cut Jain monastic shelters; **Hathigumpha Inscription** records Kharavela's military and cultural reign. |
| Dhauli Giri Shanti Stupa & Ashokan Edicts | Emperor Ashoka (261 BCE) / Peace Pagoda (1972) | Site of the **Kalinga War**; preserves Rock Edicts XI–XIII alongside the white Peace Pagoda built by the Japan Buddha Sangha. |
| Diamond Triangle (Ratnagiri, Lalitgiri, Udayagiri) | Bhauma-Kara / Gupta–Pala Eras (5th–13th c. CE) | Major Mahayana/Vajrayana Buddhist monastery complexes yielding stupas, relic caskets, and Tara/Buddha sculptures. |
| Chausath Yogini Temple, Hirapur & Ranipur-Jharial | Bhauma-Kara / Somavamshi (9th–10th c. CE) | Rare circular hypaethral (roofless) Tantric temples dedicated to the 64 Yoginis. |
| Barabati Fort, Cuttack | Eastern Ganga Dynasty (14th c. CE) | Historic nine-storeyed fort ruins on the Mahanadi riverbanks. |
| Parasurameswara & Vaital Deula, Bhubaneswar | Shailodbhava & Bhauma-Kara (7th–8th c. CE) | Early milestones demonstrating *Rekha* and wagon-vaulted *Khakhara* Kalinga temple styles. |

| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| Sun Temple, Konark | Cultural | 1984 |

*(Note: **Ekamra Kshetra – The Temple City, Bhubaneswar** [2014] and **Chilika Lake** [2014] are on India's **UNESCO Tentative List**; **Similipal** is part of the **UNESCO Biosphere Reserve Network [2009]**; **Chhau Dance** is inscribed on **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage [2010]**.)*

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **25+ GI Tags:** Includes *Kotpad Handloom*, *Orissa Ikat*, *Konark Stone Carving*, *Odisha Pattachitra*, *Pipli Applique*, *Khandua Saree*, *Gopalpur Tussar*, *Ganjam Kewda Flower & Rooh*, *Dhalapathar Parda*, *Sambalpuri Bandha*, *Bomkai*, *Habaspuri*, *Berhampur Patta*, *Araku Valley Coffee*, *Kandhamal Haladi*, *Odisha Rasagola*, *Kendrapara Rasabali*, plus **7 Jan 2024 GI Tags** (*Lanjia Saora Painting*, *Kapdaganda Shawl*, *Similipal Kai Chutney*, *Koraput Kala Jeera Rice*, *Nayagarh Kanteimundi Brinjal*, *Odisha Khajuri Guda*, *Dhenkanal Magji*).
- **National Parks (2):** **Similipal National Park** (Mayurbhanj; UNESCO Biosphere Reserve; famous for rare melanistic/black tigers, Barehipani & Joranda waterfalls) and **Bhitarkanika National Park** (Kendrapara; second-largest mangrove ecosystem in India, saltwater crocodiles).
- **Tiger Reserves & Wildlife Sanctuaries:** **Similipal** and **Satkosia** Tiger Reserves; **Gahirmatha Marine Wildlife Sanctuary** (world's largest mass-nesting/*Arribada* site for **Olive Ridley sea turtles**); **Chilika Nalabana Bird Sanctuary**, **Debrigarh**, **Chandaka-Dampara**, **Sunabeda**, **Ushakothi**, and **Nandankanan Zoological Park** (white tigers).
- **Ramsar Sites (6):** **Chilika Lake** (1981 — India's **first Ramsar site** alongside Keoladeo; Asia's largest brackish lagoon; Irrawaddy dolphins), **Bhitarkanika Mangroves** (2002), **Satkosia Gorge** (2021), **Ansupa Lake** (2022), **Hirakud Reservoir** (2022), and **Tampara Lake** (2022).
- **State Symbols:** Animal — *Sambar Deer*; Bird — *Indian Roller*; Flower — *Ashoka* (*Saraca asoca*); Tree — *Sacred Fig / Peepal*.
- **Engineering & Space Landmarks:** **Hirakud Dam** on the Mahanadi (Sambalpur) is the **world's longest major earthen/mainstream dam** (~25.8 km with dykes); **Abdul Kalam Island** (Balasore) is India's premier missile test range.

---

### 20. Punjab

**Capital:** Chandigarh (Union Territory; joint capital of Punjab and Haryana)  
**Formation:** Reorganized on linguistic basis on November 1, 1966 (Punjab Reorganisation Act, 1966 — carving out Haryana, merging hill tracts into Himachal Pradesh, and making Chandigarh a UT); earlier PEPSU merged into Punjab on November 1, 1956  
**Official Language(s):** Punjabi (written in Gurmukhi script; Eighth Schedule; sole official language under the *Punjab Official Languages Act, 1967*), English (higher administrative/judicial use)  
**Other Languages:** Hindi, Majhi, Doabi, Malwai, Puadhi, Pahari/Dogri (northern belt), Urdu

#### Brief History
- Land of **Sapta Sindhu** (Rigvedic "Land of Seven Rivers") and major **Indus Valley Civilization** sites at **Ropar (Rupnagar)** and **Sanghol**.
- **Battle of the Hydaspes (326 BCE):** Fought on the Jhelum banks between **Alexander the Great** and **King Porus**; Alexander turned back near the **Beas River**.
- **Birthplace of Sikhism:** Founded by **Guru Nanak Dev Ji** (1469); **Guru Arjan Dev Ji** compiled the *Adi Granth* (1604) and completed Harmandir Sahib; **Guru Gobind Singh Ji** founded the **Khalsa Panth** at Anandpur Sahib on **Baisakhi 1699**.
- **Sikh Confederacy & Empire:** **Banda Singh Bahadur** led an agrarian uprising (1709–15); 12 **Sikh Misls** were united into the **Sikh Empire (1799–1839)** by **Maharaja Ranjit Singh** (*Sher-e-Punjab*). Following two **Anglo-Sikh Wars** (1845–46, 1848–49), Punjab was annexed by the British in 1849.
- **Epicenter of the Freedom Struggle:** Home to the **Kuka (Namdhari) Movement** (Baba Ram Singh), **Ghadar Party** (1913, Sohan Singh Bhakna & Kartar Singh Sarabha), **Jallianwala Bagh Massacre** (April 13, 1919), **Akali/Gurdwara Reform Movement**, **Poorna Swaraj** pledge at Lahore (1929), and martyrs **Shaheed Bhagat Singh**, **Sukhdev**, **Rajguru**, **Lala Lajpat Rai**, and **Shaheed Udham Singh**. Partitioned in **1947** into East Punjab (India) and West Punjab (Pakistan).

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Bhangra | Folk | Iconic high-energy male harvest dance performed to *Dhol* rhythms around *Baisakhi*, characterized by vigorous shoulder bounces, jumps, and colorful *Tehmat*–*Turban* attire. |
| Giddha | Folk | Vibrant women's circular folk dance driven solely by rhythmic handclaps and witty improvisational folk couplets (*Boliyan*) mocking social quirks. |
| Jhumar | Folk | Graceful rhythmic male dance originating in the Sandalbar/Malwa belt performed in circles during harvests and weddings. |
| Sammi | Folk | Traditional women's circle dance of the Sandalbar region characterized by swinging arms and songs beginning *"Sammi Meri Waar"*. |
| Luddi | Folk | Celebratory victory dance featuring sinuous shoulder and head movements accompanied by *Dhol* and finger-snapping. |
| Kikli | Folk | Playful girls' dance where pairs lock hands crosswise, lean back, and spin swiftly while singing rhyming verses. |
| Malwai Giddha (*Babeyan da Giddha*) & Dhamal | Folk | *Malwai Giddha* is an exuberant male folk dance of the Malwa region played with *Chimta*, *Algoza*, *Tumbi*, and *Kato*; *Dhamal* is a Sufi-inspired ecstatic dance. |
| Gatka | Martial Art / Folk | Traditional Sikh martial art using wooden sticks (*Soti*), shields (*Farri*), and swords, showcased dramatically during *Hola Mohalla*. |

*(Note: Punjab has no indigenous classical dance form of its own, though North Indian Kathak is practiced regionally.)*

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Sacred & Folk Music:** **Shabad Kirtan** (classical *Raag*-based recitation of Gurbani); **Sufi Kafis & Qawwali** (Baba Farid, Bulleh Shah); **Dhadi** & **Kavishri** (heroic Sikh ballad traditions); **Tappa** & **Qissa** singing (*Heer*, *Mirza*). Traditional instruments include **Dhol**, **Tumbi** (single-string pluck), **Algoza** (twin flutes), **Chimta** (fire tongs), **Sapp**, **Rabab**, **Sarangi**, **Dhadh**, and **Bugchu**.
- **Painting Style:** **Sikh School of Painting** (19th-century offshoot of the Kangra/Pahari miniature style patronized under Maharaja Ranjit Singh depicting Sikh Gurus, royal courts, and murals; later carried forward by **Sobha Singh** and **Amrita Sher-Gil**).
- **Handicrafts (GI & UNESCO Listed):** **Phulkari & Bagh** (GI Tagged; intricate geometric floral embroidery done on hand-spun *Khaddar* using bright *Pat* silk thread); **Thatheras of Jandiala Guru** (Amritsar; traditional hand-hammered brass and copper utensil craft inscribed on **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage [2014]**); **Punjabi Jutti** (embroidered leather footwear of Patiala, Muktsar, and Fazilka); **Hoshiarpur Wood Inlay**; **Paranda** (ornamental braided silk hair tassels); and **Pidhi** (woven low wooden stools).
- **Folk Theatre:** **Naqal** (satirical mimicry), **Bhand** (traditional jesters), **Swang**, and vibrant modern Punjabi theatre (*Balwant Gargi*, *Gursharan Singh*).

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Baisakhi (Vaisakhi) | April 13/14 | Major rabi wheat harvest festival and anniversary of **Guru Gobind Singh Ji founding the Khalsa Panth (1699)** at Anandpur Sahib. |
| Lohri | January 13 | Winter solstice/bonfire festival celebrating warmth, longer days, newborn children, and newlyweds with *til*, *rewari*, and *Sunder Mundriye* songs. |
| Maghi | January 14 | Religious fair especially at Sri Muktsar Sahib honoring the 40 *Chali Mukte* (Liberated Ones) who died fighting the Mughals in 1705. |
| Hola Mohalla | March (day after Holi) | Martial Sikh festival instituted by Guru Gobind Singh Ji at Anandpur Sahib featuring *Nihang* mock battles, *Gatka*, horse-riding, and poetry. |
| Gurpurabs | Kartik Purnima / Nov & Jan | Birth anniversaries of Guru Nanak Dev Ji and Guru Gobind Singh Ji marked by *Akhand Path*, *Nagar Kirtans*, and *Langars*. |
| Teeyan (Teej) | July–Aug (Sawan) | Monsoon festival for women featuring tree swings, *Giddha*, *Mehndi*, and *Kheer-Puda*. |
| Basant Panchami | Jan–Feb | Spring welcome celebrated with yellow clothing and competitive kite flying. |
| Chhapar Mela | September | Famous rural folk fair at Gugga Mari (Ludhiana) honoring snake deity Gugga Pir. |
| Shaheedi Jor Mela | December | Solemn gathering at Fatehgarh Sahib commemorating the martyrdom of Guru Gobind Singh's younger *Sahibzadas*. |
| Qila Raipur Rural Olympics | February | Unique 3-day sporting fair near Ludhiana featuring bullock-cart races, tug-of-war, and traditional strength feats. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
**Important Exam Fact:** Punjab has **zero (0) notified Scheduled Tribes** under the Constitution (Scheduled Tribes) Order, 1950 (alongside Haryana, Chandigarh, Delhi, and Puducherry). Notable rural, pastoral, and nomadic communities active in Punjab's cultural/ecological heritage include:

| Community / Group | Region | Known For |
|-------------------|--------|-----------|
| Bishnoi Community | Fazilka (Abohar) | Strict ecological protection of the **Blackbuck** (Punjab's state animal) and *Khejri* trees in the Abohar Wildlife Sanctuary. |
| Gujjar Pastoralists | Sub-montane Kandi belt (Pathankot, Hoshiarpur, Rupnagar) | Traditional dairy herding and seasonal pastoralism along the Shivalik foothills. |
| Bazigar & Sansi Communities | Malwa & Doaba rural belts | Traditional itinerant acrobats, folk performers, and rural artisans (notified as Scheduled Castes/Denotified groups, not STs). |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Amritsar | **"Holy City of Sikhs"**; Sri Harmandir Sahib (Golden Temple), Akal Takht, Jallianwala Bagh, Partition Museum, Gobindgarh Fort, Attari–Wagah Border, and *Amritsari Kulcha*. |
| Ludhiana | **"Manchester of India"**; industrial giant for woolens, hosiery, and bicycles; **Punjab Agricultural University (PAU)** (cradle of India's Green Revolution). |
| Jalandhar | India's premier **sports goods** and hand-tools manufacturing hub; Devi Talab Mandir and nearby Jang-e-Azadi Memorial (Kartarpur). |
| Patiala | **"Royal City"**; Qila Mubarak, Sheesh Mahal, **Netaji Subhas National Institute of Sports (NIS)**, *Punjabi Jutti*, *Paranda*, and *Phulkari*. |
| Anandpur Sahib | Rupnagar district; **"Holy City of Bliss"** founded by Guru Tegh Bahadur; **Takht Sri Kesgarh Sahib** (birthplace of Khalsa), **Virasat-e-Khalsa**, and *Hola Mohalla*. |
| Bathinda | **"City of Lakes"**; **Qila Mubarak** (oldest surviving fort where Razia Sultan was imprisoned) and **Takht Sri Damdama Sahib** (Talwandi Sabo). |
| Kapurthala | **"Paris of Punjab"**; French-inspired Jagatjit Palace, Moorish Mosque, Rail Coach Factory, and Kanjli Wetland. |
| Fatehgarh Sahib (Sirhind) | Sacred site of the martyrdom of Guru Gobind Singh Ji's younger sons; Aam Khas Bagh and nearby **Sanghol** archaeological site. |
| Ferozepur | Houses **Hussainiwalia National Martyrs Memorial** (samadhis of Bhagat Singh, Rajguru, and Sukhdev) and historic Anglo-Sikh battlegrounds. |
| Gurdaspur & Pathankot | **Kalanaur** (where Emperor Akbar was crowned in 1556), Dera Baba Nanak (**Kartarpur Corridor**), and Ranjit Sagar Dam. |
| Mohali (SAS Nagar) | Modern IT and education hub adjoining Chandigarh; PCA International Cricket Stadium. |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Sri Harmandir Sahib (Golden Temple) & Akal Takht, Amritsar | Guru Arjan Dev (1588–1604) / Maharaja Ranjit Singh | Holiest Sikh shrine whose foundation was laid by Sufi saint Mian Mir; gold-plated in the 19th century; *Akal Takht* built in 1606 by Guru Hargobind. |
| Jallianwala Bagh, Amritsar | National Memorial (1919 Massacre) | Preserves bullet-riddled walls and the Martyrs' Well commemorating the April 13, 1919 tragedy under Brigadier-General Dyer. |
| Takht Sri Kesgarh Sahib & Virasat-e-Khalsa, Anandpur Sahib | Guru Gobind Singh (1699) / Moshe Safdie (2011) | Site where the **Khalsa Panth** was created; Virasat-e-Khalsa is an architectural museum chronicling 500 years of Sikh history. |
| Qila Mubarak, Bathinda | Origins c. 1st–12th c. CE (Kushana/Bhatti eras) | Cited as one of **India's oldest surviving forts**; Delhi Sultanate ruler **Razia Sultan** was imprisoned here in 1240 CE. |
| Qila Mubarak & Sheesh Mahal, Patiala | Maharaja Ala Singh (1763) / Narinder Singh (1847) | Iconic Sikh palace complex blending Rajput–Mughal elements; Sheesh Mahal showcases Kangra/Rajasthani frescoes and rare medals. |
| Sanghol Archaeological Site, Fatehgarh Sahib | Harappan to Kushana–Gupta Eras | Known as the **"Archaeological Storehouse of Punjab"**; famous for exquisitely carved **Kushana Buddhist stupa railings and pillars**. |
| Takht-i-Akbari, Kalanaur (Gurdaspur) | Bairam Khan (1556 CE) | Historic masonry platform where 13-year-old **Akbar was crowned Mughal Emperor** on February 14, 1556. |
| Takht Sri Damdama Sahib, Talwandi Sabo | Guru Gobind Singh Ji (1705–1706) | Fifth Sikh Takht ("Guru ki Kashi") where the Tenth Guru finalized the complete recension of Guru Granth Sahib. |
| Moorish Mosque & Jagatjit Palace, Kapurthala | Maharaja Jagatjit Singh (1908–1930) | Indo-Saracenic Moroccan-style mosque (modeled on Koutoubia Mosque, Marrakesh) and Versailles-inspired palace. |
| Durgiana Temple & Gobindgarh Fort, Amritsar | Harsai Mal Kapoor (1921) / Ranjit Singh | Durgiana Temple mirrors Golden Temple architecture; Gobindgarh Fort served as Maharaja Ranjit Singh's treasury and military stronghold. |
| Khatkar Kalan & Hussainiwala Memorials | National Martyrs Memorials | Ancestral home/museum of Shaheed Bhagat Singh (SBS Nagar) and cremation site of Bhagat Singh, Rajguru, and Sukhdev (Ferozepur). |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| None wholly within Punjab state boundaries | — | — *(Note: Joint capital **Chandigarh** [Union Territory] hosts *The Architectural Work of Le Corbusier*, inscribed in **2016**; **Thatheras of Jandiala Guru** brass/copper craft is on **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage [2014]**)* |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **GI Tags:** **Phulkari** (shared with Haryana and Rajasthan) and **Basmati Rice** (shared with 6 neighboring northern states/UTs).
- **National Parks:** **0 National Parks** (Punjab has no notified National Park).
- **Ramsar Sites (6):** **Harike Lake** (1990; Beas–Sutlej confluence, Indus river dolphins), **Kanjli Lake** (2002; Kali Bein), **Ropar Lake** (2002; Sutlej), **Beas Conservation Reserve** (2019; gharials & Indus river dolphins), **Keshopur-Miani Community Reserve** (2019; **India's first notified community reserve**), and **Nangal Wildlife Sanctuary** (2019).
- **Wildlife Sanctuaries:** **Abohar Wildlife Sanctuary** (Fazilka; blackbuck protection by Bishnois), Bir Moti Bagh, Bir Bhadson, Bir Dosanjh, Takhni-Rehmapur, Kathlaur Kushlian, and Jhajjar Bachauli.
- **State Symbols:** Animal — *Blackbuck*; Bird — *Northern Goshawk* (*Baaz*); Flower — *Gladiolus*; Tree — *Sheesham* (*Dalbergia sissoo*).
- **Demographic & Cultural Milestones:** **Zero Scheduled Tribes** and **highest percentage of Scheduled Caste (SC) population** in India (~31.94%, 2011 Census); **3 of the 5 Sikh Takhts** lie in Punjab (Akal Takht, Kesgarh Sahib, Damdama Sahib); drained today mainly by the **Sutlej, Beas, and Ravi** rivers.

### 21. Rajasthan

**Capital:** Jaipur ("Pink City")  
**Formation:** Integrated in 7 stages from March 18, 1948 (Matsya Union) to **March 30, 1949** (Greater Rajasthan — celebrated annually as **Rajasthan Diwas**); final integration completed on November 1, 1956 (States Reorganisation Act, merging Ajmer–Merwara and Mount Abu)  
**Official Language(s):** Hindi (official language under *Rajasthan Official Language Act, 1956*), English (additional official language); **Sindhi** (Eighth Schedule language widely spoken in urban belts); **Rajasthani** is not yet in the Eighth Schedule  
**Other Languages:** Rajasthani dialects — Marwari, Mewari, Dhundhari (Jaipuri), Hadoti, Shekhawati, Bagri, Mewati, Wagdi; Braj, Gujarati, Punjabi

#### Brief History
- **Prehistoric & Ancient Sites:** **Kalibangan** (Hanumangarh; Indus Valley site with ploughed field and fire altars), **Ahar–Banas** (Udaipur), **Ganeshwar** (Sikar copper culture), and **Bairat/Viratnagar** (Jaipur/Kotputli-Behror; Mauryan **Bhabru Ashokan Edict** and circular Buddhist temple).
- **Major Rajput & Regional Kingdoms:** **Gurjara–Pratiharas** (Mandore/Bhinmal); **Guhila–Sisodias of Mewar** (Bappa Rawal, **Rana Kumbha**, **Rana Sanga** [Battle of Khanwa, 1527], **Maharana Pratap** [Battle of Haldighati, 1576]); **Chauhans of Ajmer/Sambhar** (**Prithviraj Chauhan III** — Battles of Tarain, 1191 & 1192); **Rathores of Marwar/Bikaner** (Rao Jodha, Rao Bika, Veer Durgadas); **Kachwahas of Amber/Jaipur** (Raja Man Singh I, **Sawai Jai Singh II**); **Bhatis of Jaisalmer**; **Hadas of Bundi/Kota**; and **Jats of Bharatpur** (**Maharaja Surajmal**).
- **Revolt of 1857 & Freedom Struggle:** Uprisings erupted at **Nasirabad**, **Neemuch**, **Auwa** (Thakur Kushal Singh), and **Kota** (Lala Jaidayal and Mehrab Khan). Later landmarks include the **Bijolia Peasant Movement (1897–1941)** led by **Vijay Singh Pathik** (India's longest non-violent peasant movement), the **Mangarh Massacre (1913)** led by **Govind Guru** among Bhils, and **Praja Mandal** movements (Manikyalal Verma, Jai Narayan Vyas, Hiralal Shastri, Jamnalal Bajaj).

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Ghoomar | Folk | Official **State Dance of Rajasthan**; originally a Bhil ritual dance adopted by Rajput courts, performed by women in wide swirling *ghaghras* executing graceful circular spins. |
| Kalbelia | Folk | Sensuous dance of the snake-charmer community mimicking serpent movements to *Been/Pungi* and *Dafli* tunes; inscribed on **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage (2010)**; popularized globally by **Gulabo Sapera**. |
| Bhavai | Folk | Daring balancing dance where women balance 7–9 brass/earthen pots on their heads while dancing on sharp sword edges, glass shards, or brass *thali* rims. |
| Terah Taali | Folk | Devotional dance of the *Kamad* community honoring **Baba Ramdevji**, where seated women tie 13 *manjiras* (brass cymbals) across their limbs and strike them rhythmically. |
| Chari | Folk | Popular Gujjar women's dance of Kishangarh performed while balancing brass pots lit with burning cotton seeds on their heads. |
| Kachhi Ghodi | Folk | Lively Shekhawati wedding/martial dance in which men ride ornate dummy horses wielding mock swords to *Dhol* and *Bankia*. |
| Gair, Gavari & Neja | Folk | Vigorous circular stick (*Gair*), masked Shiva–Gauri ritual drama (*Gavari*), and post-Holi *Neja* dances of the Bhil tribe. |
| Chakri | Folk | Fast-paced spinning dance performed by women of the Kanjar tribe in the Hadoti (Kota–Bundi–Baran) region. |
| Bam & Agni (Fire) Dance | Folk | *Bam Rasiya* is the harvest dance of Bharatpur–Alwar using giant *Bam* drums; *Agni Nritya* involves barefoot dancing on glowing embers by the **Jasnathi** sect of Katariasar (Bikaner). |
| Kathak (Jaipur Gharana) | Classical | One of North India's three premier Kathak gharanas (alongside Lucknow and Banaras), celebrated for vigorous *chakkars* (spins), heroic *kavitas*, and intricate *tatkar* footwork. |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Folk Music & Traditions:** **Maand** (semi-classical royal ballad style famed for *"Kesariya Balam"* — legendary exponents **Allah Jilai Bai** and **Gavari Devi**), alongside hereditary desert musicians (**Manganiyars** and **Langas** of Barmer/Jaisalmer). Key instruments include **Ravanhattha** (GI Tagged 2025–26; ancient bowed spike-fiddle played by Bhopas), **Kamayacha**, **Sarangi**, **Algoza**, **Been/Pungi**, **Khartal**, **Bhapang** (Jahir Khan Mewati), **Morchang**, and **Nad**.
- **Painting Styles (Rajasthani Miniature Schools):** **Mewar** (Udaipur, Chavand, and **Nathdwara Pichhwai**), **Marwar** (Jodhpur, Bikaner, and **Kishangarh** — famed for **Bani Thani** painted by Nihal Chand, called the *"Indian Mona Lisa"*), **Hadoti** (Bundi and Kota hunting scenes), and **Dhundhar** (Amber, Jaipur, Alwar, and **Shekhawati** open-air Haveli frescoes).
- **Scroll & Wall Folk Art:** **Phad Painting** (Bhilwara/Shahpura; narrative cloth scrolls of folk deities *Pabuji* and *Devnarayan* sung by Bhopas) and **Mandana** (geometric chalk/geru floor and wall art).
- **Handicrafts (GI Tagged):** **Blue Pottery of Jaipur** (quartz-based glazed ceramic popularized by Kripal Singh Shekhawat), **Kota Doria**, **Sanganeri & Bagru Hand Block Printing**, **Thewa Art Work** (Pratapgarh; 23K gold filigree fused onto colored glass), **Nathdwara Pichhwai Painting**, **Bikaner Usta Kala** (gold embossing on camel hide), **Bikaner Kashidakari**, **Jodhpur Bandhej** (tie-dye) & **Mojari Craft of Jodhpur**, **Molela Clay Work** (Rajsamand terracotta plaques), **Pokaran Pottery**, **Udaipur Koftgari** (metal inlay on weapons), **Kathputli** (wooden string puppets), **Makrana Marble**, **Phulkari** (shared), and Jaipur **Meenakari/Kundan** jewelry.
- **Folk Theatre:** **Khyal** (Kuchamani, Shekhawati, Jaipuri), **Tamasha** (Jaipur), **Gavari** (Mewar Bhil folk drama), **Rammat** (Bikaner/Jaisalmer), and **Nautanki** (Bharatpur–Alwar).

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Chand Bardai | *Prithviraj Raso* (classic heroic epic on Prithviraj Chauhan) | Dingal / Braj |
| Meera Bai | *Meera Padavali*, *Narsi Ji Ro Mayro* (devotional Bhakti hymns) | Rajasthani / Braj |
| Padmanabha | *Kanhadade Prabandha* (1455 CE; epic on Jalore resistance) | Old Rajasthani / Maru-Gurjara |
| Prithviraj Rathore | *Veli Krisan Rukmani Ri* ("Fifth Veda" in Dingal tradition) | Dingal |
| Muhnot Nainsi | *Nainsi ri Khyat*, *Marwar ra Pargana ri Vigat* (called "Abul Fazl of Rajputana") | Marwari / Rajasthani |
| Suryamal Mishran | *Vansh Bhaskar*, *Veer Satsai* (State Poet of Bundi) | Dingal / Rajasthani |
| Kavi Raja Shyamaldas | *Vir Vinod* (monumental history of Mewar) | Hindi / Rajasthani |
| Kanhaiyalal Sethia | *Patal aur Pithal*, *Dharti Dhoran Ri*, *Leeltans* (Sahitya Akademi, Padma Shri) | Rajasthani / Hindi |
| Vijaydan Detha (*Bijji*) | *Batan ri Phulwari* (14-volume folktale collection), *Duvidha* (Padma Shri) | Rajasthani |
| Komal Kothari | Monumental documentation of Rajasthani folklore & desert music (Padma Bhushan) | Rajasthani / Hindi |
| Gaurishankar Hirachand Ojha | *History of Rajputana* (pioneering epigraphist and historian) | Hindi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Pushkar Fair (Kartik Mela) | Oct–Nov (Kartik Purnima) | World's largest camel and livestock fair held at Pushkar alongside sacred bathing in Pushkar Lake. |
| Gangaur | March–April (Chaitra) | 18-day festival where women worship *Gauri* (Parvati) and *Isar* (Shiva) for marital harmony and good husbands. |
| Teej (Hariyali & Kajli Teej) | July–August | Monsoon festival celebrating Parvati–Shiva reunion; *Hariyali Teej* is grand in Jaipur while *Kajli (Badi) Teej* is famed in Bundi. |
| Desert Festival (Maru Mahotsav) | Jan–Feb | Annual showcase at Sam sand dunes (Jaisalmer) featuring camel polo, turban-tying, folk dances, and *Mr. Desert* pageant. |
| Bikaner Camel Festival | January | Cultural fair honoring camels with decorated camel parades, dances, and *Agni* fire dance. |
| Elephant Festival & Mewar/Marwar Festivals | March & Oct | Jaipur's Holi/Elephant celebrations, Udaipur's *Mewar Festival* (spring), and Jodhpur's *Marwar Festival* (heroic ballads). |
| Urs of Ajmer Sharif | Islamic Month of Rajab | Six-day annual pilgrimage commemorating Sufi saint **Khwaja Moinuddin Chishti** at Ajmer. |
| Baneshwar Fair | Jan–Feb (Magha Purnima) | Held at the Mahi–Som–Jakham confluence in Dungarpur; widely called the **"Kumbh of Tribals / Bhils."** |
| Khatu Shyamji & Kaila Devi Fairs | Phalgun & Chaitra | Major devotional fairs in Sikar (Khatu Shyamji) and Karauli (Kaila Devi *Lakkhi* fair famed for *Languria* songs). |
| Other Major Fairs | Year-round | *Karni Mata Fair* (Deshnoke), *Sheetala Ashtami* (Chaksu), *Chandrabhaga Fair* (Jhalawar), *Gogamedi Fair* (Hanumangarh), *Brij Holi* (Bharatpur), and *Bundi Utsav*. |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
Rajasthan recognizes **12 Scheduled Tribes** (~13.48% of the state population); **Meena** and **Bhil** are the largest, while **Sahariya** is Rajasthan's **sole PVTG**.

| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Meena | Jaipur, Dausa, Sawai Madhopur, Karauli | Largest ST in Rajasthan; ancient *Matsya* lineage, *Meen Puran* lore, and high educational/administrative representation. |
| Bhil | Banswara, Dungarpur, Udaipur, Pratapgarh | Second-largest ST; famed archers (*Mahabharata* Ekalavya/Rana Punja legacy), *Baneshwar Fair*, *Gavari* theatre, *Gair* & *Ghoomar* dances. |
| Garasia | Sirohi (Abu Road), Pali, Udaipur | *Walar* (slow dance without instruments), *Mandal*, *Gaur* dances, and distinctive *Siyawa/Gour* marriage fairs. |
| Sahariya (PVTG) | Baran (Shahbad & Kishanganj) | Rajasthan's **only PVTG**; *Sitabari Fair* ("Kumbh of Sahariyas"), *Tejaji* worship, and *Lathmar* / *Indrapari* dances. |
| Damor (Damaria) | Dungarpur (Simalwara) & Banswara | *Chhadia* Holi custom and agricultural traditions along the Gujarat border. |
| Kathodi | Udaipur (Kotra, Jhadol) | Originated from Maharashtra (*Katkari*); traditional extractors of catechu (*Katha*) from Khair trees. |
| Kalbelia, Kanjar & Banjara/Gadia Lohar | Statewide / Hadoti & Thar | *Kalbelia* (UNESCO-listed snake-charmer dance); *Kanjar* (*Chakri* dance); *Gadia Lohar* (wandering blacksmiths claiming Chittorgarh lineage). |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Jaipur | Capital city; **"Pink City"** founded in 1727 by Sawai Jai Singh II; **UNESCO World Heritage City**; gems & jewelry, Blue Pottery, Sanganeri/Bagru prints, Jantar Mantar, and Hawa Mahal. |
| Udaipur | **"City of Lakes"** & **"Venice of the East"**; founded 1559 by Maharana Udai Singh II; Lake Pichola, City Palace, and nearby Zawar zinc–lead mines. |
| Jodhpur | **"Blue City"** & **"Sun City"**; founded 1459 by Rao Jodha; Mehrangarh Fort, Umaid Bhawan Palace, GI-tagged *Bandhej* and *Mojari* craft. |
| Jaisalmer | **"Golden City"**; yellow sandstone *Sonar Quila* (living fort), Thar Desert Sam dunes, and intricate *Patwon ki Haveli*. |
| Bikaner | Founded 1488 by Rao Bika; Junagarh Fort, *Bikaneri Bhujia* (GI Tagged), *Usta Kala* & *Kashidakari* art, and Deshnoke Karni Mata Temple. |
| Ajmer & Pushkar | **Ajmer Sharif Dargah** and Mayo College; Pushkar features India's most prominent **Brahma Temple** and the Pushkar Camel Fair. |
| Kota | **"Coaching Capital of India"**; Chambal River, GI-tagged **Kota Doria** fabric, Kota Stone, and Rawatbhata nuclear plant nearby. |
| Chittorgarh | Houses **Chittorgarh Fort** (India's largest fort), Vijay Stambh, Kirti Stambh, and legacies of Rani Padmini, Meera Bai, and Maharana Pratap. |
| Bharatpur | **Keoladeo Ghana National Park**, **Lohagarh Fort** ("Iron Fort" never captured by the British), and Deeg Palaces. |
| Alwar | **Sariska Tiger Reserve**, **Siliserh Lake** (Ramsar site), Bala Quila, Bhangarh Fort, and Neemrana Fort-Palace. |
| Bundi | **"City of Stepwells"** (*Baoris*), Taragarh Fort, *Chitrashala* miniatures, and *Kajli Teej*. |
| Mount Abu | Sirohi district; Rajasthan's only hill station, **Guru Shikhar** (highest Aravalli peak, 1,722 m), and **Dilwara Jain Temples**. |
| Nagaur & Makrana | **Nagaur** was where **Panchayati Raj was first launched in India (Oct 2, 1959)**; *Nagori Ashwagandha*; **Makrana** supplied white marble for the Taj Mahal. |
| Nathdwara | Rajsamand district; Shrinathji Temple, GI-tagged *Pichhwai* paintings, and *Vishwas Swaroopam* (world's tallest Shiva statue, 369 ft). |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Six Hill Forts (Chittorgarh, Kumbhalgarh, Ranthambore, Gagron, Amber, Jaisalmer) | Maurya/Sisodia/Chauhan/Kachwaha/Bhati Eras | Serial UNESCO site; **Chittorgarh** is India's largest fort; **Kumbhalgarh's 36-km wall** is the world's second-longest continuous wall; **Gagron** is a *Jal Durg* (water fort); **Jaisalmer** is a living fort. |
| Hawa Mahal, Jaipur | Maharaja Sawai Pratap Singh (1799 CE) | Five-storeyed pink sandstone *Palace of Winds* designed by Lal Chand Ustad with **953 *jharokhas*** (latticed windows). |
| Jantar Mantar, Jaipur | Maharaja Sawai Jai Singh II (1734 CE) | Largest of 5 astronomical observatories built by Jai Singh II; houses 19 instruments including *Vrihat Samrat Yantra* (world's largest stone sundial). |
| Mehrangarh Fort & Umaid Bhawan Palace, Jodhpur | Rao Jodha (1459) / Maharaja Umaid Singh (1943) | Cliff-top fortress overlooking the Blue City and one of the world's largest private heritage palaces. |
| Vijay Stambh & Kirti Stambh, Chittorgarh | Rana Kumbha (1440–48 CE) / Bagherwal Jain Merchant | **Vijay Stambh** (9-storey Victory Tower) commemorates victory over Mahmud Khilji of Malwa; **Kirti Stambh** is dedicated to Jain Tirthankara Adinath. |
| Dilwara Jain Temples, Mount Abu | Vimal Shah (1031 CE) & Vastupal–Tejpal (1230 CE) | Unmatched white marble carvings (*Vimal Vasahi* and *Luna Vasahi*) built under Solanki/Chalukya patronage. |
| Ranakpur Chaumukha Jain Temple, Pali | Dharna Shah / Rana Kumbha Era (15th c. CE) | Four-faced marble temple famous for **1,444 uniquely carved pillars** with no two pillars identical. |
| Chand Baori, Abhaneri (Dausa) | King Chanda, Nikumbha Dynasty (8th–9th c. CE) | One of India's deepest and largest stepwells featuring 3,500 symmetrical narrow steps across 13 storeys. |
| Ajmer Sharif Dargah & Adhai Din Ka Jhonpra | Sultan Ghiyasuddin / Qutb-ud-din Aibak (1199 CE) | Revered Sufi shrine of Khwaja Moinuddin Chishti; *Adhai Din Ka Jhonpra* was converted from a Sanskrit college (*Saraswati Kanthabharana*). |
| City Palace & Lake Palace, Udaipur | Maharana Udai Singh II & Jagat Singh II (16th–18th c.) | Largest palace complex in Rajasthan and floating marble *Jag Niwas* palace on Lake Pichola. |
| Junagarh Fort, Bikaner | Raja Rai Singh (1589–94 CE) | Rare plains fort never conquered by invaders; blends Rajput, Mughal, and Gujarati stonework. |
| Jaigarh Fort & Albert Hall Museum, Jaipur | Sawai Jai Singh II (1726) / Samuel Swinton Jacob (1887) | Jaigarh houses **Jaivana** (world's largest wheeled cannon); Albert Hall is an Indo-Saracenic architectural icon. |
| Brahma Temple (Pushkar) & Karni Mata Temple (Deshnoke) | Ancient / Maharaja Ganga Singh | Pushkar houses one of the world's very few ancient Brahma temples; Deshnoke is famed as the **"Temple of Rats"** (*Kabas*). |
| Kalibangan (Hanumangarh) | Indus Valley Civilization (c. 3000–1750 BCE) | Harappan site famous for earliest evidence of a **ploughed agricultural field**, terracotta plough, and seven fire altars. |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites (if any)
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| Keoladeo National Park (Bharatpur) | Natural | 1985 |
| The Jantar Mantar, Jaipur | Cultural | 2010 |
| Hill Forts of Rajasthan (Chittorgarh, Kumbhalgarh, Ranthambore, Gagron, Amber, Jaisalmer) | Cultural | 2013 |
| Jaipur City, Rajasthan | Cultural | 2019 |

*(Note: **Kalbelia folk songs and dances of Rajasthan** were also inscribed on **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage in 2010**.)*

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **20 GI Tags:** *Kota Doria*, *Blue Pottery of Jaipur*, *Molela Clay Work*, *Kathputlis of Rajasthan*, *Sanganeri Hand Block Print*, *Bagru Hand Block Print*, *Bikaneri Bhujia*, *Phulkari* (shared), *Thewa Art Work*, *Makrana Marble*, *Pokaran Pottery*, *Sojat Mehndi*, *Nathdwara Pichhwai Painting*, *Udaipur Koftgari Metal Craft*, *Bikaner Kashidakari Craft*, *Jodhpur Bandhej Craft*, *Bikaner Usta Kala Craft*, *Nagori Ashwagandha*, *Mojari Craft of Jodhpur*, and *Rajasthan Ravan Hattha*.
- **National Parks (5):** **Ranthambore National Park** (Sawai Madhopur), **Sariska National Park** (Alwar), **Keoladeo Ghana National Park** (Bharatpur; UNESCO site), **Desert National Park** (Jaisalmer/Barmer; Great Indian Bustard), and **Mukundara Hills (Darrah) National Park** (Kota/Bundi).
- **Tiger Reserves (5):** **Ranthambore**, **Sariska**, **Mukundara Hills**, **Ramgarh Vishdhari** (Bundi), and **Dholpur–Karauli** (Rajasthan's 5th Tiger Reserve).
- **Ramsar Sites (5):** **Keoladeo National Park** (1981 — India's 1st alongside Chilika, placed on the Montreux Record), **Sambhar Lake** (1990 — India's largest inland saline lake), **Khichan Wetland** (Phalodi; wintering Demoiselle Cranes/*Kurjaan*, added 2025), **Menar Wetland Complex** (Udaipur; "Bird Village", added 2025), and **Siliserh Lake** (Alwar; India's 96th Ramsar site, added December 2025).
- **State Symbols:** Animal — *Chinkara* (wildlife) & *Camel* (state livestock animal); Bird — *Great Indian Bustard* (*Godawan*); Flower — *Rohida* (*Tecomella undulata*); Tree — *Khejri* (*Prosopis cineraria*).
- **Historic & Geographic Firsts:** **Largest state of India by area** (342,239 sq km); **First state to inaugurate Panchayati Raj** (**Nagaur**, October 2, 1959); **Khejarli Movement (1730)** led by **Amrita Devi Bishnoi** (363 villagers sacrificed lives hugging Khejri trees — precursor to Chipko); **Pokhran** (Jaisalmer) hosted India's 1974 (*Smiling Buddha*) and 1998 (*Operation Shakti*) nuclear tests.
