### 22. Sikkim

**Capital:** Gangtok  
**Formation:** May 16, 1975 (Associate State via 35th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1974; 22nd State of India via 36th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1975; special provisions under Article 371F)  
**Official Language(s):** English, Nepali (8th Schedule), Sikkimese (Bhutia), and Lepcha *(8 additional languages—Gurung, Limbu, Magar, Mukhia/Sunuwar, Newari, Rai, Sherpa, and Tamang—are recognized as additional state languages for cultural preservation)*  
**Other Languages:** Tibetan, Hindi, Limbu, Tamang, Sherpa, Rai, Gurung, Magar, Newari

#### Brief History
- Earliest known inhabitants were the indigenous **Lepchas** (*Rongpa*), followed by Limbus and migrating Bhutias from Tibet.
- **Blood-Brotherhood Treaty (13th–14th century):** Signed at **Kabi Lungchok** between Lepcha chief Thekong Tek and Bhutia chief Khye Bumsa, cementing ethnic unity.
- **Namgyal (Chogyal) Dynasty (1642–1975):** Founded in **1642** when **Phuntsog Namgyal** was consecrated as the first *Chogyal* (Dharma King) at **Yuksom** by three revered Tibetan lamas (Lhatsun Chenpo, Sempa Chenpo, Rigzin Chenpo).
- **18th–19th Century Conflicts & Treaties:** Faced Bhutanese and Gorkha invasions; the **Treaty of Titalia (1817)** restored territories to Sikkim under British protection; **Darjeeling** was ceded to the British in **1835**; the **Treaty of Tumlong (1861)** made Sikkim a British protectorate; the **Anglo-Chinese Convention (1890)** demarcated the Sikkim–Tibet border.
- **Integration with India:** Became an Indian protectorate under the **Indo-Sikkim Treaty (1950)**; popular democratic movement led by **Kazi Lhendup Dorjee** and the Sikkim State Congress culminated in a 1975 referendum abolishing the Chogyal monarchy and making Sikkim the **22nd State of India on May 16, 1975**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Singhi Chham | Folk | Traditional Bhutia snow-lion mask dance symbolizing the mythical snow lion of Mt. Khangchendzonga |
| Yak Chham | Folk | Bhutia dance honoring the yak, vital to high-altitude pastoral life in northern Sikkim |
| Lu Khangthamo | Folk | Bhutia thanksgiving folk dance performed during housewarmings and festivals |
| Kagyed Dance | Folk (Monastic) | Masked monastic Cham dance performed in December before Losoong to ward off evil spirits |
| Chu Faat | Folk | Lepcha sacred dance performed on Pang Lhabsol in praise of Mt. Khangchendzonga and glacial rivers |
| Zo-Mal-Lok | Folk | Traditional Lepcha agricultural dance depicting paddy sowing, harvesting, and merriment |
| Maruni | Folk | Vibrant Nepali community dance accompanied by *Naumati Baja* (nine traditional instruments) during Tihar and weddings |
| Tamang Selo | Folk | Energetic Tamang dance performed to the rhythmic beat of the *Damphu* frame drum |
| Dhan Naach | Folk | Traditional Limbu harvest and courtship dance accompanied by *Palam* folk songs |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Painting Styles:** **Thangka Painting**—intricate Tibetan Buddhist scroll paintings on cotton or silk depicting mandalas, Buddhas, and bodhisattvas using mineral pigments.
- **Handicrafts & GI Tags:**
  - **Dalle Khursani (GI Tag):** Famous fiery round red cherry chilli native to Sikkim and adjoining hills.
  - **Sikkim Large Cardamom (GI Tag):** Aromatic black cardamom (*Amomum subulatum*); Sikkim is India's largest producer.
  - **Temi Tea (GI Tag):** Organic Himalayan tea produced at **Temi Tea Garden** (Sikkim's sole tea estate, est. 1969 in Namchi district).
  - **Traditional Crafts:** Bhutia **hand-woven woolen carpets (*Den*)** with dragon and cloud motifs, **Choktse** (carved wooden folding tables), **Lepcha bamboo and cane basketry**, **handwoven Lepcha textiles (*Thokro-Dum* and *Dumdyam*)**, and handmade **Argeli bark paper**.
- **Musical Instruments:** *Tungbuk* (Lepcha string lute), *Puntong Palit* (bamboo flute), *Damphu* (Tamang frame drum), *Madal*, *Sarangi*, *Naumati Baja*, *Gyaling* (monastic oboe), and *Dungchen* (long Tibetan brass horn).

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Sanu Lama (Gadul Singh Lama) | *Mrigatrishna* (Sahitya Akademi 1993), *Jaha Bagchha Teesta Rangeet* | Nepali |
| Birkha Bahadur Muringla | Pioneering works in Limbu literature and grammar (Padma Shri 2017) | Limbu / Nepali |
| Shiv Kumar Rai | *Khahare* (Sahitya Akademi Award), *Frontier* | Nepali |
| Prajwal Parajuly | *The Gurkha's Daughter*, *Land Where I Flee* | English |
| Chetan Raj Shrestha | *The King's Harvest*, *The Light of His Clan* | English |
| Yishey Doma Bhutia | *Legends of the Lepchas* | English |
| *Namthar* & *Rong* Manuscripts | Sacred Buddhist biographies and Lepcha *Rong* script chronicles | Sikkimese / Lepcha |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Pang Lhabsol | August–September | Unique Sikkimese festival worshipping **Mt. Khangchendzonga** as the guardian deity; features warrior dances (*Pangtoed Chham*) |
| Losoong / Namsoong | December | Sikkimese (Bhutia/Lepcha) New Year celebrating the end of the harvest season |
| Losar | February–March | Tibetan Buddhist New Year marked by prayer flags, Cham dances, and *Guthuk* soup |
| Saga Dawa | May–June (Full Moon) | Triple-blessed Buddhist festival commemorating Buddha's birth, enlightenment, and Mahaparinirvana |
| Bumchu | February–March | Held at **Tashiding Monastery**; a sacred vase of holy water is opened to foretell the year's prosperity |
| Tendong Lho Rum Faat | August | Ancient Lepcha festival thanking Mt. Tendong for saving their ancestors from a great deluge |
| Sakewa | April–May | Nine-day Rai nature-worship festival seeking bountiful harvests |
| Chasok Tongnam | December | Limbu harvest festival offering newly harvested grains to ancestral spirits and deities |
| Sonam Lhochar & Tamu Lhochar | January–February / December | New Year festivals of the Tamang and Gurung communities respectively |
| Dasain & Tihar | October–November | Major Nepali Hindu festivals corresponding to Dussehra and Diwali |

#### Tribes
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Lepcha (*Rongpa*) | Dzongu (Mangan/North Sikkim) and across Sikkim | Indigenous inhabitants ("children of the snowy peak"); own **Rong script**, bamboo/cane crafts, *Tendong Lho Rum Faat*, and *Chu Faat* dance |
| Bhutia (*Denzongpa*) | North, East, and West Sikkim | Descendants of Tibetan migrants; known for monastic Buddhism, Thangka art, carpet weaving, *Pang Lhabsol*, and *Losoong* |
| Limbu (Subba) | West and South Sikkim | Kirati hill tribe with own **Sirijonga script**, *Dhan Naach*, *Palam* songs, and *Chasok Tongnam* festival |
| Tamang | East, South, and West Sikkim | Known for *Tamang Selo* music with *Damphu* drum and *Sonam Lhochar* festival |
| Sherpa | High-altitude northern and western valleys | Renowned Himalayan mountaineers; celebrate *Losar* and *Mani Rimdu* |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Gangtok | State capital; MG Marg, Enchey Monastery, Namgyal Institute of Tibetology, Do Drul Chorten, gateway to Nathu La |
| Yuksom | First capital of Sikkim (1642); gateway to Khangchendzonga treks, Norbugang Coronation Throne, Dubdi Monastery |
| Pelling | Panoramic views of Mt. Khangchendzonga, Pemayangtse Monastery, Skywalk, Rabdentse Ruins, Khecheopalri Lake |
| Namchi | Cultural hub of South Sikkim; **Siddheshwar Dham (Char Dham)** replica complex and 135-ft Guru Padmasambhava statue at Samdruptse |
| Ravangla | **Buddha Park (Tathagata Tsal)** featuring a 130-ft Shakyamuni Buddha statue and Ralang Monastery |
| Lachung & Lachen | Twin northern Himalayan villages; gateways to **Yumthang Valley (Valley of Flowers)** and **Gurudongmar Lake** |
| Mangan | "Cardamom Capital" of Sikkim; gateway to the protected **Dzongu** Lepcha reserve |
| Pakyong | Location of **Pakyong Airport** (Sikkim's first airport, opened 2018) |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Dubdi Monastery (Yuksom) | Lhatsun Chenpo / 1701 CE | **Oldest monastery in Sikkim**, built shortly after the coronation of the first Chogyal |
| Tashiding Monastery | Ngadak Sempa Chenpo / 1641–1717 CE | Most sacred Nyingma monastery in Sikkim; hosts the **Bumchu** festival |
| Pemayangtse Monastery (Pelling) | Lhatsun Chenpo / 1705 CE | Premier Nyingma monastery; houses the seven-tiered wooden sculpture *Sangthokpalri* |
| Rumtek Monastery (Dharma Chakra Centre) | 16th Karmapa (Rangjung Rigpe Dorje) / 1960s | Largest monastery in Sikkim and seat of the Karma Kagyu lineage in exile |
| Enchey Monastery (Gangtok) | Built 1909 under Chogyal Sidkeong Tulku | Sacred Nyingma monastery blessed by Lama Druptob Karpo; hosts annual Cham dances |
| Norbugang Coronation Throne (Yuksom) | 1642 CE | Stone throne where Phuntsog Namgyal was crowned the first Chogyal of Sikkim |
| Rabdentse Ruins | 1670–1814 CE | Ruins of the **second capital** of the Kingdom of Sikkim (destroyed during Gorkha invasions) |
| Kirateshwar Mahadev Temple (Legship) | Ancient / Rebuilt modern | Sacred Shiva temple on the banks of the Rangeet River associated with the Mahabharata Kirata episode |
| Siddheshwar Dham (Char Dham, Namchi) | Government of Sikkim / 2011 | Replica pilgrimage complex of India's four Dhams and 12 Jyotirlingas |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| Khangchendzonga National Park | Mixed (Cultural & Natural) | 2016 |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **First 100% Organic State:** Sikkim became the **first fully organic state in the world** in January 2016.
- **Only Mixed UNESCO Site in India:** **Khangchendzonga National Park** (2016) is India's sole Mixed World Heritage Site and includes **Mt. Kangchenjunga (8,586 m)**—India's highest peak and the world's third highest.
- **First Ramsar Site of Sikkim:** **Khecheopalri Wetland** (West Sikkim) was designated as Sikkim's **first Ramsar Wetland site in February 2025**.
- **Protected Areas:** 1 National Park (**Khangchendzonga NP**, also a UNESCO Biosphere Reserve) and 7+ Wildlife Sanctuaries including **Fambong Lho**, **Kyongnosla Alpine**, **Maenam**, **Shingba Rhododendron**, **Barsey Rhododendron**, **Pangolakha**, and **Kitam Bird Sanctuary**.
- **Demographics:** **Least populous state of India** (Census 2011: ~6.10 lakh); also became the **first Open Defecation Free (ODF) state** in India.
- **Border & Passes:** Shares international borders with **3 countries** (Nepal, China/Tibet, Bhutan) and only **1 Indian state** (West Bengal); **Nathu La** and **Jelep La** connect Sikkim with Tibet.
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Red Panda**; State Bird: **Blood Pheasant**; State Tree: **Rhododendron**; State Flower: **Noble Orchid (*Dendrobium nobile*)**.
- **GI Tags:** **Sikkim Large Cardamom**, **Dalle Khursani**, and **Temi Tea**.

### 23. Tamil Nadu

**Capital:** Chennai (formerly Madras)  
**Formation:** November 1, 1956 (as Madras State under States Reorganisation Act, 1956; renamed **Tamil Nadu** on **January 14, 1969** via the Madras State Alteration of Name Act, 1968)  
**Official Language(s):** Tamil (Primary Official Language; 8th Schedule; **first Classical Language of India**, 2004) and English (Additional Official Language)  
**Other Languages:** Telugu, Kannada, Malayalam, Urdu, Saurashtra, Badaga, Toda, Kota

#### Brief History
- Cradle of **Sangam Age (c. 300 BCE – 300 CE)** ruled by the **Muvendar** (Three Crowned Kings): **Cheras** (Karur/Vanchi), **Cholas** (Uraiyur/Puhar), and **Pandyas** (Madurai/Korkai).
- **Pallavas of Kanchipuram (3rd–9th century CE):** Mahendravarman I and Narasimhavarman I pioneered Dravidian rock-cut and structural temple architecture at Mahabalipuram and Kanchipuram.
- **Imperial Cholas (9th–13th century CE):** Vijayalaya, Rajaraja I, and Rajendra I ("Gangaikonda") built a maritime empire extending across Sri Lanka and Southeast Asia (Srivijaya) and constructed the **Great Living Chola Temples**.
- **Later Pandyas, Vijayanagara & Nayaks (14th–18th century CE):** Thirumalai Nayak of Madurai and Gingee/Thanjavur Nayaks erected monumental temple *gopurams* and palaces.
- **Marathas of Thanjavur & Gingee:** Chhatrapati Shivaji captured Gingee Fort (1677); Serfoji II built the **Saraswathi Mahal Library** in Thanjavur.
- **Colonial & Freedom Struggle:** British established **Fort St. George (1639)**; **Polygar Wars** led by **Veerapandiya Kattabomman**, **Puli Thevar**, and **Velu Nachiyar** (first Indian queen to fight the British); **Vellore Mutiny (July 10, 1806)**—India's first major sepoy uprising; **V.O. Chidambaram Pillai** (Kappalottiya Thamizhan), **Subramania Bharati**, **Vanchinathan**, **Tiruppur Kumaran**, and **C. Rajagopalachari** (Vedaranyam Salt Satyagraha, 1930).

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Bharatanatyam | Classical | One of India's oldest classical dances, evolved from temple *Sadir Attam*; codified in *Natya Shastra* & *Abhinaya Darpana*; exponents: **Rukmini Devi Arundale, Balasaraswati, Padma Subrahmanyam, Alarmel Valli** |
| Karagattam | Folk | Balancing decorated brass/earthen pots (*karagam*) on the head in praise of Rain Goddess Mariamman |
| Kavadi Attam | Folk / Devotional | Carrying decorated semi-circular wooden/peacock-feathered arches (*kavadi*) to Lord Murugan shrines during Thaipusam |
| Kummi & Kolattam | Folk | Women's circular clapping dance (*Kummi*) and rhythmic stick dance (*Kolattam*) during Pongal and village fairs |
| Poikkal Kudirai Attam | Folk | "Dummy Horse" dance where performers wear a painted hollow horse figure around the waist |
| Mayil Attam & Puliyattam | Folk | Peacock costume dance (*Mayil Attam*) and body-painted Tiger dance (*Puliyattam*) |
| Oyilattam & Devarattam | Folk | Graceful scarf-waving dance of Madurai/Tirunelveli (*Oyilattam*) and warrior-community festival dance (*Devarattam*) |
| Therukoothu | Folk Theatre/Dance | All-night rural street dance-drama enacting Mahabharata and Ramayana epics |
| Silambattam | Folk Martial Art | Ancient Dravidian bamboo-staff martial art and rhythmic performance |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Carnatic Classical Music:** Tamil Nadu is the heartland of Carnatic music; **Tiruvarur** is the birthplace of the **Trinity of Carnatic Music**—**Tyagaraja, Muthuswami Dikshitar, and Syama Sastri**; ancient Tamil music traditions include **Panniru Thirumurai (Tevaram)** and **Thiruppugazh**; instruments: *Nadaswaram*, *Thavil*, *Saraswati Veena*, *Mridangam*, and *Parai*.
- **Folk Theatre & Ballads:** **Villu Paattu** (bow-song narrative), **Bommalattam** (string/leather puppetry), **Parai Attam**.
- **Painting Styles:** **Thanjavur (Tanjore) Painting (GI Tag)**—rich iconographic panel paintings adorned with 22-karat gold foil and inlaid semi-precious stones; **Sittanavasal Murals** (Pandya-era Jain frescoes); **Karuppur Kalamkari Paintings (GI Tag)**.
- **Sculpture & Handicrafts (Major GI Tags):**
  - **Chola Bronze Sculptures (Swamimalai Bronze Icons – GI):** Crafted using the ancient lost-wax (*Madhuchhishta Vidhana*) method, famed for the cosmic **Nataraja**.
  - **Thanjavur Veena (GI):** First musical instrument in India to receive a GI tag.
  - **Thanjavur Art Plate (GI), Nachiyar Koil Kuthuvilakku (brass lamp – GI), Mahabalipuram Stone Sculpture (GI), Chettinad Athangudi Tiles (GI), Toda Embroidery / Pukhoor (GI), Pathamadai Pai (korai grass mats – GI), Nagercoil Temple Jewellery (GI), Vilachery Clay Toys (GI), Ambasamudram Wooden Toys (GI)**.
  - **Handlooms (GI):** **Kancheepuram Silk**, **Arani Silk**, **Salem Silk/Fabric**, **Kovai Kora Cotton**, **Madurai Sungudi**, **Bhavani Jamakkalam**, **Negamam Cotton**, **Woraiyur Cotton Sarees**, **Thirubuvanam Silk**.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Tolkappiyar | *Tolkappiyam* (earliest extant Tamil grammar and poetics) | Tamil |
| Thiruvalluvar | *Thirukkural* (1,330 couplets on virtue, wealth, and love) | Tamil |
| Ilango Adigal | *Silappatikaram* (first of the Five Great Epics) | Tamil |
| Sithalai Sattanar | *Manimekalai* (Buddhist sequel epic) | Tamil |
| Andal & Nayanmars / Alvars | *Tiruppavai*, *Nalayira Divya Prabandham*, *Tevaram* | Tamil |
| Kamban | *Kamba Ramayanam* (*Ramavataram*) | Tamil |
| Sekkizhar | *Periya Puranam* (lives of 63 Nayanmar saints) | Tamil |
| Subramania Bharati | *Panchali Sabatham*, *Kuyil Pattu*, *Kannan Pattu* | Tamil |
| Bharathidasan | *Pandian Parisu*, *Kudumba Vilakku*, *Azhagin Sirippu* | Tamil |
| Kalki Krishnamurthy | *Ponniyin Selvan*, *Sivagamiyin Sabatham*, *Parthiban Kanavu* | Tamil |
| Akilan & Jayakanthan | *Chittirappavai* (Akilan, Jnanpith 1975); *Sila Nerangalil Sila Manithargal* (Jayakanthan, Jnanpith 2002) | Tamil |
| R.K. Narayan | *Swami and Friends*, *The Guide*, *Malgudi Days* (born in Madras) | English |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Pongal (Thai Pongal) | January 14–17 | Four-day harvest festival (*Bhogi, Surya Pongal, Mattu Pongal, Kaanum Pongal*); accompanied by **Jallikattu** bull-taming sport |
| Puthandu (Tamil New Year) | April 14 (Chithirai 1) | Celebrated with *Mukkani* (mango, jackfruit, banana) and *Kani* viewing |
| Thaipusam | January–February | Lord Murugan festival celebrated at Palani and Tiruchendur with *Kavadi* processions |
| Chithirai Thiruvizha | April–May | Grand 12-day festival at **Meenakshi Amman Temple, Madurai** celebrating the celestial wedding of Meenakshi and Sundareswarar |
| Karthigai Deepam | November–December | Festival of lamps; famous for the giant **Maha Deepam** lit atop Arunachala Hill at Tiruvannamalai |
| Natyanjali Dance Festival | February–March (Maha Shivaratri) | Classical dance festival held at **Nataraja Temple, Chidambaram** |
| Margazhi Music & Dance Season | December–January | World's largest annual Carnatic music and Bharatanatyam festival in Chennai |
| Aadi Perukku | July–August | River-worship festival celebrating monsoon water rise along the **Kaveri** |
| Velankanni Feast & Nagore Kanduri | August–September / Annual | Major Christian pilgrimage at Basilica of Our Lady of Good Health and Sufi Urs at Nagore Dargah |

#### Tribes
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Toda (PVTG) | Nilgiri Hills (Ooty/Coonoor) | Pastoral buffalo-herders living in barrel-vaulted **Mund** huts; famous for **Toda Embroidery (*Pukhoor*, GI)** and *Modhweth* festival |
| Kota (PVTG) | Nilgiri Hills (Kotagiri) | Traditional artisans, potters, blacksmiths, and musicians; celebrate *Kambatrayan* festival |
| Kurumba (PVTG) | Nilgiri slopes & forested hills | Skilled honey-gatherers, traditional healers, and mural/forest folklore artists |
| Irula (PVTG) | Nilgiris, Chengalpattu, Villupuram | Renowned snake-catchers and venom-extractors (Irula Snake Catchers Cooperative); medicinal herb knowledge |
| Kattunayakan (PVTG) | Nilgiris & Mudumalai forests | Traditional forest-dwellers and honey collectors known as "Kings of the Jungle" |
| Paniyan (PVTG) | Gudalur (Nilgiris) | Skilled agriculturalists and bamboo weavers |
| Malayali | Eastern Ghats (Yelagiri, Jawadhu, Shervaroy, Kolli Hills) | **Largest Scheduled Tribe in Tamil Nadu** (>50% of state ST population); hill agriculturalists distinct from Kerala Malayalis |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Chennai | State capital; "Detroit of India" (automobile hub), Marina Beach (2nd longest urban beach in world), Fort St. George, Carnatic music capital |
| Madurai | "Athens of the East" & "City of Junctions"; Meenakshi Temple, **Madurai Malli (Jasmine – GI)**, Sungudi sarees, **Keeladi** archaeological site |
| Thanjavur | "Rice Bowl of Tamil Nadu"; Chola capital, Brihadisvara Temple, Tanjore Paintings, Thanjavur Veena, Saraswathi Mahal Library |
| Kanchipuram | "City of Thousand Temples" & **Silk City**; Pallava capital, Kailasanathar & Ekambareswarar temples |
| Coimbatore | "Manchester of South India"; textile machinery, wet grinders (GI), engineering industries, Adiyogi Shiva statue |
| Tiruchirappalli (Trichy) | Rockfort Temple, **Srirangam Ranganathaswamy Temple** (largest functioning Hindu temple complex), BHEL, NIT |
| Salem | Steel plant (SAIL), mangoes, silver anklets, **Salem Fabric**, Yercaud hill station |
| Tiruppur | "Knitwear / Dollar City of India"; major cotton hosiery export hub |
| Thoothukudi (Tuticorin) | "Pearl City"; V.O. Chidambaranar Port, salt pans, **Kovilpatti Kadalai Mittai (GI)** |
| Kanyakumari | Southernmost tip of mainland India (confluence of Arabian Sea, Indian Ocean, Bay of Bengal); Vivekananda Rock Memorial, 133-ft Thiruvalluvar Statue |
| Rameswaram | Pamban Island; **Ramanathaswamy Temple** (Char Dham & Jyotirlinga), birthplace of **Dr. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam**, Dhanushkodi |
| Udhagamandalam (Ooty) | "Queen of Hill Stations" in the Nilgiris; Nilgiri Mountain Railway, Botanical Garden, Doddabetta Peak |
| Vellore | Vellore Fort (1806 Mutiny), CMC Vellore, Golden Temple (Sripuram), leather industry |
| Sivakasi | "Little Japan"; India's hub for fireworks, safety matches, and printing |
| Erode | "Turmeric City" (**Erode Manjal – GI**), handloom textiles |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Shore Temple & Pancha Rathas (Mahabalipuram) | Pallava King Narasimhavarman I & II / 7th–8th c. CE | Monolithic rock-cut *rathas*, **Descent of the Ganges / Arjuna's Penance** bas-relief, structural Shore Temple |
| Brihadisvara Temple (Thanjavur) | Rajaraja Chola I / 1010 CE | Pinnacle of Chola Dravidian architecture; 66-m *vimana* topped by a single 80-ton granite capstone |
| Gangaikondacholapuram Temple | Rajendra Chola I / 1035 CE | Built to commemorate Chola northern conquest up to the Ganga |
| Airavatesvara Temple (Darasuram) | Rajaraja Chola II / 12th c. CE | Famed for miniature *karana* stone reliefs and stone chariot-wheeled mandapam |
| Meenakshi Amman Temple (Madurai) | Pandya / Expanded by Nayaks (16th–17th c. CE) | Towering sculpted *gopurams*, Hall of Thousand Pillars, Golden Lotus Tank |
| Sri Ranganathaswamy Temple (Srirangam) | Cholas, Pandyas, Hoysalas, Vijayanagara / 9th–17th c. | Largest functioning Hindu temple complex (156 acres, 21 gopurams, 7 concentric enclosures) |
| Ramanathaswamy Temple (Rameswaram) | Pandyas & Sethupathi rulers / 12th–17th c. | Famous for the **longest temple corridor in the world** (1,212 pillars); one of 12 Jyotirlingas |
| Gingee Fort (Senji Fort, Villupuram) | Cholas / Nayaks / Marathas (Shivaji 1677) | Known as the **"Troy of the East"**; built across three hillocks (Rajagiri, Krishnagiri, Chakkilidurg) |
| Kailasanathar Temple (Kanchipuram) | Pallava King Rajasimha / c. 700 CE | Oldest surviving structural sandstone temple in Kanchipuram |
| Nataraja Temple (Chidambaram) | Cholas & Pandyas / 10th–13th c. CE | Revered as *Akasha Lingam* (space element) among Pancha Bhoota Sthalams |
| Vivekananda Rock Memorial & Thiruvalluvar Statue | Eknath Ranade (1970) / V. Ganapati Sthapati (2000) | Located off Kanyakumari; 133-ft Thiruvalluvar statue represents 133 chapters of *Thirukkural* |
| Fort St. George (Chennai) | British East India Company / 1639–44 | First English fortress in India; houses Tamil Nadu Secretariat & Museum |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| Group of Monuments at Mahabalipuram | Cultural | 1984 |
| Great Living Chola Temples (Brihadisvara at Thanjavur, Gangaikondacholapuram, Airavatesvara at Darasuram) | Cultural | 1987 (extended 2004) |
| Mountain Railways of India — Nilgiri Mountain Railway | Cultural | 2005 |
| Western Ghats (Anamalai, Mudumalai, Mukurthi, Kalakad-Mundanthurai clusters) | Natural | 2012 |
| Maratha Military Landscapes of India — **Gingee Fort** (sole site outside Maharashtra) | Cultural | 2025 |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Highest Ramsar Sites in India:** Tamil Nadu leads India with **20 Ramsar Wetlands** (including Point Calimere, Pichavaram Mangroves, Pallikaranai Marsh, Gulf of Mannar, Kazhuveli, Nanjarayan, Sakkarakottai, Therthangal, etc.).
- **National Parks (5) & Tiger Reserves (5):** NPs: **Guindy**, **Gulf of Mannar Marine**, **Indira Gandhi (Anamalai)**, **Mudumalai**, **Mukurthi**; Tiger Reserves: **Kalakad-Mundanthurai**, **Anamalai**, **Mudumalai**, **Sathyamangalam**, and **Srivilliputhur-Megamalai** (51st TR of India).
- **Biosphere Reserves (3):** **Nilgiri** (**India's first Biosphere Reserve**, 1986), **Gulf of Mannar**, and **Agasthyamalai**.
- **Top GI Tag State:** Ranks alongside Uttar Pradesh at the top nationally, with GI tags including **Palani Panchamirtham**, **Dindigul Locks**, **Kandangi Saree**, **Kumbakonam Betel Leaf**, **Madurai Malli**, **Erode Turmeric**, **Kancheepuram Silk**, **Vilachery Clay Toys**, **Thovalai Garland**, and **Panruti Jackfruit**.
- **Archaeology & Science:** **Keeladi** (Sangam-era urban civilization on Vaigai river), **Adichanallur** (Iron Age urn burials); **Kalpakkam** & **Kudankulam** Nuclear Power Plants; **ISRO Propulsion Complex (Mahendragiri)** & **Kulasekarapattinam** new spaceport.
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Nilgiri Tahr**; State Bird: **Emerald Dove**; State Tree: **Palmyra Palm**; State Flower: **Glory Lily (*Gloriosa superba*)**.

---

### 24. Telangana

**Capital:** Hyderabad *(sole capital of Telangana since June 2, 2024 after the 10-year joint capital period with Andhra Pradesh ended)*  
**Formation:** June 2, 2014 (carved out of Andhra Pradesh under the Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2014)  
**Official Language(s):** Telugu (Primary Official Language; 8th Schedule; **Classical Language**, 2008) and Urdu (Second Official Language; 8th Schedule)  
**Other Languages:** Hindi, Lambadi/Banjara, Gondi, Marathi, Kannada, English

#### Brief History
- Ancient settlements at **Kotilingala** (first capital of the **Satavahanas**, c. 2nd c. BCE), followed by Ikshvakus, Vishnukundinas, and Western Chalukyas of Kalyani.
- **Kakatiya Dynasty (12th–14th century CE):** Capital at **Orugallu (Warangal)**; illustrious rulers include **Rudrama Devi** (one of India's greatest women monarchs), Ganapati Deva, and Prataparudra II; famous for tank-irrigation chains and **Koh-i-Noor** diamond mining near Kollur/Golconda.
- **Bahmani & Qutb Shahi Dynasties (1518–1687):** **Muhammad Quli Qutb Shah** founded **Hyderabad** and built the **Charminar (1591)**; Golconda became the world's diamond trade capital.
- **Asaf Jahi Dynasty / Nizams of Hyderabad (1724–1948):** Founded by Nizam-ul-Mulk; **Telangana Armed Struggle (1946–51)** led by peasants against feudal *Jagirdari* and Razakar oppression (icons: **Chakali Ilamma**, **Doddi Komaraiah**); **Komaram Bheem** coined *"Jal, Jangal, Zameen"* fighting the Nizam.
- **Operation Polo (September 13–17, 1948):** Police action integrated Hyderabad State into the Indian Union; merged with Andhra State in 1956 before achieving separate statehood on **June 2, 2014** following prolonged agitation.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Perini Sivatandavam | Classical / Ancient Warrior Dance | Dynamic male temple dance of the **Kakatiya era** dedicated to Lord Shiva, performed before warriors marched to battle; revived by **Nataraja Ramakrishna** |
| Gussadi Dance | Folk / Tribal | Vibrant Gond tribe dance performed during Diwali/Dandari festival wearing peacock-feather headgear (*Gussadi*) |
| Oggu Katha | Folk Dance-Ballad | Traditional ballad-dance performed by *Oggu* priests singing praises of Mallanna, Beerappa, and Yellamma |
| Bathukamma Dance | Folk | Women singing and clapping in concentric circles around floral *Bathukamma* stacks |
| Lambadi / Banjara Dance | Folk / Tribal | Colorful women's dance performed in mirror-embroidered skirts (*lehenga*) and ivory/plastic bangles |
| Kommu Koya Dance | Folk / Tribal | Koya tribal dance where men wear bison-horn headdresses and play conical brass horns |
| Chindu Bhagavatam | Folk Theatre/Dance | Yakshagana-style folk theatre performed by the Chindu community |
| Dappu Nrityam | Folk | Energetic rhythmic dance performed to circular frame drums (*Dappu*) |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Painting Styles:**
  - **Cheriyal Scroll Paintings (GI Tag):** Narrative storytelling scrolls (*Nakashi* art) painted on khadi cloth with natural pigments in bright red backgrounds.
  - **Nirmal Paintings (GI Tag):** Gold-and-black traditional paintings on wood inspired by Ajanta, Mughal, and Pahari styles.
- **Handicrafts & GI Tags (15+ GI Tags):**
  - **Textiles (GI):** **Pochampally Ikat** (double-ikat geometric weave), **Gadwal Sarees** (silk-cotton with zari), **Narayanpet Handloom Sarees**, **Siddipet Gollabhama Sarees**, **Telia Rumal** (oil-treated cotton double-ikat), **Warangal Durries** (cotton rugs).
  - **Metal & Wood Crafts (GI):** **Pembarthi Metal Craft** (sheet-brass embossing), **Adilabad Dokra** (ancient lost-wax tribal bell-metal craft by Woj community), **Silver Filigree of Karimnagar**, **Nirmal Toys and Crafts** (lightweight *poniki* wood), **Nirmal Furniture**, **Hyderabad Lac Bangles** (*Laad Bazaar*).
  - **Food & Agriculture (GI):** **Hyderabad Haleem** (first non-vegetarian dish in India to get a GI tag), **Tandur Red Gram**.
- **Music:** Rich Carnatic and Hindustani traditions, **Qawwali** and Deccani *Ghazal* traditions in Hyderabad, folk songs (*Janapada Geethalu*, *Oggu* ballads, and Telangana movement songs).

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Bammera Pothana | *Andhra Maha Bhagavatam* (transcreation of *Bhagavata Purana*) | Telugu |
| Palkuriki Somanatha | *Basava Puranam*, *Panditaradhya Charitra* | Telugu / Kannada / Sanskrit |
| Gona Budda Reddy | *Ranganatha Ramayanam* (first Ramayana in Telugu) | Telugu |
| Muhammad Quli Qutb Shah | *Kulliyat-e-Quli Qutb Shah* (first *Sahib-e-Diwan* Urdu poet) | Deccani Urdu / Telugu / Persian |
| Kaloji Narayana Rao | *Naa Godava* (People's Poet; Sept 9 observed as Telangana Language Day) | Telugu |
| Dasarathi Krishnamacharyulu | *Agni Dhara*, *Rudraveena*, *Timiram Tho Samaram* | Telugu |
| C. Narayana Reddy (Cinare) | *Viswambhara* (Jnanpith Award 1988) | Telugu |
| P.V. Narasimha Rao | *The Insider*; translated *Veyi Padagalu* into Hindi as *Sahasraphan* | English / Telugu / Hindi |
| Samala Sadasiva | *Swaralayalu* (Sahitya Akademi 2011) | Telugu |
| Makhdoom Mohiuddin | *Bisat-e-Raqs* (Urdu revolutionary poet) | Urdu |
| Ande Sri | *Jaya Jaya He Telangana* (Official State Song of Telangana) | Telugu |
| Goreti Venkanna | *Vallanki Taalam* (Sahitya Akademi Award 2021) | Telugu |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Bathukamma | September–October (9 days ending on Durgashtami) | Official **State Festival**; women arrange seasonal wildflowers (*Tangedu, Gunugu*) in conical stacks worshipping Goddess Gauri |
| Bonalu | July–August (Ashadha) | Official **State Festival** in Hyderabad/Secunderabad; devotees offer *Bonam* (cooked rice/jaggery pots) to Goddess Mahankali |
| Sammakka Sarakka (Medaram) Jatara | February (Biennial, Magha Purnima) | Held at Medaram (Mulugu); **Asia's largest tribal congregation** honoring Koya goddesses Sammakka and Saralamma who fought Kakatiya taxation |
| Nagoba Jatara | January–February (Pushya Amavasya) | Second-largest tribal festival in Telangana, celebrated by Mesram clan of **Gonds** at Keslapur (Adilabad) |
| Edupayala Jatara | February–March (Maha Shivaratri) | Major fair at Durga Bhavani Temple, Medak where Manjeera river splits into seven streams |
| Peerla Panduga | Muharram | Syncretic festival symbolizing communal harmony in rural Telangana |

#### Tribes
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Lambada / Banjara (Sugali) | Across Telangana (Nalgonda, Mahabubabad, Adilabad) | **Largest Scheduled Tribe in Telangana**; distinct mirror-work dress, *Teej* & *Seetala Bhavani* festivals |
| Koya | Mulugu, Bhadradri Kothagudem, Khammam | Guardians of **Medaram Jatara**; *Kommu Koya* bison-horn dance |
| Gond & Pradhan | Adilabad, Komaram Bheem Asifabad | Worshippers of *Persapen*; **Nagoba Jatara**, *Gussadi* dance; Pradhans are traditional Gond bards playing *Kikri* fiddle |
| Chenchu (PVTG) | Nallamala Forests (Nagarkurnool / Amrabad) | Forest honey-gatherers and bow-and-arrow hunters; worshipers of Mallikarjuna |
| Kolam (PVTG) | Adilabad, Asifabad | Distinct Kolami language, bamboo basketry, and forest deity rituals |
| Thoti & Kondareddis (PVTGs) | Adilabad & Khammam-Godavari belt | Thotis are traditional Gond genealogists/tattooing artisans; Kondareddis practice *podu* shifting cultivation |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Hyderabad | Capital; "City of Pearls" & "Cyberabad" (IT/Pharma/Genome Valley), Charminar, Golconda, Ramoji Film City, Salar Jung Museum |
| Warangal | Ancient Kakatiya capital (*Orugallu*); Thousand Pillar Temple, Warangal Fort, NIT Warangal, **Warangal Durries (GI)** |
| Bhoodan Pochampally | Famous for **Pochampally Ikat (GI)**; birthplace of Vinoba Bhave's **Bhoodan Movement (1951)**; UNWTO Best Tourism Village (2021) |
| Karimnagar | Granites, **Silver Filigree (GI)**, Lower Manair Dam, Elgandal Fort |
| Nirmal | **Nirmal Wooden Toys & Paintings (GI)** |
| Bhadrachalam | Major pilgrimage town on the Godavari banks; Sri Rama Temple associated with Bhakta Ramadasu |
| Ramagundam | Major coal and power hub (NTPC super thermal plant, Singareni Collieries) |
| Nizamabad | Turmeric & rice hub, Nizam Sagar Dam |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Kakatiya Rudreshwara (Ramappa) Temple (Palampet) | Recharla Rudra (under Ganapati Deva) / 1213 CE | Built using **sandbox foundation** and **lightweight floating bricks**; intricate Perini dance sculptures |
| Thousand Pillar Temple (Hanamkonda) | Kakatiya King Rudra Deva / 1163 CE | Star-shaped (*trikuta*) temple dedicated to Shiva, Vishnu, and Surya; massive monolithic Nandi |
| Warangal Fort & Kakatiya Kala Thoranam | Ganapati Deva & Rudrama Devi / 13th c. CE | Iconic four ornamental stone gateways (*Kala Thoranam*) featured in the **Telangana State Emblem** |
| Charminar (Hyderabad) | Muhammad Quli Qutb Shah / 1591 CE | Iconic four-minaret monument built to mark the founding of Hyderabad and end of a plague |
| Golconda Fort | Kakatiyas (mud fort) / Qutb Shahis (16th c.) | Famous for acoustic architecture (clap at Fateh Darwaza heard 1 km up at Bala Hisar) and Koh-i-Noor history |
| Chowmahalla Palace & Falaknuma Palace | Asaf Jahi Nizams / 18th–19th c. | Grand royal palaces showcasing Mughal, Rajasthani, and European architecture |
| Mecca Masjid (Hyderabad) | Qutb Shahis & Aurangzeb / 1614–1693 | One of India's largest mosques; bricks made from soil brought from Mecca |
| Alampur Navabrahma Temples & Jogulamba Temple | Badami Chalukyas / 7th–9th c. CE | Early Nagara-style sandstone temples; Jogulamba is one of the 18 *Ashtadasha Shakti Peethas* |
| Yadadri (Yadagirigutta) Temple | Ancient / Rebuilt 2022 | Major hill temple of Sri Lakshmi Narasimha Swamy built entirely in black granite (*Krishna Shila*) |
| Medak Cathedral | Rev. Charles Walker Posnett / 1924 | One of the largest Gothic Revival churches in Asia |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| Kakatiya Rudreshwara (Ramappa) Temple, Telangana | Cultural | 2021 (39th UNESCO site of India) |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **National Parks (3) & Tiger Reserves (2):** NPs: **Kasu Brahmananda Reddy (KBR)**, **Mahaveer Harina Vanasthali**, **Mrugavani**; Tiger Reserves: **Amrabad** (Nallamala) and **Kawal**.
- **Important Lakes & Projects:** **Hussain Sagar** (separates Hyderabad & Secunderabad, monolithic Buddha statue), **Nagarjuna Sagar Dam** (Krishna river), **Kaleshwaram Lift Irrigation Project** (Godavari), **Ramagundam Floating Solar Power Plant** (India's largest floating solar project).
- **Museums & Heritage:** **Salar Jung Museum** (Hyderabad — one of India's three National Museums, houses *Veiled Rebecca*), **Chowmahalla Palace**, **Golconda**.
- **Bhoodan Movement (1951):** Launched by Acharya Vinoba Bhave at **Pochampally** on April 18, 1951.
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Spotted Deer (*Jinka*)**; State Bird: **Indian Roller (*Palapitta*)**; State Tree: **Jammi Chettu (*Prosopis cineraria*)**; State Flower: **Tangedu (*Senna auriculata*)**.
- **GI Tags:** **Pochampally Ikat, Cheriyal Paintings, Nirmal Toys/Paintings/Furniture, Pembarthi Metal Craft, Adilabad Dokra, Silver Filigree of Karimnagar, Gadwal Sarees, Narayanpet Sarees, Siddipet Gollabhama Sarees, Telia Rumal, Warangal Durries, Hyderabad Haleem, Tandur Red Gram, Hyderabad Lac Bangles**.

### 25. Tripura

**Capital:** Agartala  
**Formation:** Merged with India on October 15, 1949; became a Union Territory on November 1, 1956; attained full Statehood on **January 21, 1972** (under the North-Eastern Areas (Reorganisation) Act, 1971)  
**Official Language(s):** Bengali (8th Schedule), Kokborok, and English  
**Other Languages:** Chakma, Mog, Halam, Manipuri, Garo, Bishnupriya Manipuri, Hindi

#### Brief History
- Ancient **Twipra Kingdom** ruled for centuries by the **Manikya Dynasty**, chronicled in the **Rajmala** (one of India's oldest royal chronicles).
- **Maharaja Dhanya Manikya (1490–1515):** Built the **Tripura Sundari Temple (Matabari)** in 1501 CE and expanded borders up to Chittagong and Sylhet.
- **Maharaja Bir Chandra Manikya (1862–1896):** Modernized administration, abolished slavery/sati, and recognized young **Rabindranath Tagore**, inspiring Tagore's *Rajarshi*, *Visarjan*, and *Mukut*.
- **Maharaja Bir Bikram Kishore Manikya (1923–1947):** Architect of modern Agartala; planned urban Agartala, built **Neermahal**, and founded MBB College.
- **Integration & Tribal Struggles:** Witnessed the **Reang Rebellion (1942–43)** led by Ratanmani Noatia against feudal exploitation; Regent Maharani **Kanchan Prabha Devi** signed the **Instrument of Merger** on September 9, 1949 (effective Oct 15, 1949).

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Hojagiri | Folk / Tribal | Iconic **Reang (Bru)** women's dance performed while balancing on an earthen pitcher (*kalasi*) with an earthen lamp/bottle on the head |
| Garia Dance | Folk / Tribal | Celebratory dance by Tripuri and Reang youth around a bamboo pole symbolizing Lord Garia during Garia Puja |
| Lebang Boomani | Folk / Tribal | Tripuri dance depicting the catching of colorful *lebang* insects with bamboo clappers before the monsoon |
| Mamita Dance | Folk / Tribal | Post-harvest dance of the Tripuri/Kaloi communities celebrating *jhum* cultivation |
| Bizhu Dance | Folk / Tribal | Chakma celebratory dance performed during the three-day *Biju* New Year festival |
| Sangrai Dance | Folk / Tribal | Mog tribe dance during Sangrai (April) involving water-sprinkling and joyous youth processions |
| Hai-Hak Dance | Folk / Tribal | Halam community harvest thanksgiving dance |
| Dhamail | Folk | Traditional Bengali women's rhythmic clapping and singing circle dance during weddings |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Bamboo & Cane Crafts:** Tripura is nationally renowned for fine **bamboo and cane handicrafts**—split-bamboo screens (*shitalpati* mats), room dividers, furniture, baskets, and decorative lampshades.
- **Handwoven Textiles & GI Tags:**
  - **Risa (GI Tag):** Traditional handwoven Tripuri breast-cloth, headgear, and ceremonial stole adorned with geometric woven motifs.
  - **Rignai & Pachra (GI Tag):** Traditional Tripuri women's wraparound lower garment (*Rignai*) and upper shawl (*Pachra*).
  - **Tripura Queen Pineapple (GI Tag):** Golden, intensely sweet aromatic pineapple declared State Fruit.
  - **Matabari Pera (GI Tag):** Sacred milk-and-sugar sweet offered as *prasad* at the Tripura Sundari Temple (Matabari).
- **Musical Instruments & Sculpture:** *Sumui* (bamboo flute), *Sarinda* (bowed lute), *Chongpreng*, *Kham* (drum), *Dangdu*; ancient Shaivite **rock-cut bas-reliefs at Unakoti** and **Pilak** Buddhist-Hindu sculptures.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Royal Chroniclers (under Dharma Manikya I) | *Rajmala* (royal history of Tripura kings in Bengali verse) | Bengali |
| Thakur Radhamohan Debbarma | *Kokborok Grammar* & *Traipur Bhashabhidhan* (1900) | Kokborok / Bengali |
| Sudhanwa Debbarma | *Hachuk Khuri* (first Kokborok novel) | Kokborok |
| Chandrakanta Murasingh | *Haping Giringo Chibuksa Ringo*, translated *Gitanjali* (Sahitya Akademi Bhasha Samman 1996) | Kokborok |
| Nanda Kumar Debbarma | Modern Kokborok poetry and plays | Kokborok |
| Sachin Dev Burman (S.D. Burman) | Born into Tripura's royal family; legendary composer blending Tripura-Bengal folk music into Indian cinema | Bengali / Hindi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Kharchi Puja | July–August (Ashadha) | Week-long royal festival at **Chaturdasha Devata Temple** (Old Agartala) worshipping 14 deities |
| Garia Puja | April (Boishakh) | Seven-day indigenous **Tripuri & Reang** New Year festival praying to Lord Garia for prosperity and good harvest |
| Ker Puja | July–August (2 weeks after Kharchi) | Traditional protective ritual where a town's boundaries are sealed for 2.5 days under royal customs |
| Ashokastami & Unakoti Mela | March–April | Grand pilgrimage fair at the rock carvings of **Unakoti** |
| Poush Sankranti (Tirthamukh Mela) | January 14 | Sacred bathing fair at **Tirthamukh**, origin of the Gomati River |
| Biju (Bizhu) & Sangrai | April | Traditional New Year festivals of the **Chakma** and **Mog** tribes |
| Neermahal Water Festival | December & August | Boat races, swimming, and cultural festivities at **Rudrasagar Lake** |

#### Tribes
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Tripuri (Debbarma) | Across Tripura (TTAADC hill areas) | **Largest Scheduled Tribe in Tripura**; Kokborok language, *Risa-Rignai* weaving, *Garia* and *Kharchi* pujas |
| Reang (Bru) (PVTG) | North Tripura, Dhalai, Gomati | **Sole Particularly Vulnerable Tribal Group (PVTG) in Tripura**; renowned for **Hojagiri dance** and *Buisu* festival |
| Jamatia | South Tripura & Gomati | Tightly organized village governance (*Hoda*); *Garia Puja* |
| Chakma | Dhalai, North & South Tripura | Theravada Buddhists; *Biju* festival, traditional backstrap loinloom weaving |
| Mog | South Tripura & Sabroom | Arakanese-origin Buddhists; *Sangrai* water festival |
| Halam, Noatia, Lushai, Garo, Kuki | Hill tracts of Tripura | Distinct dialects, bamboo crafts, *Hai-Hak* (Halam) and *Wangala* (Garo) dances |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Agartala | State capital; Ujjayanta Palace, India–Bangladesh **Akhaura Integrated Check Post & Rail Link**, Maharaja Bir Bikram Airport |
| Udaipur (formerly Rangamati) | "City of Lakes"; **Tripura Sundari Temple (Matabari)**, **Tropic of Cancer** passes directly through Udaipur |
| Kailashahar | Historic town near the ancient **Unakoti** rock-cut pilgrimage site |
| Melaghar / Sonamura | **Rudrasagar Lake** (Ramsar site) and **Neermahal** Water Palace |
| Sabroom | Southernmost town; **Maitri Setu** over the **Feni River** connecting Tripura to Bangladesh (Chattogram port access) |
| Dharmanagar & Ambassa | Major commercial and rail hubs of North Tripura and Dhalai |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Tripura Sundari Temple (Matabari, Udaipur) | Maharaja Dhanya Manikya / 1501 CE | One of the **51 Shakti Peethas**; built in tortoise shape (*Kurmapitha*); famed for GI-tagged *Matabari Pera* |
| Ujjayanta Palace (Agartala) | Maharaja Radha Kishore Manikya / 1901 CE | Neo-classical palace designed by Sir Alexander Martin; now **Tripura State Museum** |
| Neermahal (Melaghar) | Maharaja Bir Bikram Kishore Manikya / 1930 CE | Built in the middle of **Rudrasagar Lake**; one of only two water palaces in India (alongside Jal Mahal) |
| Unakoti Rock Carvings (Kailashahar) | 7th–9th century CE | Giant Shaivite rock-cut reliefs (*Unakotiswara Kal Bhairava*); name means "one less than a crore"; on **UNESCO Tentative List** |
| Chaturdasha Devata Temple (Old Agartala) | Maharaja Krishna Manikya / 18th c. CE | Houses 14 bronze deity heads worshipped during **Kharchi Puja** |
| Pilak Archaeological Site (Jolaibari) | 8th–12th century CE | Rare syncretic site with Hindu and Buddhist terracotta plaques and stone sculptures |
| Boxanagar Buddhist Stupa | 8th–9th century CE | Brick stupa and monastery ruins showcasing early Buddhism in Tripura |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| *None inscribed yet* *(Unakoti Rock-cut Sculptures are on the UNESCO Tentative List)* | — | — |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Geography & International Border:** Surrounded by **Bangladesh on three sides (north, west, south)** with an 856-km international border ("Zero Line"); **Tropic of Cancer** passes through **Udaipur**.
- **Ramsar Site (1):** **Rudrasagar Lake** (famous for **Neermahal** and the endangered three-striped roof turtle).
- **National Parks (2):** **Clouded Leopard National Park** (inside Sepahijala Wildlife Sanctuary) and **Bison (Rajbari) National Park** (inside Trishna Wildlife Sanctuary); **Gumti Wildlife Sanctuary** (Dumboor Lake) and **Rowa WLS**.
- **GI Tags (4):** **Tripura Queen Pineapple**, **Risa**, **Rignai & Pachra**, and **Matabari Pera**.
- **Connectivity Landmark:** **Maitri Setu** (1.9 km bridge over the **Feni River** at Sabroom) and **Agartala–Akhaura cross-border railway** linking Northeast India to Bangladesh.
- **Sports Icon:** **Dipa Karmakar** (Agartala), first Indian female gymnast to compete in the Olympics (4th in Rio 2016 Vault).
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Phayre's Leaf Monkey (Phayre's Langur)**; State Bird: **Green Imperial Pigeon**; State Tree: **Agarwood (*Aquilaria malaccensis*)**; State Flower: **Nageswar (*Mesua ferrea*)**.

### 26. Uttar Pradesh

**Capital:** Lucknow  
**Formation:** January 24, 1950 (United Provinces renamed Uttar Pradesh; Uttarakhand carved out on November 9, 2000)  
**Official Language(s):** Hindi (Primary Official Language; 8th Schedule) and Urdu (Second Official Language since 1989; 8th Schedule)  
**Other Languages:** Awadhi, Bhojpuri, Braj Bhasha, Bundeli, Khariboli, Kannauji, Bagheli, Sanskrit, English

#### Brief History
- Heart of ancient **Aryavarta** and **Madhyadesha**, home to **8 of the 16 Mahajanapadas** (Kashi, Kosala, Vatsa, Panchala, Malla, Shurasena, Kuru, Chedi) and IVC easternmost site **Alamgirpur** (Meerut) & **Sanauli** (Baghpat).
- **Buddhist & Jain Centres:** Lord Buddha delivered his first sermon (*Dhammacakkappavattana*) at **Sarnath** and attained *Mahaparinirvana* at **Kushinagar**; Shravasti, Kaushambi, and Ayodhya are major Jain/Buddhist/Hindu centres.
- **Empires:** Mauryas (Ashokan pillars at Sarnath, Kaushambi, Prayagraj), Guptas (brick temple at Bhitargaon, Dashavatara temple at Deogarh), **Harshavardhana** (capital at **Kannauj**), Gurjara-Pratiharas, Gahadavalas, **Sharqi Sultanate** of Jaunpur ("Shiraz of India"), and **Mughals** (Agra and Fatehpur Sikri served as imperial capitals).
- **Nawabs of Awadh (1722–1856):** Founded by Saadat Khan; Asaf-ud-Daula shifted capital to **Lucknow**, fostering Awadhi culture and Ganga-Jamuni Tehzeeb.
- **Freedom Struggle:** Epicenter of the **Revolt of 1857**—triggered at **Meerut (May 10, 1857)**, led by **Rani Lakshmibai** (Jhansi), **Begum Hazrat Mahal** (Lucknow), **Nana Sahib & Tatya Tope** (Kanpur), **Kunwar Singh** (Ballia/eastern UP), and **Maulvi Ahmadullah Shah** (Faizabad); **Chauri Chaura incident (Feb 1922, Gorakhpur)**; **Kakori Train Action (Aug 1925)**; **Chandrashekhar Azad** martyrdom at Alfred Park, Prayagraj (1931).

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Kathak | Classical | Only classical dance of North India; evolved from temple storytellers (*Kathakars*) and flourished at Mughal/Awadh courts (**Lucknow & Banaras Gharanas**); legends: **Birju Maharaj, Shambhu Maharaj, Lachhu Maharaj, Sitara Devi** |
| Charkula | Folk | Iconic **Braj** dance where women balance a multi-tiered pyramid of **108 lighted oil lamps** on their heads |
| Raslila & Mayur Nritya | Folk | Braj devotional dance dramas depicting Radha–Krishna episodes and peacock dance |
| Nautanki | Folk Theatre/Dance | Vibrant traditional musical folk drama of Kanpur, Hathras, and Lucknow |
| Kajri & Jhoola | Folk | Monsoon and swing-song dances popular in Mirzapur, Varanasi, and Braj |
| Karma Dance | Folk / Tribal | Performed by **Gond and Kharwar** tribes of Sonbhadra and Mirzapur around a sacred *Karam* tree branch |
| Pai Danda & Rai | Folk | Stick dance (*Pai Danda*) and fast-paced festive dance (*Rai*) of the **Bundelkhand** region |
| Dhobia & Kaharuwa | Folk | Purvanchal community dances performed at weddings and auspicious ceremonies |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Hindustani Classical Gharanas:**
  - **Banaras Gharana:** **Ustad Bismillah Khan** (Shehnai), **Pt. Ravi Shankar** (Sitar), **Girija Devi** (Thumri), **Pt. Kishan Maharaj** & **Samta Prasad** (Tabla), **Chhannulal Mishra**.
  - **Lucknow Gharana:** **Begum Akhtar** (Ghazal/Dadra/Thumri).
  - **Agra Gharana** (Ustad Faiyaz Khan), **Kirana Gharana** (Abdul Karim Khan, Bhimsen Joshi), **Rampur-Sahaswan Gharana** (Ustad Rashid Khan).
- **Folk Music:** **Kajri** (Mirzapur), **Alha** (Bundelkhand heroic ballad), **Birha** (Purvanchal), **Rasiya** (Braj), **Sohar** (childbirth songs), **Chaiti & Hori**.
- **Painting Styles:** **Braj Sanjhi Art** (intricate temple paper-stencil & floral-rangoli art), **Mughal Miniature Painting** (Agra/Fatehpur Sikri ateliers), **Awadh/Lucknow Miniatures**, **Chauk Purna** & **Kohbar** folk wall art.
- **Handicrafts & GI Tags (Highest GI Tag State in India):**
  - **Textiles & Embroidery:** **Banaras Brocades and Sarees**, **Lucknow Chikan Craft (Chikankari)**, **Lucknow Zardozi**, **Varanasi Soft Stone Jali & Zardozi**, **Mirzapur & Bhadohi Handmade Carpets**, **Mau Saree**, **Gorakhpur / Mubarakpur Sarees**.
  - **Metal, Glass & Pottery:** **Moradabad Metal Craft (Brass City)**, **Firozabad Glassware & Bangles**, **Khurja Pottery**, **Nizamabad Black Pottery**, **Chunar Glaze Pottery**, **Gorakhpur Terracotta**, **Aligarh Locks**.
  - **Wood, Stone & Aromatics:** **Agra Stone Inlay (*Pietra Dura*)** & **Agra Leather Footwear**, **Saharanpur Wood Carving**, **Kannauj Perfume / Ittar**, **Varanasi Wooden Lacquerware & Toys**, **Banaras Gulabi Meenakari** (pink enamel on gold/silver), **Banda Shajar Stone Craft**, **Kalpi Handmade Paper**, **Amroha Dholak**, **Mainpuri Tarkashi** (brass-wire inlay on wood).
  - **Food & Agro:** **Malihabadi Dusseheri Mango**, **Kalanamak Rice** (Buddha's gift, Siddharthnagar), **Agra Petha**, **Banaras Langda Mango & Banarasi Paan**, **Allahabad Surkha Guava**, **Muzaffarnagar Jaggery**, **Hathras Hing**.

| Sulahkul Festival | Annual (February) | Festival of Hindu–Muslim unity in **Agra**, rooted in Akbar's policy of universal peace |
| Bateshwar Fair | Kartik (Oct–Nov) | Ancient cattle and religious fair on the Yamuna banks near Agra |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Taj Mahal (Agra) | Shah Jahan (architect Ustad Ahmad Lahauri) / 1632–1653 | Ivory-white Makrana marble mausoleum with *pietra dura* inlay; **Wonder of the World** |
| Agra Fort | Akbar / 1565–1573 (additions by Shah Jahan) | Red sandstone imperial citadel; Jahangiri Mahal, Khas Mahal, Moti Masjid |
| Fatehpur Sikri | Akbar / 1571–1585 | Mughal capital featuring **Buland Darwaza** (highest gateway in India), **Panch Mahal**, **Tomb of Salim Chishti**, Ibadat Khana |
| Ancient Buddhist Site of Sarnath | Ashoka (3rd c. BCE) & Guptas (5th c. CE) | **Dhamek Stupa**, **Chaukhandi Stupa**, Mulagandhakuti Vihara, and site of the **Ashoka Lion Capital** (National Emblem) |
| Bara Imambara & Rumi Darwaza (Lucknow) | Nawab Asaf-ud-Daula / 1784 CE | Famous for the **Bhulbhulaiya** labyrinth and massive unsupported vaulted central hall |
| Shri Ram Janmabhoomi Mandir (Ayodhya) | Consecrated Jan 22, 2024 (architect Chandrakant Sompura) | Built in traditional **Nagara style** using Bansi Paharpur pink sandstone without iron/steel |
| Kashi Vishwanath Temple & Corridor (Varanasi) | Ahilyabai Holkar (1780) / Corridor expanded 2021 | One of the **12 Jyotirlingas** on the banks of the Ganga |
| Akbar's Tomb (Sikandra) & Itimad-ud-Daulah's Tomb | Jahangir (1613) / Nur Jahan (1628) | Itimad-ud-Daulah ("Baby Taj") was India's first marble tomb using extensive *pietra dura* |
| Dashavatara Temple (Deogarh, Lalitpur) | Gupta Period / c. 500 CE | Earliest surviving stone **Nagara & Panchayatana** style temple in North India |
| Bhitargaon Brick Temple (Kanpur Dehat) | Gupta Period / 5th c. CE | Oldest surviving brick temple with a tall *shikhara* in India |
| Atala Mosque & Shahi Jama Masjid (Jaunpur) | Ibrahim Shah Sharqi / 1408 CE | Finest examples of **Sharqi architecture** featuring colossal propylon screens |
| Mahaparinirvana Temple (Kushinagar) | Gupta Period / 5th c. CE | Houses a 6.1-m reclining sandstone Buddha statue |
| Jhansi Fort & Kalinjar Fort (Banda) | Bir Singh Deo Bundela (1613) / Chandela dynasty | Historic Bundelkhand citadels tied to Rani Lakshmibai and Sher Shah Suri |

| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| Agra Fort | Cultural | 1983 |
| Taj Mahal | Cultural | 1983 |
| Fatehpur Sikri | Cultural | 1986 |
| Ancient Buddhist Site of Sarnath | Cultural | 2026 (45th UNESCO site of India) |

*(Note: **Kumbh Mela** was inscribed on UNESCO's Representative List of the **Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity** in **2017**).*

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Top GI Tag State & 2nd Highest Ramsar State:** Uttar Pradesh holds the **highest number of GI tags in India** and the **second-highest number of Ramsar Sites (14)** (including Upper Ganga River, Haiderpur, Bakhira, Nawabganj, Sur Sarovar, **Jai Prakash Narayan Bird Sanctuary / Surha Tal** [India's 100th Ramsar site, 2026], **Patna Bird Sanctuary**, **Shekha Jheel**, and **Sauj Jheel**).
- **National Parks & Tiger Reserves:** **1 National Park** (**Dudhwa NP**, Lakhimpur Kheri) and **4 Tiger Reserves** (**Dudhwa**, **Pilibhit**, **Amangarh** [Corbett buffer], and **Ranipur Tiger Reserve** in Chitrakoot—India's 53rd TR); major sanctuaries include **National Chambal Sanctuary**, **Katerniaghat**, and **Hastinapur WLS**.
- **India's Firsts & Demographics:** **Most populous state** in India (Census 2011); shares boundaries with **8 states + 1 UT (Delhi)** (maximum in India); **Narora** Atomic Power Station; **Bhimrao Ambedkar / Motilal Nehru / Banaras Hindu University (1916)**; **Prayagraj** hosts India's oldest High Court seat after Calcutta/Bombay/Madras (est. 1866 at Agra, shifted 1869).
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Barasingha (Swamp Deer)**; State Bird: **Sarus Crane**; State Tree: **Ashoka (*Saraca asoca*)**; State Flower: **Palash (*Butea monosperma*)**.

### 27. Uttarakhand

**Capital:** Dehradun (Winter/Provisional Capital) and **Gairsain / Bhararisain** (Summer Capital, declared 2020)  
**Formation:** November 9, 2000 (27th State of India carved out of Uttar Pradesh as *Uttaranchal*; renamed **Uttarakhand** on January 1, 2007)  
**Official Language(s):** Hindi (Primary Official Language; 8th Schedule) and **Sanskrit** (Second Official Language since 2010; 8th Schedule — **first Indian state to accord official status to Sanskrit**)  
**Other Languages:** Kumaoni, Garhwali, Jaunsari, Bhotia, Nepali, Punjabi, Urdu

#### Brief History
- Ancient land of **Kedarkhand** (Garhwal) and **Manaskhand** (Kumaon); Ashokan rock edict at **Kalsi** (Dehradun, 3rd c. BCE); ruled by the **Kunindas** (1st c. BCE).
- **Katyuri Dynasty (7th–11th century CE):** Unified Kumaon and Garhwal from Kartikeyapura (Joshimath/Baijnath) and built the **Jageshwar** and **Baijnath** temple complexes.
- **Chand Dynasty (Kumaon) & Panwar/Shah Dynasty (Garhwal):** Champawat/Almora became Chand hubs; **Ajay Pal** unified 52 Garhs into Garhwal; **Rani Karnavati** ("Nak-Kati Rani") repulsed Mughal forces under Shah Jahan.
- **Gorkha Rule & British Era (1790–1947):** Gorkha occupation ended after the Anglo-Nepalese War and **Treaty of Sugauli (1815)**; Kumaon & British Garhwal came under British control while **Tehri Garhwal** remained a princely state until merging with India in **1949**.
- **Freedom & Environmental Movements:** **Coolie Begar Movement (1921)** at Bageshwar; **Peshawar Incident (1930)** hero **Chandra Singh Garhwali**; **Chipko Movement (1973)** pioneered by **Gaura Devi** in Reni village (Chamoli) with **Sunderlal Bahuguna** and **Chandi Prasad Bhatt**; **Maiti Movement** (Kalyan Singh Rawat); statehood achieved on **November 9, 2000**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Chholiya | Folk (Martial) | Vigorous Kumaoni sword-and-shield dance performed in wedding processions to ward off evil spirits |
| Pandav Nritya | Folk / Ritual | Traditional Garhwal dance-drama enacting episodes from the *Mahabharata* during winter evenings |
| Langvir Nritya | Folk | Acrobatic Garhwali men's dance performed while balancing atop a long bamboo pole |
| Jhora & Chanchari | Folk | Circular Kumaoni folk dances performed by men and women singing *Jhora* ballads |
| Barada Nati | Folk | Traditional dance of the **Jaunsar-Bawar** region during festivals and weddings |
| Ramman | Folk Ritual Theatre | Annual religious mask-dance drama of **Saloor-Dungra (Chamoli)** honoring Bhumiyal Devta (**UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage, 2009**) |
| Jagar | Folk / Spiritual | Spirit-invocation singing and trance-dance tradition accompanied by *Hurka* and *Thali* |
| Thadiya & Chhapeli | Folk | Courtyard and romantic duet folk dances of Garhwal and Kumaon |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Painting Styles:**
  - **Aipan Art (GI Tag):** Sacred Kumaoni geometric floor and wall art drawn by women with white rice paste (*biswar*) on red ochre (*geru*) backgrounds.
  - **Garhwal School of Pahari Painting:** Flourished in Srinagar (Garhwal) in the 18th century under **Molaram**, famed for delicate lyrical scenes of Krishna, nature, and court life.
- **Handicrafts & GI Tags:**
  - **Ringal Craft (GI):** Eco-friendly hill-bamboo baskets, mats (*mosta*), and winnowing trays.
  - **Thulma (GI):** Coarse hand-woven woolen blankets of higher Himalayan regions.
  - **Bhotia Dann Carpets (GI):** Hand-knotted woolen rugs woven by the Bhotia community with geometric Tibetan motifs.
  - **Tamta Product (GI):** Traditional hand-beaten copper vessels (*gagri, tauli*) crafted by Almora's Tamta artisans.
  - **Uttarakhand Wax Candles (Nainital – GI)**, **Likhai** (Kumaoni wood carving), **Pithora/Pichora** (saffron-red Kumaoni bridal veil), **Bichhu Buti (Nettle) Fabrics (GI)**.
  - **Agro/Food GI Tags:** **Uttarakhand Tejpat (Bay Leaf)**, **Munsyari Rajma**, **Berinag Tea**, **Pahadi Toor Dal**, **Basmati Rice**, **Kumaon Chyura Oil**, **Chaukori Tea**, **Nainital Momos / Bal Mithai** (famous regional sweet, Almora).
- **Folk Instruments:** *Dhol–Damau*, *Ransingha*, *Mashak Baja* (bagpipe), *Hudka*, *Turhi*, *Bhenkora*, *Algorithm/Algoza*.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |

| Shailesh Matiyani | *Borivali se Boribandar Tak*, *Chitthi Rasen* | Hindi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Harela | July (Shravana) & Chaitra | Kumaon festival celebrating green monsoon sprouts, agriculture, and tree plantation |
| Phool Dei | March–April (Chaitra 1) | Children place fresh spring blossoms on doorsteps blessing households |
| Nanda Devi Raj Jat Yatra | August–September (Once every 12 years) | Known as the **"Himalayan Kumbh"**; 280-km sacred pilgrimage to Homkund led by a four-horned ram (*Chausingha Khadu*) |
| Kumbh Mela (Haridwar) | Every 12 years | One of India's 4 Kumbh venues, held on the banks of the Ganga at Har Ki Pauri |
| Uttarayani / Ghughuti Mela | January 14 (Makar Sankranti) | Major fair at **Baghnath Temple, Bageshwar**; site of the 1921 Coolie Begar agitation |
| Bagwal (Devidhura Fair) | August (Raksha Bandhan) | Champawat temple fair famous for ritual mock-stone/fruit throwing between clans |
| Kandali Festival | Once every 12 years | **Bhotia** community festival in Pithoragarh celebrating the blooming of the Kandali plant |
| Butter Festival (Anduri Utsav) | August | Celebrated at **Dayara Bugyal** alpine meadows in Uttarkashi thanking nature |
| Bissu & Magh Mela | April / January | Major harvest and winter festivals of the **Jaunsari** tribe |

#### Tribes
| Tribe | Region | Known For |

| Jaunsari | Jaunsar-Bawar (Chakrata/Dehradun) | Second-largest ST; *Pandav Nritya*, *Barada Nati*, *Magh Mela*, unique polyandrous kinship heritage |
| Buksa (Bhoksa) | Udham Singh Nagar, Nainital, Haridwar | Agrarian Terai tribe claiming descent from Panwar Rajputs |
| Bhotia (Shauka, Tolcha, Marchha, Jad) | Pithoragarh, Chamoli, Uttarkashi | High-altitude Himalayan traders; **Bhotia Dann carpets (GI)**, **Thulma (GI)**, transhumance, and **Kandali** festival |
| Raji (*Banrawat*) (PVTG) | Pithoragarh and Champawat | **Sole PVTG of Uttarakhand** ("People of the Forest"); wooden bowl carving and historic silent barter trade |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Dehradun | Winter capital; **Indian Military Academy (IMA)**, **Forest Research Institute (FRI)**, **Survey of India**, ONGC, Wadia Institute, Doon Basmati |
| Haridwar | One of 7 sacred *Sapta Puri* cities; **Har Ki Pauri**, **Kumbh Mela**, BHEL, Shantikunj |
| Rishikesh | **"Yoga Capital of the World"**; Laxman Jhula, Ram Jhula, Triveni Ghat, Beatles Ashram, river rafting |
| Nainital | "Lake District of India" (Naini Lake); Naina Devi Temple, High Court of Uttarakhand |
| Mussoorie | "Queen of Hills"; **LBSNAA** (IAS training academy), Kempty Falls, Landour (Ruskin Bond) |
| Roorkee | **IIT Roorkee** (established 1847 as Thomason College — **Asia's oldest engineering institution**), Upper Ganga Canal |
| Pantnagar | **G.B. Pant University of Agriculture and Technology** (1960 — **India's first agricultural university**, cradle of Green Revolution) |
| Almora | Cultural capital of Kumaon; **Kasardevi Temple** (Van Allen Belt anomaly), **Bal Mithai**, Tamta copperware |
| Haldwani | Commercial gateway to Kumaon |
| Tehri (New Tehri) | **Tehri Dam** on the Bhagirathi–Bhilangana confluence (**tallest dam in India**, 260.5 m) |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Chota Char Dham (Badrinath, Kedarnath, Gangotri, Yamunotri) | Adi Shankaracharya / Ancient & medieval | Four holiest Himalayan shrines representing Vishnu, Shiva (Jyotirlinga), Ganga, and Yamuna |
| Jageshwar Group of Temples (Almora) | Katyuri Dynasty / 7th–12th c. CE | Cluster of **125+ stone Nagara temples** amidst deodar groves; one of India's earliest *Nagesh* Shaivite centres |
| Tungnath Temple (Rudraprayag) | Katyuri / Pandava tradition | **Highest Shiva temple in the world** (3,680 m); part of **Panch Kedar** |
| Katarmal Sun Temple (Almora) | Katyuri King Katarmalla / 9th c. CE | One of India's few ancient Surya temples (second in prominence to Konark) |
| Baijnath Temple (Bageshwar) | Katyuri Rulers / 12th c. CE | Built at the confluence of Gomti and Garur Ganga; famed for Parvati stone sculpture |
| Ashokan Rock Edict (Kalsi, Dehradun) | Emperor Ashoka / c. 250 BCE | Only major Ashokan rock edict in the Himalayan foothills written in Prakrit & Brahmi |
| Hemkund Sahib (Chamoli) | Modern / Sikh pilgrimage (4,632 m) | Highest Gurudwara in the world dedicated to Guru Gobind Singh |
| Patal Bhuvaneshwar (Pithoragarh) | Natural limestone cave temple | Mythological underground cavern dedicated to Lord Shiva |
| Forest Research Institute (Dehradun) | C.G. Blomfield / 1929 | Iconic Greco-Roman colonial heritage building |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| Nanda Devi and Valley of Flowers National Parks | Natural | 1988 (Nanda Devi), extended 2005 (Valley of Flowers) |

*(Note: **Ramman** religious festival and ritual theatre of Garhwal was inscribed on UNESCO's **Intangible Cultural Heritage** list in **2009**).*

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **6 National Parks:** **Jim Corbett NP** (**India's first National Park**, est. 1936 as Hailey NP, site of **Project Tiger launch on April 1, 1973**), **Nanda Devi NP**, **Valley of Flowers NP**, **Rajaji NP**, **Gangotri NP** (largest NP in Uttarakhand), and **Govind Pashu Vihar NP**.
- **Tiger Reserves (2) & Biosphere Reserve (1):** **Corbett TR** & **Rajaji TR**; **Nanda Devi Biosphere Reserve**.
- **Ramsar Site (1):** **Asan Conservation Reserve** (Dehradun, at the Asan–Yamuna confluence — Uttarakhand's first Ramsar site).
- **Geography & Peaks:** **Nanda Devi (7,816 m)** is the highest peak located wholly within India; **Panch Prayag** confluences (Vishnuprayag, Nandaprayag, Karnaprayag, Rudraprayag, **Devprayag** where Alaknanda + Bhagirathi form the **Ganga**); **Gaumukh** (Gangotri glacier) and **Tehri Dam** (highest dam in India).
- **Important Institutes:** **IMA** & **FRI** (Dehradun), **LBSNAA** (Mussoorie), **Nehru Institute of Mountaineering** (Uttarkashi), **Aryabhatta Research Institute of Observational Sciences – ARIES** (Nainital — Devasthal Optical Telescope), **WII** (Wildlife Institute of India, Dehradun).
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Alpine Musk Deer (*Kasturi Mrig*)**; State Bird: **Himalayan Monal**; State Tree: **Burans (*Rhododendron arboreum*)**; State Flower: **Brahma Kamal (*Saussurea obvallata*)**.

### 28. West Bengal

**Capital:** Kolkata (formerly Calcutta; **Capital of British India until 1911**)  
**Formation:** August 15, 1947 (formed upon Partition of Bengal; Cooch Behar merged 1950, Chandannagar merged 1954; reorganized under States Reorganisation Act on **November 1, 1956**)  
**Official Language(s):** Bengali (8th Schedule), English; and **Nepali** (8th Schedule — additional official language in the hill subdivisions of Darjeeling, Kalimpong, and Kurseong)  
**Other Languages:** Hindi, Urdu, Santali, Odia, Punjabi, Kamtapuri, Rajbanshi, Kurmali *(several recognized as additional official languages in respective linguistic belts)*

#### Brief History
- Ancient regions of **Vanga, Gauda, Rarh, and Pundravardhana**; **Shashanka** (7th c. CE) was Bengal's first sovereign king at Karnasuvarna; **Gopala, Dharmapala, and Devapala** built the **Pala Empire** (8th–12th c. CE), patrons of Mahayana Buddhism and **Terracotta/Pala sculpture**.
- **Sena Dynasty (11th–13th c. CE):** Ballala Sena and Lakshmana Sena (whose court poet **Jayadeva** wrote *Gita Govinda*); followed by the **Bengal Sultanate** (Ilyas Shah, Alauddin Husain Shah) and Mughal Subah of Bengal.
- **Colonial Conquest:** **Battle of Plassey (June 23, 1757)** and **Battle of Buxar (1764)** established British East India Company dominance in India, with Calcutta as imperial capital (1772–1911).
- **Bengal Renaissance (19th century):** Led by **Raja Ram Mohan Roy** (Brahmo Samaj, abolition of Sati 1829), **Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar** (Widow Remarriage Act 1856), **Swami Vivekananda** (Ramakrishna Mission 1897), **Bankim Chandra**, and **Rabindranath Tagore**.
- **Freedom Struggle:** **Partition of Bengal (1905)** sparked the **Swadeshi & Boycott Movement** (annulled 1911); revolutionary groups **Anushilan Samiti** and **Jugantar**; heroes: **Khudiram Bose**, **Bagha Jatin**, **Surya Sen** (Chittagong Armoury Raid 1930), **Pritilata Waddedar**, **Matangini Hazra**, **Binoy–Badal–Dinesh**, **Rash Behari Bose**, and **Netaji Subhas Chandra Bose** (Forward Bloc, INA).

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Purulia Chhau | Folk / Semi-Classical Martial | Vigorous masked dance enacting epics (*Mahishasura Mardini*, Ramayana) with acrobatic leaps (**UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage, 2010**) |
| Gaudiya Nritya | Classical (Revived Tradition) | Ancient classical dance tradition of Bengal reconstructed from medieval texts and temple sculptures |
| Baul Dance & Song | Folk / Mystic | Ecstatic singing and dancing by wandering Baul minstrels with *Ektara* and *Duggi* (**UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage, 2005**) |
| Gambhira | Folk (Malda) | Masked devotional and satirical folk dance performed during Chaitra Sankranti in honor of Shiva |
| Brita / Vrita Dance | Folk | Traditional women's thanksgiving dance performed after recovery from illness or wish fulfillment |
| Santhali Dance | Folk / Tribal | Graceful synchronized group dance of the Santal tribe with *Madal* and *Dhamsa* drums |
| Raibenshe | Folk (Martial) | Vigorous athletic dance with bamboo staves, traditionally performed by men of Birbhum/Bardhaman |
| Tusu & Jhumur | Folk | Harvest dances of western Bengal (*Rarh* plateau—Purulia, Bankura, Jhargram) |
| Maruni | Folk | Traditional Nepali dance performed in the Darjeeling–Kalimpong hills |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Music Traditions:** **Rabindra Sangeet** (over 2,200 songs by Tagore), **Nazrul Geeti** (Kazi Nazrul Islam), **Baul Gaan** (Lalon Fakir), **Bhatiali** (river boatmen's songs), **Bhawaiya** (North Bengal bullock-cart drivers' folk songs), **Shyama Sangeet** (Ramprasad Sen), **Kirtan** (Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu); **Bishnupur Gharana** (only classical Hindustani Dhrupad Gharana of Bengal); **Jatra** (popular folk theatre).
- **Painting Styles:**
  - **Kalighat Patachitra:** Iconic 19th-century bold brushwork watercolors on paper from Kalighat (Kolkata), satirizing society and depicting deities (**Bengal Patachitra – GI Tag**).
  - **Bengal School of Art:** Pioneered by **Abanindranath Tagore** (*Bharat Mata*), **Nandalal Bose** (illustrated the **Constitution of India**!), **Jamini Roy** (Kalighat-inspired modernist), and **Ramkinkar Baij**.
  - **Alpana:** Traditional ritual floor art made using white rice paste.
- **Handicrafts & GI Tags:**
  - **Darjeeling Tea (2004–05):** **Very first product in India to receive a GI Tag** ("Champagne of Teas").
  - **Sarees (GI):** **Baluchari** (Bishnupur silk with mythological borders), **Shantipur**, **Dhaniakhali**, **Tangail (GI 2024)**, **Garad (GI 2024)**, and **Korial (GI 2024)** sarees.
  - **Crafts (GI):** **Nakshi Kantha** (quilted running-stitch embroidery), **Bankura Panchmura Terracotta Horse**, **Purulia Chhau Mask**, **Dokra of Bengal (Bikna & Dariapur)**, **Shantiniketan Leather Goods**, **Wooden Mask of Kushmandi**, **Madurkathi** (Midnapore reed mats).
  - **Sweets & Agro (GI):** **Banglar Rasogolla**, **Joynagar Moa**, **Bardhaman Sitabhog & Mihidana**, **Sundarban Honey / Mouban (GI 2024)**, **Kalonunia Rice (GI 2024)**, **Gobindobhog Rice**, **Tulaipanji Rice**, **Malda Fazli, Himsagar & Laxman Bhog Mangoes**.

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Durga Puja | September–October (Ashwin) | Premier 5-day socio-religious festival; **Durga Puja in Kolkata inscribed as UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage (2021)** |
| Kali Puja & Diwali | October–November | Grand midnight Shakta worship across Kolkata, Dakshineswar, Kalighat, and Tarapith |
| Poush Mela | December (7th Poush) | Historic craft, Baul music, and cultural fair started by Debendranath Tagore at **Santiniketan** |
| Basanta Utsav (Dol Jatra) | March (Phalguna Purnima) | Spring festival of colors started by Tagore at Visva-Bharati, Santiniketan |
| Ganga Sagar Mela | January 14–15 (Makar Sankranti) | Second-largest Hindu pilgrimage fair after Kumbh Mela, at **Sagar Island** (Hooghly–Bay of Bengal confluence) |
| Ratha Yatra of Mahesh | June–July | **Second-oldest Rath Yatra in India** (after Puri), held since 1396 CE at Serampore (Hooghly) |
| Kenduli Mela | January (Makar Sankranti) | Annual gathering of **Baul** minstrels at Jaydev Kenduli (Birbhum) |
| Pohela Boishakh (Naba Barsha) | April 14–15 | Bengali New Year |
| Charak Puja & Gajan | April (Chaitra Sankranti) | Folk Shiva-worship festival featuring *Gambhira* and acrobatic devotion |

| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Santal | Purulia, Bankura, Birbhum, Paschim Medinipur, Jhargram | **Largest Scheduled Tribe in West Bengal** (>50% of state STs); **Ol Chiki script** (invented by Pandit Raghunath Murmu), *Sohrai*, *Baha*, and *Karam* festivals |
| Oraon & Munda | Dooars (Jalpaiguri, Alipurduar) and Jangalmahal | Celebrate *Sarhul* and *Karma*; tea-garden and forest communities |

| Birhor (PVTG) | Purulia | Semi-nomadic PVTG known for monkey-catching heritage and rope-making |
| Lepcha & Bhutia | Darjeeling and Kalimpong hills | Buddhist and animist Himalayan communities known for monastic arts and bamboo crafts |
| Rabha & Mech | Dooars (Alipurduar, Jalpaiguri, Cooch Behar) | Sino-Tibetan plains tribes known for handloom weaving and riverine culture |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Kolkata | Capital; "City of Joy", Victoria Memorial, Howrah Bridge, **India's first Metro (1984)** and **first underwater metro tunnel (2024)**, Indian Museum (1814), National Library |
| Darjeeling | "Queen of the Hills"; **Darjeeling Tea (first GI tag)**, Darjeeling Himalayan Railway, **Himalayan Mountaineering Institute** (est. 1954), Tiger Hill |
| Santiniketan (Bolpur) | Home of **Visva-Bharati University** founded by Rabindranath Tagore (1921); Poush Mela, UNESCO World Heritage Site |
| Siliguri | "Gateway to Northeast India" located in the strategic Siliguri Corridor ("Chicken's Neck") |
| Durgapur & Asansol | **"Ruhr of India"**; Durgapur Steel Plant, Raniganj coalfields (**India's oldest coal mine**), Chittaranjan Locomotive Works |
| Kharagpur | **India's first IIT (IIT Kharagpur, 1951)** and one of the world's longest railway platforms |
| Bishnupur (Bankura) | Capital of Malla kings; **terracotta temples**, **Baluchari silk sarees**, Bishnupur Gharana of music |
| Murshidabad | Last capital of independent Nawabs of Bengal; **Hazarduari Palace**, Katra Mosque, silk sarees (**Garad & Korial**) |
| Howrah | **Belur Math**, **Acharya Jagadish Chandra Bose Indian Botanic Garden** (**Great Banyan Tree**), Howrah Junction |
| Haldia | Major riverine port and petrochemical industrial hub |
| Kalimpong | Orchids, monasteries, cheese, and Himalayan trade heritage |
| Malda (English Bazar) | Mango orchards (Fazli/Himsagar), historic ruins of **Gour and Pandua** |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Victoria Memorial (Kolkata) | Designed by Sir William Emerson / 1906–1921 | White Makrana marble Indo-Saracenic museum built in memory of Queen Victoria |
| Howrah Bridge (Rabindra Setu) | Opened 1943 (Cantilever Steel Bridge) | Iconic toll-free balanced cantilever bridge across the Hooghly River connecting Howrah and Kolkata |
| Dakshineswar Kali Temple | Rani Rashmoni / 1855 CE | *Navaratna* (nine-spired) temple on the Hooghly where **Ramakrishna Paramahamsa** served |
| Belur Math (Howrah) | Founded by Swami Vivekananda / 1897 (Temple 1938) | Headquarters of the Ramakrishna Mission; architecture blends Hindu, Islamic, Buddhist, and Christian motifs |
| Indian Museum (Kolkata) | Asiatic Society (Nathaniel Wallich) / 1814 | **Oldest and largest multidisciplinary museum in India** |
| Bishnupur Terracotta Temples | Malla Kings (Raghunath Singha, Bir Hambir) / 17th c. CE | **Rasmancha** (unique pyramidal brick tower), **Jor Bangla Temple**, **Shyamrai** (*Pancharatna*), and **Madanmohan** temples |
| Hazarduari Palace (Murshidabad) | Nawab Nazim Humayun Jah (arch. Duncan MacLeod) / 1837 | "Palace of a Thousand Doors" (900 real, 100 false doors) |
| Adina Mosque & Dakhil Darwaza (Pandua/Gour, Malda) | Sikandar Shah (1373 CE) / Barbak Shah (15th c.) | Adina Mosque was one of the largest medieval mosques in the Indian subcontinent |
| Cooch Behar Palace | Maharaja Nripendra Narayan / 1887 | Classical European Renaissance-inspired royal palace modeled on Buckingham Palace |
| Kalighat Kali Temple & Tarapith | Ancient / Current 1809 (Kalighat) | Kalighat is one of the **51 Shakti Peethas**; Tarapith (Birbhum) is a major Tantric Shakta shrine |
| St. Paul's Cathedral & Fort William (Kolkata) | 1847 / 1781 (New Fort William) | Iconic colonial heritage structures; Fort William serves as HQ of Eastern Command |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| Sundarbans National Park | Natural | 1987 |
| Mountain Railways of India — Darjeeling Himalayan Railway | Cultural | 1999 |
| Santiniketan | Cultural | 2023 (41st UNESCO site of India) |

*(Note: **Durga Puja in Kolkata** [2021], **Chhau Dance** [2010], and **Baul Songs** [2005] are inscribed on UNESCO's **Intangible Cultural Heritage** list).*

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **6 National Parks & 2 Tiger Reserves:** NPs: **Sundarbans**, **Buxa**, **Jaldapara** (famous for **Indian One-Horned Rhinoceros**), **Gorumara**, **Neora Valley**, and **Singalila** (**Red Panda** habitat & **Sandakphu**, highest peak of WB); Tiger Reserves: **Sundarbans** and **Buxa**.
- **2 Ramsar Sites (Largest Area in India):** **Sundarban Wetland** (**India's single largest Ramsar site**, ~4,230 sq km) and **East Kolkata Wetlands** (world-famous natural sewage-treatment fishery system).
- **Biosphere Reserve:** **Sundarbans Biosphere Reserve** (largest tidal halophytic mangrove forest in the world, shared with Bangladesh).
- **Firsts in India:** **First GI Tag** (**Darjeeling Tea**, 2004–05); **First Metro Rail** (**Kolkata Metro**, Oct 1984) & **India's first underwater river metro tunnel** (under Hooghly River, 2024); **First IIT** (IIT Kharagpur, 1951); **Oldest Museum** (Indian Museum, 1814); **Oldest Botanical Garden** (1787, Howrah); **First Nobel Prize for Literature in Asia** (Tagore, 1913).
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Fishing Cat**; State Bird: **White-throated Kingfisher**; State Tree: **Chatim (*Alstonia scholaris*)**; State Flower: **Sheuli / Night-flowering Jasmine (*Nyctanthes arbor-tristis*)**.
