## Union Territories of India

### 29. Andaman and Nicobar Islands

**Capital:** Sri Vijaya Puram *(renamed from Port Blair in September 2024)*  
**Formation:** November 1, 1956 (constituted as a Union Territory under the States Reorganisation Act, 1956)  
**Official Language(s):** Hindi and English *(neither is regionally exclusive; Hindi is in the 8th Schedule)*  
**Other Languages:** Bengali (most widely spoken), Tamil, Telugu, Nicobarese, Malayalam, Kurukh/Sadri, Andamanese dialects

#### Brief History
- Known in ancient times and **Chola inscriptions (11th c. CE)** under Rajendra Chola I as **Tinmaittivu / Nakkavaram**; referenced by Ptolemy and Marco Polo.
- British established a penal settlement in 1789 (Lieutenant Archibald Blair) and permanently after the **Revolt of 1857** (1858) at Port Blair and Viper Island, culminating in the infamous **Cellular Jail ("Kala Pani", 1896–1906)**.
- **World War II & Netaji:** Occupied by Japan (1942–1945); handed over to the **Azad Hind Government**; on **December 30, 1943**, **Netaji Subhas Chandra Bose** hoisted the Indian Tricolour at Gymkhana Ground (Port Blair) and renamed the islands **Shaheed** (Andaman) and **Swaraj** (Nicobar).
- **Recent Renamings:** Ross Island, Neil Island, and Havelock Island were renamed **Netaji Subhas Chandra Bose Dweep**, **Shaheed Dweep**, and **Swaraj Dweep** in December 2018; Mount Harriet became **Mount Manipur** (2021); Port Blair was renamed **Sri Vijaya Puram** in **September 2024** honoring India's maritime and Chola–Srivijaya heritage.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Nicobari Dance | Folk / Tribal | Signature circular group dance of the **Nicobarese** tribe performed under moonlight during the *Ossuary Feast* swaying to traditional songs |
| Andamanese & Onge Tribal Ritual Dances | Tribal | Indigenous dances mimicking hunting, turtle-catching, and forest spirits |
| Settler Folk Dances | Folk | Cultural dances brought by mainland settlers (Bengali, Tamil, Telugu, Ranchi/Oraon, and Punjabi communities) showcased at Island Tourism Festival |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Indigenous Crafts & GI Tags:**
  - **Nicobarese Hodi (GI Tag):** Traditional handcrafted **outrigger wooden canoe** built by the Nicobarese people for inter-island navigation and racing.
  - **Andaman Karen Musley Rice (GI Tag):** Aromatic indigenous rice variety cultivated by the Karen community settled in Mayabunder/North Andaman.
- **Handicrafts:** **Shell Craft** (ornaments and lamps from Mother-of-Pearl, trochus, and conch shells), **Bamboo and Cane Craft**, **Coconut Shell Carving**, **Palm-leaf Mats**, **Andaman Padauk Woodwork** (fine furniture and carvings).
- **Music:** Rich oral songs of the Great Andamanese, Onge, and Nicobarese tribes accompanying canoe races and ancestral rites, alongside settler folk music.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Vinayak Damodar Savarkar | *Majhi Janmathep* (*My Transportation for Life*), *Kamala* (composed/inspired during Cellular Jail imprisonment) | Marathi |
| Barindra Kumar Ghosh | *The Tale of My Exile* (memoir of Cellular Jail captivity) | English / Bengali |
| Diwan Singh Kalepani | *Vagde Pani* and patriotic Punjabi poetry written while imprisoned in the Andamans | Punjabi |
| Pankaj Sekhsaria | *Islands in Flux*, *The Last Wave* (non-fiction & fiction on island ecology and tribes) | English |
| Samir Acharya | Environmental and tribal conservation essays on the Andaman & Nicobar archipelago | English |
| Indigenous Oral Traditions | Mythological chants, ocean-lore, and creation epics of the Andamanese and Nicobarese tribes | Onge / Nicobarese / Great Andamanese |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Island Tourism Festival | January (10 days) | Mega annual cultural festival at Sri Vijaya Puram showcasing tribal arts, handicrafts, and aquatic sports |
| Ossuary Feast (*Ma-a-la-ha* / Pig Festival) | Periodic | Major ancestral veneration festival of the **Nicobarese** featuring **Nicobari dance** and *Hodi* canoe races |
| Subhash Mela | January 23 | Celebrated on **Swaraj Dweep** marking Netaji Subhas Chandra Bose's birth anniversary |
| Beach Festival & Block Mela | April / Jan–Feb | Tourism and rural craft fairs at Corbyn's Cove and Diglipur |
| Mariamman Festival & Durga Puja | Seasonal | Major religious festivals celebrated across settler communities |

#### Tribes
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Great Andamanese (PVTG) | Strait Island (formerly across Great Andaman) | **Negrito** group; speak distinct Andamanese languages, dwindling population carefully protected |
| Onge (PVTG) | Little Andaman (Dugong Creek) | **Negrito** hunter-gatherers skilled in outrigger canoe crafting and honey collection |
| Jarawa (PVTG) | South and Middle Andaman western coast | **Negrito** forest hunter-gatherers living in protected Jarawa Tribal Reserve |
| Sentinelese (PVTG) | **North Sentinel Island** | **Negrito** tribe living in complete voluntary isolation; strictly protected under "eyes-on, hands-off" policy |
| Nicobarese | Car Nicobar and Nicobar Islands | **Mongoloid** tribe; **largest tribal group in A&N**, **Hodi canoe (GI)**, *Ossuary Feast*, predominantly Christian & progressive |
| Shompen (PVTG) | **Great Nicobar** interior forests | **Mongoloid** semi-nomadic hunter-gatherers living in the Great Nicobar Biosphere Reserve |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City / Island | Famous For |
|---------------|------------|
| Sri Vijaya Puram (Port Blair) | UT Capital; **Cellular Jail**, **Chatham Saw Mill**, Samudrika Naval Marine Museum, Corbyn's Cove |
| Swaraj Dweep (Havelock Island) | **Radhanagar Beach** (Blue Flag certified, among Asia's best beaches), scuba diving |
| Shaheed Dweep (Neil Island) | Coral reefs, natural rock arch (*Howrah Bridge*), Bharatpur and Laxmanpur beaches |
| Netaji Subhas Chandra Bose Dweep (Ross Island) | Former British administrative HQ ruins, Japanese bunkers, deer sanctuary |
| Baratang Island | Famous for **Limestone Caves** and India's only **Mud Volcanoes** |
| Barren Island | **Only active volcano in India (and South Asia)** |
| Diglipur (North Andaman) | **Saddle Peak** (highest peak of A&N, 732 m), Ross & Smith twin islands joined by a natural sandbar |
| Great Nicobar (Campbell Bay & Indira Point) | **Indira Point** (southernmost point of India at 6°45' N), Great Nicobar Biosphere Reserve |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Cellular Jail (National Memorial, Sri Vijaya Puram) | British Administration / 1896–1906 | Iconic 7-winged radial prison (*Kala Pani*) designed for solitary confinement of freedom fighters (Savarkar, Batukeshwar Dutt, Barindra Ghosh) |
| Chatham Saw Mill (Sri Vijaya Puram) | British Administration / 1883 | **One of Asia's oldest and largest sawmills**, connected to main island via Chatham Bridge |
| Viper Island Ruins | British / 1867 | Site of the old jail and gallows (where Sher Ali Afridi was hanged) before Cellular Jail was built |
| Mount Manipur (formerly Mount Harriet) | Colonial / Renamed Oct 2021 | Renamed to honor Manipur freedom fighters including Maharaja Kulachandra exiled here after the **1891 Anglo-Manipur War** |
| Netaji Subhas Chandra Bose Dweep Ruins | British / 19th century | Ruins of the Chief Commissioner's house, bakery, church, and WWII Japanese bunkers |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| *None inscribed yet* | — | — |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Archipelago Geography:** **572+ islands** (approx. 38 inhabited); **10-Degree Channel** separates **Andaman** from **Nicobar**; **Duncan Passage** separates South Andaman from Little Andaman; **8-Degree / 6-Degree (Great Channel)** lies south of Great Nicobar toward Sumatra (Indonesia).
- **National Parks (9) & Biosphere Reserve:** Houses **9 National Parks** including **South Button Island NP** (**smallest National Park in India**, ~0.03 sq km), **Saddle Peak**, **Mount Manipur**, **Mahatma Gandhi Marine (Wandoor)**, **Rani Jhansi Marine**, **Campbell Bay**, **Galathea Bay**, **North Button**, and **Middle Button**; **Great Nicobar Biosphere Reserve** is part of UNESCO's MAB network.
- **Volcanoes & Extremes:** **Barren Island** (India's only active volcano) & **Narcondam Island** (dormant volcano, home to endemic Narcondam Hornbill); **Indira Point** (formerly Pygmalion Point) on Great Nicobar is India's **southernmost point** (partially submerged in the 2004 Tsunami).
- **Judiciary & Command:** Under the jurisdiction of the **Calcutta High Court** (Circuit Bench at Sri Vijaya Puram); home to India's **only Tri-Service Theatre Command** (Andaman and Nicobar Command, est. 2001).
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Dugong (Sea Cow)**; State Bird: **Andaman Wood Pigeon**; State Tree: **Andaman Padauk**; State Flower: **Pyinma (*Lagerstroemia hypoleuca*)**.

### 30. Chandigarh

**Capital:** Chandigarh *(serves as the joint capital of both **Punjab** and **Haryana**, as well as the UT)*  
**Formation:** November 1, 1966 (constituted as a Union Territory under the **Punjab Reorganisation Act, 1966**; planned city inaugurated in **1953** by Pt. Jawaharlal Nehru)  
**Official Language(s):** English *(Sole Official Language)*  
**Other Languages:** Punjabi (8th Schedule), Hindi (8th Schedule), Haryanvi

#### Brief History
- Ancient Harappan presence nearby (Chandigarh Sector 17 Harappan finds) and medieval **Manimajra** principality (18th-century fort).
- After Partition (1947), India lost Lahore to Pakistan and needed a modern capital for East Punjab; initial master plan by **Albert Mayer & Matthew Nowicki** (1949) was redesigned and executed by Swiss-French modernist architect **Le Corbusier** (alongside Pierre Jeanneret, Jane Drew, and Maxwell Fry).
- Inaugurated on **October 7, 1953** by President Dr. Rajendra Prasad as independent India's **first planned city** ("The City Beautiful").
- When Haryana was carved out of Punjab on **November 1, 1966**, Chandigarh became a **Union Territory** functioning as the **shared capital** of both states.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Bhangra | Folk | High-energy Punjabi men's harvest dance performed to *Dhol* beats during Baisakhi and celebrations |
| Giddha | Folk | Vibrant Punjabi women's circular dance accompanied by rhythmic clapping and humorous *boliyan* couplets |
| Jhumar & Sammi | Folk | Graceful rhythmic community and women's traditional dances popular in Chandigarh's Punjabi cultural events |
| Dhamal & Teej Dances | Folk | Regional Haryana–Punjab folk dances featured at cultural festivals |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Modernist Architecture & Design:** World-famous Brutalist concrete urban architecture by **Le Corbusier** and custom **Chandigarh Chairs / Jeanneret Furniture** designed by Pierre Jeanneret.
- **Recycled Sculpture Art:** **Rock Garden of Chandigarh**, created by **Nek Chand Saini** using urban and industrial waste (broken ceramics, bangles, electrical fittings, stones).
- **Museum Collections:** **Government Museum and Art Gallery (Sector 10)** houses one of India's richest collections of **Gandhara sculptures**, **Pahari & Rajasthani miniature paintings**, and modern Indian art.
- **Crafts & GI Context:** Showcases regional **Phulkari** embroidery and Punjabi *Jutis*; Chandigarh holds no standalone exclusive GI tag (though Sector-17 bodies facilitated the multi-state *Phulkari* GI registration).

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Kashmiri Lal Zakir | *Karmavali*, *Ab Mujhe Sone Do* (Padma Shri Urdu novelist based in Chandigarh) | Urdu |
| Mohan Singh Diwana | *History of Punjabi Literature*, Sufi & Persian scholarship (Panjab University) | Punjabi / English |
| V.N. Tiwari | *Garaj Ton Footpath Teek* (Sahitya Akademi Punjabi poet, Panjab University) | Punjabi |
| Kishwar Desai | *Witness the Night* (Chandigarh Literary Society Chairperson) | English |
| Madhur Kapila | *Satwan Swar*, *Bhatke Raahi* (art critic and novelist) | Hindi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Chandigarh Rose Festival | February–March | Asia's premier floral festival held at **Zakir Hussain Rose Garden** featuring 1,600+ rose varieties |
| Chandigarh Carnival | November | Annual cultural, float, and art festival at Leisure Valley |
| Mango Festival | June–July | Joint tourism festival celebrating hundreds of mango varieties in the Chandigarh–Pinjore belt |
| Baisakhi & Lohri | April 13–14 / January 13 | Major Punjabi harvest and winter bonfire festivals |
| Literati (Chandigarh Literature Festival) | November | Annual literary festival hosted at Sukhna Lake Club |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
- *No Scheduled Tribes (STs) are notified under Article 342 of the Constitution of India in the Union Territory of Chandigarh.*

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City / Zone | Famous For |
|-------------|------------|
| Sector 1 (Capitol Complex) | **UNESCO World Heritage Site** housing the Punjab & Haryana High Court, Secretariat, Legislative Assembly, and Open Hand Monument |
| Sector 17 (The Plaza) | Pedestrian-only commercial heart and cultural plaza of Chandigarh |
| Sector 10 & Sector 14 | Cultural & educational hubs: **Government Museum & Art Gallery** (Sector 10) and **Panjab University** (Sector 14) |
| Sector 16 | **Zakir Hussain Rose Garden** (Asia's largest rose garden) |
| Manimajra | Historic pre-Chandigarh township featuring **Manimajra Fort** and proximity to Mansa Devi shrine |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Chandigarh Capitol Complex (Sector 1) | Le Corbusier / 1950s | Comprises **Palace of Assembly**, **High Court**, **Secretariat**, **Tower of Shadows**, and **Geometric Hill** |
| Open Hand Monument | Le Corbusier / Planned 1950s (Completed 1985) | 26-m high rotating metal sculpture; official emblem of Chandigarh symbolizing *"Open to give, open to receive"* |
| Rock Garden | Nek Chand Saini / Started 1957 (Opened 1976) | 40-acre sculpture garden crafted entirely from recycled ceramic, glass, and industrial waste |
| Sukhna Lake | Le Corbusier & P.L. Verma / 1958 | 3 sq km rain-fed man-made reservoir at the Shivalik foothills |
| Zakir Hussain Rose Garden | Dr. M.S. Randhawa / 1967 | Asia's largest rose garden spanning 30 acres |
| Gandhi Bhawan (Panjab University) | Pierre Jeanneret / 1962 | Iconic modernist lotus-shaped auditorium surrounded by a water pool |
| International Dolls Museum (Sector 23) | Est. 1985 | Houses over 250 traditional dolls and puppets from across the world |

| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Sector Numbering Quirk:** **Sector 13** was originally omitted by Le Corbusier (superstition / Western numbering convention), though Manimajra areas were later designated Sector 13 in 2020.
- **Urban Planning:** Designed on a **human-body analogy** by Le Corbusier—Head (Capitol Complex), Heart (Sector 17 City Centre), Lungs (Leisure Valley & green belts), Circulatory system (**7 Vs** road hierarchy).
- **Protected Area:** **Sukhna Wildlife Sanctuary** (adjoining Sukhna Lake) and **City Bird Sanctuary** (Sector 21); **0 National Parks**.
- **Shared Institutions:** Seat of the **Punjab and Haryana High Court**, **PGIMER**, and **Panjab University**.
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Indian Grey Mongoose**; State Bird: **Indian Grey Hornbill**; State Tree: **Mango Tree**; State Flower: **Dhak / Palash (*Butea monosperma*)**.

### 31. Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu

**Capital:** Daman *(Silvassa is the largest city and administrative hub of Dadra and Nagar Haveli)*  
**Formation:** **January 26, 2020** *(formed by merging the two former UTs of Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu under the Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu (Merger of Union Territories) Act, 2019)*  
**Official Language(s):** Gujarati (8th Schedule), Hindi (8th Schedule), Marathi (8th Schedule), and English  
**Other Languages:** Konkani, Warli, Dhodia, Kokni, Damanese Portuguese Creole (*Língua da Casa*)

#### Brief History
- **Portuguese Colonial Era:** Portuguese acquired **Daman** (1559) and **Diu** (1535 via treaty with Sultan Bahadur Shah of Gujarat) and **Dadra & Nagar Haveli** (1783–1785 via treaty with the Marathas).
- **Liberation of Dadra & Nagar Haveli (August 2, 1954):** Liberated from Portuguese rule by volunteers of the **Azad Gomantak Dal**, RSS, and United Front of Goans; functioned as **Free Dadra and Nagar Haveli** until formally becoming an Indian UT on **August 11, 1961** (following the landmark **1960 ICJ *Right of Passage* ruling**).
- **Liberation of Daman & Diu (December 19, 1961):** Liberated alongside Goa by the Indian Armed Forces under **Operation Vijay**; administered together as *Goa, Daman and Diu* until Goa attained statehood on **May 30, 1987**, when Daman and Diu became a separate UT.
- **Merger (January 26, 2020):** Merged into a single Union Territory (**DNHDD**), bringing India's tally to **28 States and 8 Union Territories**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Tarpa Dance | Folk / Tribal | Signature **Warli** circular dance where dancers lock arms and circle a player blowing the *Tarpa* (dried bottle-gourd wind instrument) |
| Bhawada Dance | Folk / Tribal | Traditional mask dance of the **Kokna and Warli** tribes performed after harvest |
| Dhol Dance | Folk / Tribal | Energetic tribal dance performed to large double-headed drums during religious festivals |
| Tur and Thali Dance | Folk / Tribal | Accompanied by *Tur* (clay drum) and bronze *Thali* (plate) |
| Mando & Verdigao | Folk | Graceful Indo-Portuguese folk dances performed in Daman and Diu |
| Siddi Dhamal | Folk / Tribal | Dynamic drum-and-rhythm dance of the Afro-Indian **Siddi** community around Diu |
| Vira Dance | Folk | Traditional celebratory dance of Daman's coastal fishing community |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Painting Styles:**
  - **Warli Painting (GI Tag):** Ancient tribal geometric wall art created predominantly by Warli women using **white rice paste** on mud-ochre backgrounds; motifs include triangles, circles, squares, **Tarpa dancers**, and *Palaghata* (fertility goddess). *(The official GI registration for Warli Painting explicitly covers Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu alongside Maharashtra and Gujarat).*
- **Handicrafts:** **Bamboo and Cane Weaving**, **Khajur (Palm-leaf) Mats and Baskets**, **Marine Shell Craft** (Daman & Diu), **Tortoise-shell/Wood Carving heritage**, and **Leather Craft**.
- **Music:** Traditional tribal *Tarpa*, *Ghangli*, *Madal*, and *Kahali* music in Silvassa/Khanvel, contrasted with **Portuguese-influenced choral ballads and hymns** in Daman and Diu.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Indigenous Tribal Oral Epics | *Tarpa* songs, Warli & Dhodia creation myths, *Dhavle* wedding chants | Warli / Dhodia / Kokni |
| Damanese Creole Oral Literature | *Língua da Casa* folk ballads, proverbs, and maritime hymns | Damanese Creole / Portuguese |
| Purushottam Shripad Lele | *Dadra and Nagar Haveli: Past and Present* (historical documentation) | English / Marathi |
| Manuel Maria Barbosa du Bocage | 18th-century Portuguese poet who served and wrote in Daman | Portuguese |
| Regional Gujarati & Marathi Writers | Folkloristics and socio-cultural poetry linking South Gujarat and Konkan | Gujarati / Marathi |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Mavaliya Festival | October–November (Post-Diwali) | Major Warli tribal harvest thanksgiving festival |
| Diwaso | July–August | Traditional **Dhodia** tribal monsoon festival featuring clay dolls and community feasts |
| Bohada | April–May | Three-day tribal mask-procession festival in Dadra & Nagar Haveli |
| Waghbaras | October–November | Tribal ritual honoring the tiger (*Waghdeo*) to protect cattle |
| Nariyal Purnima | August (Shravana Purnima) | Major coastal festival marking the reopening of fishing season in Daman and Diu |
| Diu Festival & Christmas / Carnival | December–February | Cultural beach festival and vibrant Portuguese-influenced Christian celebrations |
| Tarpa Festival | December–January | Annual tourism showcase of tribal arts and Tarpa dance in Silvassa |

#### Tribes
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Warli | Dadra & Nagar Haveli (Silvassa, Khanvel) | **Largest tribal group in DNH**; **Warli Painting (GI)**, *Tarpa* dance, *Waghbaras* tiger worship |
| Dhodia | Dadra & Nagar Haveli | Agrarian tribal community known for *Diwaso* festival and *Dhodia* dialect |
| Kokna / Kokni | Dadra & Nagar Haveli | Known for *Bhawada* mask dance and bamboo crafts |
| Dubla (Halpati) | Dadra & Nagar Haveli & Daman | Traditional coastal/agrarian tribal community |
| Kathodia / Katkari (PVTG) | Dadra & Nagar Haveli | **PVTG** historically associated with catechu (*katha*) extraction and forest foraging |
| Siddi | Diu & nearby Saurashtra coast | Afro-Indian tribal community known for **Siddi Dhamal** dance |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Daman | UT Capital; **Moti Daman Fort**, **Nani Daman Fort**, Devka and Jampore beaches, coastal trade |
| Silvassa | Largest city and commercial hub of Dadra & Nagar Haveli; **Tribal Cultural Museum**, Vanganga Lake Garden |
| Diu | Island off Gujarat's Saurashtra coast; **Diu Fort**, **Naida Caves**, **Gangeshwar Mahadev**, **INS Khukri Memorial**, **100% daytime solar milestone** |
| Khanvel | Eco-tourism resort town along the **Sakartod River**, **Madhuban Dam** backwaters |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Diu Fort | Portuguese Governor Nuno da Cunha / 1535–1541 | Massive sea-facing stone fortress with triple moat, canons, and lighthouse overlooking the Arabian Sea |
| St. Paul's Church (Diu) | Jesuits / 1601–1610 | Ornate **Portuguese Baroque** church with intricate carved wooden façade |
| Fort of Moti Daman | Portuguese / 1559 | 10-bastioned polygonal fort enclosing the **Cathedral of Bom Jesus** and **Chapel of Our Lady of Rosary** |
| Fort of St. Jerome (Nani Daman) | Portuguese / 1614–1627 | Houses **Our Lady of the Sea Church** and a giant statue of St. Jerome |
| Naida Caves (Diu) | Quarried by Portuguese / 16th c. | Labyrinthine interconnected sunlit sandstone caves outside Diu Fort |
| Gangeshwar Mahadev Temple (Diu) | Ancient (Pandava legend) | Coastal cave shrine where **five Shiva Lingams** are naturally washed by sea waves |
| INS Khukri Memorial (Diu) | Indian Navy | Honors Captain Mahendra Nath Mulla and sailors lost on INS *Khukri* during the **1971 Indo-Pak War** |
| Church of Our Lady of Piety (Silvassa) | Portuguese / 1889 | Prominent colonial church in Dadra and Nagar Haveli |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| *None inscribed yet* | — | — |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Geography:** Consists of **3 geographically separate enclaves**—**Dadra & Nagar Haveli** (landlocked between Gujarat & Maharashtra), **Daman** (on Gujarat's southern coast), and **Diu** (an island off Saurashtra/Gir Somnath coast).
- **100% Solar Milestone:** **Diu** became the **first city/district in India to run 100% on solar energy during daytime**.
- **Protected Areas:** **Dadra and Nagar Haveli Wildlife Sanctuary** (Satmalia Deer Park & Vasona Lion Safari) and **Fudam Bird Sanctuary (Diu)**; **0 National Parks**.
- **High Court & River:** Falls under the jurisdiction of the **Bombay High Court**; **Daman Ganga** is the primary river (site of **Madhuban Dam**).
- **GI Tag:** **Warli Painting** covers Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu in its official GI geographical area.
- **State Symbols:** Notified UT symbols historically vary across merged units; commonly cited for the former DNH/DD units include **Blue Rock Pigeon** (former DNH bird) and **Four-horned Antelope / Chowsingha** & **Daman Ganga flora** (official unified DNHDD state symbols remain under administrative consolidation).

### 32. Delhi (NCT)

**Capital:** New Delhi *(also Capital of India)*  
**Formation:** Made a Union Territory on November 1, 1956; designated **National Capital Territory (NCT) of Delhi** via the **69th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1991** (Articles 239AA & 239AB; enforced February 1, 1992)  
**Official Language(s):** Hindi (First Official Language; 8th Schedule), Punjabi (Second Official Language; 8th Schedule), and Urdu (Second Official Language; 8th Schedule — enacted under the *Delhi Official Languages Act, 2000/2003*)  
**Other Languages:** English (widely used in administration/judiciary), Haryanvi, Bhojpuri, Maithili, Bengali, Tamil

#### Brief History
- Mythological **Indraprastha** of the Pandavas (archaeological excavations at **Purana Qila** revealed Painted Grey Ware layers); Ashokan Minor Rock Edict found at **Srinivaspuri (East of Kailash)**.
- **Seven Historical Cities of Delhi:**
  1. **Lal Kot / Qila Rai Pithora** (Tomar ruler Anangpal Tomar II, 1052 CE & Chauhan ruler Prithviraj Chauhan).
  2. **Siri** (Alauddin Khilji, 1303).
  3. **Tughlaqabad** (Ghiyasuddin Tughlaq, 1321).
  4. **Jahanpanah** (Muhammad bin Tughlaq, 1326).
  5. **Firozabad / Feroz Shah Kotla** (Firoz Shah Tughlaq, 1354).
  6. **Dinpanah / Shergarh** (Humayun & Sher Shah Suri, 1533–1545).
  7. **Shahjahanabad / Old Delhi** (Shah Jahan, 1638–1648).
- **Modern New Delhi (8th City):** Capital shift from Calcutta announced at the **Delhi Durbar (December 12, 1911)** by King George V; designed by **Sir Edwin Lutyens** and **Sir Herbert Baker** and inaugurated on **February 13, 1931**.
- **Freedom Struggle:** Epicenter of **1857 Revolt** under **Bahadur Shah Zafar**; **1929 Central Legislative Assembly bombing** by **Bhagat Singh and Batukeshwar Dutt**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Kathak | Classical | Major institutional hub (**Kathak Kendra**, Shriram Bharatiya Kala Kendra); **Pt. Birju Maharaj** established *Kalashram* in Delhi |
| Cosmopolitan Classical & Folk Showcase | Classical & Folk | As the national capital, hosts all 8 classical dances and regional folk dances at the **Republic Day Parade**, **Sangeet Natak Akademi**, and **Bharat Rang Mahotsav** |
| Sufi Qawwali & Devotional Movement | Folk / Mystic | Weekly spiritual *Qawwali* assemblies at **Hazrat Nizamuddin Dargah** (associated with Amir Khusrau) |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Hindustani Classical Music:** Home to the **Delhi Gharana**—the **oldest Khayal Gharana** of Hindustani music (associated with *Miyān Achpal*, Ustad Chand Khan, Ustad Iqbal Ahmad Khan); **Qawwali**, *Khayal*, *Tarana*, and *Sitar/Tabla* traditions pioneered by **Amir Khusrau** at the Delhi Sultanate court.
- **Painting & Fine Arts:** **Delhi Kalam** (Late Mughal miniature painting), **National Gallery of Modern Art (NGMA)**, **Lalit Kala Akademi**, **Triveni Kala Sangam**, and **Kiran Nadar Museum of Art**.
- **Handicrafts & GI Status:** **Zardozi embroidery**, **Kundan & Meenakari jewellery** (Dariba Kalan), **Attar/Perfumery** (Old Delhi), **Kite making**, **Terracotta & Pottery** (Kumhar Gram, Uttam Nagar). *(Delhi holds no standalone exclusive GI tag, though it falls within the **Basmati Rice** multi-state GI region).*

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Amir Khusrau | *Nuh Sipihr*, *Tughlaq Nama*, *Hasht Bihisht*, Hindavi *paheliyan* | Persian / Hindavi |
| Mirza Ghalib | *Diwan-e-Ghalib*, *Dastambu* (lived in Ballimaran, Old Delhi) | Urdu / Persian |
| Mir Taqi Mir & Sheikh Ibrahim Zauq | *Kulliyat-e-Mir*; classical *Ghazals* of Mughal Delhi | Urdu |
| Bahadur Shah Zafar | *Kulliyat-e-Zafar* (last Mughal emperor-poet) | Urdu |
| Maulana Abul Kalam Azad | *India Wins Freedom*, *Ghubar-e-Khatir* | Urdu / English |
| Khushwant Singh | *Train to Pakistan*, *Delhi: A Novel* | English |
| Anita Desai & Arundhati Roy | *Clear Light of Day* (Desai); *The God of Small Things* (Roy, Booker Prize 1997) | English |
| Bhisham Sahni & Nirmal Verma | *Tamas* (Sahni); *Kavve aur Kala Pani* (Verma, Jnanpith 1999) | Hindi |
| William Dalrymple | *City of Djinns*, *The Last Mughal* | English |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Phool Walon Ki Sair | October–November | Historic secular **floral-pankha festival** at Mehrauli uniting Jogmaya Temple and Qutbuddin Bakhtiar Kaki Dargah (revived since Akbar Shah II) |
| Republic Day Parade & Beating Retreat | January 26–29 | Grand national cultural-military spectacle at **Kartavya Path** and **Vijay Chowk** |
| Jashn-e-Rekhta | December | World's largest Urdu literary and cultural festival |
| Qutub Festival | November–December | Classical music and dance concerts illuminated against Qutb Minar |
| New Delhi World Book Fair | January–February | Asia's largest book fair hosted at **Bharat Mandapam (Pragati Maidan)** |
| Surajkund / Dilli Haat Craft Melas | Seasonal | Year-round pan-Indian craft and culinary exhibitions |
| Ramlila & Dussehra | September–October | Traditional theatrical performances at Ramlila Maidan and Red Fort grounds |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
- *No Scheduled Tribes (STs) are notified under Article 342 of the Constitution of India in the National Capital Territory of Delhi.*

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City / Zone | Famous For |
|-------------|------------|
| Old Delhi (Shahjahanabad) | **Red Fort**, **Jama Masjid**, **Chandni Chowk**, **Khari Baoli** (Asia's largest wholesale spice market), Ghalib Ki Haveli |
| New Delhi (Lutyens' Delhi / Central Vista) | **Rashtrapati Bhavan**, **New Parliament Building**, **Samvidhan Sadan**, **India Gate**, **National War Memorial**, **Kartavya Path**, Connaught Place |
| Mehrauli | **Qutb Complex**, **Iron Pillar**, Mehrauli Archaeological Park, Yogmaya Temple |
| Nizamuddin | **Humayun's Tomb**, **Hazrat Nizamuddin Auliya Dargah**, Khan-i-Khanan's Tomb |
| Hauz Khas & Pragati Maidan | Alauddin Khilji's reservoir & Firoz Shah's Madrasa; **Bharat Mandapam** (**2023 G20 Summit venue**) |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Qutb Minar Complex & Iron Pillar | Qutbuddin Aibak (1199) & Iltutmish (1220) / Iron Pillar: Chandragupta II (4th–5th c. CE) | 72.5-m tallest brick minaret; **Quwwat-ul-Islam Mosque** (India's earliest mosque); **rust-resistant Gupta Iron Pillar** |
| Humayun's Tomb | Haji Begum (architect Mirak Mirza Ghiyas) / 1569–70 | **First garden-tomb in India** (*Charbagh* layout), architectural precursor to the Taj Mahal |
| Red Fort (Lal Qila) | Shah Jahan / 1638–1648 | Red sandstone palace-fortress; PM hoists the National Flag from Lahori Gate on August 15; houses Diwan-i-Khas & Aurangzeb's Moti Masjid |
| Jama Masjid | Shah Jahan / 1650–1656 | **One of India's largest mosques** (can hold 25,000 worshippers) |
| Purana Qila (Old Fort) | Humayun & Sher Shah Suri / 1533–1545 | Houses **Qila-i-Kuhna Mosque** and **Sher Mandal** (library where Humayun fatally fell) |
| Jantar Mantar (New Delhi) | Maharaja Jai Singh II of Jaipur / 1724 | Monumental masonry astronomical observatory featuring *Samrat Yantra* |
| Safdarjung's Tomb & Lodhi Gardens | 1754 / 15th–16th c. Sayyid & Lodi tombs | Safdarjung's Tomb is the last grand Mughal garden-tomb; Lodhi Gardens houses **Bara Gumbad** and **Sikandar Lodi's Tomb** |
| India Gate & National War Memorial | Sir Edwin Lutyens (1931) / NWM inaugurated 2019 | WWI memorial arch on Kartavya Path; **Amar Jawan Jyoti** merged into the NWM eternal flame |
| Rashtrapati Bhavan & New Parliament Building | Edwin Lutyens (1929) / Bimal Patel (2023) | Former Viceroy's House (340 rooms, **Amrit Udyan**); triangular **New Parliament** houses the **Sengol** |
| Lotus Temple (Bahá'í House of Worship) | Fariborz Sahba / 1986 | Iconic 27 marble-clad lotus-petal architectural landmark open to all faiths |
| Swaminarayan Akshardham Temple | BAPS / 2005 | Massive carved pink sandstone and marble cultural temple complex on Yamuna bank |
| Gurdwara Bangla Sahib & Gurdwara Sis Ganj Sahib | 1783 (Baghel Singh) | Sacred Sikh shrines linked to Guru Har Krishan and Guru Tegh Bahadur's martyrdom |

|------|------|-------------------|

| Red Fort Complex | Cultural | 2007 |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Constitutional Polity:** **69th Constitutional Amendment Act (1991)** created a **70-member Legislative Assembly** and restricted the Council of Ministers to **10% (7 ministers including CM)**; Police, Public Order, and Land remain with the Union Government (LG); **239AA** & **239AB**.
- **Biodiversity & Sanctuaries:** **Asola Bhatti Wildlife Sanctuary** (Southern Ridge), **Yamuna Biodiversity Park**, **National Zoological Park**, and **Okhla Bird Sanctuary** (bordering UP); **0 National Parks**.
- **India's Firsts & Milestones:** Hosted **1st Asian Games (1951)**, **1982 Asian Games**, **2010 Commonwealth Games**, and **2023 G20 Summit** (*Bharat Mandapam*); **Supreme Court of India** (Jan 28, 1950); **Delhi High Court** (1966); **Pradhanmantri Sangrahalaya** (Teen Murti Bhavan); **National Museum** (Janpath).
- **State Symbols:** State Animal: **Nilgai**; State Bird: **House Sparrow**; State Tree: **Flamboyant / Gulmohar** *(widely cited)*; State Flower: **Alfalfa**.

### 33. Jammu and Kashmir

**Capital:** Srinagar (Summer Capital) and Jammu (Winter Capital)  
**Formation:** Princely State acceded to India on October 26, 1947; reorganized as a **Union Territory (with a Legislative Assembly)** on **October 31, 2019** under the *Jammu and Kashmir Reorganisation Act, 2019* (following abrogation of Article 370 on August 5, 2019)  
**Official Language(s):** Kashmiri (8th Schedule), Dogri (8th Schedule), Urdu (8th Schedule), Hindi (8th Schedule), and English *(under the Jammu and Kashmir Official Languages Act, 2020)*  
**Other Languages:** Gojri, Pahari, Punjabi, Shina, Bhaderwahi, Kishtwari

#### Brief History
- **Ancient & Buddhist Legacy:** Mauryan Emperor **Ashoka** founded **Srinagari**; Kushan Emperor **Kanishka** convened the **4th Buddhist Council** at **Kundalvana** (Kashmir, 1st c. CE), leading to Mahayana Buddhism's spread; **Burzahom** is a major Neolithic pit-dwelling site and **Manda** (Akhnoor) is the northernmost Harappan site.
- **Golden Hindu Dynasties:** **Karkota Dynasty** ruler **Lalitaditya Muktapida** (8th c. CE) built the **Martand Sun Temple**; **Utpala Dynasty** (Avantivarman, Queen Didda); **Lohara Dynasty** saw **Kalhana** compose *Rajatarangini* (1148 CE).
- **Sultanate & Mughal Eras:** **Shah Mir Dynasty** peaked under **Zain-ul-Abidin ("Budshah" / Akbar of Kashmir, 1420–1470)** who promoted arts, paper-making, and communal harmony; **Akbar** annexed Kashmir in **1586**, and Jahangir/Shah Jahan laid out the **Mughal Gardens**.
- **Sikh & Dogra Rule (1819–1947):** Maharaja Ranjit Singh annexed Kashmir (1819); **Maharaja Gulab Singh** acquired J&K via the **Treaty of Amritsar (1846)** founding the **Dogra Dynasty**; **Maharaja Hari Singh** signed the **Instrument of Accession on October 26, 1947**; bifurcated into UTs of **J&K** and **Ladakh** on **October 31, 2019**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Rouf | Folk | Iconic Kashmiri women's dance performed in two facing rows during Eid and spring festivities |
| Kud | Folk | Energetic Jammu hillside night dance performed around bonfires thanking *Lok Devtas* (local deities) |
| Dumhal | Folk | Traditional ceremonial dance of the **Wattal** tribe men wearing tall conical beaded caps and carrying a sacred banner |
| Bhand Pather | Folk Theatre/Dance | Traditional Kashmiri satirical folk drama blending witty dialogue, music, and dance |
| Hafiza Nagma & Bacha Nagma | Folk | Graceful Kashmiri classical-folk dances accompanied by *Sufiana Kalam* and *Santoor* |
| Hikat, Chajja & Geetru | Folk | Traditional Kashmiri girls' spinning game-dance (*Hikat*) and Jammu Lohri/Dogra dances (*Chajja, Geetru*) |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Music:** **Sufiana Kalam** (Kashmiri classical Sufi music featuring *Santoor*, *Kashmiri Saz*, *Setar*—pioneered globally by **Pt. Shivkumar Sharma** and **Pt. Bhajan Sopori**), **Chakri** (folk singing with *Sarangi* and *Tumbaknari*), **Wanwun** (wedding songs), **Bhakhan** & **Karkaan** (Dogra folk singing).
- **Painting Styles:** **Basohli Painting (GI Tag)**—earliest and boldest school of **Pahari Miniature Painting** (17th–18th c., Kathua), noted for vivid primary colors, *Gita Govinda* & *Rasamanjari* themes, and beetle-wing highlights; **Jammu & Poonch Kalams**.
- **Handicrafts & GI Tags:**
  - **Textiles & Shawls (GI):** **Kashmiri Pashmina**, **Kani Shawl** (woven using wooden bobbins called *kanis*), **Basohli Pashmina (Jammu)**, **Kashmiri Sozni Craft** (fine needlework), **Kashmiri Hand-Knotted Carpet (*Kal Baffi*)**.
  - **Wood & Decorative Crafts (GI):** **Kashmiri Walnut Wood Carving**, **Kashmir Papier-Mâché**, **Khatamband** (geometric interlocking walnut/deodar wooden ceilings), **Rajouri Chikri Wood Craft**.
  - **Agro/Food (GI):** **Kashmir Saffron** (grown on **Karewa** alluvial plateaus in Pampore—world's highest-altitude saffron), **Kishtwar Saffron**, **Bhaderwah Rajmash**, **Ramban Sulai Honey**, **Ramban Anardana**, **Udhampur Kaladi** (dense milk cheese), **Mushqbudji Rice**, **Kashmiri Gucchi Mushroom**.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Kalhana | *Rajatarangini* (1148 CE — oldest systematic historical chronicle of India) | Sanskrit |
| Abhinavagupta | *Tantraloka*, *Abhinavabharati* (Kashmir Shaivism philosophy & aesthetics) | Sanskrit |
| Somadeva & Bilhana | *Kathasaritsagara* (Somadeva); *Vikramankadevacharita* (Bilhana) | Sanskrit |
| Lal Ded (Lalleshwari) | *Lalla Vakhs* (14th-c. mystic poetry shaping Kashmiri language) | Kashmiri |
| Nund Rishi (Sheikh Noor-ud-din Wali) | *Shruks* (patron saint of Kashmir & Rishi order) | Kashmiri |
| Habba Khatoon | *Lol* romantic lyrics ("Nightingale of Kashmir") | Kashmiri |
| Mahjoor & Zinda Kaul | *Gulshan Watan Chu Sony* (Mahjoor); *Sumran* (Zinda Kaul, 1st Kashmiri Sahitya Akademi) | Kashmiri |
| Rehman Rahi | *Siyah Rood Jaren Manz* (**first Kashmiri Jnanpith Award, 2004**) | Kashmiri |
| Padma Sachdev | *Meri Kavita Mere Geet* (Sahitya Akademi, "First Modern Dogri Poetess") | Dogri / Hindi |
| Dinu Bhai Pant | *Guttlun* (pioneer of modern Dogri poetry) | Dogri |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Herath (Maha Shivratri) | February–March | Prime multi-day Kashmiri Pandit festival featuring *Vatuk Pooja* and walnut rituals |
| Kheer Bhawani Mela | May–June (Jyeshtha Ashtami) | Major pilgrimage at **Tulmulla (Ganderbal)**; devotees check the color of the sacred spring water |
| Tulip Festival | March–April | Held at **Indira Gandhi Memorial Tulip Garden, Srinagar** (**Asia's largest tulip garden**) |
| Amarnath Yatra | June–August (Shravana) | Sacred pilgrimage to the naturally formed ice *Shivlinga* in Pahalgam/Baltal |
| Navratri & Vaishno Devi Festival | Sept–Oct & March–April | Pilgrimages to **Vaishno Devi Shrine (Katra)** and cultural events in Jammu |
| Lohri & Baisakhi | January 13 / April 13 | Major Jammu harvest celebrations marked by *Chajja* dancing |
| Jhiri Mela | October–November | Annual Jammu fair honoring farmer-martyr **Baba Jitto** |
| Eid-ul-Fitr, Eid-ul-Adha & Shikara Festival | Lunar / Summer | Major community celebrations and Dal Lake Shikara boat parades |

#### Tribes
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Gujjar & Bakarwal | Pir Panjal, Poonch, Rajouri, Anantnag, Baramulla | **Largest STs in J&K**; semi-nomadic pastoralists practicing seasonal transhumance to alpine *dhoks*/*margs*; *Gojri* language |
| Gaddi & Sippi | Bhaderwah, Kishtwar, Udhampur, Kathua | Traditional transhumant shepherds of the Chenab and Pir Panjal valleys |
| Pahari Ethnic Group, Paddari Tribe, Koli, Gadda Brahmin | Rajouri, Poonch, Kishtwar (Paddar), Baramulla | Newly notified as Scheduled Tribes under the **Constitution (J&K) ST Order (Amendment) Act, 2024** (bringing J&K's ST list to 16 tribes) |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City | Famous For |
|------|-----------|
| Srinagar | Summer Capital; **Dal & Nigeen Lakes**, Houseboats, Shikaras, Mughal Gardens, Tulip Garden, Hazratbal |
| Jammu | Winter Capital; **"City of Temples"**, Raghunath Temple, Bahu Fort, Mubarak Mandi Palace |
| Katra | Base camp for **Shri Mata Vaishno Devi Shrine** (Trikuta Hills) |
| Pampore | **"Saffron Town of India"**; Karewa saffron fields |
| Gulmarg & Pahalgam | World-famous ski resort & highest cable car (*Gulmarg Gondola*); scenic valley & Amarnath base (*Pahalgam*) |
| Anantnag & Avantipur | Ancient **Martand Sun Temple** and **Avantiswami Temple** ruins |
| Sopore | **"Apple Town of India"** (horticulture hub) |
| Basohli (Kathua) | Historic birthplace of **Basohli Paintings (GI)** and **Basohli Pashmina (GI)** |
| Udhampur | Northern Command HQ of Indian Army; **Kaladi cheese (GI)** |
| Bhaderwah & Kishtwar | "Mini Kashmir" (Rajmash – GI); Kishtwar National Park & sapphire deposits |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Martand Sun Temple (Anantnag) | Karkota King Lalitaditya Muktapida / 8th c. CE | Monumental Kashmiri stone temple blending Gandhara, Gupta, and Greco-Roman motifs |
| Avantiswami Temple (Avantipur) | King Avantivarman (Utpala) / 9th c. CE | Ornate twin Vaishnava–Shaiva stone temple complex on the Jhelum bank |
| Shankaracharya Temple (Srinagar) | Ancient / Gopadari Hill | Revered hilltop Shiva shrine overlooking Dal Lake |
| Mughal Gardens (Shalimar, Nishat, Chashme Shahi, Pari Mahal) | Jahangir (1619), Asaf Khan (1633), Shah Jahan, Dara Shikoh | Terraced Persian *Charbagh* gardens fed by Dal Lake springs (**UNESCO Tentative List**) |
| Hazratbal Shrine & Jamia Masjid (Srinagar) | 17th c. / Sultan Sikandar (1394) | Hazratbal houses *Moi-e-Muqqadas* (holy relic); Jamia Masjid features **378 deodar wooden pillars** |
| Shri Mata Vaishno Devi Shrine (Katra) | Ancient cave shrine | One of India's most visited Shakti pilgrimages inside Trikuta Hills |
| Raghunath Temple & Bahu Fort (Jammu) | Gulab Singh & Ranbir Singh (1857) / Raja Bahulochan | Largest temple complex in Jammu and historic fortress overlooking the Tawi River |
| Hari Parbat Fort & Mubarak Mandi Palace | Akbar / Atta Muhammad Khan (1808) & Dogra Kings | Historic hilltop citadel in Srinagar and former Dogra royal residence in Jammu |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| *None inscribed yet* *(Mughal Gardens of Kashmir and Neolithic Burzahom are on the UNESCO Tentative List)* | — | — |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **4 National Parks:** **Dachigam NP** (last global stronghold of the **Hangul / Kashmir Stag**), **Kishtwar NP**, **Kazinag NP** (**Markhor** habitat), and **City Forest (Salim Ali) NP**.
- **5 Ramsar Sites:** **Wular Lake** (**largest freshwater lake in India**), **Hokersar (Hokera) Wetland**, **Surinsar–Mansar Lakes**, **Hygam Wetland**, and **Shallbugh Wetland**.
- **Engineering & Archaeology Landmarks:** **Chenab Rail Bridge** (Reasi — **world's highest railway arch bridge** at 359 m); **Anji Khad Bridge** (**India's first cable-stayed rail bridge**); **Dr. Syama Prasad Mookerjee (Chenani–Nashri) Tunnel**; **Manda** (northernmost Harappan site) & **Burzahom** (Neolithic pit dwellings).
- **Karewa Formations:** Lacustrine glacial clay deposits in Kashmir Valley ideal for **Saffron (GI)** cultivation.
- **State Symbols:** UT Animal: **Kashmir Stag (Hangul)**; UT Bird: **Kalij Pheasant**; UT Tree: **Chinar (*Platanus orientalis*)**; UT Flower: **Common Rhododendron**.

### 34. Ladakh

**Capital:** Leh *(originally 2 districts—Leh & Kargil; **5 new districts announced in August 2024**: **Nubra, Sham, Changthang, Zanskar, and Drass**, taking the total to **7 districts**)*  
**Formation:** October 31, 2019 (constituted as a Union Territory without a legislature under the *Jammu and Kashmir Reorganisation Act, 2019*)  
**Official Language(s):** English, Hindi (8th Schedule), Urdu (8th Schedule), Bhoti, and Purgi *(officially notified via the **Ladakh Official Languages Regulation, 2025** on June 2, 2025)*  
**Other Languages:** Ladakhi, Balti, Shina, Brokskat (Dardic), Changthang Tibetan dialects

#### Brief History
- Ancient **Maryul** and **Zhangzhung** crossroads along the **Silk Route**; influenced by Kushan, Kashmiri, and Tibetan Buddhism (**Rinchen Zangpo** founded 11th-century temples like Alchi).
- **Namgyal Dynasty (1460–1842):** Founded by Lhachen Bhagan; reached its zenith under **Sengge Namgyal ("The Lion King", 1616–1642)** who built **Leh Palace** and **Hemis Monastery**.
- **Dogra Conquest (1834–1842):** General **Zorawar Singh Kahluria** conquered Ladakh and Baltistan for Maharaja Gulab Singh; Ladakh became part of J&K under the **1846 Treaty of Amritsar**.
- **Modern Era:** **1962 Sino-Indian War** (Rezang La hero Major Shaitan Singh), **Operation Meghdoot (1984)** on Siachen Glacier, **Kargil War (1999 – Operation Vijay)**; separated from J&K into an independent **Union Territory on October 31, 2019**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Shondol | Folk (Royal Dance) | Ancient Ladakhi royal dance originally performed for the King of Ladakh; entered the **Guinness World Records** as the largest Ladakhi dance |
| Cham Dance | Monastic / Mask | Sacred Buddhist mask-and-costume dance performed by lamas at **Hemis, Dosmoche, and Losar** festivals symbolizing triumph over evil |
| Jabro Dance | Folk / Tribal | Nomadic dance of the **Changpa** tribe performed by men and women holding hands in a circle |
| Spao Dance | Folk | Traditional Ladakhi warrior dance performed with swords and shields |
| Khatok Chenmo & Koshan | Folk | Royal banquet dance (*Khatok Chenmo*) and traditional horse-polo dance (*Koshan*) accompanied by *Daman–Surna* music |
| Loshon & Mentok Stanmo | Folk | Harvest dance (*Loshon*) and flower-gathering festival dance (*Mentok Stanmo*) |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Painting Styles:** **Ladakh Thangka Painting (GI 2026)** and **Alchi Monastery Murals**—11th–12th century Kashmiri-Gandhara Buddhist frescoes featuring unique indigo and gold palettes.
- **Music:** **Daman & Surna** (kettle drums and oboe played by *Beda* and *Mon* musicians), **Buddhist Chanting of Ladakh** (sacred monastic recitation inscribed on **UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage list, 2012**), and traditional folk ballads (*Gesar* epic songs).
- **Rich GI Tag Portfolio (Foundational + June 2026 Additions):**
  - **Foundational GI Tags (4):** **Ladakh Pashmina** (from Changthangi goats), **Ladakh Raktsey Karpo Apricot** (sweet white-stone apricot), **Ladakh Shingskos (Wood Carving)**, and **Ladakh Seabuckthorn** ("Ladakh Gold" vitamin-C berry).
  - **June 2026 GI Additions (8):** **Ladakh Challi Textiles**, **Ladakh Thigma** (tie-and-dye wool craft), **Ladakh Painting**, **Pabu of Ladakh** (traditional handmade woolen boots), **Ladakh Nambu / Snambu Textile**, **Ladakh Pashmina Textile**, **Ladakh Chilling Metal Work** (copper/silver/brass craft of Chilling village), and **Ladakh Likir Pottery** (black/glazed pottery).

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| *La-dwags rgyal-rabs* | Royal Chronicles of the Kings of Ladakh (17th-c. compilation) | Classical Tibetan / Bhoti |
| *Kesar Saga* | Oral heroic epic of King Gesar of Ling sung across Ladakh | Ladakhi / Bhoti |
| Tashi Rabgias | *History of Ladakh* (*Mar-yul La-dwags kyi sngon rabs*); documented hundreds of folk songs | Ladakhi / Tibetan / English |
| Kacho Sikander Khan Sikander | *Qadeem Ladakh*, *Ladakh in the Mirror of Her Folklores* | Urdu / English |
| Akhone Asgar Ali Basharat | Pioneering Balti poetry and Tibetic linguistic preservation | Balti |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Hemis Tsechu | June–July (5th Tibetan month) | Ladakh's largest monastic festival celebrating **Guru Padmasambhava**; huge silk *Thangka* displayed once every 12 years |
| Losar | December–January | Ladakhi Buddhist New Year celebrated with *Cham* dances, illuminations, and Metho processions |
| Sindhu Darshan Festival | June (Guru Purnima) | Three-day national harmony festival held at Shey Manla on the banks of the **Indus (Sindhu)** River |
| Dosmoche | February | "Festival of the Scapegoat" initiated by Ladakhi kings at Leh, Likir, and Diskit monasteries |
| Korzok Gustor | July–August | Nomadic **Changpa** monastic festival held near **Tso Moriri Lake** |
| Yuru Kabgyat & Phyang Tsedup | June–August | Major masked monastic festivals at Lamayuru and Phyang monasteries |
| Bono-na | Harvest (Oct–Nov) | Traditional thanksgiving festival of the **Brokpa (Dard Aryan)** community in Dha–Hanu |

#### Tribes
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Bot / Boto | Leh, Sham, Zanskar | **Largest Scheduled Tribe in Ladakh**; predominantly Tibetan Buddhists preserving *Bhoti* script and monastic culture |
| Changpa | **Changthang Plateau** | Nomadic high-altitude pastoralists rearing **Changthangi Pashmina goats** and living in yak-hair *Rebo* tents |
| Purigpa & Balti | Kargil, Drass, Suru Valley, **Turtuk** | Shia Muslim Tibetic tribes; Purgi & Balti languages, *Nauroz* and *Muharram* observances, apricot farming |
| Brokpa / Drokpa (Dard / Shin) | Dha–Hanu, Garkhon, Batalik ("Aryan Valley") | Unique Indo-Aryan pastoralists famed for colorful floral headgear, *Bono-na* festival, and grape wine |
| Mon, Beda & Garra | Across Ladakh | Traditional artisan/musician tribes: *Beda* & *Mon* (Daman–Surna musicians) and *Garra* (blacksmiths) |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| City / Region | Famous For |
|---------------|------------|
| Leh | UT Capital; **Leh Palace**, **Shanti Stupa**, **Hall of Fame**, Magnetic Hill, gateway to Nubra & Pangong |
| Kargil | Second-largest town; Suru Valley, gateway to Zanskar and **Kargil War Memorial** |
| Drass | "Gateway to Ladakh"; **second coldest inhabited place in the world**, Kargil War Memorial (Tololing/Tiger Hill) |
| Nubra Valley | **Hunder cold desert sand dunes**, **double-humped Bactrian camels**, Diskit Monastery, **Turtuk** (last northern Balti village) |
| Zanskar (Padum) | Famous for **Chadar Trek** (frozen Zanskar River), **Phugtal** & Karsha monasteries |
| Hanle (Changthang) | **India's first Dark Sky Reserve** and **Indian Astronomical Observatory** (world's highest optical-IR telescope sites) |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Hemis Monastery | Stagsang Raspa / Rebuilt 1672 by Sengge Namgyal | Largest and wealthiest **Drukpa Kagyu** monastery in Ladakh |
| Alchi Monastery (*Choskor*) | Rinchen Zangpo / 11th century CE | Oldest surviving Buddhist monastic complex famous for unique Kashmiri-style woodwork and murals |
| Thiksey Monastery | Palden Sherab / 15th century CE | 12-story hilltop complex resembling Tibet's Potala Palace; houses a 49-ft **Maitreya Buddha** |
| Leh Palace | King Sengge Namgyal / c. 1600 CE | Nine-story stone-and-mud royal palace overlooking Leh |
| Diskit Monastery (Nubra) | Changzem Tserab Zangpo / 14th c. CE | Oldest and largest monastery in Nubra Valley; features a 106-ft outdoor **Maitreya Buddha** statue |
| Lamayuru Monastery | Rinchen Zangpo / 11th c. CE | Famous for bizarre eroded "Moonland" topography |
| Phugtal Monastery (Zanskar) | Gangsem Sherap Sampo / 12th c. CE | Dramatic honeycomb-like monastery built into a natural limestone cliff cave |
| Shanti Stupa (Leh) | Gyomyo Nakamura & Japanese Buddhists / 1991 | White-domed peace pagoda enshrining relics of the Buddha |
| Mulbekh Chamba & Kargil War Memorial (Drass) | Kushan–7th c. / Indian Army (1999) | Mulbekh features a 9-m rock-cut Maitreya Buddha; Drass honors 1999 Operation Vijay martyrs |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| *None inscribed yet* | — | — |

*(Note: **Buddhist Chanting of Ladakh** was inscribed on UNESCO's **Intangible Cultural Heritage** list in **2012**).*

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Largest UT & Cold Desert:** **Largest Union Territory in India by area** (~59,146 sq km administered); India's premier cold-desert ecosystem where **over 79% of the population belongs to Scheduled Tribes**.
- **Largest National Park in India:** **Hemis National Park** (4,400 sq km) is **India's largest National Park** and the **Snow Leopard Capital of the World**; Wildlife Sanctuaries: **Changthang Cold Desert WLS** and **Karakoram (Nubra–Shyok) WLS**.
- **Ramsar Sites (2):** **Tso Moriri** and **Tso Kar Wetland Complex** (breeding ground of the **Black-necked Crane**).
- **World Records & Science:** **Umling La Pass** (19,024 ft — **world's highest motorable road**, built by BRO under Project Himank), **Khardung La**, **Siachen Glacier** (world's highest battlefield), **Hanle Dark Sky Reserve** (**India's first Dark Sky Reserve**), **Puga Valley** (geothermal energy site), and **Zoji La Tunnel**.
- **2024–2026 Governance & GI Updates:** **5 new districts** announced in Aug 2024 (total **7 districts**); **Ladakh Official Languages Regulation, 2025** added Bhoti & Purgi; **12 GI tags** including 8 added in June 2026.
- **State Symbols:** UT Animal: **Snow Leopard**; UT Bird: **Black-necked Crane** *(State Tree and Flower not yet officially notified)*.

### 35. Lakshadweep

**Capital:** Kavaratti  
**Formation:** November 1, 1956 (formed as the UT of *Laccadive, Minicoy and Amindivi Islands* under the States Reorganisation Act, 1956; renamed **Lakshadweep** on **November 1, 1973**)  
**Official Language(s):** Malayalam (8th Schedule) and English  
**Other Languages:** **Mahl** (a dialect of Dhivehi written in *Thaana* script, spoken natively in **Minicoy Island**), Jeseri (Island Malayalam dialect)

#### Brief History
- Early settlements linked to the **Chera Dynasty** (legend of last Chera ruler Cheraman Perumal) and Tamil Sangam maritime trade routes; Chola inscriptions refer to the islands as *Munnir-palam*.
- **Arrival of Islam (7th century CE):** **Hazrat Ubaidullah** preached Islam in the islands (buried at **Andrott**, where the oldest Juma Mosque stands); almost 96% of inhabitants are Sunni Muslims while uniquely preserving Kerala's matrilineal **Marumakkathayam** property inheritance system.
- **Arakkal Kingdom & Colonial Era:** Governed by the **Arakkal royal family of Kannur** (Kerala's only Muslim dynasty); Tipu Sultan gained Amindivi in 1787; passed to British East India Company control (1792–1908) and administered under Madras Presidency until becoming a UT on **November 1, 1956**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Lava Dance | Folk | Signature vibrant dance of **Minicoy** men wearing red trousers, white robes, and feathered headgear, dancing briskly to *Dhol* drums |
| Kolkali | Folk | Popular rhythmic men's group dance performed in circles clicking painted wooden sticks |
| Parichakali | Folk (Martial) | Traditional martial dance performed with wooden swords and shields mimicking combat steps |
| Oppana | Folk | Traditional women's bridal dance and clap-song performance during island weddings |
| Daffmuttu | Folk / Devotional | Group dance performed to circular tambourines (*duff*) reciting Arabic/Malayalam devotional verses |
| Bandiya Dance | Folk | Traditional colorful water-pot dance performed by Minicoy women |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Music:** Rich oral **Jeseri and Mahl sea-faring ballads**, *Mappila Pattu* variants, wedding songs, and **Kolkali/Daffmuttu** choral rhythms.
- **Art & Handicrafts:** **Coir Weaving and Coconut-based Crafts** (coir rope, mats, carved **coconut shell** lamps and utensils), **Marine/Tortoise Shell Craft**, **Traditional Boat Building** (*Odam* cargo vessels and Minicoy *Masodi* tuna-fishing boats), and intricate **Islamic wood carving** (exemplified by Kavaratti's Ujra Mosque).
- **GI Focus:** **Lakshadweep Coconut Jaggery** (progressed through IP India GI registry via Organic Coconut Producers Society, Kavaratti) and 100% organic certified coconut oil/copra.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Oral *Mappila* & *Maliku* Traditions | Maritime folk songs, *Kolkali* chants, and Minicoy ballads | Jeseri (Malayalam) / Mahl |
| Jazeem Sherin | *Pehla Kadam* (early Hindi poetry anthology originating from Lakshadweep) | Hindi |
| Regional Island Chroniclers | Prose and folklore documenting matrilineal *Marumakkathayam* society and coral ecology | Malayalam / Mahl |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Eid-ul-Fitr & Eid-ul-Adha | Islamic Lunar Calendar | Major community-wide island celebrations with *Lava, Kolkali*, and traditional feasts |
| Milad-un-Nabi & Muharram | Islamic Lunar Calendar | Celebrated with devotional gatherings, *Moulid* recitations, and *Daffmuttu* |
| Lakshadweep Formation Day | November 1 | Celebrates the 1956 UT formation and 1973 renaming with boat races and cultural fairs |
| Independence & Republic Day | August 15 / January 26 | Celebrated across islands with traditional *Kolkali* and *Lava* displays |

#### Tribes
| Tribe | Region | Known For |
|-------|--------|-----------|
| Inhabitants of Lakshadweep (Scheduled Tribes — Koyas, Malmis, Melacheris; Minicoy: Manikfans, Thakrufans, Thakrus, Raveris) | All 10–11 inhabited islands | Nearly **95% of Lakshadweep's population is notified as Scheduled Tribes** under the Constitution; renowned for **matrilineal (*Marumakkathayam*) kinship**, *Taravad* ancestral houses, *Mas* (dried tuna) making, and *Lava/Kolkali* traditions |

#### Important Cities and What They Are Famous For
| Island / Settlement | Famous For |
|---------------------|------------|
| Kavaratti | UT Capital; **Ujra Mosque**, Marine Aquarium, lagoon water sports, desalination plant |
| Agatti | Houses **Lakshadweep's only operational airport** |
| Minicoy (*Maliku*) | Second-largest and southernmost inhabited island; **Mahl language**, **1885 Lighthouse**, **Lava Dance**, tuna-canning |
| Andrott | **Largest island in Lakshadweep**; **Juma Mosque & Hazrat Ubaidullah Shrine** |
| Bangaram & Kadmat | Uninhabited/eco-tourism coral atolls renowned for scuba diving and lagoons |
| Bitra | **Smallest inhabited island** in Lakshadweep |
| Pitti Island (*Pakshi Pitti*) | Uninhabited coral islet designated as a **Bird Sanctuary** (breeding ground of terns) |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Ujra Mosque (Kavaratti) | Sheikh Mohammad Qasim / 17th c. | Famous for **intricately carved driftwood ceilings** and a spring well believed to have curative powers |
| Juma Mosque & Hazrat Ubaidullah Shrine (Andrott) | 7th century CE | **Oldest mosque in Lakshadweep**, housing the tomb of Islamic missionary Saint Ubaidullah |
| Minicoy Lighthouse | British Administration / 1885 | Historic 300-ft coral-stone lighthouse guiding ships along the **9-Degree Channel** |
| Golden Jubilee Museum (Kavaratti) | UT Administration | Houses medieval Buddha sculptures excavated on Andrott/Minicoy islands and maritime heritage |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| *None inscribed yet* | — | — |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Smallest Union Territory:** Area of just **32 sq km** comprising **36 coral islands/atolls** (10–11 inhabited) in the Arabian Sea; formed entirely of **coral deposits (atolls)**.
- **Channels (Extremely High-Yield):**
  - **8-Degree Channel:** Separates **Minicoy** from the **Maldives**.
  - **9-Degree Channel:** Separates **Minicoy** from the main Lakshadweep islands (Suheli/Kalpeni/Kavaratti).
  - **11-Degree Channel:** Separates **Amindivi** islands (north) from **Laccadive/Cannanore** islands (south).
- **100% Organic UT:** Declared **India's first 100% Organic Union Territory** in **2020** (entire agricultural area certified organic under Participatory Guarantee System).
- **Protected Area & Judiciary:** **Pitti Bird Sanctuary** (sole wildlife sanctuary; **0 National Parks**); falls under the jurisdiction of the **Kerala High Court** (Ernakulam).
- **State Symbols:** UT Animal: **Butterflyfish (*Chaetodon falcula*)**; UT Bird: **Sooty Tern / Brown Noddy**; UT Tree: **Breadfruit Tree (*Artocarpus altilis*)**; UT Flower: **Neelakurinji (*Strobilanthes kunthiana*)**.

### 36. Puducherry

**Capital:** Puducherry (formerly Pondicherry)  
**Formation:** *De facto* transfer from France on **November 1, 1954**; *de jure* transfer on **August 16, 1962**; integrated as a Union Territory via **14th Constitutional Amendment Act, 1962** and **Government of Union Territories Act, 1963**; officially renamed from Pondicherry to **Puducherry** in **2006**  
**Official Language(s):** Tamil (8th Schedule), Malayalam (used in Mahe; 8th Schedule), Telugu (used in Yanam; 8th Schedule), and English *(French also holds special cultural/educational recognition)*  
**Other Languages:** French, Urdu, Hindi

#### Brief History
- **Ancient Port of Arikamedu (*Poduke*):** Flourished (2nd c. BCE – 2nd c. CE) as a major **Indo-Roman maritime trading port** exporting textiles and beads for Roman wine amphorae; later ruled by **Pallavas, Cholas, Pandyas, Vijayanagara**, and Gingee Nayaks.
- **French East India Company (1673–1954):** **François Martin** established the French settlement in **1673–74**; under Governor **Joseph François Dupleix** (1742–54), Pondicherry became the rival of British Madras in the **Carnatic Wars**.
- **Sanctuary for Freedom Fighters & Philosophers:** Served as French-ruled refuge where **Subramania Bharati** (1908–18) wrote revolutionary Tamil classics and **Sri Aurobindo** (1910–50) founded **Sri Aurobindo Ashram**; **V. Subbiah** led the local anti-colonial merger movement.
- **Integration:** *De facto* merger with India occurred on **November 1, 1954** (Liberation Day), followed by *de jure* Treaty of Cession ratification on **August 16, 1962**.

#### Classical and Folk Dances
| Dance | Type | Features |
|-------|------|----------|
| Garadi | Folk | Puducherry's signature folk dance enacting the *vanaras* (monkey army) celebrating Lord Rama's victory over Ravana; dancers wear iron rings (*anjali*) on ankles and perform rhythmic stick acrobatics |
| Bharatanatyam | Classical | Deeply rooted Tamil classical dance taught across Pondicherry and Kalakshetra-linked schools |
| Therukoothu, Kummi & Kolattam | Folk | Traditional Tamil street dance-drama and village festive dances performed in Karaikal and Puducherry |
| Oppana & Kolkali (Mahe) / Kuchipudi & Burrakatha (Yanam) | Folk / Classical | Regional dance forms reflecting Mahe's Kerala and Yanam's Andhra heritage |

#### Music and Art Forms
- **Indo-French Architecture:** Iconic grid-planned **White Town (French Quarter)** with mustard-yellow colonial villas, boulevards, and Franco-Tamil hybrid heritage houses.
- **Handicrafts & GI Tags:**
  - **Villianur Terracotta Works (GI Tag):** Traditional handmade clay figurines, horse statues (*Aiyanar* horses), and decorative pottery from Villianur.
  - **Tirukanur Papier-Mâché Craft (GI Tag):** Handcrafted decorative dolls, masks, and containers made from paper pulp, tamarind seed paste, and natural pigments.
  - **Auroville Crafts:** Handmade paper, stoneware studio pottery, incense, and natural-dyed textiles.
- **Music:** Carnatic vocal and *Nadaswaram* traditions alongside Western choral and classical music traditions from French institutions.

#### Literature and Literary Figures
| Author/Poet | Work | Language |
|-------------|------|----------|
| Subramania Bharati | *Panchali Sabatham*, *Kuyil Pattu*, *Kannan Pattu* (composed during his 1908–18 Pondicherry exile) | Tamil |
| Bharathidasan (Kanagasabai Subburathinam) | *Pandian Parisu*, *Kudumba Vilakku*, *Azhagin Sirippu*; penned Puducherry's State Song (*Tamizh Thaai Vazhthu*) | Tamil |
| Sri Aurobindo | *Savitri*, *The Life Divine*, *Synthesis of Yoga* | English |
| Prapanjan (S. Vaidyalingam) | *Vaanam Vasappadum* (Sahitya Akademi 1995), *Mahanadi* | Tamil |
| Vani Dasan | *Tamizhachi*, *Kodimullai* | Tamil |
| Manoj Das & K.D. Sethna (Amal Kiran) | *Amritaphala* (Das, Saraswati Samman); mystical poetry (Sethna) at Sri Aurobindo Ashram | Odia / English |

#### Festivals
| Festival | Time/Month | Significance |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| Bastille Day (French National Day) | July 14 | Celebrates Franco–Indian heritage with parades by retired French military personnel and illuminations |
| Pondicherry International Yoga Festival | January (4–7) | Major annual yoga and wellness festival hosted by Puducherry Tourism |
| Masi Magam | February–March | Grand coastal ritual where deities from temples across Puducherry and Tamil Nadu are taken to the sea for ceremonial bath |
| Villianur Temple Car Festival | May–June | Grand chariot procession at Sri Gokilambal Thirukameswarar Temple |
| Veerampattinam Car Festival | August | Six-week coastal festival at Sengazhuneer Amman Temple |
| Fête de Puducherry | August 15–17 | Cultural showcase coinciding with Indian Independence Day and **De Jure Transfer Day (Aug 16)** |
| St. Teresa's Shrine Feast (Mahe) | October | Major 11-day Christian pilgrimage festival in Mahe |

#### Tribes (if applicable)
- *No Scheduled Tribes (STs) are notified under Article 342 of the Constitution of India in the Union Territory of Puducherry.*

#### Important Cities / Districts and What They Are Famous For
| District (Enclave) | Famous For |
|--------------------|-----------|
| Puducherry (enclaved in Tamil Nadu) | UT Capital; White Town (French Quarter), **Sri Aurobindo Ashram**, **Auroville** (just across border in Viluppuram/Puducherry belt), **Arikamedu**, Promenade Beach |
| Karaikal (enclaved in Tamil Nadu) | Major port and coastal town; **Thirunallar Saneeswaran Temple** (famous Shani shrine), Karaikal Ammaiyar Temple |
| Mahe (enclaved in Kerala) | **Smallest district in India by area (9 sq km)**; picturesque Malabar-coast town, **St. Teresa's Shrine** |
| Yanam (enclaved in Andhra Pradesh) | Located on the **Godavari delta**; blending Telugu and French colonial heritage |

#### Monuments, Heritage Sites and Temples
| Monument/Site | Built By / Period | Significance |
|---------------|-------------------|-------------|
| Arikamedu Archaeological Site | 2nd c. BCE – 2nd c. CE (Excavated by Mortimer Wheeler, 1945) | Ancient **Indo-Roman trading port (*Poduke*)** yielding Roman amphorae, Arretine ware, and glass beads |
| Sri Aurobindo Ashram | Sri Aurobindo & Mirra Alfassa ("The Mother") / 1926 | World-renowned spiritual community in White Town |
| Matrimandir & Auroville | Founded 1968 by The Mother (arch. Roger Anger) | Universal experimental township; **Matrimandir** is a giant golden geodesic sphere |
| Basilica of the Sacred Heart of Jesus | French Missionaries / 1908 | Iconic **Gothic Revival** red-and-white church featuring rare stained-glass panels |
| Aayi Mandapam (Bharathi Park) | Napoleon III Era / 1850s | Greco-Roman white monument honoring *Aayi*, a courtesan who dug a water reservoir for Krishnadevaraya's thirsty troops |
| French War Memorial & Old Lighthouse | 1938 / 1836 | Landmarks along Goubert Avenue (Promenade Beach) |
| Manakula Vinayagar Temple | Pre-French (17th c.) | Historic Ganesha temple featuring a golden spire and painted ceiling murals |
| Eglise de Notre Dame des Anges | 1855 | Peach-and-white church housing a rare oil painting of Our Lady of the Assumption gifted by Emperor Napoleon III |

#### UNESCO World Heritage Sites
| Site | Type | Year of Inscription |
|------|------|-------------------|
| *None inscribed yet* | — | — |

#### High-Yield RRB NTPC Facts
- **Unique Geography:** Comprises **4 non-contiguous districts surrounded by 3 different states**—**Puducherry & Karaikal** (in Tamil Nadu), **Mahe** (in Kerala), and **Yanam** (in Andhra Pradesh); **Mahe (9 sq km)** is **India's smallest district by area**.
- **Legislature & Judiciary:** One of three UTs (with Delhi and J&K) having its own **Legislative Assembly and Council of Ministers**; falls under the jurisdiction of the **Madras High Court**.
- **Protected Areas:** **Oussudu Lake (Ousteri) Bird Sanctuary** (shared with Tamil Nadu); **0 National Parks**.
- **GI Tags (2):** **Villianur Terracotta Works** and **Tirukanur Papier-Mâché Craft**.
- **State Symbols:** UT Animal: **Indian Palm Squirrel**; UT Bird: **Asian Koel**; UT Tree: **Bael / Vilva Tree (*Aegle marmelos*)**; UT Flower: **Nagalinga / Cannonball Flower (*Couroupita guianensis*)**.

## Quick Reference Master Tables

### Classical Dances of India
| Dance | State | Key Feature |
|-------|-------|-------------|
| Bharatanatyam | Tamil Nadu | Oldest classical dance, temple dance tradition |
| Kathak | Uttar Pradesh | Mughal influence, intricate footwork and spins |
| Kathakali | Kerala | Male dancers, elaborate makeup, Ramayana/Mahabharata stories |
| Odissi | Odisha | Tribhangi pose, based on temple sculptures |
| Kuchipudi | Andhra Pradesh | Dance-drama tradition, Tarangam on brass plate |
| Manipuri | Manipur | Raas Leela, gentle fluid movements |
| Mohiniyattam | Kerala | Female solo, graceful swaying |
| Sattriya | Assam | Vaishnavite tradition, recognized in 2000 |

| Mysore | Karnataka | Gold leaf work, muted colors |
| Kerala Murals | Kerala | Temple murals, mythological themes |
| Thangka | Sikkim / Ladakh | Tibetan Buddhist scroll paintings |
| Cheriyal | Telangana | Scroll painting, narrative storytelling |

## Exam Strategy
1. **Classical dances + states** — Almost guaranteed 1–2 questions every shift
2. **Monuments + builders** — Very frequent; know builder, year, and location
3. **Folk dances** — Know at least 20 state-dance pairs
4. **UNESCO sites** — Know all 42 with their states and type
5. **State-wise GI Tags** — Increasingly asked in recent papers
6. **Tribes + festivals** — Focus on NE India states and tribal areas
7. **Painting styles** — Know the state and medium for each
8. **National Parks** — Know at least 15 major ones with their states and famous animals
